# Certification: engine support catalog

Which DSL effect types the engine actually supports, and the precedent set by
each card certified so far. This is the reference for **step 4** of the
`handle-certify-card` skill: check every effect on the card against the lists
below before deciding whether the engine can already play it.

This catalog lives here, and NOT in the skill prompt, on purpose. It grows by
one entry per certified card; when it lived inline in
`.claude/commands/handle-certify-card.md` it pushed that prompt past the 128KB
`MAX_ARG_STRLEN` limit on a single `execve` argument, and every certification
began failing instantly with "Argument list too long". Keep new precedent here.

See also `docs/card-effects-dsl.md` for the DSL reference itself.

## Fully implemented

- `add-constraint` `constraint: "region-shortcut"` (Ash Mountains tw-194) — a company-bound constraint for the "movement enhancer" family of end-of-org resource short-events ("tap a ranger to move as if these region pairs were adjacent … faces an attack … alternatively, if the site moved to is in one of the regions listed, hazard limit -N"). Carries `pairs` (region-name pairs), `requiredSkill`, an optional `attack` (`race`/`strikes`/`prowess`), and `hazardLimitReduction` (`value`/`floor`) — all built from generic `add-constraint` fields already used by other kinds (`race`/`strikes`/`prowess`, `value`/`floor`). Added `company.skills` to the end-of-org `play-target: "company"` filter context (`endOfOrgEligibility`, `legal-actions/organization.ts`), mirroring the existing item/ally `company.skills` field (`organization-events.ts`), so `{ "company.skills": { "$includes": "ranger" } }` gates play on "a company containing a ranger". `companyRegionShortcutPairs` (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) widens region-movement `declare-path` enumeration via `withVirtualAdjacency` (`movement-map.ts`, clones and extends only `regionGraph` — `regionPathEdges`, consulted by org-phase destination reachability, is untouched, so the shortcut only widens which *path* justifies an already-chosen destination, never which destinations org-phase planning offers) while the company still has an untapped character with `requiredSkill`. `checkRegionShortcutUsage` (`mh-steps.ts`, called from `handleRevealNewSite`'s `declare-path` branch) detects when the resolved path actually crosses a granted pair, taps the character, removes the constraint, and injects the printed `attack` as a `region-shortcut-attack` combat via a new `region-shortcut-attack` `MHStep` (dispatched in `reducer-movement-hazard.ts`, offering a bare `pass` like `order-effects`) that resumes at `set-hazard-limit` once the combat resolves. If the shortcut was *not* used, the constraint survives to `snapshotHazardLimit`, which applies `hazardLimitReduction` (floored) when the company's resolved destination region matches one of the constraint's named regions — mutually exclusive with the attack payoff on a single move since firing the attack removes the constraint first. Used by Ash Mountains (tw-194): "tap the ranger to move as if the following pairs of regions were adjacent: Dagorlad and Gorgoroth, Horse Plains and Gorgoroth. The company faces an attack … Orcs — 4 strikes with 8 prowess. Alternatively, if the site moved to is in one of the regions listed above, the hazard limit is reduced by 2 (to a minimum of 2)." (CRF 22: the printed "otherwise" reads as "alternatively" — the two clauses are independent, not a forced-attack-vs-safe-reduction player choice made at play time.)
- `grant-action` `action: "cancel-chain-entry"` as a static ability on an **in-play ally** (`cost: { tap: "self" }`, `apply: { type: "cancel-chain-entry", select: "most-recent-unresolved-hazard" }`) — the ally taps itself, rather than a character, to negate the latest unresolved hazard on the chain while its company is the active M/H company (`legal-actions/chain.ts` `emitAllyCancelChainActions`, offered only when `hazardCount > 0` and the ally is untapped; the shared `handleGrantActionApply` taps the ally in place since its source card ≠ the bearer character). An optional `when` gates the offer against a context exposing `destinationRegion`/`chain.hazardCount` — present on Tom Bombadil (tw-350, "moving to a site in: Arthedain, Cardolan, Rhudaur, or The Shire") but **absent** on Leaflock (tw-265, "Tap to cancel a hazard that targets … Leaflock's company or an entity associated with his company" — no region restriction on the tap ability itself, only on the separate discard clause), so Leaflock's cancel is offered unconditionally whenever a hazard is unresolved against his own company. Both pair the ability with `on-event: company-arrives-at-site` self-discard (see `no-attack-site-keyed` below, Treebeard tw-353) when their company leaves its home regions.
- `target.woundedByRaceThisTurn` (play-target character filter context field) — races of attacks that wounded a character so far this turn, backed by `CharacterInPlay.woundedByRaceThisTurn`, appended in `combat-finalize.ts` from `combat.creatureRace` whenever a strike wounds a character, and cleared for every character at the start of each new turn in `enterUntapPhase` (`reducer-untap.ts`). Exposed in both `buildPlayOptionContext` (`legal-actions/organization.ts`) and the bespoke hazard-menu character play-target filter context (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`). Used by Pale Dream-maker (dm-78): "Playable on a non-Wizard character wounded by an Undead attack this turn" — `play-target` `filter: { "$and": [{ "target.race": { "$ne": "wizard" } }, { "target.woundedByRaceThisTurn": { "$includes": "undead" } }] }`.
- `on-event: card-discarded-from-hand` — fires once per card a player discards from their own hand during their own turn, for every matching on-event effect on a card attached to that player's characters. No single reducer call site discards from hand (end-of-turn hand-size reduction, named-card play costs, hazard-limit discards, …), so it is implemented as a prev/next diff (`hand-discard-trigger.ts` `applyHandDiscardCorruptionChecks`, hooked into `postReduce`): a card counts as "discarded from hand" when its instance left `hand` and landed in `discardPile` within the same step, distinguishing a discard from the card being played elsewhere. Does not currently detect CoE rule 2.09's "discarded card becomes the new play deck" case (both play deck and discard pile empty) as a discard. Used by Pale Dream-maker (dm-78): "makes a corruption check each time his player discards a card from his hand during his turn" — `{ "type": "on-event", "event": "card-discarded-from-hand", "target": "bearer", "apply": { "type": "force-check", "check": "corruption" } }`.
- `grant-action` cost `tap: "sage-in-company-excluding-bearer"` — the `sage-in-company` sibling that additionally excludes the bearer character itself from the eligible sages enumerated in `legal-actions/organization.ts`'s grant-action scan. Used by Pale Dream-maker (dm-78): "a sage in target character's company (other than character) may tap to attempt to remove this card."
- `region-transform` — a resource short-event that lets the player permanently retype one **named** region on the map, chosen at play time (paired with a `play-target` `character` tap-cost effect naming the sage who pays the ritual's cost). `options` is a list of `{ from, to }` {@link RegionType} pairs; `collectRegionTransformTargets` (`legal-actions/organization.ts`, shared by both generic short-event dispatchers — `playResourceShortEventActions` and `heroResourceShortEventActions`) scans every named `region` card in the pool, reads its *effective* type via `getEffectiveRegionType` (so a region already transformed by an earlier casting is read correctly), and offers one `play-short-event` action (carrying `targetRegionName`/`newRegionType`) per (sage × matching region × option) combination. Unlike the `region-type-override` add-constraint used by Deeper Shadow (le-179, `scope: "turn"`, bound to a moving company's destination), this is player-chosen and installs a **permanent** (`scope: "until-cleared"`) `region.type` `override` `attribute-modifier` constraint — nothing ever removes it, and `getEffectiveRegionType` (`engine/effective.ts`) folds it into every consumer (movement, creature keying, detainment), not just keying. Per CoE 9.4/9.5 the play rides the chain of effects like Marvels Told's discard-in-play mode: `handlePlayResourceShortEvent` (`reducer-events.ts`) pays the sage's tap cost immediately, then pushes the card onto the chain carrying `regionTransformName`/`regionTransformType` (plus `costTapCharacterId`); on un-negated resolution `applyShortEventRegionTransform` (`short-event-discard.ts`, called from `chain-reducer.ts`) installs the constraint and enqueues the sage's follow-up corruption check (rule 7.4: skipped when the tap-cost payer is an ally). Used by Master of Wood, Water, or Hill (td-136): "Tap a sage to change one Wilderness to a Border-land or Shadow-land or one Shadow-land to a Wilderness or one Border-land to a Wilderness. Sage makes a corruption check." — `options: [{w→b},{w→s},{s→w},{b→w}]`, no `corruptionCheck.modifier` (defaults to 0, since the text states no modifier).

- `stat-modifier` — prowess, body, direct-influence, corruption-points modifiers with value expressions, max caps, and override mechanism (`packages/shared/src/engine/effects/resolver.ts`). Direct-influence modifiers are also resolved during faction-influence-check (in `reducer.ts` and `site.ts`) and influence-check (in `organization.ts` `availableDI`). The attack-stat targets `all-attacks` (every automatic-attack and hazard creature) and `all-automatic-attacks` (site automatic-attacks only) are honoured by **both** `prowess` and `strikes` resolution (`resolveAttackProwess`/`resolveAttackStrikes` each take `isAutomaticAttack` and collect `all-automatic-attacks` for it). Used by Redoubled Force (dm-83): +2 prowess / +3 strikes to Orc/Troll automatic-attacks only. Its self-discard rides the `on-event: attack-defeated` trigger, whose context now also exposes `attack.isAutomaticAttack` (true only for a site automatic-attack / played-auto-attack, not a hazard creature) so the discard fires only when an Orc/Troll **automatic**-attack is defeated. The `faction-influence-check` context also exposes `faction.playableRegions` — the geographic regions a faction can be played in, resolved from each named `playableAt` site's `region` (plus explicit `region:` entries) via `buildFactionPlayableRegions` (`recompute-derived.ts`), added to the three faction-influence context builders (`reducer-site.ts`, `legal-actions/site.ts`, `legal-actions/pending.ts`). Used by Firiel (dm-10): "+2 direct influence against Dúnedain and factions that can be played in Anfalas, Anórien, Belfalas, Lamedon, and Lebennin" — a single direct-influence `stat-modifier` whose `$or` gate matches `influence-check`/`target.race: dunadan`, `faction.race: dunadan`, or `faction.playableRegions $includes` any of the five named regions. The `general-influence` stat is a player-level pool modifier collected in `recompute-derived.ts` from **both** a character's items / attached permanent-events (Bade to Rule le-167, Great Shadow ba-62) **and** a bare stage permanent-event in the player's `cardsInPlay` (not on any character); it raises `PlayerState.generalInfluenceBonus` (full pool, used by the defensive unused-GI subtraction). An optional `controlLimit` caps how many of the `value` points may control characters — the excess (`value - controlLimit`) is accumulated into `generalInfluenceControlPenalty`, so the **character-control budget** is `generalInfluenceControlLimit = effectiveGeneralInfluence - generalInfluenceControlPenalty` (consulted by every character-play / follower-to-GI gate: `legal-actions/organization-characters.ts`, `organization-companies.ts`, `reducer-site.ts`, `pending-reducers.ts`), while the full +value still counts for defense — mirroring the Ringwraith/Balrog +5 (CoE 1.12.R1/1.12.B1). Used by Truths of Doom (wh-108): "+6 general influence; you may only use 2 of these 6 points to control characters" (`value: 6, controlLimit: 2`, on a Pallando-specific stage permanent-event). The `all-characters` effective-stats `when` context also exposes `bearer.atOrMovingUnderDeeps` (true when the character's company's `currentSite`/`destinationSite` carries the `under-deeps` keyword — `currentSite` stays the origin for the whole M/H phase, so it covers "at", "moving from", and, via `destinationSite`, "moving to"; `companyAtOrMovingUnderDeeps` in `recompute-derived.ts`). Used by The Sun Shone Fiercely (ba-25): "-1 prowess to all Orc, Troll, Dwarf, and Ringwraith characters not at, nor moving to or from, an Under-deeps site (-2 if Doors of Night is not in play)" — a base -2 prowess `all-characters` modifier gated on `target.race $in [orc,troll,dwarf,ringwraith]` + `bearer.atOrMovingUnderDeeps $ne true`, plus a +1 softening additionally gated on `inPlay: "Doors of Night"` (net -1 with DoN, -2 without), and `duplication-limit` scope game. Site automatic-attack strike/prowess resolution also exposes the site's **effective** type as `site.siteType` in the resolver context (`reducer-site.ts` threads `effectiveSiteType` into every site auto-attack `resolveAttackStrikes` call — main, The Moon Is Dead / Incite Defenders duplicates, and Troll-purse / rescue re-faces), so an `all-automatic-attacks` modifier can gate on the site type. Used by Awaken Minions (tw-10): "The number of strikes for each automatic-attack at a Shadow-hold [{S}] or Dark-hold [{D}] site is doubled. Cannot be duplicated." — a `strikes` `op: multiply` `value: 2` `target: all-automatic-attacks` gated `when site.siteType $in [shadow-hold, dark-hold]` (multiply runs after additive modifiers, so it doubles the already-modified total) plus `duplication-limit` scope game. The `site.siteType` path is populated only for site automatic-attacks (not hazard creatures nor card-triggered/Tidings attacks), matching "automatic-attack at a … site". Direct-influence modifiers are ALSO folded into an **opponent-influence attempt** (influencing an opponent's in-play card) when they carry a `when` gated on the `opponent-influence-check` context: `reducer-site.ts` collects the influencer's `when`-gated stat-modifiers via `collectCharacterEffects` and adds them to the influencer's DI contribution. Only `when`-gated modifiers are folded there (an unconditional direct-influence modifier is already baked into effective DI / `availableDI`, so re-adding it would double-count). Used by Trifling Ring (le-346): "+3 to direct influence against characters" — two conditional direct-influence modifiers, one `when reason: influence-check` (controlling a character as a follower, via `availableDI` with a target `CharacterCard`) and one `when reason: opponent-influence-check, target.kind: character` (influencing an opponent's character); neither matches `faction-influence-check`, so the bonus is correctly excluded against factions, and `target.kind: character` excludes ally/item opponent-influence targets — plus `duplication-limit` scope `character`. Both influence contexts additionally expose stat fields for stat-gated DI bonuses: `target.mind` (omitted for avatars — `{ "target.mind": { "$gt": 0 } }` is a "has a mind" gate), `target.prowess`, and `bearer.prowess` (the influencer's **effective** prowess; effective target stats in the opponent-influence context, printed stats in `availableDI`). The condition-matcher's comparison operators (`$gt/$gte/$lt/$lte`) accept a context-path string operand resolved at match time (`resolveComparisonOperand` in `effects/condition-matcher.ts`), enabling stat-vs-stat gates. Used by Whip (le-348): "Orc or Troll only: provides +2 direct influence against one character with a mind and prowess less than the bearer's. Cannot be duplicated on a given character." — the two Trifling-Ring-style conditional DI modifiers additionally gated on `bearer.race $in [orc, troll]`, `target.mind $gt 0`, and `target.prowess $lt "bearer.prowess"`, plus `duplication-limit` scope `character` (enforced for normally-played items in `legal-actions/site.ts`). Global `all-attacks` modifiers also reach **agent hazard attacks** (rule 2.V.iii): `handleDeclareAgentAttack` (`reducer-site.ts`) now runs the rule-3.iv.6.1 agent prowess/body and the 1-or-2 strike count through `resolveAttackProwess`/`resolveAttackBody`/`resolveAttackStrikes` with the agent's own race as `enemy.race` and the attacked company as the boost context (so company-bound "any attack against this company" modifiers — Swarm of Bats le-237 — apply). The attack context exposes `attack.isAgentAttack` (true only there, absent elsewhere) so cards whose text covers creature attacks only opt out with `{ "attack.isAgentAttack": { "$ne": true } }` — added to Rank upon Rank (dm-80, "all **non-agent** Man attacks") and Sun (tw-335, "each automatic-attack and hazard creature"). Used by Chill Them with Fear (le-106): "All Elf, Dwarf, Dúnadan, and Hobbit attacks receive +2 prowess and +2 strikes (+1 prowess and +1 strike if Doors of Night is in play). Cannot be duplicated." — base +2 prowess / +2 strikes `all-attacks` modifiers gated on `enemy.race $in [elf, dwarf, dunadan, hobbit]`, plus -1 softening modifiers additionally gated on `inPlay: "Doors of Night"` (net +1 with DoN, the ba-25 softening pattern), plus `duplication-limit` scope game. `resolveAttackProwess`/`resolveAttackStrikes`'s `buildAttackContext` now also exposes `attack.isAutomaticAttack` (true only for a genuine site automatic-attack, including its tidings-queued/duplicated variants — absent, not false, otherwise), the counterpart to `attack.isAgentAttack` for cards naming only **hazard creatures**. A creature played as a site's dynamic auto-attack (`played-auto-attack`) is *not* flagged — it stays fundamentally a hazard creature, matching the existing `isAutomaticAttack` argument already passed `false` for it at that call site (`reducer-site.ts`). This is scoped to the `all-attacks`/`all-automatic-attacks` stat-modifier context only: the similarly-named `attack.isAutomaticAttack` built for `on-event: attack-defeated` (see above, Redoubled Force) and `attack.automatic` built for `modify-attack` both count a played auto-attack as automatic instead — a deliberate difference for those triggers, not a bug. Used by Clouds (tw-22): "Environment. If Doors of Night is in play, the prowess of each hazard creature is modified by +2. Cannot be duplicated." — a +2 prowess `all-attacks` modifier gated on `{ "$and": [{ "inPlay": "Doors of Night" }, { "attack.isAutomaticAttack": { "$ne": true } }, { "attack.isAgentAttack": { "$ne": true } }] }`, plus `duplication-limit` scope game. The self-modifier (no-`target`) context on a hazard-creature card also exposes `company.facedNames` — printed names of hazard-creature attacks the company has already faced this turn, the name sibling of `company.facedRaces`, backed by the same turn-scoped company stamp (`facedHazardNames`/`facedHazardRaces`, `combat-finalize.ts` `recordHazardEncountered`, cleared in `enterUntapPhase`) so it survives the M/H → Site phase transition. Used by Orc-lieutenant (tw-073): "receives an additional +3 prowess if played on a company that has already faced Uruk-lieutenant this turn" — `{ "stat": "prowess", "value": 3, "when": { "company.facedNames": { "$includes": "Uruk-lieutenant" } } }`. The rule is printed on *Uruk-lieutenant* (le-96)'s own text, not Orc-lieutenant's, but names Orc-lieutenant as the beneficiary — since the two cards are never the same attack, it is implemented as a second self-effect on the beneficiary card (Orc-lieutenant) rather than on the announcing card.
- `check-modifier` — bonus/penalty to corruption, faction-influence checks (`resolver.ts`). For corruption-check resolutions the modifier is collected from attached hazards **and** items (`legal-actions/pending.ts`), with a context exposing `source.keywords` (the keywords on the card that enqueued the check) so items can gate on "spell", "ritual", etc. An optional `target: "company"` broadens the modifier from the bearer alone to **every character in the bearer's company** — collected once per company from items / attached permanent-events on any company member by `collectCompanyItemEffects` (`resolver.ts`, mirroring the company-scoped `stat-modifier`; the bearer's per-character item pass skips it to avoid double-counting) and folded into each member's check by `resolveCheckModifier` in both corruption subsystems (`legal-actions/pending.ts` `corruptionCheckActions` and `legal-actions/free-council.ts`). Used by I'll Be At Your Heels (le-195): a Command permanent-event on a leader — `play-target` character (`target.keywords $includes leader`) + `on-event self-enters-play` bounce of other Command cards (`move` `filter-all` `items-on-target`→`hand`, `keywords $includes Command`) + `stat-modifier direct-influence -2` (`min: 0`) + `check-modifier corruption +1 target: company` + `grant-action return-self-to-hand` (`move` self→hand). Siblings I'll Report You (le-196, +1 prowess) and Smart and Secret (le-229, +1 body) use the same shape with a `stat-modifier target: company` instead. Orcs of Dol Guldur (as-121) is the precedent for a faction playable at a **named haven**: `playableAt: [{ site: "Dol Guldur" }]` works unchanged against the minion Darkhaven le-367 (a haven's empty `playableResources` list does not gate factions — CoE 2.V.3 puts faction playability on the faction card), the influence attempt taps the haven on success, and the "Standard Modifications" line is the usual pair of `check-modifier` effects gated on `controller.inPlay` (Orcs of Moria -2 / Orcs of the Red Eye +2) plus `inPlayInfluenceNumber: 0` for re-influence.
- `body-check-modifier` — adds `value` to the 2d6 body-check roll made against the bearer during combat (negative protects the bearer). Collected from attached items in `reducer-combat.ts` (`bodyCheckRollModifier`); applies in both hazard/automatic-attack and CvCC body checks. Used by Helm of Fear (as-126). An optional `scope: "all-attacks"` makes it **global** instead of item-bound: carried by an in-play permanent-event (either player's `cardsInPlay`), it applies to every combat body check, gated by `when` against a context exposing `attack.creatureRace` (attacking creature's normalized race) and `target.race` (body-checked character's race); collected by `globalBodyCheckRollModifier` (`combat-actions.ts`). Used by Spawn of Ungoliant (ba-24): "+1 to all body checks for Elves, Dwarves, Hobbits, Dúnedain, and Men resulting from Spider attacks." — also a `permanent-event-auto-attack` (`onDefeat: "remove-from-play"`) adding a Spawn 3×15/8 auto-attack to The Pûkel-deeps (ba-94/dm-34) and The Gem-deeps (ba-90/dm-30). An optional `scope: "bearer-combat"` instead applies to body checks arising from the **bearer's own combat**: carried by an item / attached permanent-event on a participating character, collected by `bearerCombatBodyCheckModifier` (`combat-actions.ts`) at every body-check path. The relevant bearer is the **parrying defender** for a `creature`/`attacker-character` check (a failed strike against the bearer, so the striker body-checks) and the **successful CvCC attacker** for a `character` check; the `when` context exposes `bodyCheck.target`, `bodyCheck.fromFailedStrike`, and `combat.isCvCC`. Used by Flame of Udûn (ba-58): a Demon fána permanent-event played on The Balrog (`play-target` character `target.name: "The Balrog"` + `demon-fana` keyword) — `+3 prowess` / `-2 direct influence` (`stat-modifier`), "+1 to all body checks resulting from failed strikes against The Balrog" (`bearer-combat`, `when bodyCheck.fromFailedStrike: true`) and "+1 to defending character's body check" when The Balrog attacks successfully in CvCC (`bearer-combat`, `when bodyCheck.target: character` + `combat.isCvCC: true`); the fána swap reuses `on-event self-enters-play` → move `items-on-target` (filter `demon-fana`) → hand + `grant-action return-self-to-hand`; "Discard his allies" is `on-event self-enters-play` → move **`allies-on-target`** → discard (new move zone in `reducer-move.ts`); "No other characters or allies can be in his company outside of the organization phase" is the new **`play-flag: "no-allies-in-company"`** (borne on an item, consulted by `companyHasNoAllyRestriction` in the ally-play emitter `legal-actions/site.ts`; allies are site-phase-only so no phase gate is needed, and characters only join a company during the org phase the card permits).
- `company-modifier` — applies stat modifiers to all characters in bearer's company (`resolver.ts`)
- `in-play-item-modifier` — a game-wide corruption-point / marshalling-point modifier to every in-play item matching an `itemFilter` (per-item context `{ item: { keywords, name, cardType, subtype } }`, absent → all items), sourced from a permanent-event in either player's `cardsInPlay`. Collected once per recompute by `collectInPlayItemModifiers` (`recompute-derived.ts`); `itemModifierDeltas` sums the matching `corruptionPoints`/`marshallingPoints` per item. The corruption delta is folded into each matching item's bearer corruption total in `computeEffectiveStats` (under the same Balrog-avatar exclusion as the item's printed corruption); the MP delta is added flat to the item's marshalling category in the MP tally (independent of the cross-alignment / MEWH §4 clamps on the item's printed MP). Reaches items of **both** players ("all ring items"). Used by Rumor of the One (le-224): "+1 to the corruption points and the marshalling points for all ring items. Discard when any play deck is exhausted. Cannot be duplicated." — paired with `on-event: play-deck-exhausted` self-discard `move` (the Tokens to Show as-101 / Safe from the Shadow as-54 pattern, fired by `completeDeckExhaust`) and `duplication-limit` scope `game`. Also used by Scorba at Home (td-65): "each major item gives an additional corruption point." — `itemFilter` `{ "item.subtype": "major" }` (the `subtype` context path was added for it), `corruptionPoints: 1`, no MP delta. And by Itangast at Home (td-38): "each greater item gives an additional corruption point" — `itemFilter` `{ "item.subtype": "greater" }`, `corruptionPoints: 1`, paired with its `dragon-at-home` Gold Hill augmentation (below).
- `dragon-at-home` — augments a Dragon's lair (every site version whose `lairOf` matches the carrying permanent-event's `manifestId`) with an additional Dragon automatic-attack while the At-Home is in play, suppressed while the same Dragon's Ahunt long-event is also in play; a defeated manifestation chain instead strips the lair's Dragon attacks entirely. Implemented in `engine/manifestations.ts` (`getActiveAutoAttacks` / `collectAtHomeAttacks` / `isAhuntInPlay`). Augment attacks are modeled strikes+prowess only (no `body`), matching the codebase convention for printed Dragon-lair automatic-attacks. Used by Daelomin at Home (td-11, + `discard-for-hazard-limit`) and Scorba at Home (td-65): Dragon 3×13 on Zarak Dûm — both site versions (hero td-181, minion le-417) carry `lairOf: "td-63"` (the minion tag was added with td-65's certification). Also by Itangast at Home (td-38): Dragon 3×19 on Gold Hill (hero td-176, `lairOf: "td-36"`), suppressed by Itangast Ahunt (td-37) via shared `manifestId` td-36; td-38 additionally carries the greater-item `in-play-item-modifier` above. And by Smaug at Home (td-71): Dragon 2×18 on The Lonely Mountain — both site versions (hero tw-428, minion le-387) carry `lairOf: "tw-90"` (the minion tag was added with td-71's certification), suppressed by Smaug Ahunt (td-70); td-71 additionally carries the `appliesTo: 'any-company'` `draw-modifier` above. And by Scatha at Home (td-62): Dragon 3×16 on Gondmaeglom — both site versions (hero td-177, minion le-379) carry `lairOf: "td-60"` (the minion tag was added with td-62's certification), suppressed by Scatha Ahunt (td-61); td-62 additionally carries a game-wide influence `check-modifier` (`all-in-play`, below).
- `corruption-source-multiplier` — a game-wide effect (carried by a permanent/long event in either player's `cardsInPlay`) that scales **one** of every character's corruption sources by `multiplier` (default 2 — "doubled"). Collected once per recompute by `collectCorruptionSourceMultipliers` (`recompute-derived.ts`), one multiplier per in-play copy. In `computeEffectiveStats` each character's individual corruption sources are tracked (only while a copy is in play and the bearer isn't the Balrog avatar, whose borne items contribute no corruption): each corruption-bearing item's total CP (printed + `in-play-item-modifier` delta) is one source, each attached `hazard-corruption` card is one source, and each card contributing corruption via a `stat-modifier` on `corruption-points` (grouped by source card instance — e.g. The One Ring, Durin's Axe on a Dwarf) is one source. `corruptionSourceMultiplierDelta` adds the extra corruption: the controlling player minimises, so each copy scales the character's smallest remaining source (largest multiplier paired with smallest source, per the rearrangement inequality). No source → no effect; reaches characters of **both** players ("each character"). Used by The Balance of Things (tw-93): "Each character has the corruption points doubled for one of his sources of corruption (the player controlling the character chooses)." — "Unique" is deck-level (1 copy/deck), so no in-play `duplication-limit` is declared.
- `duplication-limit` — enforced for scopes "game", "player", "site", "character", and "company" (the company scope covers both allies and items, in `legal-actions/site.ts`) plus combat scopes "turn"/"attack". The "character" scope covers **two** cases: a card that *attaches* to the character (rings/permanent events — copies counted via the bearer's `items`, `legal-actions/site.ts`), and a *non-attaching* filter-target short event that leaves a character-targeted active constraint on play (counted by matching `sourceDefinitionId` + `target.kind: "character"` + `target.characterId` in the org-phase filter-target emitter, `legal-actions/organization.ts`). Used by Words of Menace and Deceit (le-258): its rest-of-turn +5 `character-stat-modifier` marks the target, so a second copy is unplayable on that same character but still playable on a different eligible one. The **"turn" scope** is also enforced outside combat, in the organization/site resource short-event emitter (`playResourceShortEventActions`, `legal-actions/organization.ts`): before any target is enumerated, `countConstraintsFromDefinition(state, def.id)` counts the definition's live active constraints, so a card whose own rest-of-turn effect leaves one blocks every further copy that turn. Used by Wizard's Voice (tw-366): "Cannot be duplicated on a given turn" — its +6 direct-influence constraint bars a second copy on *any* Wizard (unlike le-258's character scope), and the blocked copy is reported as `not-playable` rather than silently dropped.
- `character-stat-modifier` (short-event, no gate) — the `on-event: self-enters-play` → `add-constraint character-stat-modifier` path (`reducer-events.ts`) applies a turn-scoped stat bonus to `action.targetCharacterId`, the character chosen by a `play-target: character` filter. Used unconditionally (no `requiresCardInPlay`) by Words of Menace and Deceit (le-258): "+5 to the character's direct influence for the rest of the turn" — a `direct-influence` +5 folded into the target's `effectiveStats.directInfluence` (and thus `availableDI`) for the turn. Companion `enqueue-corruption-check modifier -4` gated `when $not target.race ringwraith` ("Unless he is a Ringwraith, he makes a corruption check modified by -4"), plus `duplication-limit` scope character. Filter is `target.skills $includes "spirit-magic"`, so a Ringwraith without spirit-magic (Dwar — sorcery) is not a legal target. Also used by Wizard's Voice (tw-366): "Spell. Wizard only. +6 to direct influence for the Wizard for the rest of the turn. Wizard makes a corruption check modified by -3. Cannot be duplicated on a given turn." — the same shape with an ungated `enqueue-corruption-check modifier -3`, `duplication-limit` scope **turn**, filter `target.race: "wizard"`, and `keywords: ["spell"]` so Wizard's Staff (td-170) adds its +2 to the check. Carrying **no** `play-window`, it is offered in both the organization and the site phase (CoE 2.1.1). No engine change was needed for either card; the +6 reaches real play through `effectiveStats.directInfluence` → `availableDI`, letting the Wizard take a follower his printed DI could not pay for.
- `name-alias` — while the bearer is in `cardsInPlay`, its `as` name is added to the in-play names list (`inPlayNamesOf` in `recompute-derived.ts`, which backs `buildInPlayNames` and `buildControllerInPlayNames`), so every DSL `when: { inPlay: "<alias>" }` gate is satisfied. The card's own name and `duplication-limit` counts (`countCopiesInPlay`, by definition name) are unaffected, so an aliasing card never collides with the real aliased card. Used by Skies of Fire (le-228), which "acts as Gates of Morning for the purposes of interpreting hazards". The few inline in-play-name builders (`legal-actions/combat.ts`, `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) were switched to `buildInPlayNames` so the alias is honored on every hazard-interpretation path.
- `environment-override` — while the bearer is in `cardsInPlay`, reshapes game-wide which named environments are considered in/out of play: `considerInPlay` names are treated as in play (with no actual card) and `considerNotInPlay` names as out of play (even while their card stays in `cardsInPlay`). `collectEnvironmentOverride` (`reducer-utils.ts`) is consulted by `buildInPlayNames` (the `inPlay` condition context), `inPlayNamesForPlayerDeep` (combat `defender.inPlay`), and the name-in-play predicates behind `card-in-play`/`card-not-in-play` play-conditions (`isCardNameInPlayOrCharacters`, `isCardNameInPlayForPlayer`) — so every "is X in play?" query stays consistent. Removals precede additions. Only the named environments are touched (not `countCopiesInPlay`), so playing an actual copy is never blocked. Used by Peril Returned (td-54): Doors of Night considered in, Gates of Morning considered out (the Gates of Morning card remains removable normally).
- `region-keying-boost` — turn-scoped environment that softens creature keying: one region of type `from` in a company's site path counts as `count` regions of type `asType` (boosts are alternatives, at most one applied per keying check, never combined). On play the short-event adds a `region-keying-boost` active constraint; the creature-keying matchers (`findCreatureKeyingMatches`, `checkCreatureKeying`) consult it via `engine/region-keying.ts` (`collectRegionKeyingBoosts`, `regionPathsWithBoosts`). Used by Withered Lands (td-85), gated on Doors of Night via a `play-condition`/`site-path`/`inPlay` clause.
- `draw-modifier` — adjusts the resource/hazard draw counts at the movement/hazard draw step (`draw: 'resource' | 'hazard'`, numeric or expression `value`, optional `min` floor). Resolved in `mh-steps.ts` `transitionToDrawCards` via `resolveDrawModifier`. Effects are collected from **both** the moving company's characters (`collectCharacterEffects`) **and** the active (moving) player's own in-play events/environments in `cardsInPlay` (`collectPlayerInPlayEffects`), so a resource long-event not carried by any character can contribute; collecting only from the active player's cardsInPlay keeps the bonus from ever helping the opponent. The value-expression / `when` context exposes `sitePath` counts (`wildernessCount`, …, plus `regionCount` = total path length) and the top-level `movementType` (`starter`/`region`/`special`/`under-deeps`). Used by Radagast/Alatar (character-carried) and A Short Rest (td-95): a Long-event granting `"4 - sitePath.regionCount"` extra resource draws to each moving company, gated on an actual region site path (`movementType` in region/starter, `regionCount` 1..3) so under-deeps/special movement and 4+-region paths grant nothing and the bonus is never negative (CRF 22 ruling). An optional `appliesTo: 'own-companies' | 'any-company'` (default `own-companies`) widens that scoping: an `any-company` modifier is also collected from the **opponent's** `cardsInPlay`, for the "each moving company …" wording where the hazard player holds the card but the moving player's draws shrink. `min` is applied by `applyDrawModifier` (`mh-steps.ts`) as a floor on the *reduction* only — a net negative adjustment is additionally clamped to the unmodified count, so a `min` can never raise a pool that was already lower (0 resource draws for a company with no character of mind ≥ 3, CoE 2.IV.v). Used by Smaug at Home (td-71): "each moving company draws one less card to a minimum of one at the start of its movement/hazard phase" — `draw: 'resource'`, `value: -1`, `min: 1`, `appliesTo: 'any-company'` (the company's own draws are the resource pool, cf. A Short Rest's "each moving company may draw an extra card").
- `absorb-wound` — prevents wound from a successful strike; attacker rolls to potentially discard the item (`packages/shared/src/engine/reducer-combat.ts` `resolveStrike` + `handleShieldDiscardRoll`)
- `face-strike-on-tap` — activated ability on an in-play item (or character-attached permanent event) that lets its bearer face one of an attack's strikes **regardless of the attack's normal capabilities and the bearer's status**. During the `assign-strikes` defender phase, while the item is untapped, its bearer is in the defending company, and an unassigned strike remains, the defending player may tap it (the `face-strike-on-tap` action, `legal-actions/combat.ts` `assignStrikeActions` defender branch → `handleFaceStrikeOnTap` in `combat-actions.ts`) to add a strike-facing to the bearer even while he is tapped/wounded (ordinary status gate bypassed). The assignment is flagged `StrikeAssignment.reduceAttackBodyOnParry`; when the bearer parries that strike (`characterTotal >= creature prowess`, the strike fails to wound him), `combat.creatureBody` is reduced by `bodyReductionOnParry` for the rest of the combat (applied in `combat-strike.ts` `resolveStrikeCore`, including to that strike's own creature body check). Used by Bow of Alatar (wh-90): placed on Alatar (`play-target` character filter `{ target.name: "Alatar" }`), tap to face a strike and reduce the attack's body by 1 on a parry; also carries `stage-points` 2.
- `stat-modifier` prowess `when: { "combat.strikeMode": "tap" }` — a prowess modifier that applies only when the character **taps to face a strike**. `computeCombatProwess` (`recompute-derived.ts`) takes the strike-resolution `strikeMode` (`'tap'`/`'untap'`/`'dodge'`/`'reroll'`) and exposes it as `combat.strikeMode`; the creature-facing path (`combat-strike.ts` `resolveStrikeCore`) passes the actual mode, and the tap/untap need shown by `legal-actions/combat.ts` `resolveStrikeActions` is computed per-mode (`baseProwessTap`/`baseProwessUntap`). Because `combat.strikeMode` is absent outside strike resolution, the modifier never leaks into effective-stats. Used by Stabbing Tongue of Fire (ba-81) and Whip of Many Thongs (ba-82): "+1 prowess when tapping to face a strike".
- `combat-cancel-weapon` — an in-play item ability usable only during a company-vs-company combat (CvCC) in which the item's bearer's company is a participant. The controller pays `cost` (`{ tap: "self" }`) and picks one `weapon`-keyword item borne by a character in the *opposing* company; its instance is appended to `CombatState.suppressedWeaponInstanceIds` (`cancel-weapon-effects` action, `cancelWeaponActions` in `legal-actions/combat.ts` → `handleCancelWeaponEffects` in `combat-cancel.ts`, wired through `COMBAT_HANDLERS`). While suppressed, `collectCharacterEffects` (`effects/resolver.ts`) drops every effect the weapon sources and `computeEffectiveStats` (`recompute-derived.ts`) skips its structural `prowessModifier`/`bodyModifier`, so the weapon contributes nothing to its bearer's combat stats (CvCC reads `effectiveStats.prowess`); the weapon is **not** discarded and the suppression clears when combat finalizes (the list lives on the combat state). A weapon just declared onto a character during the current chain is suppressed identically (contribution is always read live from the item), backing "(even declared in the same chain of effects)". Offered only to the item's controller, whichever side The Balrog is on; the scan deliberately looks at items on the Balrog avatar (the Balrog-specific Whip is exempt from the general "items on the Balrog have no effect" ban, unlike `modifyAttackActions`). Used by Whip of Many Thongs (ba-82): "If The Balrog is in company vs. company combat, tap this item to cancel all effects of one weapon of your choice (even declared in the same chain of effects) in an opponent's company until the end of the combat. This does not discard the weapon." — plus the shared `item-play-site` (non-Darkhaven Under-deeps, `allowTapped`), `play-target` `{ target.name: "The Balrog" }`, and the strike-mode prowess gate above.
- `join-combat-force-strike` — a resource short-event played by the defending player in the pre-assignment window of the `assign-strikes` sub-phase (same window as `company-combat-boost`: `combat.phase === 'assign-strikes'`, no strikes assigned, `playerId === defendingPlayerId`). It brings a named character (`characterName`) into the defending company if absent — "considered movement with no movement/hazard phase", so only the company membership arrays change and the character is **not** restored after combat (unlike an Alatar haven-jump) — adds it to `CombatState.forcedStrikeTargets` (the defender-assignment status gate is now bypassed for a forced target so it must face a strike "regardless of any conflicting effects" even while tapped/wounded), and, when `tapAfterAttack`, schedules a `PostAttackEffect` `tapIfUntapped` (the same Alatar "following the attack, tap" mechanism). Playability gates: `requiresSiteKeyword` (the defending company's `currentSite` or `destinationSite` def carries the keyword, e.g. `under-deeps`) and `notInPlay` (the named card must not be in `buildInPlayNames`). Offering `joinCombatForceStrikeActions` + the forced-target bypass in `assignStrikeActions` (`legal-actions/combat.ts`); reducer block in `reducer-events.ts` `handlePlayResourceShortEvent`. Used by Vanguard of Might (ba-79): "Playable if a company at or moving to an Under-deeps site is facing an attack and Flame of Udûn is not in play. If not in the company, The Balrog immediately joins the company. … The Balrog must face a strike from the attack, regardless of any conflicting effects. Following the attack, if untapped, tap The Balrog." (`characterName: "The Balrog"`, `tapAfterAttack`, `requiresSiteKeyword: "under-deeps"`, `notInPlay: "Flame of Udûn"`; "Balrog specific" is a deck-construction keyword, no play-time gate).
- `combat-discard-opponent-item` — a Balrog resource short-event played during a company-vs-company combat (CvCC) in which The Balrog is untapped and a participant on the acting player's side; on play the card-player chooses one item borne by the *opposing* company and discards it (to the opponent's discard pile). Field-less: the opposing company and Balrog gate are read from the live combat state. Offered by `combatDiscardOpponentItemActions` (`legal-actions/combat.ts`, wired into the CvCC windows of `combatActions` alongside the Whip's `cancel-weapon`) as one `play-short-event` per eligible hand card when the acting player owns one of the two CvCC companies with The Balrog untapped in it, the opposing company bears ≥1 genuine item, the `card-in-play` play-condition (Flame of Udûn) holds, and the turn-scoped `duplication-limit` is not reached (`countConstraintsFromDefinition`); offered to whichever side the Balrog is on (mirrors `cancelWeaponActions`). On play, `handlePlayResourceShortEvent` (`reducer-events.ts`) discards the spent event, records a turn-scoped `attack-card-played` marker (swept at `turn-end`, blocking a second copy all turn), and enqueues a `discard-one-company-item` resolution (the Brigands primitive) on the opposing company with the card-player as `actor`; the chosen item (`discard-item-from-company`) moves to the opponent's discard pile. The top-level reducer now skips combat routing while a resolution is queued for the acting player, so `discard-item-from-company` (shared with An Article Missing's combat sub-phase) is not misrouted. Paired on the card with `play-window { phase: "combat" }` (keeps the generic emitters from offering it out of combat), the `balrog-specific` keyword, the Flame-of-Udûn `play-condition`, `duplication-limit` scope `turn`, and the shared `select: "self"` sideboard→deck `move` (ba-78). Used by Scourge of Fire (ba-75): "Playable if Flame of Udûn is in play. … Choose and discard one item an opponent's company bears if The Balrog is untapped and in company vs. company combat with that company. Cannot be duplicated on a given turn."
- `site-storm-devastation` (+ `wound-or-eliminate` dice-check verb) — a Balrog CvCC resource short-event that devastates **everyone at the site** (both CvCC companies). On play (`handlePlayResourceShortEvent`, `reducer-events.ts`), in fixed order: (1) discard all no-body allies at the site (effective body `0`/absent — Great Bats, Goldberry, …) to their owners' discard piles; (2) tap every untapped ally and every untapped character with a mind stat (avatars have `null` mind → spared); (3) enqueue one generic `dice-check` per character with mind `< characterMindBelow` and per ally whose printed MP is `< allyMpBelow`. The rolls use `comparison: 'gt'`, `threshold = body`, a constant `-1` modifier (pass = `roll - 1 > body`), `requireTargetPresent: true`, and a `wound-or-eliminate` onPass verb — so each roll surfaces as its own explicit `resolve-dice-check` action. `wound-or-eliminate` (new branch in `applyDiceCheckBranch`, `pending-reducers.ts`) is roller-agnostic: it locates the target's owner and acts on a character (`targetCharacterId`) or ally (`targetInstanceId`) — not-yet-wounded → status `inverted`; already-wounded → eliminated (character → out-of-play, ally → owner's discard). Playability is gated by `siteStormAtSiteActions` (`legal-actions/combat.ts`, wired into the CvCC combat windows alongside `combat-discard-opponent-item`): CvCC, the acting player owns one of the two companies with The Balrog in it, that company's site lacks the `under-deeps` keyword, and the opposing company holds a Wizard (`race: "wizard"`); offered to whichever side the Balrog is on. Paired with `play-window { phase: "combat" }` and the `balrog-specific` keyword. Used by Crowned with Storm (ba-54): "Playable if The Balrog's company is not at an Under-deeps site and is in company vs. company combat against a company with a Wizard. Discard all allies with no body at the site. Make a roll: for each character at the site with a mind less than 8 and for each ally normally worth less than 3 marshalling points. If the result minus 1 is greater than the character's/ally's body, he is wounded or, if already wounded, eliminated. Tap all untapped allies and characters with a mind stat."
- `assign-strike-when-tapped` — marker effect on an ally that keeps it a **legal strike target** during the defender's strike-assignment phase even while it is tapped or wounded (its status treated as untapped for assignability only). Unlike `strike-shield` + `alwaysCountsAsUntapped` (Noble Hound), it does **not** force strikes onto the ally before its controlling character — the controlling character stays assignable while the ally is unassigned. The defender-phase ally offer in `legal-actions/combat.ts` OR-s this effect into the existing `alwaysUntapped` check. Used by Great Troll (ba-46): "Even if tapped or wounded, you may assign a strike to this ally as though it were untapped." (its playableAt is the Cave Troll shape — `any` + `when` (`site.keywords $includes under-deeps`, `site.siteType $ne haven` = non-Darkhaven), plus `company-overt`; "only by The Balrog" rides the `balrog-specific` keyword).
- `free-strike-assignment` — environment effect: while the carrying card is in a player's `cardsInPlay`, the defender of any **hazard-creature-sourced** attack (`attack.source` of `"creature"`, `"on-guard-creature"`, or `"played-auto-attack"` — the same set `tap-on-strike-assignment`/dm-129 uses for "hazard creature attack") may assign strikes to any character or ally in the defending company regardless of tapped/wounded status, and the attack's own `combat-attacker-chooses-defenders` rule (if any) is suppressed for that attack. `resolveDefenderFreeStrikeAssignment` (`reducer-utils.ts`) scans both players' `cardsInPlay` for the effect (mirroring `resolveAttackerChoosesDefenders`'s `globalAttackerChoosesDefenders` scan), called from every hazard-creature-sourced combat-initiation site (`chain-reducer.ts` `initiateCreatureCombat`; `reducer-site.ts`'s played-auto-attack path) — NOT from site automatic-attack, agent, CvCC, tidings-attack, ahunt, or great-hunt-attack initiation, which are not "hazard creature attacks" by this codebase's own convention. When granted, the initiation site forces `assignmentPhase` to `'defender'` (dropping any `cancel-window`/`'attacker'` phase the printed rule would otherwise open) and sets `CombatState.defenderFreeStrikeAssignment`, which `assignStrikeActions` (`legal-actions/combat.ts`) consults to drop its untapped-only gate for both characters and allies. Used by Cloudless Day (td-104): "Whenever a company faces a hazard creature attack, the defender may choose which characters in the company will be the targets of the attack's strikes (regardless of tapped status, wounded status, and the normal abilities of the attack)."
- `under-deeps-roll-modifier` — bonus to the 2d6 roll required for a company to move between adjacent Under-deeps sites (CoE 2.IV.i.1). Carried by an item/ally/character; while present on any character in the moving company, `value` is added to the roll — modeled as an equivalent reduction of the required roll (floored at 0), the same trick already used for the Balrog's built-in +3 (`companyContainsBalrogAvatar`). Collected via `collectCharacterEffects` over every character in the moving company at the `getUnderDeepsRequiredRoll` call site in `mh-steps.ts`; modifiers from multiple company members stack. Used by Iron Shield of Old (as-127): "+2 to all rolls required for bearer's company to move to adjacent Under-deeps sites"; and by Goblin Earth-plumb (as-125), the `value: 1` sibling paired with a `play-target` `target: 'character'` race filter (`$in: ["orc", "troll"]`) for "Playable only on an Orc or Troll." An optional `scope: "minion-companies"` instead makes the modifier a **game-wide environment** (an in-play resource long-event): collected from either player's `cardsInPlay` in `mh-steps.ts` and applied only when the moving player's alignment is Ringwraith (multiple in-play copies stack). Paired with `prohibit-card-play` (`cardNames`): while the carrying card is in play, the named cards are barred from play (`isCardPlayProhibited` in `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts` refuses to offer them) and any copy already in play is discarded to its owner the moment the carrying card enters play (`applyProhibitCardPlayOnResolve` in `chain-reducer.ts` `resolveLongEvent`) — distinct from `cancel-card-effects`, which only suppresses an in-play card's constraints. Used by The Under-roads (as-106): "The roll required for minions to move between adjacent Under-deeps sites is decreased by 3. Discards and prohibits the subsequent play of The Way is Shut." A sibling `scope: "all-companies"` is the same environment shape but names no side, so it applies to every company's Under-deeps roll regardless of alignment (the `mh-steps.ts` collection loop applies it unconditionally, alongside the alignment-gated `minion-companies` check). Used by Secret Ways (dm-157): "The roll required to move between adjacent Under-deeps sites is decreased by 4. Cannot be duplicated." (paired with `duplication-limit` scope `"game"`).
- `extra-under-deeps-mh-phase` — carried by an in-play permanent-event; grants repeated Under-deeps movement/hazard phases (Gangways over the Fire ba-60). While the controlling player has any card with this effect in play (`playerHasExtraUnderDeepsMH` in `reducer-utils.ts`), each of their **moving** companies may — at the end of its M/H phase — attempt another Under-deeps movement to a site it has not used yet this turn; a new site card is played and a fresh M/H phase immediately follows. `advanceAfterCompanyMH` (`mh-hazard-play.ts`) records the per-company completed-phase count and occupied-site list on the phase state (`gangwaysPhaseCounts` / `gangwaysSitesUsed`, keyed by company id), then routes to the dedicated `gangways-offer` step (`gangwaysOfferActions` in `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) when the company moved and an Under-deeps-adjacent unused site remains in the site deck. The active player either selects a destination (`gangways-extra-move` → `handleGangwaysOffer` re-enters `reveal-new-site` with the per-phase state reset) or passes to finalize the company (`finalizeCompanyMH`, the extracted original advance body). The Under-deeps roll for each extra phase is penalised by the completed-phase count (applied in `handleRevealNewSite` where an actual roll is required — "Subtract the number of complete movement/hazard phases the company has taken so far this turn"). Ba-60 also carries `starting-company-placement` + the `starting-item` keyword ("may start the game … in lieu of playing a minor item") and `duplication-limit` scope `game` ("Cannot be duplicated").
- `grant-extra-mh-phase` — carried by a **resource short-event** played at the end of a company's M/H phase; grants that company **one** additional movement/hazard phase this turn (Forced March le-185, Leg It Double Quick le-202, Bridge tw-202 — `requiresDestinationSiteType: "haven"` with no alignment gate, so any Haven qualifies, not just a Darkhaven; also the un-certified family Ûvatha Unleashed le-248). Optional gate fields: `requiresDestinationSiteType` (the moving company's destination must be this `SiteType`, e.g. `"haven"`) and `requiresDestinationAlignment` (destination site alignment, e.g. `"ringwraith"` — a Darkhaven). The card is offered during the play-hazards step by `extraMHPhaseResourceActions` (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) only when the active company is moving and its destination meets the requirements, and is excluded from the generic resource-short-event path (`heroResourceShortEventActions` skips any card with this effect). Resolving it (`handlePlayResourceShortEvent`, `reducer-events.ts`) sets `extraMHPhasePending` on the active company (a new `Company` field). After the company commits its move (`endCompanyMH`), `advanceAfterCompanyMH` (`mh-hazard-play.ts`) consumes the flag and enters the dedicated `extra-mh-move-offer` step: `extraMHMoveOfferActions` offers one `extra-mh-move` per site reachable from the current site via normal starter/region movement still in the site deck (`extraMHMoveDestinations` uses `getReachableSites`), or a `pass`. `handleExtraMHMoveOffer` installs the chosen destination and re-enters `reveal-new-site` with the per-phase state reset (`resetCompanyMHFields`), so the company runs a full second M/H phase (declare-path → hazards → reset-hand → finalize). Distinct from `extra-under-deeps-mh-phase`: normal (not Under-deeps) movement, exactly one extra phase, no roll penalty, driven by a one-shot short-event rather than a persistent in-play effect. Leg It Double Quick (le-202): "Playable at the end of the movement/hazard phase on a moving company. Tap all untapped characters in the company. The company may move to an additional site this turn…" — confirms neither gate field is required (any moving company qualifies, unlike Forced March's Darkhaven gate) and pairs the bare effect with `company-tap-characters` (no `mindBelow`/`filter`) as the tap cost; both blocks in `chain-reducer.ts` key off the same bare (no `targetCharacterId`) short-event chain entry, so they resolve together with no extra wiring.
- `run-home-to-haven` — an ally ability (Bill the Pony tw-198) offered during the controlling player's **end-of-turn** phase (the `discard` and `signal-end` steps). While the ally is in a company at a non-Haven, non-Under-deeps site whose character count is `maxCompanySize` or fewer and the site's `nearestHaven` is known, `runHomeActions` (`legal-actions/end-of-turn.ts`, offered alongside Great-road's `havenReturnActions`, resource player only) emits a `run-home` action per eligible ally. `handleRunHome` (`reducer-end-of-turn.ts`) discards the ally to its owner's discard pile and relocates the company to the site's nearest Haven — pulled from the location deck, or shared (`siteCardOwned = false`) when a sibling company already holds it. Per the card errata this is movement with no M/H phase, so the departure site follows the ordinary site-card lifecycle (haven/untapped → location deck, tapped → site discard pile) like `haven-return`. Paired on the card with `play-flag: playable-at-tapped-site` ("playable even if the site is tapped") and `playableAt` naming Bree/Bag End. Used by Bill the Pony (tw-198): "Playable at Bree or Bag End; playable even if the site is tapped. If at a non-Haven/non-Under-deeps site and if his company's size is three or less, you may discard Bill the Pony at the end of his company's turn and replace its site with the nearest Haven."
- `surface-region-adjacency` — a Balrog permanent-event played on an Under-deeps site during the organization phase (paired with a `play-target: site` filtered to the `under-deeps` keyword; the org-phase site-target play path in `legal-actions/organization-events.ts` now admits it alongside Fallen-wizard Stage resources, gated to the organization phase). While in play, bound to the Under-deeps site U (`attachedToSite`), it has two effects: **(1) permanence** — the card is exempt from the site-attached orphan sweep (`discardOrphanedSiteAttachedEvents`, `reducer-utils.ts`) so it persists while U is unoccupied, and when a company leaves U the site is always returned to the owner's location deck (never discarded — the `alwaysReturnToDeck` branch in `mh-hazard-play.ts` step 8), keeping U re-accessible ("This site is never discarded or returned to its location deck"); **(2) surface-region adjacency** — each *other* site of the card owner that is normally one of `siteTypes` (Shadow-hold / Ruins & Lairs / Border-hold) and lies in U's region is Under-deeps-adjacent to U at a required roll of 0. An Under-deeps site and its surface site always share a region, so U's own `region` names the surface region. The dynamic adjacency is `cavernsUnchokedAdjacencyRoll` (`legal-actions/organization-companies.ts`), consulted by `isUnderDeepsAdjacent` and `getUnderDeepsRequiredRoll` via the moving player (`forPlayer`, threaded from org-phase plan-movement, the M/H declare-path, and the reveal-step roll), so only the owner's own companies benefit ("of yours") and player-agnostic callers (hazard-creature keying) see no dynamic adjacency. `playerOwnsSiteDef` restricts the "other" site to one in the owner's `siteDeck` / a `currentSite` / a `destinationSite`. Used by Caverns Unchoked (ba-51). ("Balrog specific" is a deck-construction keyword with no play-time gate, per the ba-45/ba-46 precedent.)
- `surface-site-roll-zero` — carried by a Balrog `trigger-attack-on-play` permanent-event played on an Under-deeps site during the **site phase** (paired with a `play-target: site` filtered by name and a `play-target: character` restricting the keep to The Balrog; kept via `afterAttack: "move-to-mp-pile"`). While in play bound to the Under-deeps site U (`attachedToSite`) it has two effects: **(1) surface-site roll zero** — when one of the owner's companies at U ascends to U's **surface site** (the single non-Under-deeps site in U's `adjacentSites`), the required Under-deeps movement roll is 0 instead of the printed value; added by `breachTheHoldSurfaceRoll` (`legal-actions/organization-companies.ts`) and consulted by `getUnderDeepsRequiredRoll` via the moving player (`forPlayer`), so only the owner benefits and a move to a *different* Under-deeps site is unaffected; **(2) permanence** — identical to `surface-region-adjacency`: exempt from the site-attached orphan sweep so U persists while unoccupied, and U is always returned to the owner's location deck (never discarded) when a company leaves it (`mh-hazard-play.ts` step 8). Both effects share the `cardKeepsBoundSitePermanent` predicate (`reducer-utils.ts`). Used by Breach the Hold (ba-50): "Playable during the site phase on The Drowning-deeps or The Rusted-deeps if The Balrog is there. The Balrog's company faces 3 attacks (Spawn — 1/15, Dwarves — 4/9, 2/12). Following the attacks, tap The Balrog or discard this card. The roll required to move to the surface site is reduced to zero. This site is never discarded or returned to its location deck. Cannot be duplicated on a given site." — `play-target` site (`name $in [The Drowning-deeps, The Rusted-deeps]`) + `play-target` character (`target.name: "The Balrog"`) + `duplication-limit` scope site + `trigger-attack-on-play` (Spawn 1/15, Dwarves 4/9, Dwarves 2/12; `move-to-mp-pile`; per-attack `creatureType` — no `tap-site-on-play`/`untapped-site-required`, so the site stays untapped and a tapped site is fine) + `surface-site-roll-zero`. ("Balrog specific" is a deck-construction keyword with no play-time gate, per the ba-45/ba-46 precedent.)
- **Dual-mode "tap your Ringwraith" resource short-event** (`discard-in-play` + sideboard `fetch-to-deck`, keyed to the revealed avatar) — a resource short-event that taps the player's own revealed avatar to do **either** of two alternatives. New play-target filter field `target.isRevealedAvatar` (added to `buildPlayOptionContext`'s `target` context, `legal-actions/organization.ts`) is `true` only for the player's own revealed avatar (`findPlayerAvatar`), never a Ringwraith follower controlled by it — so `{ "target.isRevealedAvatar": true }` pins the tap to "your Ringwraith". The emitter `playResourceShortEventActions` recognizes the pairing of a `discard-in-play` move (`select: target`, `from: in-play`, `to: discard`) with a **sideboard** fetch move (`select: target`, `from: [sideboard]`, `to: deck`, `count: N`): it offers **both** modes — one `(tap × hazard-permanent-event)` discard action per in-play target (carrying `discardTargetInstanceId`) and, **during the organization phase only** when ≥1 sideboard card matches the fetch filter, a `sideboard-fetch` action (carrying the avatar to tap, no discard target, `optionId: "sideboard-fetch"`). A card whose discard mode has no legal target is still playable when the sideboard mode is available (the "no discard target → not playable" bail is skipped). The reducer `handlePlayResourceShortEvent` (`reducer-events.ts`) discriminates purely by `discardTargetInstanceId`: present → resolve the discard inline (tap avatar, move target hazard perm-event → owner's discard, discard the event) and **skip** the fetch; absent → tap the avatar and enqueue the count-N sideboard fetch sub-flow (card to `cardsInPlay`, fetch-from-pile picks, "up to N" via `pass`, event discarded on the last pick). Used by Ancient Secrets (ba-36): "Tap your Ringwraith to discard one hazard permanent-event. Alternatively, during your organization phase, tap your Ringwraith to take up to two resources from your sideboard to your play deck and reshuffle." — discards **any** hazard permanent-event (environments included, no corruption check — contrast Marvels Told td-134) and fetches up to two minion resources (`minion-resource-item/ally/faction/event`) sideboard → play deck.
- **Play-deck-or-discard magic-card self-tutor** (`move` fetch with a `deck` source) — a resource short-event "playable on your revealed Ringwraith avatar" that searches the player's **own play deck and discard pile** for a magic card and takes it to hand, reshuffling the play deck when it was searched. The `move` fetch primitive now accepts `'deck'` as a `from` zone: `moveToFetchToDeckPayload` (`reducer-move.ts`) maps `deck → 'deck'` alongside `discard → 'discard-pile'` / `sideboard`, and both the legal-action generator (`fetchFromPileLegalActions`, `legal-actions/index.ts` — offers one `fetch-from-pile` per matching deck/discard card, tagging deck picks `source: 'deck'`) and the reducer (`handleFetchFromPile`, `reducer-utils.ts`) already handle a `'deck'` source, reshuffling the play deck after removing the chosen card (a discard-sourced card is not reshuffled). Combined with `to: 'hand'` this is a plain fetch sub-flow (card to `cardsInPlay`, one `fetch-from-pile` or `pass`, event discarded on the last pick). The play restriction reuses `play-target` `character` filtered to `target.name` **and** `target.isRevealedAvatar` (the ba-36 revealed-avatar field), with no tap cost. The magic-card filter is the shared `$or` over keywords `spell` / `sorcery` / `spirit-magic` / `shadow-magic` (same as Indûr's fetch, le-54). Used by Akhôrahil Unleashed (le-162): "Playable on Akhôrahil the Ringwraith (as your Ringwraith). You may take a magic card from your play deck or discard pile to your hand (reshuffle play deck if searched)."
- `company-movement-restriction` + `voluntary-discard` + `cancel-attack` roll — a permanent-event bound to a company (`play-target` `target: "company"`, `CardInPlay.companyId`) that restricts its movement, adds a roll-to-cancel CvCC ability, and may be voluntarily discarded. The `company-movement-restriction` effect (`effects/company-restrictions.ts` `companyMovementRestrictions`, aggregating across bound cards) exposes `noStarterMovement` (drop starter destinations at org plan-movement `organization-companies.ts` and suppress the starter path at M/H declare-path `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`), `noUnderDeepsMovement` (skip the entire Under-deeps destination pass at org plan-movement and suppress the Under-deeps declare-path option at M/H), `regionMovementMax` (hard cap on region distance, "in all cases" — applied at plan-movement, M/H select-company `mh-steps.ts` `handleSelectCompany` → `phaseState.maxRegionDistance`), and `hazardLimitModifier`/`hazardLimitFloor` (added to the company's hazard limit in `snapshotHazardLimit`, floored, **gated on `movementType === region`** per CRF 22 "the hazard limit reduction only works if the company is moving"). The `cancel-attack` effect gains two fields for the in-play company-bound source (scanned in `legal-actions/combat.ts` with a `companyId === combat.companyId` and CvCC gate): `requiresCvCC: true` (only vs a company-vs-company attack — "an attack against them by an opponent's company") and `roll: { threshold, comparison, skillBonus }` (paying `cost: { discard: self }` discards the card and enqueues a generic `dice-check` — `handleCancelAttackByInPlayFaction` in `combat-cancel.ts`, roller = defender, `+1` per company member with the named {@link Skill} — whose `onPass: { type: "cancel-current-attack" }` verb, new in `applyDiceCheckBranch`, calls `resolveCancelAttackEntry`; failure lets combat continue). The `voluntary-discard` effect (`phase: "organization"`) offers a `voluntary-discard-in-play` action (`legal-actions/organization.ts` `voluntaryDiscardInPlayActions`, reducer `reducer-organization.ts` `handleVoluntaryDiscardInPlay`) that discards the in-play card to the pile, lifting its restrictions. Used by Going Ever Under Dark (ba-37): "cannot use starter movement … limited in all cases to 3 regions maximum and their hazard limit is reduced by one (to a minimum of two) … Discard this card from play and make a roll to attempt to cancel an attack … If the roll plus the number of scouts (`skillBonus: "scout"`) in the company is greater than 7, the attack is canceled. Discard during your organization phase if you choose. Cannot be duplicated on a given company." (ba-37 is a movement *restriction*, not a movement grant, so it never lifts the Balrog Under-deeps-only lock.) Also used by Crept Along Carefully (ba-29), which adds `noUnderDeepsMovement: true` ("cannot use starter movement or move to an Under-deeps site"), `skillBonus: "ranger"` ("the number of rangers in the company"), and an `on-event: play-deck-exhausted` self-discard `move` alongside its own `voluntary-discard` ("Discard when any play deck is exhausted or, if you choose, during your organization phase") — no `duplication-limit` (ba-29 carries no "cannot be duplicated" clause).
- `company-movement-tax` + hazard site-path enforcement + skill-gated `cancel-chain-entry` — a **hazard** permanent-event bound to the *opponent's* moving company (`play-target` `target: "company"`, so `CardInPlay.companyId` is set on the just-resolved entry — the same company-binding path as ba-37, but the card lives in the hazard player's `cardsInPlay`). Three pieces: **(1) movement tax** — `company-movement-tax { taxTapCharacters }` read by `effects/company-restrictions.ts` `companyMovementTax` (scanning **both** players' `cardsInPlay` by `companyId`, largest tax wins) / `isMovementTaxSatisfied` (satisfied when `paid >= taxTapCharacters` **or** the company has no untapped character left). `companyMovementTaxUnpaid` (`legal-actions/organization-companies.ts`) skips the bound company in `planMovementActions` and `splitCompanyActions` while unpaid; `payMovementTaxActions` (`legal-actions/organization.ts`) offers one `pay-movement-tax` per untapped character, and `handlePayMovementTax` (`reducer-organization.ts`) taps it and bumps `OrganizationPhaseState.movementTaxPaid[companyId]` (reset each org phase). **(2) site-path play gate** — the `play-condition` `requires: "site-path"` check (`checkSitePathCondition`, `sitePath.wildernessCount` etc.) is now also enforced in the **long/permanent-event** branch of `playHazardsActions` (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`), not only the short-event branch, so a permanent hazard can gate on the moving company's resolved path. **(3) ranger-cancel before it resolves** — a static `grant-action` `{ action: "cancel-chain-entry", cost: { tap: "character" }, when: { actor.skills $includes "ranger" }, apply: { cancel-chain-entry, select: most-recent-unresolved-hazard } }` on the hazard itself; while it sits as an unresolved chain entry, `emitHazardSelfCancelBySkillActions` (`legal-actions/chain.ts`, offered only to the active/resource player during M/H chain declaring) emits an `activate-granted-action` per untapped character in the target company whose printed skills satisfy the grant's `when`. The shared `handleGrantActionApply` → `cancel-chain-entry` path taps the ranger and negates the entry, returning the card to its owner's (hazard player's) discard. The self-discard at a Haven reuses the existing company-bound `on-event: organization-phase-start` self-discard sweep (`reducer-untap.ts`, `when: { "company.atHaven": true }` — the Black Rider le-170 mechanism), routing the card to its owner. Used by Enchanted Stream (as-27): "Playable on a moving company with at least one Wilderness in its site path. A ranger in the company can tap to cancel this card before it resolves. The company cannot voluntarily split or move to a new site unless it taps all of its untapped characters to a maximum of two during its organization phase. Discard during any organization phase if the company is at a Haven/Darkhaven."
- `cancel-chain-attack-cancel` — marker on a Balrog resource short-event (the counter-cancel counterpart of `cancel-chain-return-to-origin`/Goldberry, but sourced from a discarded hand card rather than a tapped ally). While a chain is active during a **company-vs-company attack made by The Balrog's company** (the combat `isCvCC`, `attackSource.type === "company-attack"`, `attackingPlayerId` is the responding player, and the attacking company contains the Balrog avatar via `companyContainsBalrogAvatar`), the card may be played from hand to target and negate an unresolved chain entry — declared by the opponent — that carries a `cancel-attack` effect (the effect that would cancel the attack). Legal actions: `counterCancelAttackChainActions` (`legal-actions/chain.ts`, wired into `chainActions` under `restriction === 'normal'`, only the priority player) emit one `counter-cancel-attack` action per (hand card with the marker, opponent-declared cancel-attack entry). Reducer: `handleCounterCancelAttack` (`chain-reducer.ts`, routed via `handleChainAction` + the chain-action dispatch list in `reducer.ts`) discards the played card hand → discard, marks the target entry `negated: true` (so its `cancel-attack` never fires on resolution — the Balrog's attack survives), and flips priority to the opponent. Used by Great Fissure (ba-61); its other mode is a plain `cancel-attack` gated on `attack.atUnderDeeps` (the cancel `when` context in `legal-actions/combat.ts` exposes `attack.atUnderDeeps` = defending company at, or moving to/from, an Under-deeps site). ("Balrog specific" is a deck-construction keyword with no play-time gate, per the ba-45/ba-46 precedent.)
- `trigger-attack-on-play` — a resource permanent-event that makes the active company immediately face one or more automatic attacks when it enters play (`chain-reducer.ts` `resolvePermanentEvent` sets `state.combat` with `attackSource: card-triggered-attack`; the multi-attack form chains via `remainingAttacks` in `combat-finalize.ts`). After the attacks, if any character remains untapped a `select-card-bearer` pending resolution offers the keep — a chosen character taps and the card is either attached with a `bearer-cannot-untap` constraint (`afterAttack: "attach-with-constraint"`, Rescue Prisoners tw-315 / The Windlord Found Me dm-164) or kept bare in `cardsInPlay` (`afterAttack: "move-to-mp-pile"`, Burning Rick le-173); otherwise the card is discarded. The keep target now honours the card's `play-target: character` filter (`selectCardBearerActions` in `legal-actions/pending.ts`): with no filter every untapped member is offered; with one, only matching untapped members. A `move-to-mp-pile` card may also carry **ongoing** effects — those must not apply while the self-inflicted attacks resolve, so the entry is flagged `pendingTriggerAttack` (`CardInPlay`) on play and `collectGlobalEffects` ignores a pending card; the flag is cleared when the card is kept (`applySelectCardBearerResolution`), and `computeCombatProwess` was extended to collect `own-characters` effects (scoped to the character's controller) so such a buff applies in later real combats too. Used by Descent through Fire (ba-56): "Playable during the site phase if The Balrog is at an untapped The Under-galleries or The Under-courts. Tap the site. The Balrog's company faces 3 attacks (Trolls – 5/8, 4/10, 3/12). Following the attacks, tap The Balrog or discard this card. … place this card in your marshalling point pile. All your characters receive +1 prowess and all your leaders receive +1 direct influence. Cannot be duplicated at a given site." — `play-target` site (`name $in [The Under-galleries, The Under-courts]`) + `play-flag untapped-site-required` + `play-flag tap-site-on-play` + `play-target` character (`target.name: "The Balrog"`, restricting the keep to The Balrog) + `duplication-limit` scope site + `trigger-attack-on-play` (3× Trolls, `move-to-mp-pile`) + two `own-characters` `stat-modifier` effects (prowess +1 always; direct-influence +1 gated `bearer.keywords $includes leader`). Two extra `trigger-attack-on-play` fields ship for Tempest of Fire (ba-77): `creatureTypeBySiteType` resolves every attack's creature race from the played site type at play time (`{ "border-hold": "Men", "shadow-hold": "Orcs" }` — applied to the first attack and all `remainingAttacks` in `chain-reducer.ts`), and `returnFactionsAtSite` returns, on the `move-to-mp-pile` keep, every **unique** faction in play (either player's `cardsInPlay`) playable at the company's current site to its **owner's** hand (via `ownerOf`, in `applySelectCardBearerResolution`) — distinct from `discardFactionsAtSite` (active player only, discards). Ba-77's `play-target: site` filter excludes Under-deeps sites and their surface sites via `$not keywords $includes under-deeps` + a new `isUnderDeepsSurface` boolean added to the site-phase play-target match context (`isUnderDeepsSurfaceSite` in `legal-actions/organization-companies.ts`: true for a site named at roll 0 in some Under-deeps site's `adjacentSites`). Ba-77 has no character `play-target` (any untapped member may be tapped to keep) and no `duplication-limit` (non-unique, 3 misc MP). Smoke on the Wind (le-230) is a data-only sibling of Burning Rick le-173 — same shape (`play-target` site `siteType: free-hold` + `play-flag tapped-site-only` + `play-target` character + `duplication-limit` scope site + `trigger-attack-on-play` Men 5/8 + 1/10, `move-to-mp-pile`, `discardFactionsAtSite`), keyed to a Free-hold instead of a Border-hold, 3 misc MP (printed MP fixed 0→3, cards.json authoritative); no new engine work.
- `site-rule: deep-mines-movement` + `stage-points: whileCompanyAtSite` — a Fallen-wizard site that hangs off a *protected Wizardhaven* as a personal Under-deeps destination. The `deep-mines-movement` site-rule makes the site reachable **only** from one of the moving player's protected Wizardhavens (a Wizardhaven for that player — `isHavenForPlayer` — carrying an active `site-protected` constraint owned by them) and **only** while he has more than six stage points; the surface Wizardhaven is adjacent with a required roll of 0, so the descent resolves as roll-0 Under-deeps movement, and the adjacency runs both ways so a company at the site may ascend back to a protected Wizardhaven at roll 0 (no stage-point gate on ascent). The site is excluded from ordinary starter/region movement. Descent/ascent legality is `isDeepMinesDescentLegal` / `isDeepMinesAscentLegal` (`legal-actions/organization-companies.ts`), consumed by both the plan-movement pass there and the declare-path (reveal) pass in `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`; the stage-point gate is checked at both, so a drop before movement leaves the company put (rule 5.04 / CRF 22 "does not move at all"). "Cannot be duplicated on a given Wizardhaven" is enforced by rule 2.II.7.1 (no two same-origin companies to the same site definition). The companion `stage-points` effect flagged `whileCompanyAtSite: true` grants its value while any of the player's companies occupies the site — tallied in `recompute-derived.ts` once per distinct occupied `currentSite` instance (Fallen-wizard only, `siteOccupancyStagePointsOfCard`), so two companies at the same site do not double it while two occupied sites each count. Used by Deep Mines (wh-55): "A company may move to this site only from one of your protected Wizardhavens [{H}] and only if you have more than 6 stage points … the movement roll required to move between them is 0. You receive the three stage points if any of your companies are at the site. … Cannot be duplicated on a given Wizardhaven."
- `grant-action` — activatable abilities from characters/items/allies/hazards (`legal-actions/organization.ts` `grantedActionActivations`, reducer in `reducer-organization.ts` `runGrantApply`). Supported applies include `untap-site`, `set-character-status`, `shuffle-deck-top`, `discard-target-character`, `roll-then-apply`, `enqueue-corruption-check`, `enqueue-pending-fetch`, and `add-constraint`.
