The `when` gate is evaluated against a context exposing `bearer.*`, `company.*`, `site.*` (`siteType`, `region`, `isTapped`, `hasDragonAutoAttack`, `hasOneRing`). The `enqueue-pending-fetch` apply supports `fetchTo: "hand"` and `playableAtBearerSite: true` — the latter captures the bearer's company's current site at activation and restricts fetch candidates to cards playable there (items via the site's printed `playableResources`/`item-play-site`, allies/factions via `playableAt` — `isCardPlayableAtSiteDef` in `reducer-utils.ts`, consulted by both the candidate enumeration in `legal-actions/index.ts` and the `fetch-from-pile` reducer validation). Used by Strider (ba-1): tap to fetch one item/ally/faction playable at his current site from the discard pile to hand, gated on `site.region` in Arthedain/Cardolan/Rhudaur/The Shire. A grant-action flagged `corruptionCheckWindow: true` (action `modify-corruption-check`) is activatable **only** while a corruption check by a character in the bearer's company awaits its roll, in **both** corruption-check subsystems: the unified pending resolution (`legal-actions/pending.ts` `corruptionCheckActions`) and the Free Council end-of-turn support window (`legal-actions/free-council.ts` / `reducer-free-council.ts` `resolveCorruptionCheck`, which now reads and consumes one-shot `check-modifier` constraints). Both windows emit the activation via the shared `modifyCorruptionCheckGrantActions` helper (one per untapped bearer in the resolving character's company), carrying the resolving character on `targetCardId`; the generic per-phase scanner skips it (`extractGrantActions`). Routed to `handleGrantActionApply` from every phase reducer (including `handleFreeCouncil` and the corruption-check pending fall-through in `pending-reducers.ts`). The grant-action `add-constraint` apply now also builds a one-shot `check-modifier` payload, and `resolveConstraintTarget` resolves `action-target-character` to the action's `targetCardId`. Used by When I Know Anything (td-166): tap a sage to add +3 to one company corruption check; the sage then makes a corruption check. A grant-action flagged `endOfTurnOnly: true` is excluded from the generic per-phase scanner's organization-phase default scan (`extractGrantActions`, same treatment as `corruptionCheckWindow`) and is instead emitted only by the dedicated end-of-turn discard-pile fetch scanner (`legal-actions/end-of-turn.ts` `endOfTurnGrantActions`), which recognizes both a discard-to-hand `move` apply (one activation per matching discard card, e.g. Saruman) and a discard-to-deck `enqueue-pending-fetch` apply (a single activation once any card matches, the specific card chosen via a follow-up `fetch-from-pile` pending resolution). Used by Great Shadow (ba-62): "During your end-of-turn phase, you may take one non-short-event resource or character from your discard pile ... and shuffle it into your play deck" — the same `recall-to-deck` action name and `enqueue-pending-fetch` apply as The Mouth (le-24), but restricted to end-of-turn instead of organization phase. The end-of-turn scanner's cost check keys `tap: "self"` on the **source's own status**: for a character-direct grant that is the character (Saruman), and for an item-borne grant it is the **attachment's** status (so a card that "taps X during end-of-turn" is offered whenever X itself is untapped, regardless of the bearer's status; the bearer-tap path remains `tap: "bearer"`). Used by Huntsman's Garb (wh-92): a stage permanent-event placed on Alatar (`play-target` character filter `{ target.name: "Alatar" }`, attaches to his `items`, `stage-points` 1) whose `grant-action` `huntsmans-garb-fetch` (`cost: { tap: "self" }`, `endOfTurnOnly`, a discard→hand `move` filtered to `name $in [Risky Blow, True Fána, The Hunt]`) taps the Garb itself — usable even while Alatar is tapped. A character-direct grant with `targets: { scope: "player-companies" }` (action `modify-company-corruption-checks`) emits one activation per controlled company (carrying `targetCompanyId`) and adds a **turn-scoped, company-targeted** corruption `check-modifier` (`add-constraint` `check: corruption`, `target: action-target-company`, `scope: turn`); both corruption resolvers now collect a company-scoped corruption `check-modifier` for every check by a character in the matching company, **without consuming it**, narrowed by the constraint's optional `when` (built from the apply's `constraintWhen` and evaluated against a `{ target: { cardType, race, name } }` context describing the checking character — le-56 carries `{ "target.cardType": "minion-character" }` for its "by minions" clause; a constraint without one aids every character in the company) (`corruptionCheckActions` in `legal-actions/pending.ts`; `resolveCorruptionCheck` in `reducer-free-council.ts`). The grant-action context gained `bearer.atDarkhaven` (a `haven` site whose alignment is `ringwraith`/`balrog` — a minion Darkhaven, stricter than `bearer.atHaven`). Used by Ren the Ringwraith (le-56): "As your Ringwraith, if at a Darkhaven, he may tap during your organization phase to modify all corruption checks made this turn by minions in any one of your companies by +2" (`when: { bearer.isRevealedAvatar, bearer.atDarkhaven }`). Also used by Stave of Pallando (wh-107): the wh-105 Pallando's-Hood shape (`play-target` character `{ target.name: "Pallando" }`, `stage-points` 2, `endOfTurnOnly` `tap: self` discard→hand `move`) with the fetch filter keyed on card type instead of names — `cardType $in [hero-resource-faction, minion-resource-faction]` for "take a faction from your discard pile" (a Fallen-wizard may hold both alignments' factions) — and its printed 1 corruption point modeled as a `stat-modifier` `corruption-points` +1 on the attached card (collected from the bearer's `items` by `collectCharacterEffects`). `ActionCost` components now compose instead of the first one short-circuiting: `applyCost` (`cost-evaluator.ts`) pays `tap` first (factored into `applyTapCost`), then falls through to `discard`/`check`/`wound`, so a cost object combining `tap: "bearer"` and `discard: "self"` pays both — previously the `tap` branch always returned early and the item was never discarded. Used by Healing Herbs (tw-255): "tap and discard this item" to heal a wounded character (`heal-company-character`, targets Inverted company members, the le-310 precedent) or, in a second mode sharing the same tap-and-discard cost, to untap a non-wounded company member (`untap-company-character`, new action — emits one activation per company member with status Tapped; a bearer that could pay the tap cost is by definition Untapped at generation time, so it never appears as its own target). Both modes reuse the existing `set-character-status` `target: "target-character"` `status: "untapped"` apply.
- `add-constraint` (grant-action / on-event apply) — adds an active constraint. Payload kinds include `company-stat-modifier`, `hand-size-modifier`, `site-resource-unlocked` (Records Unread: makes a resource category playable at a site type for the rest of the turn; consulted by the `site-has-resource` play-condition check), `site-type-override` (an `attribute-modifier` on `site.type`, filtered by `site.definitionId`; during the site phase the bound site is the active company's current site), and `auto-attacks-detainment` (an `attribute-modifier` on `auto-attack.detainment`, filtered by `site.definitionId`). The `site.type` override is read everywhere a site's effective type matters via `engine/effective.ts::getEffectiveSiteType` (detainment keying, movement keying, faction/ally playability in `siteMatchesEntry`, haven/untap check); `auto-attacks-detainment` is read via `siteAutoAttacksForcedDetainment` and OR-ed into every site automatic-attack's detainment decision (forces detainment for any defending alignment). When the source card is a site-bound permanent event (`attachedToSite`), `discardOrphanedSiteAttachedEvents` (postReduce sweep) discards the card and clears its constraints once no company occupies the bound site. Used by Hold Rebuilt and Repaired (as-88): a Ruins & Lairs becomes a Shadow-hold and all automatic-attacks become detainment, discarded when the site leaves play. The `cross-alignment-resources-unlocked` payload binds to the active company's current site during the site phase (filtered by `site.definitionId`, targeted at the controlling `player`, scope `until-cleared`) and lifts the MEWH §10 cross-alignment site-tap block (`siteTapCrossAlignmentBlocked` in `legal-actions/site.ts`) at that one site, so a Fallen-wizard may play the opposite alignment's items/allies/factions there; cleared by `discardOrphanedSiteAttachedEvents` when the site leaves play. Used by Double-dealing (wh-66): hero resources playable at a minion site, minion resources at a hero site. The `site-protected` payload binds to the active company's current site during the site phase (filtered by `site.definitionId`, targeted at the controlling `player`, scope `until-cleared`) and bars any player other than the protector from playing a marshalling-point card (item/ally/faction worth ≥1 MP) at any version of the bound site (`siteIsProtectedAgainstPlayer` + `givesMarshallingPoints` in `legal-actions/site.ts`); cleared by `discardOrphanedSiteAttachedEvents` when the site leaves play. Used by Guarded Haven (wh-74): "The site is protected. Cards that give marshalling points may not be played at any version of the site by your opponent in all cases." Its `play-target` site filter uses `effectiveSiteType: "haven"` (post wizardhaven-conversion) excluding three named sites; the site duplication-limit counter (scope `site`) also tallies site-attached copies in `cardsInPlay`. The "may not be a starting stage card" clause is modeled by omitting the `starting-item` keyword (so the card is never offered as a draftable Stage resource), not a new flag. The `technology-item-unlocked` payload binds to the active company's current site during the site phase (filtered by `site.definitionId`, targeted at the controlling `player`, scope `until-cleared`) and lets the owner play **one** `Technology`-keyword item at the bound site this site phase whether the site is tapped or untapped — bypassing both the site-tap precondition and the item's own `item-play-site` restriction (`playResourcesActions` in `legal-actions/site.ts`, via `siteHasTechnologyItemUnlock`); the one-per-phase limit is tracked by `SitePhaseState.technologyItemPlayed` and the played item leaves the site untapped (`reducer-site.ts`). Cleared by `discardOrphanedSiteAttachedEvents` when the site leaves play. Used by Saruman's Machinery (wh-120): "One Technology item is playable at the site during your site phase whether the site is tapped or untapped." Its companion `play-target` restricts the site to Isengard / The White Towers, a `play-condition` `requires: 'player-state'` gates on `player.avatar: "Saruman"`, and a `play-condition` `requires: 'site-protected'` requires the site to already carry an active `site-protected` constraint owned by the player ("your protected" site, from wh-68/wh-69/wh-74). The `site-resource-unlocked` payload also accepts a compound `siteCondition` (a DSL `Condition` evaluated against `site.siteType` / `site.regionType` / …) in place of a single `siteType`, for "such a site" rules that are not one fixed type — read by the generalized `isSiteResourceUnlocked` (`legal-actions/organization.ts`). Used by A Panoply of Wings (wh-37): Information unlocked at "any non-Haven, non-Shadow-hold, non-Dark-hold site in a Wilderness". wh-37's discard-to-unlock ability rides on an **in-play faction** (a card in `cardsInPlay`, not attached to a character): `bareCardGrantActions` (`legal-actions/organization.ts`, wired into the organization and active-player site phases) offers a bearer-less `activate-granted-action` (`characterId` self-references the faction instance), and `handleInPlayCardGrantAction` (`grant-action-apply.ts`) discards the faction to its owner's discard pile and adds the constraint — supporting only the `discard: self` cost and `add-constraint` apply for such sources. A `playableAt` **any-site** entry `{ any: true, when }` (`PlayableAtAny`, matched in `siteMatchesEntry`) lets a faction/ally express "any site satisfying `when`" rather than a fixed site type/name/region (wh-37's compound Wilderness gate).
- `cancel-return-and-site-tap` (`add-constraint` payload, scope `turn`, target a company) — genuinely negates, for the rest of the turn, every engine mechanism that forces a company back to its site of origin or taps its current/new site. Consulted via `hasCancelReturnAndSiteTap` (`engine/pending.js`) at all four production sites: CoE rule 5.31 environment enforcement (`findForcingEnvironment` in `mh-hazard-play.ts`, short-circuits before scanning `force-return-to-origin` environments), CoE rule 2.IV.4 successful-strike return-to-origin (`combat-finalize.ts`'s `attack-strike-successful` → `company-return-to-origin` handler), the `agent-discard-return-to-origin` legal-action emitter (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`, the card is simply not offered against a shielded company), and `tap-sites-in-play` environment resolution (`applyTapSitesInPlayOnResolve` in `chain-reducer.ts`, skips tapping a shielded company's `currentSite` while still tapping every other qualifying site). Two production paths add the constraint: a `grant-action` (tap bearer → `add-constraint` + `enqueue-corruption-check`, precedent Promptings of Wisdom wh-34 / Piercing All Shadows wh-47), and a **direct short-event** `on-event: self-enters-play` → `add-constraint` (no `target` field needed — the generic company-resolution fallback in `reducer-events.ts`'s `applyShortEventOnEntersPlay` resolves the company from `action.targetCharacterId` via `findCharacterCompany`), paired with a race-gated `enqueue-corruption-check` (`when: { "$not": { "target.race": "ringwraith" } }`, modifier -4) — used by Govern the Storms (wh-45): "Magic. Sorcery. Cancel all hazard effects for the rest of the turn that: force a sorcery-using character's company to return to its site of origin or would tap its current or new site. Unless he is a Ringwraith, the sorcery using character makes a corruption check modified by -4." Its `play-target` filter is the `$or: [{ "target.race": "ringwraith" }, { "target.skills": { "$includes": "sorcery" } }]` pattern (a Ringwraith is a sorcery-user regardless of listed skills, the Deeper Shadow le-179 precedent for shadow-magic) `$and`-ed with `company.moving: true`. Before this card the constraint kind existed (added by wh-34/wh-47) but was never consulted anywhere — wh-45's certification added the four enforcement call sites, making all three certified cards' text fully live.
- `play-flag` — closed keyword set consulted at play/setup time. Includes `no-starting-company` (item-draft rejects the card as a starting minor item; `rules/definitions/item-draft.ts` + `legal-actions/item-draft.ts`) and `grants-followers` (overrides the Balrog's default "may not have any followers" restriction — `isBalrogAvatarDef` in `state-utils.ts` — at the three DI-controller eligibility sites in `legal-actions/organization-characters.ts`, `organization-companies.ts`, and `recruit-via-event.ts`, each now also checking `hasFollowerGrantPermission` (`effects/play-flags.ts`) against the Balrog's attached items. Used by Great Shadow (ba-62): "The Balrog gains ... and may have followers.").
- `play-flag: no-attack-site-keyed` — a combat play-flag on an **ally** that removes it from strike-assignment (both the defender and attacker windows in `legal-actions/combat.ts`, via `isAllyImmuneToSiteKeyedAttack`) for attacks faced *at a site*: unconditionally against a site automatic-attack (`attackSource.type` `automatic-attack`/`played-auto-attack`), and against a `creature`/`on-guard-creature` only when the attack's `attackSiteKeyingTypes` includes the defending company's effective site type. A creature keyed only by region type (no site-type keying) is *not* "at a site", so the ally remains a legal target. Used by Quickbeam (tw-307 and its alternate-art printing tw-308, "May not be attacked by automatic-attacks or hazards keyed to his site") and Blackbole (le-152, "May not face any strike at a site or from an automatic-attack" — its minion Ringwraith counterpart; its `playableAt` uses a `{ any: true, when }` entry gating `site.region` `$in` the three Mirkwood regions with `site.name` `$ne` "Dol Guldur").
- `play-flag: agent-may-move-to-haven` — a per-agent exemption from the rule-9.07 "agents cannot move to haven sites" ban. The agent-move legal-action generator (`agentTurnActions` in `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) skips haven destinations unless the acting agent's card carries this flag (`hasPlayFlag`), in which case a haven reachable via starter movement (its `nearestHaven`) is offered like any other destination. Composes with (and is independent of) the restrictive `agent-move-restriction` effect (site-type/allow-list filters used by Baugúr dm-181 / Lobelia dm-28). Used by Elwen (dm-8) and its sibling Nimloth (dm-20): "Agent only: may move to a Haven [{H}]." Elwen's "+2 direct influence against Elves and Elf Factions" reuses conditional `stat-modifier` `direct-influence` effects gated on `reason: "influence-check"` (follower-control, `availableDI`) and `reason: "opponent-influence-check"` with `target.race: "elf"` (seizing an opponent's elf character or elf faction — the opponent-influence target context threads the target's race for both kinds, so one clause covers "Elves and Elf Factions" on the steal path). The site-phase faction-influence roll is deliberately omitted (no minion elf faction exists to bring under control).
- `manifestation-swap` + character manifestation chains — a character card's `manifestId` field declares it a manifestation of another character (one-sided tag allowed; `sameManifestationEntity` in `engine/manifestations.ts` compares symmetrically). Two generic rules ride on the chain: glossary g.man.1 (a character cannot be played — normal play or `recruit-character` event — while a manifestation of the same entity is in play for either player; `manifestationOfEntityInPlay`) and rule 1.9 draft collisions (two picks manifesting the same entity are both set aside; `resolveDraftRound` in `reducer-setup.ts`). The `manifestation-swap` effect (`cardName`) is the sanctioned replacement play: while the bearer is in a company, its controller may play the named character from hand as a `manifestation-swap` action — a resource-style play per CRF 22 (Strider), offered in the organization/M-H/site aggregators (`legal-actions/manifestation-swap.ts`), never consuming the one-character-per-turn slot. The reducer (`handleManifestationSwap` in `reducer-organization.ts`, routed from all three phase reducers) brings the new manifestation into the old one's company at the same position untapped, transfers all attachments (items/allies/hazards/trophies) and control relationships (controlledBy, followers, leader-controlled in-play cards), and removes the old card from the game (owner's `outOfPlayPile`). Used by Strider (ba-1): "You may bring Aragorn II into play with Strider's company, removing Strider from the game and automatically transferring all cards on Strider to Aragorn II."
- `discard-to-recruit` — a generalized cousin of `manifestation-swap` for "discard <bearer> to play a character from your hand with his company" abilities. Effect fields: `requireHaven` (bearer's company must be at a Haven) and `filter` (a condition matched against the incoming hand character's definition, exposed as `target` — e.g. `{ "target.race": "hobbit" }`). While the bearer is in a company (and, with `requireHaven`, at a Haven), the controller may play a matching character from hand as a `discard-to-recruit` action — a resource-style play per CRF 22 (Folco Boffin), offered in the organization/M-H/site aggregators (`legal-actions/discard-to-recruit.ts`), never consuming the one-character-per-turn slot. The reducer (`handleDiscardToRecruit` in `reducer-organization.ts`, routed from all three phase reducers) brings the incoming character into the bearer's company at the same position untapped, transfers all attachments and control relationships exactly as `manifestation-swap` does, and — differing from a swap — sends the discarded bearer's card to its owner's **discard pile** (recyclable), not the out-of-play pile. Bypasses normal character-play gates (influence, home-site, once-per-turn) since the discard is the cost. Used by Folco Boffin (dm-180): "You may discard Folco Boffin at a Haven to play any Hobbit from your hand with his company."
- `ringwraith-follower-slots` — carried by a Ringwraith avatar; while it is the player's revealed Ringwraith, up to `count` other Ringwraith avatar cards may be played as Ringwraith followers in his company with no influence (CoE 2.II.2.1.R4–R5). Legal-action emitter `ringwraithFollowerPlayAction` in `legal-actions/organization-characters.ts`; the play reuses the standard `play-character` reducer with `controlledBy` = the avatar's instance. Also enforces rule 2.II.2.1.1 (no second avatar reveal once one is in play). Used by The Witch-king (le-58). The follower-side counterpart `ringwraith-self-follower` (a marker on the *follower* card) lets that card be played as a follower of the player's revealed Ringwraith **regardless** of whether the host carries `ringwraith-follower-slots` — it grants its own slot and is exempt from the host's slot-budget check (all other follower gates — Darkhaven/home-site, no-influence, one-character-per-turn — apply unchanged, via the same `ringwraithFollowerPlayAction`). Used by Ûvatha the Ringwraith (le-57): "He may join another Ringwraith's company during your organization phase and requires no influence to control." — plus a `grant-action recall-to-deck` (`enqueue-pending-fetch` discard→deck reshuffle filtered to `minion-resource-event`, gated `bearer.isRevealedAvatar` + `bearer.atHaven`), the same recall shape as The Mouth le-24 but narrowed to resource events and restricted to a Darkhaven. (Ûvatha's data JSON `text` had been altered to an incorrect "untap one character" ability; certification corrected it to the authoritative data/cards.json text.)
- `agent-discard-return-to-origin` — the agent may be discarded (hazard player's choice, not as an agent action, not against the hazard limit) at the moving company's new site during the opponent's M/H phase to force that company to return to its site of origin (CoE rule 2.IV.4: the company's M/H phase immediately ends — end-of-M/H corruption triggers still fire — the company no longer has a site path nor has moved this turn, and its site phase is blocked via a `site-phase-do-nothing` constraint, the same mechanism as `force-return-to-origin` environments). Eligibility mirrors `agent-tap-attack` (in play at turn start, not wounded, at the company's destination site) except no tap is required (tapped agents qualify) and face-down agents need no home-site binding — the discard reveals the card and returns its stack sites to the location deck. Legal actions: `agentDiscardReturnToOriginActions` in `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`; reducer: `handleAgentDiscardReturnToOrigin` in `mh-hazard-play.ts` (discards the agent, adds the constraint, sets `returnedToOrigin`, ends the company's M/H via `endCompanyMH`). Used by Baduila (dm-2).
- `agent-attack-modifier` — modifies the agent's own **standard site-phase attack** (rule 2.V.iii, the `declare-agent-attack` step), as opposed to `agent-tap-attack` (a card-granted special M/H-phase attack). Fields: `attackerAssigns` (the attacking player assigns strikes regardless of the agent's face-down/at-home state — overrides rule 3.ii.4, which grants attacker assignment only to a face-down agent at home; also sets `forceSingleTarget`) and `strikeEffect: "discard-item"` (a successful strike does not wound the defending character; the company must instead discard one item of the defender's choice — threaded onto `CombatState.strikeEffect` and resolved by the generic discard-item path in `combat-strike.ts` shared with `tap-agent-at-site`, dm-43; detainment attacks vs Ringwraith/Balrog defenders tap as usual and never trigger the discard). Consumed in `handleDeclareAgentAttack` (`reducer-site.ts`); no legal-action change (the standard declare action already exists for every agent). Used by Taladhan (dm-25): "Agent only: chooses defending characters; for each successful strike, the company must discard one item (of defender's choice), but the defending character is not harmed." Third field `tapForExtraStrike` (Elerína dm-7: "Agent only: may tap for an extra strike"): `declareAgentAttackActions` (`legal-actions/site.ts`) offers each declare action in a second variant carrying `tapForExtraStrike: true` when the agent is **untapped** (tapped/wounded agents get only the plain attack); the reducer validates the effect + untapped status, taps the agent as part of the declaration, and builds the combat with `strikesTotal: 2`. `forceSingleTarget` is now guarded on `strikesTotal === 1` — a 2-strike agent attack follows standard strike assignment (each strike to a different character where possible) even when the attacker assigns (face-down at home).
- `agent-tap-return-character` — hazard short-event played on one of the hazard player's **untapped agents** to roll an opponent character (whose printed home site matches the agent's current site) back to its owner's hand. Legal actions (`movementHazardActions` short-event branch, `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) offer one `play-hazard` per `(untapped agent, matching-home-site opponent character)` pair carrying `agentInstanceId` + `targetCharacterId` — company-independent (target may be in any of the opponent's companies) and blocked vs a minion/Balrog opponent (`isMinionOrBalrog`). The reducer `handleAgentTapReturnCharacter` (`mh-agents.ts`) taps the agent, discards the event, counts the hazard, and enqueues a generic `dice-check`: roller = hazard player, `comparison: "gt"`, `threshold = targetMind + mindBonus`, a `constant` `atHomeSiteBonus` modifier when the agent is at home, and `onPass: return-character-to-hand` with `allowItemTransfer: true`. The `return-character-to-hand` branch (`applyDiceCheckBranch`, `pending-reducers.ts`) now locates the target's **actual owner** (the returned character is the roller's *opponent*'s) before returning it. With `allowItemTransfer`, `returnCharacterToHand` enqueues a `transfer-returned-item` resolution so the owner may pull **one** discarded item back onto a company-mate ("one item may be transferred to another character in the same company") or decline — `transferReturnedItemActions` (`legal-actions/pending.ts`) / `applyTransferReturnedItemResolution` (`pending-reducers.ts`). Used by Pilfer Anything Unwatched (as-33).
- `agent-tap-influence` — the base rule-10.14 character effect: "Agent only: may tap to make an influence check on an ally, faction, or character." `targetKinds` (subset of `character` / `ally` / `faction`) lists which target kinds the agent may attempt. Legal actions (`agentInfluenceActions`, `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) offer one `agent-influence-attempt` per (in-play-at-turn-start, unwounded agent carrying the effect, eligible target): character/ally targets require the agent to be at the active company's site (`companyTargetSiteName` — the company's destination this turn, else its current site); faction targets require the agent to be at a site (of the hazard player's own alignment) where an opponent in-play faction is playable, independent of the company. The reducer `handleAgentInfluenceAttempt` (`mh-agents.ts`) reveals **and** taps the agent (not an agent action — `remainingActions` untouched, no hazard-limit count), applies the rule-10.14 bonuses (+2 DI if the agent is at one of its home sites, plus its own conditional `direct-influence` stat-modifiers via `agentConditionalDirectInfluence`; target mind or faction value treated as 0 with a +2 roll bonus when a character/ally target shares a home site with the agent, or a faction is playable at the agent's home site), and enqueues the standard `opponent-influence-defend` resolution with `revealedCard: null` ("the hazard player cannot reveal an identical card from their hand", so no item-target route). Used by Golodhros (dm-14): "Unique. Agent. Can use spirit-magic. Agent only: may tap to make an influence check on an ally, faction, or character." — this is also the reference fixture for the `rule-10.14-agent-influence` rules test. `agent-tap-faction-influence` (below) is the hazard-card variant that *grants* the same attempt to an agent without this effect of its own.
- `agent-tap-faction-influence` — hazard short-event that **grants** one of the hazard player's own untapped agents a rule-10.14 influence attempt against an **opponent faction in play** playable at the agent's current site (the agent needs no `agent-tap-influence` effect of its own — "if an effect allows an agent hazard to make an influence attempt"). `agentFilter` is a DSL condition on the agent's definition (`{ target: { name, race, skills, keywords } }`) rather than a hardcoded keyword; `attemptBonus` is a flat modifier on the attacker's side; `autoSuccessAtHomeSite` makes the attempt succeed without any roll when the faction is playable at one of the agent's *home* sites. Legal actions (short-event branch of `movementHazardActions`, `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) emit one `play-hazard` per (eligible untapped agent, opponent faction whose `playableAt` names the agent's site) carrying `agentInstanceId` + `targetFactionInstanceId`, company-independent and blocked vs a minion/Balrog opponent (`isMinionOrBalrog`). The reducer `handleAgentTapFactionInfluence` (`mh-agents.ts`, dispatched from `mh-hazard-play.ts`) taps **and reveals** the agent (declaring an influence attempt reveals it), discards the event, counts the hazard, leaves `remainingActions` untouched (the attempt is not an agent action), applies the rule-10.14 bonuses (+2 DI at a home site plus the agent's conditional DI modifiers; faction playable at a home site → value 0 and +2 roll) and enqueues the standard `opponent-influence-defend` resolution with `boostModifier = attemptBonus` and, where applicable, `autoSuccess: true`. `resolveOpponentInfluenceDefend` (`reducer-site.ts`) honours `autoSuccess` by skipping the defence roll (the defender keeps its `cancel-influence` window) and now derives the **defending side from `attempt.targetPlayer`** instead of `state.activePlayer`, so an attempt declared by the non-active hazard player discards the resource player's card (the two coincide for a site-phase attempt). Shared helpers `agentCurrentSiteName` / `agentMatchesFilter` (`reducer-utils.ts`) keep the emitter and the reducer agreeing on "the agent's site". Used by Twisted Tales (dm-96): "Playable on an untapped diplomat agent. Tap the agent who may then make an influence attempt against a faction playable at the agent's site. +6 to influence attempt. Attempt is automatically successful if target faction is playable at the agent's home site. Cannot be played if your opponent is a minion player."
- `agent-tap-opponent-influence` — hazard short-event that **grants** one of the hazard player's own untapped agents a rule-10.14 influence attempt against an opponent's in-play **character or ally** — the sibling of `agent-tap-faction-influence` (above) for non-faction targets. `targetKinds` lists which of `character`/`ally` the grant covers; `agentFilter` is a DSL condition on the agent's definition (omit for any untapped agent); `attemptBonus` is the flat attacker-side modifier, optionally overridden by `diplomatAttemptBonus` when the agent has the diplomat skill; `homeSiteBonus` adds further when the target character shares a home site with the agent, or the target ally is playable at one of the agent's home sites. Legal actions (short-event branch of `movementHazardActions`, `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) emit one `play-hazard` per (eligible untapped agent, opponent character that is not an avatar, or opponent ally attached to any of the opponent's characters) carrying `agentInstanceId` + `targetCharacterId`/`targetAllyId` — company-independent, like Pilfer Anything Unwatched's `agent-tap-return-character`, and blocked vs a minion/Balrog opponent (`isMinionOrBalrog`). The reducer `handleAgentTapOpponentInfluence` (`mh-agents.ts`, dispatched from `mh-hazard-play.ts`) taps **and reveals** the agent, discards the event, counts the hazard, leaves `remainingActions` untouched, applies the rule-10.14 bonuses (+2 DI at a home site plus the agent's conditional DI modifiers against the target's race; target mind treated as 0 with +2 to the roll when a character shares a home site with the agent — `parseHomesiteNames` overlap — or an ally is playable at one of the agent's home sites — `playableAt` overlap, mirroring the faction-at-home-site check), then layers `boostModifier = (diplomat ? diplomatAttemptBonus : attemptBonus) + (sharesHome ? homeSiteBonus : 0)` and enqueues the standard `opponent-influence-defend` resolution (no `autoSuccess` path — the defender always rolls). Used by Your Welcome Is Doubtful (dm-104): "Playable on an untapped agent. Tap the agent who may then make an influence attempt against an ally or character. +6 to influence attempt (+10 if the agent is a diplomat). An additional +7 to the attempt if target character has the same home site as the agent or if target ally is playable at the agent's home site. Cannot be played if your opponent is a minion player."
- `opponent-influence-override` + `discard-self-when` — a stage permanent-event that reshapes a **named influencer's** opponent-influence attempts (CoE 10.10). While in play, every opponent-influence attempt made by the influencer whose name equals `influencer` (the active player's avatar) is modified: `fromAnySite` lifts the same-site requirement so he may target the opponent's cards at any of their companies / any in-play faction (`opponentInfluenceActions` in `legal-actions/site.ts` builds a per-company `eligibleInfluencers` set — everyone at the same site, else only the override influencer; the faction loop likewise); `generalInfluenceSubstitution` `{ divisor, roundUp?, max }` replaces the influencer's unused DI with `unusedGI / divisor` (rounded, capped) in the queued `opponent-influence-defend` payload; and `regionDistancePenalty` records the inclusive region distance to the target's site (CRF 22: both endpoints count; for a faction, the nearest region it is playable in via `factionPlayableSiteRegions`), which `resolveOpponentInfluenceDefend` subtracts from the attacker's final result (`reducer-site.ts` `handleOpponentInfluenceAttempt` detects the override from state via `getOpponentInfluenceOverride`, `generalInfluenceSubstitutionValue`, `companySiteRegion`, `influenceRegionPenalty` in `reducer-utils.ts`; `regionDistanceInclusive` in `movement-map.ts`). The companion `discard-self-when` effect discards the carrying card once a player-state condition holds (`sweepDiscardSelfWhen` in `discard-self-when.ts`, a `postReduce` sweep evaluating the condition against `buildPlayerStateContext`). Used by Prophet of Doom (wh-106): "Playable if you are Pallando and have at least 12 stage points and 5 factions in play. Pallando need not be at the appropriate site … modified by half (rounded up) of Pallando's unused general influence (max 10) instead of his unused direct influence. Subtract … the number of regions between Pallando's site and the site … Discard if you have fewer than 5 factions in play. Cannot be duplicated." — `play-condition` player-state (`player.avatar: "Pallando"`, `stagePoints >= 12`, `factionCount >= 5`), `opponent-influence-override`, `discard-self-when` (`factionCount < 5`), `duplication-limit` scope game.
- `check-modifier` constraint `constraintWhen` (opponent-influence booster) + **item** opponent-influence target — two additions to the opponent-influence subsystem (CoE rules 8.1/8.3). (1) A one-shot influence `check-modifier` constraint (the Muster/`play-option → add-constraint` shape) may carry an optional `constraintWhen` condition (threaded from the `add-constraint` apply through `chain-reducer.ts` onto `constraint.kind.when`). `handleOpponentInfluenceAttempt` (`reducer-site.ts`) builds an `opponent-influence-check` resolver context (`bearer` = influencer, `target` = `{ kind, race, name }`) and folds in **and consumes** matching one-shot constraints — but only those carrying a `when` (no-`when` constraints stay for the faction path); the sum is stored as `attempt.boostModifier` and added to the attacker's side in `resolveOpponentInfluenceDefend`. Symmetrically `resolveInfluenceAttemptRoll` skips consuming a one-shot constraint whose `when` fails to match the faction context, so an opponent-influence booster is never swallowed by a faction check. (2) `targetKind: "item"` is now a full opponent-influence target: `opponentInfluenceActions` (`legal-actions/site.ts`) offers an item attempt only when the item has no permanent-event attached (`attachedToItem`) and an identical item sits in the influencer's hand (mandatory reveal, rule 8.1); the reducer compares against the controlling character's mind (subtracting its DI), does **not** zero the value on reveal (rule 8.3 non-item clause), and `discardInfluencedCard` removes the item on success. On a successful reveal-influence the un-played revealed card now returns to hand (rule 8.4 play not automated) rather than vanishing. Used by Mine or No One's (ba-68): "+10 to an influence attempt by The Balrog against an opponent's item, ally, Troll faction, or Orc faction. Cannot be duplicated on a given attempt." — `play-target` The Balrog + `play-option` add-constraint check-modifier +10 with a `constraintWhen` (`reason: opponent-influence-check`, `$or` over item/ally/Orc-or-Troll-faction) + `duplication-limit` scope `active-check`. The `target.kind: "character"` branch of the same `constraintWhen` shape is exercised by Old Friendship (tw-293): "Diplomat only. +5 to an influence check against a character by a diplomat. Alternatively, +4 to a corruption check by a character in a diplomat's company." — `play-target` character filtered to `company.containsDiplomat`, one `play-option` (`when: target.skills includes diplomat`) adding the +5 `constraintWhen: { reason: "opponent-influence-check", "target.kind": "character" }` constraint, and a second `play-option` (`when: pending.corruptionCheckTargetsMe`) adding a plain +4 corruption `check-modifier`, reusing the exact New Friendship (tw-292) shape for the reactive corruption boost.
- `ahunt-attack` grouped multi-attack + `faction-influence-restriction` — a single hazard permanent-event may carry **multiple** `ahunt-attack` effects (each fires as its own order-effects combat, one at a time) plus two optional ahunt fields: `body` is now **optional** (`effect.body ?? null` — omitted = no printed body, so a defeated strike triggers no "body check vs creature", used by region attacks that list only strikes/prowess), `noEffectOnMinion: true` skips the attack when the moving (defending) player is a Ringwraith/Sauron minion (`collectMatchingAhuntAttacks` in `mh-steps.ts`), and `groupReward: { toDefenderKillPile: true }` moves the card into the defending (moving) player's kill pile once **every** ahunt attack from that card instance was defeated in one company's order-effects step. Per-attack outcomes are recorded by `finalizeCombat` (`combat-finalize.ts`) into `MovementHazardPhaseState.ahuntGroupOutcomes` (reset per company in `mh-hazard-play.ts`) and the reward is applied by `applyAhuntGroupRewards` (`mh-steps.ts`) when `handleOrderEffects` finishes the sequence; a companion `mp-in-pile` (`{ category: "kill", value }`) scores the reward MPs from the kill pile. The `faction-influence-restriction` environment (`regionNames`, `modifier`, `blockCards`, `noEffectOnMinion`) penalises a character's faction influence check at a site in the named regions and suppresses one-shot influence boosts sourced from any card in `blockCards` ("cannot be done with Muster"); ignored for a minion influencer when `noEffectOnMinion` is set. Applied by both the influence-attempt legal-action generator (displayed `need`) and the roll resolver (`resolveInfluenceAttemptRoll` in `reducer-site.ts`) via the shared `collectFactionInfluenceRestriction` helper (`reducer-utils.ts`). Used by Mordor in Arms (dm-72): three Orc/Orc/Troll attacks in Nurn (2 kill MP to the opponent if all defeated), -6 to faction influence at seven named regions with no Muster, no effect on a minion player.
- `influence-modification` + `cancel-manifestation-attacks` + faction `manifestId` — the Dragons "Roused" faction primitives (Smaug Roused le-285). A minion-resource-faction may carry a `manifestId` (chain tag, e.g. `tw-90` for Smaug): the faction-play legal-action generator now blocks it while another manifestation of the same entity is in play on either side (glossary g.man.1) via `manifestationOfEntityInPlay` (characters/allies/agents) plus the new `manifestationInCardsInPlay` (in-play factions/permanent-/long-events) helper in `engine/manifestations.ts`. `influence-modification` (`options[].discardItemSubtype` + `value`) models an optional cost-bearing faction-influence modifier: `legal-actions/site.ts` emits one extra `influence-attempt` per eligible **carried** item of a matching subtype (with `need` already lowered and a `discardForBonus` `{ itemInstanceId, value }` payload); `handleInfluenceAttemptDeclare` (`reducer-site.ts`) discards the item and threads `bonusModifier` onto the chain payload → `faction-influence-roll` pending kind → `resolveInfluenceAttemptRoll` (applied whether or not the check succeeds). `cancel-manifestation-attacks` (`manifestId`) is a passive in-play cancellation: `collectMatchingAhuntAttacks` (`mh-steps.ts`) skips any Ahunt whose source card's `manifestId` matches, while the carrier's controller is the moving player (`playerCancelsManifestationAttacks`) — covering the faction's own region attack against its controller and an opponent's same-chain Ahunt; manifestation uniqueness makes the Ahunt the reachable attack vector. Used by Smaug Roused (le-285): playable at The Lonely Mountain (influence # 13), discard a major item (+3)/greater item (+6), cancels Smaug's attacks vs your companies, and an `ahunt-attack` region attack (Dragon 2×17/8, attacker chooses defenders) over Withered Heath / Northern Rhovanion / Grey Mountain Narrows / Iron Hills. (deck-validation's hazard-count heuristic now guards its `ahunt-attack`/`dragon-at-home` check on `cardType === 'hazard-event'` so a resource faction with an Ahunt is not miscounted.) Also used unchanged by Scorba Roused (le-284, manifestId `td-63`): playable at Zarak Dûm (influence # 12), same discard modifications, cancels Scorba's attacks vs your companies, region attack Dragon 3×12/8 (attacker chooses defenders) over Angmar / Gundabad / Forochel — no new engine work needed.
- `auto-influence-faction` — marker granting no-check influence of a **named faction**: `{ "type": "auto-influence-faction", "faction": "Riders of Rohan" }`. Carried by an item on the influencing character (or printed on the character), it flows to the influencer through `collectCharacterEffects` in the `faction-influence-check` context, so an item's grant reaches its bearer. `resolveAutoInfluenceFaction` (`resolver.ts`) tests the collected effects against the faction being influenced (exact name match); when it matches, the faction-influence legal-action generator (`legal-actions/site.ts`) surfaces the attempt with `need: 0` / "Automatic influence" and `resolveInfluenceAttemptRoll` (`reducer-site.ts`) skips the 2d6 roll, forces `total = influence #` (guaranteed success), consumes no RNG, and emits no dice-roll effect — the site still taps as usual. The companion **direct-influence** clause reuses the ordinary `stat-modifier` `stat: direct-influence` shape gated on `reason: influence-check` + `target.homesite $includes "Edoras"` (the dm-10 Firiel / Grima precedent), resolved through `availableDI` which already collects item effects. Used by Red Arrow (tw-312): "Bearer may automatically influence the Riders of Rohan. +5 to the bearer's direct influence against any character with Edoras as a home site."
- `company-strike` — hazard short-event that makes each character in the active M/H company face one strike (not a creature attack): fixed `prowess`, no creature race, optional `uncancelable` and `bodyCheckModifier`. Initiates a `CombatState` with `attackSource: company-strike-event` and `strikesTotal = company size` (`chain-reducer.ts`); the `bodyCheckModifier` is applied to each character body-check roll in `reducer-combat.ts` `handleBodyCheckRoll`. Paired with the `play-condition` `requires: 'region-through-or-leave'` (gates on region movement that leaves/passes through named regions, excluding the destination region — `checkRegionThroughOrLeave` in `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`). Used by Cruel Caradhras (td-9).
- `company-tap-characters` — hazard short-event effect that, on chain resolution during the M/H phase, taps every **untapped** character in the active company whose **effective mind** is strictly below a `mindBelow` value-expression threshold and that matches an optional per-character `filter` (`chain-reducer.ts`, on a bare — no `targetCharacterId` — short-event entry). The threshold context exposes `spawnCardsInPlay` = the number of `spawn`-keyword cards in play across both players (characters, allies, attached hazards/items, and bare `cardsInPlay` permanent-events — `countSpawnCardsInPlay` in `reducer-utils.ts`; "Eliminated Spawn do not count" is automatic since eliminated cards leave the in-play zones). Wizards/Ringwraiths are avatar races with `null` printed mind (treated as `0`, always under the threshold), so the `filter` is what excludes them. Played on the company via `play-target` `target: 'company'` — the company-targeting **short-event** branch in `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts` gates on the current-or-destination site's type/keywords (`target.siteType`/`target.siteKeywords`) and cross-multiplies a `play-discard-cost` so the player picks the cost card. Used by The Reek (ba-23): `play-target` company (`$or` [`target.siteType: ruins-and-lairs`, `target.siteKeywords $includes under-deeps`]) + `play-discard-cost` (an Animal/Spider hazard-creature from hand) + `company-tap-characters` (`mindBelow: "2 + spawnCardsInPlay"`, `filter` excluding wizard/ringwraith races).
- `company-tap-roll` (+ `company-tap-characters` on-guard apply, `on-guard-reveal` `playedFilter`) — Heedless Revelry (le-114) primitives. **Normal play**: hazard short-event on the whole company (`play-target` `target: 'company'`, filter context now also exposes `target.moving` — the company has a declared `destinationSite` — and `target.hasRingwraith` — any company character has race `ringwraith`; `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) for "playable on a non-Ringwraith company that is not moving". On bare short-event chain resolution during the M/H phase (`chain-reducer.ts`), every **untapped** character matching the per-character `filter` (context `{ target: { race, mind, name, skills, cardType } }`) is collected with the summed `rollModifiers` whose `when` matches (e.g. -2 for `target.cardType: hero-character`), and one `company-tap-roll` pending resolution is enqueued (`pending-reducers.ts` / `legal-actions/pending.ts`): the company's controller rolls 2d6 per character, one action at a time; roll + modifier **strictly greater** than the character's effective mind taps the character; after the last roll the chain entry resolves. No qualifying characters → no-op. **On-guard mode**: two `on-guard-reveal` effects. Trigger `resource-play` now honors an optional `playedFilter` condition matched against the deferred played card's definition (items/allies only for le-114) in `onGuardWindowActions`; trigger `influence-attempt` reveals on the influence chain as before (and is **no longer** accepted in the resource-play window — that was a latent over-permissive branch). On the revealed short-event's site-phase chain resolution (`chain-reducer.ts`), the `apply` of type `company-tap-characters` taps every untapped company character matching its filter (no roll, no mind gate — `mindBelow` on `company-tap-characters` is now optional, absent = no threshold). The deferred play is not interfered with: it still runs when the window closes (the deferred reducer does not re-validate untapped status). A revealed **short** event is moved to the revealing player's discard pile at reveal time (both `applyOnGuardRevealAtResource` in `reducer-site.ts` and `handleChainRevealOnGuard` in `chain-reducer.ts`), mirroring hand-played shorts so the instance never vanishes from state.
- `fw-mp-full (cards: items)` — Fallen-wizard item marshalling-point exemption (MEWH §4 exception). MEWH §4 clamps every non-stage card a Fallen-wizard controls to a flat 1 MP; this effect, carried by a Fallen-wizard avatar, exempts the player's items matching its `filter` so they score full printed MP instead. Collected once per player from the player's in-play characters in `recompute-derived.ts` and consumed in `addItemMP` (`fwItemMpExempt` path). Items on any character of the player are covered ("Your items"), not only items on the avatar. Used by Saruman (wh-9): non-weapon/non-armor/non-shield/non-helmet items worth full MP.
- `fw-character-ally-mp` — Fallen-wizard character/ally marshalling-point floor (MEWH §4 exception). Carried by an in-play stage permanent-event; the player's **characters and allies** whose *printed* MP is at least `threshold` each score `value` MP instead of the flat §4 1-MP clamp (cards below the threshold keep their normal value; factions/items/other cards-in-play are untouched). Collected per player from in-play cards in `recompute-derived.ts` (`fwCharacterAllyMpCaps`) and consumed in `addMP`'s `fwCharAllyCaps` path for character and ally scoring only; the override never applies to stage cards or to non-Fallen-wizard players. Used by Great Patron (wh-72): characters/allies normally worth 2+ MP are each worth 2.
- `fw-mp-full (cards: characters)` — Fallen-wizard **character** marshalling-point exemption (MEWH §4 exception), the character-scoring sibling of `fw-mp-full (cards: allies)`. MEWH §4 clamps every non-stage card a Fallen-wizard controls to a flat 1 MP, including his characters (which normally score their printed character MP). This effect exempts characters matching its `filter` (omit → every character) so they score full printed character MP; `inAvatarCompany: true` restricts to the avatar's company. Collected per player from in-play characters and `cardsInPlay` in `recompute-derived.ts` (`fwCharMpFullEntries` / `cardExemptFromFwClamp`) and consumed at the character-scoring loop via `addMP`'s `fwFullMp` path (precedence over any `fw-character-ally-mp` cap; never affects stage cards or non-FW players). Used by the Fallen-wizard Gandalf (wh-4): "Your characters … are each worth full marshalling points" — carried player-wide (no `filter`), paired with player-wide `fw-mp-full (cards: allies)` (`filter cardType hero-resource-ally`) for the "and hero allies" half. wh-4's remaining clauses reuse existing primitives: `grant-action untap-self` (cost-free, `oncePerTurn`, `when bearer.status tapped`, apply `set-character-status target bearer status untapped` — "May untap at the end of your organization phase"), `grant-action test-gold-ring` (like tw-156), and the `spirit-magic` skill entry ("Can use spirit-magic", accepted by spirit-magic play-target filters via `getEffectiveSkills`).
- `ally-movement-restriction-exemption` — exempts matching allies the source's controller has in play from their printed "Discard if he/she moves to …" movement restriction (a `bearer-company-moves` self-discard; CRF 22 defines an ally's "movement restriction" as exactly that clause). During the end-of-movement discard sweep (`mh-hazard-play.ts` step 8a-2) the effect is collected from the moving player's in-play characters and `cardsInPlay`; when an ally that would be discarded by a `bearer-company-moves` self-discard (its own or an attached converted-creature event's) matches an exemption `filter` (a `null`/absent filter exempts every ally), the discard is skipped and the ally is kept. Used by Radagast (wh-8): "Hero allies Radagast controls have no movement restrictions." — `filter` `{ cardType: "hero-resource-ally" }`. Radagast's other clauses reuse existing primitives: `faction-mp-override` (his unique non-Man/Dwarf/Dúnadan/Hobbit/Orc/Troll factions worth 2, `when` `$and` of `faction.unique` + `$not` `faction.race $in […]`), `fw-mp-full` (`cards: "allies"`) player-wide (`filter cardType hero-resource-ally`), and `draw-modifier` (`value: "sitePath.wildernessCount"`, like tw-178 Radagast).
- `permanent-event-mp` — overrides the marshalling-point value of the controller's in-play permanent-events that "require a site where a resource category is playable" (i.e. carry a `play-condition` with `requires: 'site-has-resource'` and the matching `subtype`). While the carrying card is in play, each such permanent-event scores exactly `value` MP in its own marshalling category, overriding its printed value and (for a Fallen-wizard) the MEWH §4 flat-1-MP clamp. Collected once per player from `cardsInPlay` and consumed in `recompute-derived.ts` (`permanentEventMpOverrides`/`permanentEventMpOverride`). Used by Man of Skill (wh-119): "Your permanent-events that require a site where Information is playable are each worth 2 marshalling points."
- `noncharacter-mp-override` — re-values a Fallen-wizard's **non-character** cards in play. Carried by an in-play stage permanent-event; every MP-scoring non-character card the player controls — items and allies borne by his characters, and factions / misc permanent-events in `cardsInPlay` — that matches the effect's `when` condition scores exactly `value`, overriding both its printed MP and (for a Fallen-wizard) the MEWH §4 flat-1-MP clamp. **Characters are never touched** — the override is applied only in the item/ally/cards-in-play scoring loops, not the character loop, so "non-character" is enforced structurally. Each candidate is matched against `{ card: { unique, normalMp, cardType, name, race } }` (`normalMp` = the card's *printed* marshalling points; last matching rule wins). Collected per player in `recompute-derived.ts` (`nonCharacterMpOverrideRules`) from both `cardsInPlay` **and** every character's `items` — the carrier is placed "on the avatar" so it lives in the avatar's `items`, which also satisfies the "if on Gandalf" gate (the card can only ever be attached to Gandalf via its `play-target`). Resolved by `nonCharacterMpOverride` (cards with no printed MP are never overridden). Used by Give Welcome to the Unexpected (wh-99): "your unique non-character cards normally worth 1 marshalling point are each worth 2 marshalling points" — `when { card.unique: true, card.normalMp: 1 }`, `value: 2`; paired with `stage-points` 2 and `play-target` `character` filter `{ target.name: "Gandalf" }` ("place this card on Gandalf", attaching to his `items` like Pallando's Hood wh-105 / Wizard's Myrmidon wh-84). Also used by Grey Embassy (wh-100), which re-values only **unique hero factions** (matched by `card.cardType: "hero-resource-faction"`, so items/allies/other cards are untouched) via **two** rules keyed on the printed value: `when { …, card.normalMp: { $lte: 2 } }` → `value: 2` and `when { …, card.normalMp: { $gte: 3 } }` → `value: 3` (the two thresholds partition all factions, so a faction worth ≤2 becomes 2 and one worth ≥3 becomes 3); paired with `stage-points` 3 and the same `play-target` `character` filter `{ target.name: "Gandalf" }`.
- `leader-control` — faction "control by a leader" mechanic on the LE *Orcs of Udûn*-style factions (le-262/275/279/281/282/291). When an Orc/Troll **Leader** (races + keyword from the effect) influences the faction, the legal-action generator emits a second `influence-attempt` variant carrying `placeUnderLeaderControl: true` (`legal-actions/site.ts`); on success the faction enters `cardsInPlay` with `controlledBy` = the leader and the site is left **untapped** (`reducer-site.ts` `resolveInfluenceAttemptRoll`). The faction is discarded when the leader **moves** (M/H step 8, `reducer-movement-hazard.ts`) or **leaves play** (post-action sweep `discardOrphanedControlledFactions` in `reducer-utils.ts`, called from `postReduce` in `reducer.ts`). A leader controlling `groupBonus.count`+ such factions earns `groupBonus.mp` extra faction MPs (`recompute-derived.ts`). Eligibility shared via `leaderControlEligibility`/`getLeaderControlEffect` (`reducer-utils.ts`). The "Standard Modifications" line is modeled separately with `check-modifier` effects gated on `controller.inPlay`. Used by Orcs of Udûn (le-282).
- `recruit-character` — short resource-event that brings one character from hand into play under relaxed recruitment rules. The legal-action helper `recruitViaEventActions` (`legal-actions/recruit-via-event.ts`) is wired into the organization, movement/hazard, and site phase aggregators ("any phase the company is at a site") and emits a `play-character` carrying `viaEventInstanceId` per eligible (recruit, company at a `siteTypes` site, `controlledBy: "direct-influence"` controller with enough unused DI) combination; avatars, recruits failing the optional `filter`, and unique-already-in-play characters are skipped. The shared `handlePlayCharacter` (`reducer-organization.ts`, now also routed from `reducer-site.ts`/`reducer-movement-hazard.ts` for this path) brings the recruit in under the controller's direct influence, discards the event, and — when `bypassOneCharacterLimit` is set — skips the one-character-per-turn bookkeeping (organization phase state is only touched when actually in the organization phase). Used by A Chance Meeting (tw-188). Extended for We Have Come to Kill (le-252): "A character may be brought into play under general or direct influence (if you have enough unused) at any Shadow-hold, Ruins & Lairs, or Border-hold … May be used to bring in Ringwraith followers or agents, but not Ringwraiths or Fallen-wizards." — **(1)** `controlledBy` is now `"direct-influence" | "general-influence" | "either"`; the general-influence branch emits `controlledBy: "general"` gated on `generalInfluenceControlLimit − generalInfluenceUsed` and overrides rule 2.II.2.2's "GI play only at the avatar's site" exactly as `siteTypes` overrides the haven/home-site restriction. **(2)** `allowAgents` — an agent may be recruited, overriding rule 2.II.2.2.5's home-site confinement (the passive counterpart of Bree le-356's `site-rule: allow-agent-play`); Wizard/Balrog players still treat agents as hazards (1.3.W2/1.3.B2). **(3)** `allowRingwraithFollowers` — the event counts as "a card or ability that allows a Ringwraith follower to be played" (rule 2.II.2.1.R4): a Ringwraith avatar card in hand joins the player's revealed Ringwraith, whose company must be at a `siteTypes` site (in place of the Darkhaven/home-site condition), costing one point of that Ringwraith's unused DI per rule 2.II.2.1.R5 — free when a `ringwraith-follower-slots` slot is open (The Witch-king le-58) or the card carries `ringwraith-self-follower` (Ûvatha le-57); barred while the player counts as Sauron. Avatars are otherwise never recruits, so "not Ringwraiths or Fallen-wizards" holds structurally as well as via le-252's `filter`. The loop also now skips companies closed by `block-company-joins` (Fell Rider).
- `draw-cards` — resource short-event play effect that draws `count` cards from the top of the playing player's play deck into their hand (stopping at deck exhaustion, no instance lost). Resolved directly in `handlePlayResourceShortEvent` (`reducer-events.ts`) on the final disposal path. When `removeFromGame` is true, the spent event card is routed to the player's `outOfPlayPile` (removed from the game) instead of the discard pile, so it can never be recurred. Used by Dark Tryst (as-80): "Draw three cards and remove this card from the game."
- `general-influence-exempt` + `own-mp-not-counted` + `bearer-cannot-move` reuse + `on-event: resource-taps-or-requires-site` — the "park a character at a site" permanent-event primitives (Await the Advent of Allies dm-117). The card is a resource permanent-event played on a character (stored in the host's `items`, like every other character-attached permanent-event), gated by a `play-target: character` filter (`target.isAvatar $ne true`, `target.mind $lte 6`) and a `play-condition: site-type` listing every non-Haven type (free-hold / border-hold / ruins-and-lairs / shadow-hold / dark-hold — the le-165 non-Darkhaven list). Two new **marker** effects are read off the host via `characterBearsAttachedEffect` (`reducer-utils.ts`, scans `char.items` by effect type — the `getControlRestriction` pattern): `general-influence-exempt` makes the host's mind skip the `generalInfluenceUsed` accumulator (`recompute-derived.ts`), and `own-mp-not-counted` nullifies the host's **own** printed character MP in the character-scoring loop (items/allies it bears still score). "The character's company may not move" reuses the existing `play-flag: bearer-cannot-move` (Shifter of Hues wh-115) — `companyHasImmobileCharacter` already gates both `planMovementActions` and `handlePlanMovement` — so no constraint lifecycle: discarding the card lifts every marker at once. Three discard triggers: "leaves play" is the generic `discardCharacter`/`eliminateCombatantFromStrike` items-to-discard path (no card code); "becomes wounded" extends the `bearer-wounded` combat-finalization scan to a wounded character's attached **items/events** (previously allies-only — `combat-finalize.ts`); "play a resource that taps or requires the site" is the new `on-event: resource-taps-or-requires-site` self-discard, fired by `fireResourceTapsSiteDiscards` (`reducer-site.ts`) whenever a site-tapping resource play (item/ally attach path and faction-influence path, gated on the site actually tapping) lands in the bearer's company. Rounded out by `duplication-limit` scope `character`. Used by Await the Advent of Allies (dm-117).
- `on-event: company-mh-end-at-site` + `offer-restore-character` — Haven restore offer. A permanent event bound to a Haven (`play-target` target `site`, `attachedToSite` set on play) may carry this trigger. When a company controlled by the same player finishes its movement/hazard phase at the bound haven (`endCompanyMH` → `fireHavenRestoreTriggers` in `mh-hazard-play.ts`) **and** it has at least one tapped or wounded character, a `haven-restore-character` pending resolution is enqueued for the controlling player (scoped to the upcoming Site phase; pending resolutions short-circuit every phase action so it is resolved immediately following the M/H phase). The player may pass or pick one character via `restore-character-by-effect`: a tapped character is untapped, a wounded (inverted) character is healed one step to tapped. Legal actions in `legal-actions/pending.ts` (`havenRestoreCharacterActions`), resolver in `pending-reducers.ts` (`applyHavenRestoreCharacterResolution`). The card is discarded when no company occupies the bound haven (the `discardOrphanedSiteAttachedEvents` sweep — "site card returned to the location deck"). Used by Hall of Fire (dm-134). **Character-attached extension** (Lure of Creation tw-56): the same event name, declared on a hazard attached to a character (not a site), supports `apply: { type: "force-check", check: "corruption" }` gated by an optional `when` evaluated against `{ bearer: { atHaven } }` — the same context shape `untap-phase-end` uses, computed via `isHavenForPlayer` (so a Fallen-wizard bearer's Wizardhavens count as `{H}`). Implemented as a sibling scan, `fireCharacterCorruptionAtSiteTriggers` in `mh-hazard-play.ts`, called from `endCompanyMH` right after `fireHavenRestoreTriggers`: for every character in the company that just ended its M/H phase, scans attached hazards for this on-event and enqueues a `corruption-check` pending resolution (phase-scoped to `MovementHazard`, `allowSupport: true` per CoE 7.1.1) on a match. Used by Lure of Creation (tw-56): "…makes a corruption check at the end of any movement/hazard phase in a turn during which his company moved to a Haven [{H}]."
- `reshuffle-from-discard` — resource short-event play effect that, for each affected player (`scope`: `"all-players"` default, or `"self"`), pulls every card whose definition matches `filter` out of that player's discard pile and shuffles them into their play deck. Resolved directly in `handlePlayResourceShortEvent` (`reducer-events.ts`) before the spent event card lands in the playing player's discard pile (the event itself never matches). No instance is lost — matched cards move discard→deck, non-matches stay in discard. Used by Horns, Horns, Horns (dm-140): "Each player removes all factions from his discard pile and shuffles them into his play deck."
- `new-hand` — resource short-event effect that shuffles the playing player's entire hand and discard pile into their play deck and draws a fresh hand of `handSize` cards. Declared on the chain of effects (CoE 9.4/9.5, the Dark Tryst pattern — `handlePlayResourceShortEvent` routes it, `resolveEntry` in `chain-reducer.ts` applies it once both players pass priority). Site cards are structurally unaffected (separate `siteDeck`/`siteDiscardPile` zones); drawing stops at deck exhaustion; the reshuffle is not a rule-1.31 deck exhaustion. The spent card lands in the discard pile after the shuffle (never swept into the deck), where a companion `play-flag: remove-from-game` relocates it to the out-of-play pile. Used by Favor of the Valar (tw-239): "Playable during your organization phase. Shuffle your hand and your discard pile into your play deck (site cards remain in the discard pile). Draw a new hand of 8 cards. Remove Favor of the Valar from the game." — with `play-window` phase `organization` (the standard phase gate in `playResourceShortEventActions` / `heroResourceShortEventActions`) and the rule-1.18 `deck-restriction` FW ban.
- `force-opponent-discard` — hazard short-event effect that forces the card-player's **opponent** (the resource/active player) to discard one card of a named category, chosen by the opponent, or — if none is available — reveal their hand. Resolved when the event resolves on the chain (`chain-reducer.ts` `resolveEntry`): the effect gathers the opponent's candidates from the sources in `sources` (`"hand"` and/or `"carried"` = items held by the opponent's in-play characters), where `match: "ring"` matches any card carrying the `ring` keyword or the `gold-ring` subtype. When at least one candidate exists a `force-discard-card` pending resolution (actor = opponent) is enqueued so the opponent picks exactly one to discard (`forceDiscardCardActions` / `applyForceDiscardCardResolution`, wired in `pending-handlers.ts`); the chosen card moves from wherever it sits (hand or a character's items) to the opponent's discard pile. When no candidate exists and `fallbackRevealHand` is set, the opponent's current hand identities are revealed to the card-player instead (`revealInstances` over the opponent's hand, recorded in `GameState.revealedInstances`). Used by Rolled down to the Sea (wh-29): "Opponent must discard a ring from his hand or from one of his companies if available. If no rings are available as such, he must reveal his hand to you."
- `cycle-hand` — (hazard) short-event effect that lets the **playing player** (`entry.declaredBy` — the hazard player, for a hazard event) cycle their own hand when the event resolves on the chain (`chain-reducer.ts` `applyCycleHand`). It optionally reveals the hand to the opponent (`revealHand`, via `revealInstances`), partitions the hand by the `keepInHand` DSL filter (matching cards stay in hand, the rest are set aside), draws from the top of the play deck until the hand reaches the player's effective hand size (`resolveHandSize`, gated by `drawToHandSize`, default true; stops at deck exhaustion — no card lost), and places the set-aside cards face-down on top of the play deck (`setAsideTo: "deck-top"`). When two or more cards were set aside, an `arrange-deck-top` pending resolution (actor = the playing player) lets them order those top cards one at a time via the `arrange-deck-top-card` action (`arrangeDeckTopActions` / `applyArrangeDeckTopResolution`, wired in `pending-handlers.ts`); the resolution is independent of the chain (the entry is still marked resolved) and the opponent waits until it clears. The set-aside cards sit on the deck the whole time, so no instance ever floats. Used by Revealed to all Watchers (dm-85): "Reveal your hand to opponent. Place all non-hazard cards from your hand off to the side. Draw cards from your play deck until your hand size is reached. Place the non-hazard cards from off to the side face down on top of your play deck in any order you choose."
- `reveal-remove-from-discard` — (hazard) short-event effect that, when the event resolves un-negated on the chain (`chain-reducer.ts` `resolveEntry`), reveals a random slice of the **opponent's** (resource player's) discard pile and lets the card-player remove one non-unique card from the game. It draws `count` cards **at random** from the opponent's discard pile (`shuffle(pile, state.rng)` → first `count`, so replays stay deterministic; fewer than `count` reveals all; empty pile fizzles), reveals them to the card-player (`revealInstances`), and — if any revealed card is removable (non-unique; **sites treated as unique** per the French errata, via `!isSiteCard` + `unique !== true`) — enqueues a `reveal-remove-from-discard` pending resolution (actor = the card-player, `entry.declaredBy`). The card-player picks one via a `remove-revealed-card` action (moves the card from the opponent's discard pile to their `outOfPlayPile` — removed from the game) or declines with `pass` ("You may choose…"); the un-chosen revealed cards stay in the discard pile. The resolution is independent of the chain (entry still marked resolved); legal actions in `legal-actions/pending.ts` (`revealRemoveFromDiscardActions`), reducer in `pending-reducers.ts` (`applyRevealRemoveFromDiscardResolution`), wired in `pending-handlers.ts`. Used by Aware of their Ways (dm-46): "Opponent reveals four cards at random from his discard pile. You may choose a non-unique one and remove it from play. Opponent discards the other three."
- `reveal-deck-choose-penalty` — (hazard) short-event effect that, when the event resolves un-negated on the chain (`chain-reducer.ts` `resolveEntry`), reveals the **top** N cards of the **opponent's** play deck and forces the opponent to pay one of two penalties. N = the number of cards in play (either player's `cardsInPlay`) matching `countInPlayMatching` (for ba-16, Spawn cards — `{ keywords: { $includes: "spawn" } }`; "eliminated spawn do not count" is automatic since eliminated cards leave `cardsInPlay`), capped by deck length; a zero count fizzles. The event card is **removed from the game** (its own discard → `outOfPlayPile`) as part of resolution. When N>0 the top N are revealed (`revealInstances`) and a two-step interaction runs: `desire-belly-choose-card` (actor = card-player, one `desire-choose-shown-card` per revealed card, mandatory) picks the shown card, then `desire-belly-choose-penalty` (actor = opponent, a `desire-choose-penalty` with `remove-from-game` or `reduce-hand-size`, mandatory). `remove-from-game` moves the shown card to the opponent's `outOfPlayPile` and shuffles the *other* revealed cards back on top of the deck; `reduce-hand-size` adds an `until-cleared` player-scoped `hand-size-modifier` of `-1` (permanent, consumed by `resolveHandSize`) and shuffles *all* revealed cards back on top. The rest of the deck below the revealed cards is untouched (no instance lost). Legal actions in `legal-actions/pending.ts` (`desireBellyChooseCardActions`/`desireBellyChoosePenaltyActions`), reducers in `pending-reducers.ts` (`applyDesireBellyChooseCardResolution`/`applyDesireBellyChoosePenaltyResolution`), wired in `pending-handlers.ts`. The "discard a Spawn card from your hand" play cost is a companion `play-discard-cost` (now offered on untargeted short events too, not just character-targeting ones). Used by Desire All for Thy Belly (ba-16).
- `region-movement-limit` — environment effect carried by an in-play hazard permanent-event that reduces the max region distance for region movement game-wide (every player's companies). Each contributing card subtracts `reduce`, or `reduceWithDoorsOfNight` while Doors of Night is in play, summed across cards and never floored below `min`. Collected by `collectRegionMovementReduction`/`applyRegionMovementReduction` (`recompute-derived.ts`) and applied both at movement-plan time (`organization-companies.ts` `planMovementActions`) and at company selection in the Movement/Hazard phase (`reducer-movement-hazard.ts` `handleSelectCompany`, which sets `phaseState.maxRegionDistance` consulted by `declare-path`). The "Discard when any play deck is exhausted" rule reuses `on-event: play-deck-exhausted` + `discard-self` (`completeDeckExhaust`). Used by No Way Forward (dm-75): "The number of region cards that may be played by a moving company using region movement is reduced by one (by two if Doors of Night is in play) to a minimum of two."
- `grant-action: boost-company-influence` — a permanent enchantment played on a sage taps the sage during the active player's site phase to add `+value` to one influence attempt by **another** untapped character in his company, then the sage makes a corruption check. The legal-action generator (`legal-actions/organization.ts`, items loop) emits one activation per eligible company-mate carrying the chosen character on `targetCardId` (the bearer is excluded; tapped mates skipped). The `apply` is a `sequence` of an `add-constraint` (`constraint: "check-modifier"`, `check: "influence"`, `target: "action-target-character"` → `{ kind: "character", characterId: targetCardId }`, `scope: "turn"`) and an `enqueue-corruption-check` on the bearer; the one-shot `+influence` constraint is consumed by the targeted character's next influence attempt (`reducer-site.ts` / `legal-actions/site.ts` / `legal-actions/pending.ts`). The grant-action `add-constraint` path now also builds the payload-carrying `check-modifier` kind (`buildPayloadConstraintKind`) and resolves the `action-target-character` target (`resolveConstraintTarget`) in `reducer-organization.ts`. The play-side "Tap sage and site" reuses `play-flag: tap-character-on-play` + `play-flag: tap-site-on-play`, and "at a site where Information is playable" reuses a `play-target: site` filter on `playableResources`. Used by When You Know More (dm-163).
- `grant-action: discard-boost-influence` — a permanent event attached to a character (typically via the after-attack play window, §32a) may be discarded during the active player's site phase to add `+value` to one influence attempt by **any** untapped character in the bearer's company — including the bearer itself, since the cost is discarding the card rather than tapping the bearer (contrast `boost-company-influence` above, which excludes the bearer because its tap-bearer cost would otherwise leave a nonsensical self-target). The legal-action generator (`legal-actions/organization.ts`, items loop) emits one activation per untapped company member, carrying the chosen target on `targetCardId`. The `apply` is a single `add-constraint` (`constraint: "check-modifier"`, `check: "influence"`, `target: "action-target-character"`, `scope: "turn"`) — no corruption check, unlike `boost-company-influence`. `cost: { discard: "self" }` is the pre-existing generic discard-attachment cost (`cost-evaluator.ts` `applyDiscardSelf`); once the card is stored via `storable-at` instead, it leaves the bearer's `items` and the items-loop scan no longer finds it, naturally closing the ability — matching "if not stored, discard for +3…" without extra gating. Used by Dark Numbers (dm-123).
- `keywords: "stolen-knowledge"` — tag-only card-data keyword (added to the closed `Keyword` union in `types/common.ts`) marking a resource event as belonging to the "Stolen Knowledge" category referenced by other cards' text ("discards a Stolen Knowledge card it controls"); no engine rule beyond filterability, mirroring the existing `ritual`/`light-enchantment`/`dark-enchantment` tag-only keywords. Used by Dark Numbers (dm-123).
- `grant-action: oncePerTurn` + `set-character-status target: "company"` + `phase`-gated `when` — three composable grant-action primitives for once-per-turn, whole-company, phase-restricted abilities. (1) `oncePerTurn: true` on a `grant-action` effect: the reducer (`handleGrantActionApply`) records a turn-scoped `granted-action-used` constraint (keyed by source instance + `action`, targeted at the player) on first use, and every grant-action loop in `grantedActionActivations` (`legal-actions/organization.ts`) skips the ability while that constraint is live (cleared at turn-end). (2) `set-character-status` with `target: "company"` (`grant-action-apply.ts` `runGrantApply`): sets the status on every character in the bearer's company (untap is idempotent for already-untapped members). (3) `buildGrantActionContext` now exposes the current `phase`, so `when: { "phase": "movement-hazard" }` combined with `anyPhase: true` restricts an otherwise any-phase ability to exactly the M/H phase (the org-phase default scan suppresses it because the `when` fails there). Used by Strangling Coils (ba-76): a Demon fána on The Balrog (`play-target target.name: "The Balrog"`, `demon-fana` keyword, `+3 direct influence` / `-1 body`, `grant-skill diplomat`, `grants-followers`, the ba-62 fána-bounce + `return-self-to-hand` shape) whose "Once during his movement/hazard phase, you may untap all tapped characters in The Balrog's company; if then untapped, tap The Balrog" is a `oncePerTurn` `anyPhase` grant-action gated on `phase: "movement-hazard"` with a `sequence` apply of `set-character-status target: "company"` (untapped) then `target: "bearer"` (tapped); the empty `cost` lets it fire even while the Balrog is tapped.
- `play-condition` on factions — the faction influence-attempt generator in `legal-actions/site.ts` honours two `play-condition` gates (in addition to the existing `playableAt` site match, uniqueness, and `playable-at-tapped-site` flag): `requires: 'card-in-play'` (the named `cardName` must appear in the **controller's own** in-play names via `buildControllerInPlayNames`, so an opponent's copy does not satisfy "if you have … in play") and `requires: 'site-protected'` (the company's current site must carry an active `site-protected` constraint owned by the controller — `siteIsProtectedByPlayer`, the mirror of `siteIsProtectedAgainstPlayer`). Used by Half-orcs (wh-87): "Playable at one of your protected Wizardhavens [{H}] (if tapped or untapped) if you have A Strident Spawn in play and if the influence check is greater than 9." A card may repeat the `card-in-play` gate — the generator checks EVERY such condition (`findPlayConditionEffects`, the plural sibling of `findPlayConditionEffect` in `reducer-utils.ts`), so all named cards must be in the controller's play area. Used by Greater Half-orcs (wh-86): "Playable at one of your protected Wizardhavens [{H}] (if tapped or untapped) if you have A Strident Spawn and Half-orcs in play and if the influence check is greater than 11."
