Cannot be duplicated." — the first card carrying **two independent** `region-type-remap` effect entries on one definition, each with its own `when` gate (`{ inPlay: "Gates of Morning" }` and `{ inPlay: "Doors of Night" }` respectively); `collectRegionTypeRemaps` already iterates every `region-type-remap` effect on a card and evaluates each `when` independently, so no engine change was needed — the two remaps activate/deactivate on their own gating card regardless of the other's state. Plus `duplication-limit` scope game.
- `region-type-conversion` + `play-condition: supporters-in-region` — a persistent Fallen-wizard **stage** resource that converts a set of *named* regions to Wilderness for creature keying, gated on a bespoke supporter count. **(1) `region-type-conversion`** — a top-level effect `{ to: RegionType, includeAdjacent?: boolean }` read live from either player's `cardsInPlay` on a card bound to a site (`attachedToSite`). `collectRegionTypeConversions(state)` (`engine/region-keying.ts`) resolves each into the set of region **names** it converts — the bound site's own `region` plus, when `includeAdjacent`, that region card's `adjacentRegions` — and the target type; `applyRegionTypeConversions(pathTypes, pathNames, conversions)` replaces every traversed region **by name** (index-parallel with the name path, last conversion wins). Unlike `region-type-remap` (whole *type classes*), this converts specific named regions. Wired into both keying matchers — `checkCreatureKeying` (`mh-hazard-play.ts`, after the `region-type-remap` step) and `findCreatureKeyingMatches` (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`, on the name-parallel base path before the additive constraint overrides) — so the offering and validation sides agree. The card is a permanent stage MP contributor, so it is exempt from the site-attached orphan sweep (`cardKeepsBoundSitePermanent` gains `region-type-conversion`) and persists when the company leaves the anchor Wizardhaven (a haven, never discarded regardless). **(2) `play-condition: supporters-in-region`** (`{ min: number }`) — in the org-phase site-target branch (`legal-actions/organization-events.ts`), the combined count of the player's allies in play (every `CharacterInPlay.allies` entry) plus their **unique** factions in play playable at a site in the anchor Wizardhaven's region or an adjacent region (`buildFactionPlayableRegions` ∩ the region+`adjacentRegions` set) must reach `min`; the parenthetical region restriction applies only to factions. Used by Girdle of Radagast (wh-110): "Playable on one of your protected Wizardhavens [{H}] if you are Radagast and have at least 12 SPs and 6 allies and/or unique factions in play (the factions must be playable at sites in the Wizardhaven's region or adjacent regions). The Wizardhaven's region and all adjacent regions become Wilderness [{w}]. Cannot be duplicated." — `stage-points` 3 + `play-target` site (`effectiveSiteType: haven`) + `play-condition player-state` (`player.avatar: "Radagast"`, `player.stagePoints ≥ 12`; the `radagast-specific` keyword also gates via `wizardSpecificName`) + `play-condition site-protected` + `play-condition supporters-in-region` (`min: 6`) + `region-type-conversion` (`to: wilderness`, `includeAdjacent: true`) + `duplication-limit` scope game. CRF 22: "Does not affect the regions in starter movement, except for the starting and ending regions" — the per-region-name model naturally covers this (only regions actually in a company's resolved path are converted).
- `create-site-auto-attack` + `extra-automatic-attack` constraint, and `modify-attack` `removeDetainment` — the two modes of a dual-mode hazard short-event that manipulates site automatic-attacks. **Mode A (`create-site-auto-attack`)**: played in the M/H phase on a company **moving to** a site of one of `siteTypes` (gate in `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`, mirroring `only-at-site-with-auto-attack`), it installs — on chain resolution (`chain-reducer.ts`) — a turn-scoped `extra-automatic-attack` active constraint keyed to the destination site *instance* (which becomes the company's `currentSite` on arrival). `manifestations.ts` `getActiveAutoAttacks` appends the constraint's `AutomaticAttack` when the queried site instance matches, so the company faces **one additional real automatic-attack** in the site phase (counted for the enter/skip decision and resolved through the normal `reducer-site.ts` flow — unlike Tidings' `tidings-attack`, which is not an automatic-attack). The injected attack carries `creatureType: ""` ("no attack type") and a new `AutomaticAttack.forceDetainment` flag; `handleSiteAutomaticAttacks` (and the two duplicate-attack paths + `buildSiteRepeatedAttackCombat`) force detainment for it, still overridden by the defender's `detainment-attacks-normal` (Alatar wh-1). **Mode B (`modify-attack` `removeDetainment`)**: an attacker-played from-hand `modify-attack` sets `CombatState.detainment` to `false` (`combat-actions.ts` `handleModifyAttack`) so the attack wounds normally; the from-hand `when` context (`legal-actions/combat.ts`) now also exposes `attack.detainment` (live detainment status) and `defender.minionCompany` (`true` when the defending player is Ringwraith). Used by FEAR! FIRE! FOES! (as-29): Mode A `create-site-auto-attack` (`["free-hold","border-hold"]`, 5 strikes / 8 prowess / detainment) + Mode B `modify-attack` (fromHand attacker, `prowessModifier: -1`, `removeDetainment`, gated on `attack.automatic` + `attack.detainment` + `defender.minionCompany`).

- `auto-attack-boost` effect + `auto-attack-boost` constraint — a hazard short-event that boosts **one** of a site's automatic-attacks (the attacker's choice = the first the company faces) and can make it uncancelable, for the turn. Gate mirrors `create-site-auto-attack`: played in M/H on a company **moving to** a site of one of `siteTypes` (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`). On chain resolution (`chain-reducer.ts`) it installs a single-use `auto-attack-boost` active constraint against the moving company, scope `company-site-phase`, carrying `prowessBonus`, `uncancelable`, and the destination `siteDefinitionId`. The site auto-attack initiation (`reducer-site.ts`, right after the Choking Shadows `auto-attack.prowess` consumption) finds the first matching constraint on the company, adds `prowessBonus` to the attack's effective prowess, sets `CombatState.uncancelable` when flagged, then removes the constraint — so exactly one attack is affected (the same "one automatic-attack = the first faced" modelling as Choking Shadows tw-21). "Cannot be duplicated on a given site" reuses `duplication-limit` scope `site`, whose M/H counter (`movement-hazard.ts`) now also tallies resolved `auto-attack-boost` constraints bound to the destination site (alongside Greed's `item-play-corruption-check`). Used by Arouse Defenders (le-101): `auto-attack-boost` (`["free-hold","border-hold"]`, `prowessBonus: 2`, `uncancelable: true`) + `duplication-limit` scope site max 1. Arouse Denizens (tw-6, same text as le-102) reuses the same primitive unmodified: `auto-attack-boost` (`["ruins-and-lairs"]`, `prowessBonus: 3`, `uncancelable: false`) + `duplication-limit` scope site max 1 — confirming `siteTypes`/`uncancelable` are genuinely generic and need no card-specific branching. Also used by Arouse Minions (tw-7), its minion-side counterpart: `auto-attack-boost` (`["shadow-hold","dark-hold"]`, `prowessBonus: 3`, `uncancelable: false`) + `duplication-limit` scope site max 1.

- `auto-attack-prowess-boost` add-constraint (site-scoped, persistent) + `set-site-phase-flag firstItemNoTapAvailable` — a **resource short-event** played at a Border-hold that weakens every automatic-attack the company faces there and lets one item be played free. Playability: a `play-window` (`phase: "site"`, `siteTypes: ["border-hold"]`) restricts it to the site phase while the active company is at a Border-hold (enforced by `playResourceShortEventActions` at the enter-or-skip window and `heroResourceShortEventActions` elsewhere in the site phase). **(1) auto-attack prowess** — `applyShortEventOnEntersPlay` (`reducer-events.ts`) gained a dedicated `auto-attack-prowess-boost` branch (the generic add-constraint switch resolves a company only from a target character, which this no-target card lacks): it resolves the active site-phase company from `phaseState.activeCompanyIndex`, computes the value (doubled when `doublesWithDoorsOfNight` and `buildInPlayNames` includes `"Doors of Night"` — baked at play time, since the card is played immediately before the auto-attacks resolve), and adds an `attribute-modifier` constraint (`attribute: "auto-attack.prowess"`, `op: "add"`, `filter: { "site.type": <type> }`, `persistent: true`) targeting the company, scope `company-site-phase`. The site auto-attack initiation (`reducer-site.ts`) reads it through `resolveEffective`; a new `persistent` flag on the `attribute-modifier` constraint kind makes `resolveEffective` (`effective.ts`) **not** report it as consumed, so — unlike the single-use Choking Shadows / Arouse Defenders boosts — it keeps weakening every automatic-attack the company faces at the site until its scope sweeps it. **(2) first item free** — a second `set-site-phase-flag` sets the new `SitePhaseState.firstItemNoTapAvailable`; the item-play path (`reducer-site.ts` `handleSitePlayResources`) treats the first item played (any subtype) like Thorough Search — leaves the site untapped, consumes the flag, and does not count as the site-tapping resource (the item still taps its *bearer* normally). Used by Come By Night Upon Them (le-176): "Playable on a Border-hold [{B}]. -1 to the prowess of all automatic-attacks at the site (-2 if Doors of Night is in play). The first item played at the site does not tap the site." — `play-window` (site / border-hold) + `on-event self-enters-play → add-constraint auto-attack-prowess-boost` (`value: -1`, `siteType: "border-hold"`, `doublesWithDoorsOfNight: true`, scope `company-site-phase`) + `on-event self-enters-play → set-site-phase-flag firstItemNoTapAvailable`.

- `site-rule: first-minor-item-no-tap` (+ `hoard` keyword) — the passive, per-site counterpart to the event-driven `firstItemNoTapAvailable` flag (Come By Night Upon Them, le-176), restricted to **minor** items: "The first minor item played at this site each turn does not tap the site." A new `SitePhaseState.firstMinorItemNoTapAvailable` flag is seeded at the `select-company` step (`reducer-site.ts` `handleSiteSelectCompany` via `siteFirstMinorItemNoTap`) whenever the company's current site carries the rule, so the allowance refreshes each turn/site-phase. The item-play path (`handleSitePlayHeroResource`) consumes it only for a `minor`-subtype item (`usingFirstMinorItemNoTap`): like Thorough Search / `firstItemNoTapAvailable`, it leaves the site untapped, does **not** count as the site-tapping resource, and does not fire the opening minor-item bonus (the item still taps its *bearer*); a subsequent item taps the site normally. No legal-actions change — because the free item leaves the site untapped, further minor items stay legal through the ordinary untapped path. "Contains a hoard" is the existing `keywords: ["hoard"]` (enables hoard items via each hoard item's `item-play-site` `{ "site.keywords": { "$includes": "hoard" } }` restriction, plus Bounty of the Hoard). Used by Framsburg (as-146): "The first minor item played at this site each turn does not tap the site. Contains a hoard." — rest is structural (ruins-and-lairs, minor-item playability, Men 1×10 auto-attack, {d}{s} path from Dol Guldur, 1/2 draws).

- `set-site-phase-flag hoardKeywordGranted` — a **resource short-event** that makes the active company's current site "considered to contain a hoard until the end of the turn," widening which sites count as a hoard site without touching the site's own printed `keywords`. Playability: `play-window` (`phase: "site"`, `siteTypes: ["shadow-hold", "dark-hold"]`) + `play-target` `target: "character"` (`filter`: race + untapped + `company.siteType` in the same site types, `cost: { tap: "character" }`), following the Lucky Search (tw-269) pattern. On resolution, `on-event self-enters-play → set-site-phase-flag hoardKeywordGranted` sets the new `SitePhaseState.hoardKeywordGranted` (generic handler, `reducer-events.ts`). Three consumers widen their `hoard`-keyword read to honor it while the flag is set: `buildActiveCompanyContext` (`legal-actions/organization.ts`, backing every `play-condition requires: "active-company"` check, e.g. Bounty of the Hoard td-101's `{ "site.keywords": { "$includes": "hoard" } }`) and, in `legal-actions/site.ts`, both the `itemOwnSiteRestrictionMatches` filter context (a hoard item's own `item-play-site` restriction) and the `hoardBountyBonus` `siteIsHoard` check — each synthesizes an extra `hoard` entry onto the site's keyword list, mirroring the existing Deep Mines `under-deeps` synthesis already in `itemOwnSiteRestrictionMatches`. Flag is absent (→ false) by default and resets when a new company's site phase begins, which is what bounds "until the end of the turn" in practice (a company's site phase happens once per turn). Used by Dwarven Hoard (td-109): "Tap a Dwarf at a Dark-hold [{D}] or Shadow-hold [{S}]. The site is considered to contain a hoard until the end of the turn."

- `press-gang-capture` + `character-pressed` constraint — a hazard permanent-event that intercepts the *discard* of an opponent's character and holds it "off to the side". While an in-play `press-gang-capture` card (Press-gang, ba-22) belongs to a character owner's opponent, `findCapturingPressGang` / `capturePressGang` (`engine/press-gang.ts`) redirect every "discard from play" seam — `discardCharacter` (dice-check discards, `pending-reducers.ts`), the corruption-check `discard` outcome, the rule-3.22 voluntary organization discard (`reducer-organization.ts`), the combat body-check discard band (`combat-actions.ts` `discardCharacterAfterBodyCheck`), and the Abductor `discardWoundedCharacters` effect (`combat-finalize.ts`) — replacing the discard with a capture. *Elimination* seams (out-of-play: `eliminateCharacter`, combat death `> body`, corruption hard-fail) are deliberately **not** intercepted ("discarded", not "eliminated"). On capture the character's items/allies go to its owner's discard and hazards to their owners' discards, its followers revert to general influence (CRF: not discarded), and the bare card stays in its owner's `characters` map (in no company, pruned from any leader's follower list) marked with a new `character-pressed` active constraint. That constraint is scored exactly like the prisoner rule (CoE 8.35): the recompute-derived negative-MP / 0-GI block and the `reducer-untap.ts` no-untap set were both generalized from `character-is-prisoner` to also match `character-pressed`, so a pressed character is worth **negative** character MP to its owner and never untaps/heals — with no rescue site or HazardHost record. The card holds at most one character (a new capture returns the prior to its owner's hand), and `sweepPressGang` (a `postReduce` sweep beside `sweepSetAside`) returns the held character to its owner's hand when the card leaves play. Used by Press-gang (ba-22): "When a character would otherwise be discarded from play, discard all cards on him, place him 'off to the side' with this card, and return any character already with this card to its owner's hand. A character with this card gives his player negative character marshalling points. Cannot be duplicated." — `press-gang-capture` + `duplication-limit` scope game max 1.

- `eliminate-instead-of-discard` — an in-play card that turns the **next** matching "discard from play" of a character into an **elimination**: the character card lands in its owner's out-of-play pile instead of the discard pile, so it can never be recycled. `findEliminateInsteadOfDiscardHost` / `consumeEliminateInsteadOfDiscardHost` (`engine/eliminate-instead-of-discard.ts`) are wired into the same five discard seams Press-gang covers — `discardCharacter` (dice-check discards, `pending-reducers.ts`), the corruption-check `discard` outcome, the rule-3.22 voluntary organization discard (`reducer-organization.ts`), the combat body-check discard band (`combat-actions.ts` `discardCharacterAfterBodyCheck`), and the Abductor `discardWoundedCharacters` effect (`combat-finalize.ts`). Only the character card's destination changes: possessions still go to the discard piles and followers still revert to general influence (CoE 7.1), and no kill MPs change hands. `filter` is a plain card-definition condition (`matchesDefinition`), so "which characters" stays data-driven; `discardSelf: true` discards the host the moment the replacement fires, which is what makes it one-shot. `press-gang-capture` is checked first and wins (a captured character is never discarded at all). Used by Pallando the Soul-keeper (as-17): "As a permanent-event, the next non-Ringwraith minion discarded from play is instead eliminated. Discard when a minion is so eliminated." — `{ "cardType": "minion-character", "race": { "$ne": "ringwraith" } }` + `discardSelf`.

- `tap-character` on a plain short hazard-event + `on-event company-arrives-at-site` override modes — a dual-mode ("Alternatively") short hazard-event that either taps one filter-matching character or, with Doors of Night in play, reinterprets a region/site type for the turn. `tap-character` (previously only an on-tap effect of `creature-alt-event` cards, Adûnaphel tw-2) now also resolves as a **top-level effect on a plain short hazard-event**: the short-event branch of `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts` emits one `play-hazard` action per eligible untapped, filter-matching character in the resource player's companies (`targetCharacterId`), and `applyTapCharacter` (`chain-reducer.ts`) taps it on resolution. `filter` is matched via `matchesDefinition` against the raw character def (`{ "race": "elf" }`). When the same card also carries `on-event company-arrives-at-site` override modes (the Choking Shadows tw-21 pattern: `site-type-override` / `region-type-override` gated on `environment.doorsOfNightInPlay` + `company.destinationSiteType` / `company.destinationRegionType`), the legal-action generator additionally offers a **single untargeted** Mode-B action whenever the active company is moving and an override mode's `when` matches the arrival context. The two modes are mutually exclusive: `applyShortEventArrivalTrigger` skips all arrival-override modes when the resolving short-event carries both a `tap-character` effect and a `targetCharacterId` (tap mode chosen). Used by New Moon (tw-68): "Tap one Elf character. Alternatively, if Doors of Night is in play, treat one Free-domain [{f}] as a Border-land [{b}] or one Free-hold [{F}] as a Border-hold [{B}] until the end of the turn. Cannot be duplicated." — `duplication-limit` scope turn max 1 + `tap-character` (`filter { race: elf }`) + `on-event company-arrives-at-site` → `site-type-override` (free-hold→border-hold) + `on-event company-arrives-at-site` → `region-type-override` (free→border, destination).

- `play-condition requires: card-in-play` gating a plain `tap-character` short hazard-event (Darkness Under Tree le-108) — closes the one hazard-event shape that didn't yet honour the `card-in-play` gate: the M/H long/permanent-event branch (Snowstorm tw-91) and resource events already checked it, but the short-event branch of `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts` had no check at all, so a plain short-event's `tap-character` targets were offered regardless of any named-card requirement. Added one `findPlayConditionEffect(def, 'card-in-play')` / `isCardNameInPlayOrCharacters` check at the top of the short-event branch (before duplication-limit), reusing the exact helper the long-event branch already calls — no new effect type. `filter` on the `tap-character` effect uses `{ "race": { "$in": [...] } }` to match multiple races in one condition (matched via `matchesDefinition`/`matchesCondition`, which already supports `$in`). Used by Darkness Under Tree (le-108): "Playable on an untapped Orc, Troll, or Man character if Doors of Night is in play. Tap the character." — `play-condition` `card-in-play` "Doors of Night" + `tap-character` (`filter { race: { $in: [orc, troll, man] } }`).

- `enemy-modifier` (`stat: prowess | body`, `op: halve-round-up | subtract`) — collected from a character (and its active items/attached-hazards) during combat and applied against the enemy the character is fighting, via `resolveEnemyBody` / the prowess path in `resolver.ts`. For **body** it lowers the enemy's body-check target: against a hazard **creature** (`combat-actions.ts` creature body-check band) and against a **character** in CvCC (`combat-actions.ts` `bodyCheckTarget === 'character'`, gated on `combat.isCvCC && strike.attackingCharacterId` — the *attacking* character's effects modify the *defending* character's body). The `subtract` op clamps at 0; `halve-round-up` uses `Math.ceil(body/2)`. Each effect's `when` is matched against a combat context exposing `bearer.*` (the fighting character) and `enemy.*` (`race`, `name`, `prowess`, `body`), so the modifier can be skill-gated (`bearer.skills` includes warrior) or enemy-race-gated (`enemy.race`). Used by Ancient Black Axe (as-122): "Warrior only: -1 to strike's body" (skill-gated, item-borne); and by Khamûl the Ringwraith (le-55): "-2 to the body of any Elf character targeted by a strike from Khamûl" — an intrinsic character `enemy-modifier` (`op: subtract`, `value: 2`) gated `when: { "enemy.race": "elf" }`, reducing an Elf defender's CvCC body-check target by 2. le-55 also reuses `stat-modifier` per-mode (`bearer.ringwraithMode`) and `ringwraith-follower-slots` (`count: 1`, the single-slot sibling of The Witch-king le-58's two).

- `magic-discard-to-deck` (passive Ringwraith-avatar flag, Akhôrahil le-51) — while an avatar carrying this flag is a player's revealed Ringwraith (`findPlayerAvatar`, controlled by general influence), any *magic card* that player casts is shuffled back into their play deck (deck reshuffled) when it would otherwise be discarded, instead of going to the discard pile. A "magic card" is any card whose `keywords` include `spell` / `sorcery` / `spirit-magic` / `shadow-magic` (`isMagicCard`, `reducer-utils.ts`). The redirect is a single helper, `discardOrRecyclePlayedEvent(state, playerIndex, card)` (`reducer-utils.ts`): non-magic cards, or magic cards cast by a player without the flag, fall through to a normal discard; otherwise the card lands in the play deck via the shared `shuffle(playDeck ++ card, rng)`. It is wired at every seam a just-played magic event lands in the *caster's* discard pile — the resource short-event fall-through in `handlePlayResourceShortEvent` (`reducer-events.ts`, covers on-character magic events like Words of Menace le-258 / Well-preserved as-108), the cancel-attack discard in `combat-cancel.ts` (The Tormented Earth as-102), and the cancel-influence discard in `pending-reducers.ts` (Poisonous Despair le-219). No card instance is lost — it moves to exactly one of playDeck / discardPile. The avatar's per-mode stat changes reuse the existing `stat-modifier` `when: { "bearer.ringwraithMode": … }` pattern shared by every certified Ringwraith avatar. Used by Akhôrahil the Ringwraith (le-51): "+3 direct influence in Heralded Lord mode. +1 prowess in Fell Rider mode. As your Ringwraith, when a magic card used by him has to be discarded, return it to the play deck and reshuffle."
- `hand-discard-recycle-option` (bare in-play permanent-/long-event, Enduring Tales dm-125) — game-wide: while a copy sits in **either** player's `cardsInPlay`, whenever *any* player discards a card from their hand, that player may move it to the top of their own play deck (face down) instead of leaving it in their discard pile. Like `on-event: card-discarded-from-hand` (below), there is no single reducer call site for "a card left the hand and reached the discard pile" (end-of-turn hand-size reduction, forced discards, cost payments, …), so it is detected reactively as a prev/next diff after every reducer step — `hand-discard-recycle-trigger.ts` (`applyHandDiscardRecycleOffers`, hooked into `postReduce` right after `hand-discard-trigger.ts`'s corruption check): a card counts as "discarded from hand" when its instance left `hand` and landed in `discardPile` within the same step, distinguishing a genuine discard from a card merely being played (which leaves the hand for `cardsInPlay`/a character/etc.). Unlike `card-discarded-from-hand`, both players' hands are scanned every step — the card text says "any player", not "his own controller" — and the marker need not belong to the discarding player (`handDiscardRecycleOptionInPlay`, `reducer-utils.ts`, mirrors `influenceModificationsNullified`'s either-player bare-card scan). The trigger enqueues a `hand-discard-recycle-offer` pending resolution (the card has *already* landed in the discard pile by the time the offer is made); the discarding player accepts with `recycle-hand-discard` (moves that exact instance from `discardPile` to the top of `playDeck`, via `forgetDeckReveals` to scrub any prior public identity — no `faceDown` flag needed, since play-deck membership already hides a card's identity) or declines with `pass`. Resolved by `applyHandDiscardRecycleOfferResolution` (`pending-reducers.ts`); legal actions by `handDiscardRecycleOfferActions` (`legal-actions/pending.ts`). Used by Enduring Tales (dm-125): "When any player discards a card from his hand, he may discard it to the top of his play deck (and always face down) instead of to his discard pile."

- Radagast Shapeshifter form — `stat-modifier` `op: "set"` + `override-skills` + `play-flag: bearer-cannot-move` / `bearer-cannot-use-items` + `lasting` company check-modifier + `next-organization-phase` scope + `return-to-hand` `replaced-by-keyword`. A cluster of primitives for the Shapeshifter family (wh-112/115/116), permanent-events placed on Radagast (`play-target` character `{ target.name: "Radagast" }` — such cards live in the bearer's `items`).
  - **`stat-modifier` `op: "set"`** — an absolute override of the printed base, applied **before** all `add`/`multiply` modifiers in `resolveStatModifiers` (`effects/resolver.ts`) so ordinary bonuses stack on the adopted value; last `set` wins. This is what "adopting the given attributes" means: the reading is forced by the data, since Winged Change-master (wh-116) prints prowess 3 against Radagast's printed 6 and Shifter of Hues prints direct influence 3 against his printed 5 — a delta cannot lower a stat from a positive attribute line. For the player-level `general-influence` stat the override is recorded separately as `PlayerState.generalInfluenceOverride` (`recompute-derived.ts` `applyGeneralInfluenceEffect`) and substituted for the Fallen-wizard avatar's printed white-hand number (or the base 20) by `effectiveGeneralInfluence` (`reducer-utils.ts`), with `generalInfluenceBonus` still added on top. The printed `corruption -N` attribute is ordinary corruption **points** (`stat-modifier` `corruption-points`), matching every other card whose attribute box carries `corruption`; the Shapeshifter forms are just the only ones printing a negative.
  - **`override-skills`** — **replaces** the bearer's printed skills, the counterpart to the additive `grant-skill`. Both compose in the new shared `getEffectiveSkills(state, char, charDef)` (`effects/resolver.ts`), which replaced the `[...charDef.skills, ...getItemGrantedSkills(state, char)]` spread at every engine consumer (organization/site/pending/mh legal-actions and reducers). `grant-skill` still stacks on top of an override — the override speaks for the character card, not for other cards' contributions.
  - **`play-flag: "bearer-cannot-move"`** — character-level immobility, the first of its kind (every prior movement gate is company-scoped). A company is stationary while it contains such a character: `companyHasImmobileCharacter` (`reducer-utils.ts`) gates both movement-planning sites — the org-phase emitter (`legal-actions/organization-companies.ts`) and `handlePlanMovement` (`reducer-organization.ts`) — alongside the `company-cannot-move` constraint. Splitting the others off is the intended escape hatch.
  - **`play-flag: "bearer-cannot-use-items"`** — the card-driven form of the Ringwraith/Balrog avatar item bans in `computeEffectiveStats` (`recompute-derived.ts`): every effect sourced from an `isItemCard` on the bearer is dropped and structural `prowessModifier`/`bodyModifier` skipped, while borne items' corruption points **still apply** (the "may bear, but may not use" wording of MEWH §9 / rule 9.20, unlike the Balrog ban which also suppresses CP). The flag is read *before* the filter; its own source is a permanent-event, so it never filters itself away.
  - **`lasting` check-modifier + `next-organization-phase` scope** — a company-targeted corruption `check-modifier` constraint carrying `lasting: true` is applied but **never consumed** by the check it modifies (`pending-reducers.ts`), standing until its scope sweeps it. The new `ConstraintScope` `{ kind: 'next-organization-phase', playerId, afterTurn }` stamps `state.turnNumber` at creation; the new `ScopeBoundary` `{ kind: 'organization-phase-end', playerId, turnNumber }` — raised by `handleOrganizationPass` (`reducer-organization.ts`), the first phase-end boundary the engine actually invokes — drops it only once it sees a strictly greater turn number, so the organization phase it was created in does not expire it but the player's next one does.
  - **`targets.movingThroughRegionType`** — narrows a `targets.scope: "player-companies"` enumeration to companies that have declared movement whose destination's printed site path crosses that region type (`grantActionTargetCompanies`, `legal-actions/organization.ts`). The same helper now serves the item-borne grant loop, which gained per-company enumeration (`targetCompanyId`) — previously only character-definition grants (le-56) could enumerate companies, so an attached permanent-event's grant emitted a single untargeted activation.
  - **`return-to-hand` `replaced-by-keyword`** — with `replacedByKeyword: "<kw>"`, an attached card returns to hand when a later card carrying that keyword is placed on the same character. Swept in `postReduce` by `sweepKeywordReplaced` (`engine/keyword-replaced.ts`): array order in `char.items` is arrival order, so the last carrier stays and earlier ones go to **hand**, not the discard pile. Idempotent. The `organization` trigger's emitter (`returnAttachedToHandActions`) and reducer (`handleReturnAttachedToHand`) were extended from `allies` to also cover `items`, where character-attached permanent-events live.
  - Used by Shifter of Hues (wh-115): "Radagast specific. Shapeshifter. Place this card on Radagast if he is in play. Return this card to your hand: when you play another Shapeshifter card or, if you choose, during your organization phase. In addition to adopting the given attributes, Radagast's skills become Warrior/Diplomat. Radagast may not move. You may keep one more card than normal in your hand. Radagast can tap give +2 to the corruption checks of the characters in one company through your next organization phase (this company must be moving with at least one Wilderness [{w}] in their site path). Radagast may bear, but may not use, items."

- `targets.movingThroughRegionNames` (+ bearer-less in-play-faction grant-action generalization + `bearer.homesiteRegions`) — three additions letting a tapped in-play faction grant a per-company movement bonus keyed to named regions.
  - **`targets.movingThroughRegionNames`** — sibling of `movingThroughRegionType` on `GrantActionTargets`: narrows a `targets.scope: "player-companies"` enumeration to companies that have declared movement whose destination site's own named `region` (not its abstract sitePath terrain types) is in the given list (`grantActionTargetCompanies`, `legal-actions/organization.ts`).
  - **Bearer-less in-play-faction grant-action, generalized** — `bareCardGrantActions` (`legal-actions/organization.ts`) previously emitted only a discard-self / `add-constraint` shape (A Panoply of Wings wh-37). It now also accepts `cost.tap: "self"` and, when the effect carries `targets.scope: "player-companies"`, enumerates one activation per eligible target company (carrying `targetCompanyId`), mirroring the item-borne grant loop. `handleInPlayCardGrantAction` (`grant-action-apply.ts`) gained a matching `apply.type === "increment-company-extra-region-distance"` branch that resolves the target company from `action.targetCompanyId` — there is no bearer character to derive it from, unlike Cram (td-105)'s bearer-borne version of the same apply.
  - **`bearer.homesiteRegions`** — a new `ResolverContext['bearer']` field, populated only in the `faction-influence-check` context (`reducer-site.ts`, `legal-actions/pending.ts`, `legal-actions/site.ts`), listing the named regions of the influencing character's home site(s) via the new `characterHomeSiteRegions` (`reducer-utils.ts`, the region-name sibling of `characterHomeSiteTypes`). Lets a faction's own `check-modifier` `when` read `bearer.race` + `bearer.homesiteRegions` for a "Men with home sites in <region list> (+N)" standard modification.
  - Used by Wild Horses (wh-39): "Playable at any tapped or untapped non-Haven site in Rohan, Khand, Dorwinion, Horse Plains, Southern Rhovanion, or Harondor if the influence check is greater than 11. Standard Modifications: Men with home sites in the regions listed above (+3). Tap this faction to allow any company with one of the regions listed above in its site path to move up to 1 additional region."

- `hand-size-modifier` — adjusts how many cards the player may keep at end of turn, resolved by `resolveHandSize` (`effects/resolver.ts`) on top of the `HAND_SIZE` constant. Collected both from cards on the player's characters (via `collectCharacterEffects`, with a `self.location` / `self.atDarkhaven` context so a `when` can gate on where the company stands) and from player-targeted `hand-size-modifier` **active constraints**. Consumed by the end-of-turn discard/refill reducers and legal actions. Note the effect's `value` must be a plain number — a MathJS expression contributes 0. Used by Book of Mazarbul (tw-201, as a turn-scoped constraint from a sage's tap) and Shifter of Hues (wh-115: "You may keep one more card than normal in your hand", a flat `value: 1` while the form is on Radagast).

- `avatar-home-site-restriction` (+ `on-event: avatar-enters-play` + `player.avatarInPlay` player-state) — three pieces that let a Wizard permanent-event care about whether the avatar is "revealed" (in play) and constrain its reveal. **(1)** `player.avatarInPlay` is a new `buildPlayerStateContext` field (`legal-actions/organization.ts`) = `findPlayerAvatar(state, player) !== undefined`; distinct from `player.avatar` (the declared *Fallen-wizard* identity, which persists in the deck/hand), it is the true in-play signal, so `{ "player.avatarInPlay": false }` is a `player-state` play-condition meaning "your Wizard is not revealed". **(2)** `avatar-home-site-restriction` is a marker effect: while the carrying card sits in the controller's `cardsInPlay`, `playerHasAvatarHomeSiteRestriction` (`legal-actions/organization-characters.ts`) is true, and `playCharacterActions` passes a new `avatarHomeSiteOnly` flag to `findPlayableSites`, which OR-s it into `homeSiteOnly` for the acting player's avatar — suppressing the extra-haven reveal (a Wizard's Rivendell, a Ringwraith's Minas Morgul / Dol Guldur; `avatarExtraHavenNames`, rule 2.II.2.1.W1/R1) so the avatar can only be brought into play at its home site. Non-avatar play is untouched. **(3)** `on-event: avatar-enters-play` fires the moment the controller brings *their own* avatar (mind === null) into play — distinct from `self-enters-play` (which fires for the entering card itself). `handlePlayCharacter` (`reducer-organization.ts`, `applyAvatarEntersPlayEffects`) scans the acting player's `cardsInPlay` for this on-event and runs its `move` apply (self → discard), reusing the generic `applyMove` primitive. Used by Saw Further and Deeper (dm-156): "Playable only if your Wizard is not revealed. Your general influence is increased by 5. Your Wizard may only be brought into play at his home site. Discard when you bring your Wizard into play. Cannot be duplicated by a given player." — the `general-influence` `stat-modifier` +5 (collected from a bare permanent-event in `cardsInPlay`, so the bonus applies exactly while the Wizard is unrevealed) and `duplication-limit` scope `player` are shipped primitives.

- `agent-home-site-faction-lock` (+ `target.homeSiteTypes` play-target context) — a minion permanent-event kept attached to an **agent character** (a minion character carrying the `agent` keyword, played as a normal company character per rule 2.II.2.2.5 — *not* an `AgentInPlay` roaming hazard; the card lands in `char.items` like any character-attached resource permanent-event) whose ongoing effect switches on only while the bearer is **unwounded and standing at one of his home sites** of a type in `homeSiteTypes`. When active it (1) bars **every** faction play at any version of that site — matched by the site's printed *name*, so all in-play copies of the same site card are covered — via a new branch in the site-phase faction legal-action gate (`legal-actions/site.ts`, `siteFactionLockedByAgentHomeSite`, beside the ba-65 `site-instance-transform` `noFactions` branch), and (2) credits the carrying card's printed marshalling points to its controller — the MP is **conditional**, so the card's own printed MP is suppressed in the normal item-MP tally (`recompute-derived.ts`) and added back only while the lock holds (no `conditional-mp` effect needed; the printed `marshallingPoints` stays on the card and is gated by the same predicate `agentHomeSiteFactionLockState` in `reducer-utils.ts`). Wounding the bearer or moving it off its home site switches both off dynamically; the card is discarded only when the bearer leaves play. The play restriction is a `play-target` `character` filter — `target.keywords` `$includes` `"agent"` AND a `$or` over the new `target.homeSiteTypes` play-target context field (`characterHomeSiteTypes`, the printed site types of the character's home sites, exposed in `legal-actions/organization-events.ts`) `$includes` `"border-hold"` / `"free-hold"` — plus a `play-condition` `site-type: ["haven"]` ("at a Darkhaven"; a minion company at a haven). Rounded out by `duplication-limit` scope `character`. Used by Faithless Steward (as-83): "Playable on an agent character at a Darkhaven [{DH}] who has a Border-hold [{B}] or Free-hold [{F}] as a home site. If target character is unwounded and at one of his Border-hold [{B}] or Free-hold [{F}] home sites, no factions can be played at any version of that site and you receive this card's marshalling points. Cannot be duplicated on a given character."
- `play-as-sauron` + `discard-named-in-play` + Sauron's dual-mode org ability — the machinery behind The Lidless Eye (le-203), a bare general permanent-event that makes its controller count as *Sauron*. **(1)** `play-as-sauron` is a marker effect on the card in `cardsInPlay`, detected by `playerPlaysAsSauron` (`reducer-utils.ts`, by effect type not card id). While it holds, two legal-action hooks in `organization-characters.ts` fire: `playCharacterActions` offers any Ringwraith-race avatar reveal as **non-viable** ("you are Sauron — you may not reveal a Ringwraith"), and `ringwraithFollowerPlayAction` blocks every Ringwraith-follower play ("you may not play Ringwraith followers"). **(2)** `discard-named-in-play` is an `on-event: self-enters-play` apply (`chain-reducer.ts` `discardNamedCardsInPlay`) that, when the card enters play, discards every in-play instance of a named card — scanning both players' `cardsInPlay` **and** every character's attached `items`/`hazards` (Bade to Rule le-167 lives on a Ringwraith as an item), routing each to its owner's discard pile with no instance lost. The "prevents subsequent play" half is data: le-167 now carries two `card-not-in-play` play-conditions (The Lidless Eye and its sibling Sauron ba-43), and `playPermanentEventActions` (`organization-events.ts`) was generalized to honor **every** `card-not-in-play` condition on a card, not just the first. **(3)** the once-per-organization-phase dual-mode granted ability is `sauronOrgGrantActions` (`legal-actions/organization.ts`), gated by the new `OrganizationPhaseState.sauronOrgActionUsed` flag (fresh each org phase): `sauron-sideboard-fetch` offers one `activate-granted-action` per eligible sideboard card (a minion resource or a character) whose reducer branch (`handleInPlayCardGrantAction`, `grant-action-apply.ts`) moves it into the play deck and shuffles; `sauron-peek-hand` offers one per hand card (the discard cost) when the opponent holds ≥1 card, and its reducer discards the chosen card then reveals `min(5, oppHandSize)` random opponent-hand instances via `revealInstances` (the cards stay in the opponent's hand). The `general-influence` +7 `stat-modifier` and `hand-size-modifier` +1 are bare `cardsInPlay` effects — the latter now collected from bare permanent-events by `resolveHandSize` (`effects/resolver.ts`), not only from character-borne cards — and `duplication-limit` scope `game` caps it at one copy. The sibling Sauron (ba-43) shares the `play-as-sauron` / `discard-named-in-play` mechanics.

- `faction-siege` + `play-target` `target: "faction"` for permanent events + `duplication-limit` scope `"faction"` — Long Grievous Siege (ba-40): a minion permanent-event played on one of the controller's own in-play unique non-Dragon factions (`filter` on `{ target: { name, race, unique } }`; bound via `CardInPlay.attachedTo`) that sets a Border-hold from the controller's location deck "off to the side" with the card (standard set-aside; the site card is returned to the owner's **location deck**, not discarded, when the host leaves play). Eligibility: the site must be in the same region as (or adjacent to) a site where the target faction is playable (`factionSiegeEligibleSites`); CRF requires an eligible Border-hold for the play to be offered. On resolution every in-play faction playable at the site returns to its owner's hand. While in play, matched against **any version** of the site by printed name: `factionInfluenceModifier` (-5) is added by `siteFactionInfluenceModifier` (now name-based and also applied in the roll resolver, both shared with ba-72's `site-lock`), and every version gains an automatic-attack whose `creatureType` derives from the target faction's race (`factionRaceToAttackType`) and which carries `AutomaticAttack.detainmentAgainstPlayer` — detainment only against the siege controller's own companies. The host is discarded when the target faction leaves play (`discardOrphanedFactionAttachedEvents`).

- Dual-mode grant-action (shared action name) + `targets.scope: "opponent-cards-in-play"` + `discard-target-in-play` apply — "you may: A **or** B" once per turn as **two** `grant-action` effects with the **same** `action` string, each `oncePerTurn: true`: the `granted-action-used` lock is keyed by (source instance, action id), so either activation suppresses both for the rest of the turn; `handleGrantActionApply` (`grant-action-apply.ts`) discriminates the modes by target presence (an activation carrying `targetCardId` selects the `targets`-declaring effect). The new targets scope enumerates the opponent's `cardsInPlay` matching `targets.filter` (item loop of `grantedActionActivations`, `legal-actions/organization.ts`), one activation per candidate; not offered when nothing matches. The `discard-target-in-play` apply discards the chosen instance from whichever player's `cardsInPlay` holds it via `discardCardsInPlayWhere` and clears every active constraint it sourced (a discarded Fortress of the Towers wh-69 takes its `site-protected` constraint with it). Used by Keys to the White Towers (wh-89): a stage special item (`item-play-site` `sites: ["Barrow-downs"]`, `stage-points` 1) whose org-phase choice is mode A `enqueue-pending-fetch` `fetchFrom: ["deck", "discard-pile"]` → hand filtered to `name: "Fortress of the Towers"` (the shipped fetch machinery reshuffles the play deck exactly when the deck was the searched source — "Reshuffle your play deck if searched") or mode B discarding the opponent's in-play Fortress. Sibling: Keys of Orthanc (wh-88, The Fortress of Isen).

- `play-with-stored-card` + `storage-site-transfer` + `storedAtSite` stored-site tracking (Wizard's Trove wh-85) — a Stage permanent-event playable only in one of two combo modes, generated in `legal-actions/organization-events.ts` and resolved by `resolveStoredComboEvent` (`chain-reducer.ts`). **Mode 1** `play-with-stored-card`: plays a named companion from hand (The White Tree) at a `siteFilter`-matching site where a `requiresStored` card is stored (`CardInstance.storedAtSite`, stamped by both store flows); both cards enter play mutually `linkedInstanceId`-linked (mutual discard), the companion `mpPinned` to its printed MP (`fullMarshallingPoints`, beats the MEWH §4 clamp) and `textIgnored` (`ignoreCardText` — excluded from in-play names/uniqueness and effect collection via `playerCardsInPlayDefs`), plus an `until-cleared` `site-protected` constraint for the site (`siteBecomesProtected`, Guarded Haven machinery). Neither card gets `attachedToSite`, so the orphaned-site sweep never discards them. **Mode 2** `storage-site-transfer`: stores a bearer's `storable-at`-with-`marshallingPoints` item at a `siteFilter`-matching site (full store-item semantics: MP pile + corruption check + `bearer-cannot-untap` cleanup), stamps `storedAtSite`, and enters play `attachedToStored`-linked to it; while linked and `fullMarshallingPoints`, the stored card scores full storage MP (no §4 clamp / cross-alignment halving, `recompute-derived.ts`); `discardOrphanedStoredAttachedEvents` (`reducer-utils.ts`) discards the event if the stored card leaves the pile.

- `on-event: corruption-check-failed` + `traitor-attack` — Traitor (tw-105): a hazard permanent-event trigger fired from **every** failed-check path of the unified corruption-check resolver (`applyTraitorTrigger` in `pending-reducers.ts`: discard, eliminate, Press-gang capture, `discard-ring-only`), for a failing character of **either** player. Firing consumes the source: every in-play copy carrying the trigger (both players' `cardsInPlay`) is discarded on the one failed check — duplicates have no extra effect (CRF erratum). If the traitor's company still has a character, a combat starts with the new `traitor-attack` `AttackSource`: prowess = traitor's **printed** prowess + `prowessBonus` (default 10), `strikes` (default 1) strikes, the traitor's race (CRF), no creature body, `CombatState.bodyCheckModifier` = `bodyCheckModifier` (Cruel Caradhras machinery), and the opponent of the traitor's controller chooses the struck character via the attacker-chooses-defenders flow (`assignmentPhase: 'cancel-window'` → `'attacker'`, `attackerChoosesDefenders: true`). If a combat is already active when the check fails (Corpse-candle pre-defense checks), the attack is queued as a `traitor-attack-queued` active constraint (`types/pending.ts`) and initiated by `initiateQueuedTraitorAttack` (`combat-finalize.ts`) at every combat-completion exit (normal finalize tail, trophy taken/declined in `combat-actions.ts`, cancellation paths in `combat-cancel.ts`) — the CRF "chain immediately following" timing. "Cannot be revealed as an on-guard card" holds structurally (the card matches none of the on-guard reveal paths).
- `play-target` `target: 'character'` company-site context (The Burden of Time tw-94) — the M/H character-targeting hazard filter context (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) also exposes `company.siteType` / `company.atHaven`, resolved from the target company's **destination** site when moving (a moving company is "at" its new site for hazard purposes), else its current site; `atHaven` is `true` for any `haven`-type site (Havens and Darkhavens). tw-94 combines it with `target.race: "elf"` for "Playable on an Elf not in a Haven/Darkhaven", plus shipped primitives: `duplication-limit` scope:character, `stat-modifier` corruption-points +2, `on-event untap-phase-end` gated `bearer.atHaven: false` → `force-check` corruption (inverse of Lure of the Senses tw-60), and `grant-action remove-self-on-roll` threshold 8 / `removalNumber` 7 with the rule-10.08 no-tap −3 variant (via the `corruption` keyword).
- `play-option` `untargeted` + `force-discard-stage-card` + `play-flag: remove-from-game` (Echoes of the Song wh-17) — three composable primitives for a dual-mode hazard short-event whose two modes disagree about targeting. **(1) `untargeted`** — a `play-option` may declare `untargeted: true` to opt out of the card's own `play-target`: the hazard short-event emitter (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`, character branch) offers it exactly once (a `play-hazard` with `optionId` and **no** `targetCharacterId`) and evaluates its `when` against a card-level context `{ opponent: { stagePoints, stageCardCount }, inPlay }` describing the resource player, instead of the per-character context the targeted options use; if every option is untargeted the per-character loop is skipped so no bare option-less action leaks out. `chain-reducer.ts` dispatches untargeted options from a dedicated branch keyed on `optionId && !targetCharacterId`, so the two mode families can never both fire. **(2) `force-discard-stage-card`** — a triggered-action verb: `stageCardsInPlay` (`reducer-utils.ts`) collects every card the opponent controls whose definition carries `alignment: "stage"` (MEWH §1 Stage cards — in `cardsInPlay`, in a bearer's `items` when the stage permanent-event was played on a character, or in a bearer's `allies`), and the verb raises a `force-discard-card` pending resolution **actored by the opponent** with those candidates, so *he* chooses. `applyForceDiscardCardResolution` (`pending-reducers.ts`) now also locates a chosen candidate in `cardsInPlay` and in a bearer's `allies` (previously hand + items only) and recomputes derived state, so the stage-point total drops with the discard. **(3) `play-flag: remove-from-game`** — the generic "Remove this card from the game" clause for a short-event: on un-negated resolution the spent card moves from the declaring player's discard pile (hazard shorts are pre-discarded at play time) to their `outOfPlayPile`; a negated entry stays in the discard pile. Generalises the per-verb `removeFromGame` riders on `draw-cards` (as-80) and `cancel-chain-entry` (wh-24). wh-17 composes all three: "If your opponent has more than one stage card and 4 or more stage points, he must discard one stage card of his choice. Alternatively, force a target character to make a corruption check. Remove this card from the game." — the gate is the untargeted option's `when` (`opponent.stageCardCount > 1`, `opponent.stagePoints >= 4`), the alternative reuses the certified le-149 `force-check` corruption path, and the card carries no Corruption keyword in `data/cards.json` so CoE 7.2.1's per-character lock does not apply.

- `cancel-deck-search` `affects: "non-minion"` + `in-play-item-modifier` `corruptionMultiplier` / `bearerFilter` (Bane of the Ithil-stone tw-13, the mirror of Lady of the Golden Wood as-13). **(1)** `cancel-deck-search` gains an optional `affects` field: `"minion"` (default, as-13) cancels the play-deck / discard-pile searches of Ringwraith and Balrog players, `"non-minion"` cancels those of Wizard and Fallen-wizard players. `deckSearchCancellerFor` (`reducer-utils.ts`) now scans each in-play card's `cancel-deck-search` effects and matches the acting player's minion-ness against that field, so the single `gateDeckSearchFetch` chokepoint (org-phase fetches, short/permanent-event chain fetches, grant-action `enqueue-pending-fetch`, agent tutors, site-phase fetches) serves both directions unchanged: `deck`/`discard-pile` sources are stripped, a `sideboard` source survives, and a fetch left with no source fizzles (grant-actions reject the activation instead). **(2)** `in-play-item-modifier` gains `corruptionMultiplier` (applied to each matching item's corruption *after* the `corruptionPoints` delta; multipliers from several sources compound) and `bearerFilter` (a condition on the bearer's controlling player, context `{ bearer: { alignment, minion } }`, absent → all players). Both are threaded through `collectInPlayItemModifiers` / `itemModifierDeltas` in `recompute-derived.ts`, which now receives the bearer's alignment at both call sites. Together they encode "Corruption points for Palantíri are doubled" + "This card has no effect on a minion player" as `itemFilter { item.keywords $includes palantir }`, `bearerFilter { bearer.minion: false }`, `corruptionMultiplier: 2` — the "no effect on a minion player" clause is modelled per-rule rather than as a play restriction (the card is legal to play in any matchup, it simply skips minion players). The remaining tw-13 rules reuse existing machinery: `on-event: play-deck-exhausted` self-discard `move` (fired by `completeDeckExhaust`) and `duplication-limit` scope `game`. Data fix alongside: Palantír of Elostirion (tw-298, le-332) was missing the `palantir` keyword that `data/cards.json` gives it.

- `influenceTarget` resolver context + `character-mp-override` + attached-card deck-exhaust discard (Fool's Bane wh-19). **(1) `influenceTarget`** — a new `ResolverContext` sub-object describing the card an influence check is made *against*: `alignment` (read off the card-type prefix by `buildInfluenceTargetContext` in `effects/resolver.ts`), `kind` (`faction`/`character`/`ally`/`item`), `name`, `race`. Unlike `faction` / `target` (each present in only one path) it is populated in **both** the three faction-influence contexts (`legal-actions/site.ts`, `legal-actions/pending.ts`, `reducer-site.ts`) and the `opponent-influence-check` context, so "influence checks against <kind of card>" is one condition rather than one per path; `bearer.stagePoints` (previously effective-stats only) is now populated in those contexts too. **(2)** The influencer's **ongoing** influence `check-modifier` effects (his own, plus those on his items and attached hazards) are folded into an opponent-influence attempt (`handleOpponentInfluenceAttempt`, added to `boostModifier`), matching the faction path — an influence attempt against an opponent's card is an influence check like any other. Data fix alongside: the five hero characters whose text reads "-1 to influence checks **against factions**" (Beregond tw-127, Dori tw-141, Haldir tw-164, Háma tw-165, Orophin tw-174) gained the `when: { reason: "faction-influence-check" }` gate their siblings (Kíli, Bofur, Fíli, Forlong, Barliman) already carried, so the penalty stays out of the new path. **(3) `character-mp-override`** — the character-scoring sibling of `noncharacter-mp-override`, same `{ when, value }` shape and `{ card: { unique, normalMp, cardType, name, race } }` context, consumed at the top of the character-MP branch in `recompute-derived.ts` so it beats the §4 clamp, a Great Patron cap, a wh-4 full-MP exemption and the wh-96 pin ("in all cases"). Both kinds are now collected by the shared `mpOverrideRules`, which also scans the **hazards attached to a player's characters** — the route by which an opponent's hazard re-values that player's cards. **(4)** `completeDeckExhaust` (`reducer-utils.ts`) now fires `play-deck-exhausted` self-discards for character-attached items/hazards as well as `cardsInPlay`, routing each card to *its owner's* discard pile. wh-19 composes all four: `play-target` character `target.race: fallen-wizard`, `duplication-limit` scope character, five mutually exclusive `check-modifier` influence tiers (`influenceTarget.alignment: hero` + `influenceTarget.kind $ne character` — a character is not a resource card — over half-open `bearer.stagePoints` bands), `character-mp-override` + faction-scoped `noncharacter-mp-override` at 0 for Elves, and the `on-event: play-deck-exhausted` self-discard.
- `permanent-event-auto-attack` `boundSite` + `site-type-override` `allVersions` + the shared site play-target context (`buildSiteFilterContext`) — the three pieces behind a **hazard permanent-event played on a site** that retypes it and arms it. **(1) `buildSiteFilterContext`** (`engine/effective.ts`) is now the single builder for every `play-target` `target: "site"` filter context — site phase (`legal-actions/site.ts`), organization phase (`legal-actions/organization-events.ts`) and, newly, the movement/hazard hazard branch (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`, which previously matched the bare definition via `matchesDefinition`). It exposes the site definition plus `regionType`, `effectiveSiteType` and two new derived flags: `isWizardhaven` (a Fallen-wizard haven for some player — printed FW-alignment haven or a `wizardhaven-conversion` site) and `isProtected` (`isSiteProtectedForPlayer` for either player — a `site-protected` constraint or an inherently protected Wizardhaven). **(2) `allVersions: true`** on a `site-type-override` add-constraint emits a **name-scoped** constraint filter (`{ 'site.name': … }`) instead of `{ 'site.definitionId': … }`, so the override reaches every printing of the location (hero / minion / FW / Balrog are distinct definitions sharing one name). All four readers of a site-scoped `site.type` override — `getEffectiveSiteType` (`effective.ts`), the untap healing sweep (`reducer-untap.ts`), the creature-keying offer (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) and its validator (`mh-hazard-play.ts`) — now route through the shared `siteConstraintFilterMatches`, so definition- and name-scoped filters can never diverge. **(3) `boundSite: true`** on `permanent-event-auto-attack` appends the attack to the site the carrying card is bound to (`attachedToSite`) and every same-named version, matched in `collectPermanentEventAttacks` (`manifestations.ts`) the way `faction-siege` (ba-40) already matched versions by name. Additionally, `discardOrphanedSiteAttachedEvents` (`reducer-utils.ts`) now counts a company's declared **`destinationSite`** as occupying that site alongside `currentSite`: a revealed destination site card is on the table during the M/H phase, which is exactly when a site-targeting hazard binds to it — without this the sweep discarded such a hazard the instant it entered play. Used by Nature's Revenge (wh-27): "Playable on a site in a Wilderness [{w}] that normally is a Border-hold [{B}] or a Shadow-hold [{S}], or on a non-protected Wizardhaven [{H}] in a Wilderness [{w}]. All versions of the site become Ruins & Lairs [{R}] and each gains an additional automatic-attack: Animals — each character faces 1 strike with 7 prowess. Discard this card when the site is discarded or returned to its location deck." — `play-target` site (`regionType: wilderness` AND (`siteType $in [border-hold, shadow-hold]` OR (`isWizardhaven` AND NOT `isProtected`))) + `on-event self-enters-play → add-constraint site-type-override` (`ruins-and-lairs`, `allVersions`, `until-cleared`) + `permanent-event-auto-attack` (`boundSite`, Animals 1×7, `each-character`); the "discard when the site is discarded / returned" clause is the existing site-attached orphan sweep, which also clears the card's constraint.

- `play-window` `{ phase: "combat", step: "after-attack", when }` + `post-attack-play-offer` resolution — the "Playable … **immediately after** his company faces …" window for a **resource permanent-event**. Per CoE rule 8.03 a company has "faced" an attack once combat is initiated (even if canceled), so the trigger is a prev/next diff on `prev.combat → next.combat` (`engine/post-attack-play.ts` `enqueuePostAttackPlayOffers`, called from `postReduce`) rather than a hook per combat-teardown site — strikes-resolved, canceled-on-the-chain and canceled-by-tap teardowns all open the window exactly once. The optional `when` is matched against the ended attack's `{ enemy: { race }, attack: { source } }` context (the same discriminators as a `cancel-attack` `when`, so one expression gates both a card's play window and the ability it grants). The window is a `post-attack-play-offer` pending resolution owned by the defending player — `play-permanent-event` (offered card × legal bearer, recomputed live by `afterAttackPlayTargets`) or `pass` to decline; the play delegates to `handlePlayPermanentEvent`, so it routes through the chain, pays the `play-target` `cost: { tap: "character" }` on resolution and attaches normally. A `duplication-limit` scope `"company"` closes the window while a copy is already borne in that company (`countAttachedInCompany`, the wh-34/wh-47 precedent). Permanent-events carrying **any** `play-window` are now offered only in that window (new phase gate in `legal-actions/organization-events.ts` and `legal-actions/site.ts`; no other permanent-event declares one). Used by No News of Our Riding (le-211): "Playable on an untapped character immediately after his company faces an Elf, Dúnadan, or Man hazard creature. Tap the character. The character can later tap to cancel an Elf, Dúnadan, or Man hazard creature attack against his company. Cannot be duplicated in a given company." — the window (`attack.source $in [creature, on-guard-creature]` ∧ `enemy.race $in [elf, dunadan, man]`), a `play-target` character (`target.status: untapped`, `cost: { tap: "character" }`), `duplication-limit` company/1, and a `cancel-attack` `cost: { tap: "bearer" }` under the identical filter. **No new cancel plumbing**: a resource permanent-event played on a character attaches into that character's `items`, so the existing in-play-item `tap: "bearer"` cancel path (Star-glass tw-330) grants the "later tap to cancel" — bearer must be untapped and taps, the card stays untapped and in play. Card data also corrected to its authoritative 1 misc MP. Also used by Dark Numbers (dm-123): "Playable on an untapped scout immediately after facing an Orc, Troll, or Man attack" — text says "attack", not "hazard creature", so the window's `attack.source` list is widened to `$in [creature, on-guard-creature, automatic-attack, played-auto-attack]` (site automatic-attacks carry a populated `enemy.race` too, from `combat.creatureRace`, so the same `when` context works unchanged), combined with `enemy.race $in [orc, troll, man]` and a `play-target` `target.skills $includes "scout"` filter alongside `target.status: untapped`.

- `play-window` `crossTurn: true` — extends CoE 2.1.1's default ("any phase of the resource player's own turn") to *also* offer the card during the **opponent's** matching phase, without restricting the owner's own-turn play by phase at all. `heroResourceShortEventActions` (`legal-actions/long-event.ts`) now derives `isOwnTurn = state.activePlayer === playerId`: on the owner's own turn a `crossTurn` window's `phase` is ignored entirely (falls through unrestricted, same as a card with no `play-window`); off-turn, only a card whose `crossTurn` window names the *current* phase survives, everything else is skipped silently (no `not-playable` spam for the rest of the hand). The sibling `playResourceShortEventActions` (`legal-actions/organization.ts`) — used by the organization/site-phase own-turn paths — never runs off-turn, so it only needed the one-line "ignore `phase` when `crossTurn`" half of the fix. The only off-turn *call site* added is the M/H `play-hazards` step's hazard-side branch (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`): `heroResourceShortEventActions(state, playerId, 'movement-hazard')` is now also invoked there for the non-active player, relying on the function's own `isOwnTurn` gate to filter out every ordinary resource short-event. Every pre-existing call site (`organization.ts`, `site.ts`, `long-event.ts`'s own M/H select-company offer, `chain.ts`, `end-of-turn.ts`, `combat.ts`) already only ever runs with `playerId === state.activePlayer`, so `crossTurn` is a no-op there and no existing card's behavior changes. Used by Sated Beast (td-149): "Target ahunt Dragon manifestation is discarded. … This card may also be played during opponent's movement/hazard phase." — `{ "type": "play-window", "phase": "movement-hazard", "crossTurn": true }` alongside a `move` (`select: "target"`, `from: "in-play"`, `to: "discard"`, `filter: { cardType: "hazard-event", eventType: "long", manifestId: { "$exists": true } }` — the `manifestId` + `eventType: "long"` combination is unique in the card pool to the nine Dragon "Ahunt" long-events, cleanly excluding the sibling "at Home" permanent-events which share the same `manifestId` but carry `eventType: "permanent"`) resolved via the existing single-target discard-in-play machinery (Voices of Malice le-250 / Marvels Told td-134 precedent, `short-event-discard.ts`'s `applyShortEventDiscardInPlay`, which already searches **both** players' `cardsInPlay`). The card's alternative "-1 strike, minimum one" mode against a Dragon/Drake attack needed no new engine work at all: a from-hand `modify-attack` (`player: "defender"`, `strikesModifier: -1`, `when: enemy.race $in [dragon, drake]` ∧ `inPlay $includes "Doors of Night"`) is resolved by `modifyAttackActions`/`handleModifyAttack` (`legal-actions/combat.ts` / `combat-actions.ts`), which key off `combat.defendingPlayerId`/`attackingPlayerId` rather than `state.activePlayer` — already turn-agnostic — and the strikes floor is hardcoded to a minimum of 1 (`Math.max(1, …)`), matching "to a minimum of one" verbatim.

- `cancel-attack` `siteSwap` + `SitePhaseState.autoAttacksSkipped` (Farmer Maggot as-48) — a site-swap cancellation carried by an in-play resource permanent-event with `cost: { discard: "self" }`. The `siteSwap: { regions }` payload names the region names (site cards' `region` field) that both the defending company's current site and the replacement site must belong to. `siteSwapCancelActions` (`legal-actions/combat.ts`) offers the option only while the company is **at** such a site — a moving company is not "at" one, so `destinationSite` must be null — and emits one `cancel-attack` per eligible site left in the controller's location deck, each carrying `replacementSiteInstanceId`. `handleCancelAttackBySiteSwap` (`combat-cancel.ts`) disposes the replaced site exactly as a departure site (CoE 2.IV.viii: tapped non-haven → site discard pile, otherwise → location deck; a site still occupied by a sibling company stays in play), installs the replacement as the company's untapped `currentSite`, discards the host card and cancels the attack via `resolveCancelAttackEntry` (in-play source → immediate, no chain). Because the company is *placed* at the replacement rather than moving to it, it never enters that site: `SitePhaseState.autoAttacksSkipped` ends the company's site-phase automatic-attack sequence (also suppressing The Moon Is Dead race-duplicates) and is cleared when the next company is selected.

- `peek-shuffle-deck-top` + `player.characterSiteNames` play-condition context (Mirror of Galadriel tw-282) — a resource short-event that (1) reveals the **opponent's whole hand** to the card-player (`revealOpponentHand` → `revealInstances`, the cards stay in the opponent's hand — the same mechanism `peek-opponent-hand` uses for le-203) and (2) enqueues a `choose-peek-deck` pending resolution so the deck is chosen *after* the hand was seen. The player answers `choose-peek-deck` with `deckOwner: 'self' | 'opponent'` — the top `count` cards of that deck are shuffled among themselves and returned to the top, cards beneath untouched — or declines with `pass` ("You **may** … choose to look at …"). `deckChoice` (`any` default / `self` / `opponent`) restricts which decks are offered; only decks holding cards are offered, and when neither qualifies no resolution is enqueued (the deck step fizzles, the hand look still happened). An in-play `cancel-deck-search` (Bane of the Ithil-stone tw-13 against a non-minion, Lady of the Golden Wood as-13 against a minion) additionally withholds the actor's **own** deck via the shared `deckSearchCancellerFor` helper — checked at enqueue, legal-action, and reduce time — and never the opponent's, which those cards do not cover ("any portion of *his* play deck"). The deck look is deliberately **not** recorded in `revealedInstances` — that map is public to both players, so recording it would leak the cards to the player who may not see them; this matches the certified Palantír of Minas Tirith (tw-299 / le-333), which models "look at the top five cards …; shuffle these 5 cards and return them to the top" as the `shuffle-deck-top` alone. Wired in `reducer-events.ts` (`handlePlayResourceShortEvent`), `legal-actions/pending.ts` (`choosePeekDeckActions`), `pending-reducers.ts` (`applyChoosePeekDeckResolution`). The companion play gate "Only playable if any of your characters are at Lórien" is a plain `play-condition` `player-state` over a new context field `player.characterSiteNames` — the de-duplicated names of every site the player has characters at (one per non-empty company's `currentSite`, matched by name so all reprints of a site count), so the gate spans *all* the player's companies rather than the phase's active one (`buildPlayerStateContext`, `legal-actions/organization.ts`).

- `extra-mh-phase` active constraint — the organization-phase sibling of `grant-extra-mh-phase` (Master of Esgaroth td-135: "Playable at the end of the organization phase on a moving company. If the company moves to a Border-hold [{B}], it can take a second movement/hazard phase immediately following its first"). Because the destination is not final when the card is played, the card is playable on **any** moving company (end-of-org `play-window` + `play-target` `target: "company"` with filter `{ "company.moving": true }`, the le-181 shape) and the "if it moves to …" clause becomes a turn-scoped constraint on the chosen company: `add-constraint` `constraint: "extra-mh-phase"` with the new `requiresDestinationSiteType` field on the apply (`buildConstraintKind` in `constraint-kind.ts` and the short-event switch in `reducer-events.ts`). When that company's M/H phase ends, `advanceAfterCompanyMH` (`mh-hazard-play.ts`) matches it via `extraMHPhaseConstraint` — the company must have `moved` and its new `currentSite` must be of the required `SiteType` — then **consumes** the constraint (`removeConstraint`) and enters the shared `extra-mh-move-offer` step, so the second phase reuses the whole Forced March path (`extra-mh-move` → `handleExtraMHMoveOffer` → fresh `reveal-new-site`). Consuming the constraint is what bounds the grant to exactly one extra phase even when the second move also lands on a qualifying site; a company that moved elsewhere (or did not move) leaves it in force but inert until the turn-end sweep. Distinct from `grant-extra-mh-phase`, which is played at the *end* of the M/H phase and can therefore gate its own play window on the already-known destination.

- `win-game` `destroysOneRing` (Cracks of Doom tw-205) — the "**The One Ring is destroyed**" half of the two Mount Doom win cards, which the shipped `win-game` apply previously dropped. The optional flag makes `oneRingWin` (`reducer-free-council.ts`) first sweep every in-play item borne by the winner's characters whose definition carries the `the-one-ring` keyword (keyword, not definition id, so any printing resolves) off its bearer and into that player's `outOfPlayPile` — the terminal, unrecyclable pile, i.e. removed from the game rather than discarded — and only then call `endGame`, so the destroyed Ring contributes no item marshalling points to the final scores. Threaded from both `win-game` seams: the corruption-check `onSuccess` branch (`pending-reducers.ts` `applyCorruptionCheckResolution`, tw-205's route) and the on-play branch (`reducer-events.ts` `applyShortEventOnEntersPlay`, Gollum's Fate tw-247's route). A New Ringlord (wh-60) and Challenge the Power (ba-52) win *with* the Ring, not by destroying it, so they leave the flag unset and are unchanged. The card's remaining clauses are pure reuse: `play-window` phase `site`, a `play-condition` `active-company` over `site.name` + `company.itemNames`, and a `play-target` `character` filtered on `target.itemNames $includes "The One Ring"` (so the check falls on the bearer). "Cannot be included in a Fallen-wizard's deck" is the shipped rule-1.18 deck ban — tw-205 is already listed in `FALLEN_WIZARD_BANNED_CARD_IDS` (`deck-validation.ts`).

- `move` `select: 'filter-all'` from `in-play` to `discard` on a **short event** (Wizard's River-horses tw-364) — the sweep sibling of the single-target `discard-in-play` shape. Discards **every** in-play card whose definition matches the effect's `filter` (both players' `cardsInPlay` and character attachments; each card routes to its own owner's discard pile via the shared `applyMove`). The legal-action layer (`heroResourceShortEventActions`, `legal-actions/long-event.ts`) offers the mode only while at least one card matches, so the Wizard is never asked to make the `corruptionCheck` for an empty sweep. Like the single-target shape it rides the chain of effects (CoE 9.4/9.5): the declaration flags `discardAllInPlay: true` on the `short-event` chain payload, and `chain-reducer` calls `applyShortEventDiscardAllInPlay` (`engine/short-event-discard.ts`) on un-negated resolution — the sweep plus a `corruptionCheck` made by the effect's `play-target` character (the Wizard), then the spent card to discard. The payload flag is load-bearing: it keeps the sweep from firing when the same card is played in its *other* mode (a `cancel-attack`, which pushes a bare `short-event` payload from `handleCancelAttack`). Paired on tw-364 with a `cancel-attack` `when: { "bearer.companySize": 1 }` ("if he is the only character in the company", Gollum tw-246 precedent) — the dual-mode-in-two-windows shape of The Cock Crows (tw-342). Nazgûl are matched by the `Nazgûl` keyword, which the Nine now all carry in data.

- `combat-attacker-chooses-defenders` `scope: "all-attacks"` (Alatar the Hunter as-7) — the **global** sibling of the printed creature rule. While the carrying card sits in either player's `cardsInPlay`, every attack whose race satisfies `when` hands strike assignment to the attacker (`assignmentPhase: "cancel-window"` + `attackerChoosesDefenders: true`), instead of only the creature that bears the effect. `globalAttackerChoosesDefenders` (`reducer-utils.ts`) scans both `cardsInPlay` lists and matches `when` against `{ attack: { creatureRace } }` — the same vocabulary as the global `body-check-modifier`; `resolveAttackerChoosesDefenders` ORs it with the attack's own printed rule and is called from **every** combat-creation site: hazard-creature attacks (`chain-reducer.ts` `initiateCreatureCombat`), site automatic-attacks and their Moon-Is-Dead / Incite-Defenders / No-Strangers duplicates and Troll-purse re-face (`reducer-site.ts`), the site-played dynamic auto-attack (`reducer-site.ts`), Tidings of Bold Spies attacks (`chain-reducer.ts`, `combat-finalize.ts`), and Dragon Ahunt attacks (`mh-steps.ts`, which now also sets the `attackerChoosesDefenders` flag its `cancel-window` phase always implied). Unscoped copies of the effect are deliberately excluded from the global scan (and from the creature's own `some()` check) so a dual creature/permanent-event card in `cardsInPlay` never exports its own creature rule to the whole game. Used by as-7's permanent-event half — "all Maia attacks: receive +1 prowess and +1 strike **and** attacker chooses defending characters" — where the +1s are plain `stat-modifier` `target: "all-attacks"` with `when: { "enemy.race": "maia" }` (Wake of War tw-108 shape); the rest of the card follows the as-11 dual-mode manifestation template (`creature-alt-event` persistent permanent-event, `combat-detainment`/`stat-modifier -3` vs hero, `discard-self-when charactersInPlayAnywhere`, `manifestId`, `on-event play-deck-exhausted` self-discard).
- `stat-modifier` `stat: "untap-penalty"` + `target.prowess` in the item bearer filter — a warrior weapon that cancels the stay-untapped prowess penalty for its bearer. **(1) `untap-penalty` stat.** The prowess penalty for choosing *not* to tap to face a strike (CoE 3.iv.3) was a pure function of the card definition (`stayUntappedPenalty`, `state-utils.ts`: 3, or 1 for The Balrog avatar). The new `computeStayUntappedPenalty(state, char, charDef)` (`recompute-derived.ts`) takes that as the base and runs the character's collected effects (slot-aware, via `collectCharacterEffects`) through `resolveStatModifiers` for the synthetic stat `untap-penalty`, in a `reason: 'combat'` / `combat.strikeMode: 'untap'` context — so `op`/`max`/`min`/`when` all behave as for any other stat-modifier, and the result is floored at 0 (cancelling the penalty gives full prowess, never a bonus). `untap-penalty` is never folded into `effectiveStats`; only this function reads it. All four penalty consumers now route through it: the creature/automatic-attack strike path (`resolveStrikeCore`, `combat-strike.ts`), both CvCC sides (`resolveCvccStrike`), the tap/untap **need** shown to the defender (`resolveStrikeActions`, `legal-actions/combat.ts`) and the CvCC attacker's declare options. An ally target (no `char`) keeps the base penalty. **(2) `target.prowess` / `target.baseProwess` in the item bearer filter.** The `play-target target: "character"` filter context built by the site-phase item emitter (`legal-actions/site.ts`) previously exposed only `race`/`skills`/`status`/`name`; it now also carries the candidate's *effective* prowess and printed prowess, so "may only be borne by a character with a prowess of N or more" is a plain `{ "target.prowess": { "$gte": N } }` filter. The item being played is not attached yet, so its own prowess bonus never feeds its own gate. Used by Thong of Fire (as-132): "Unique. Hoard item. Weapon. May only be borne by a character with a prowess of 6 or more. Warrior only: +1 body; +1 prowess; if bearer chooses not to tap against a strike, he receives no prowess penalty." — `item-play-site` `site.keywords $includes hoard` (hoard gate, Framsburg as-146 precedent), the `weapon` keyword (one-weapon-per-character slot, `item-slots.ts`), the `target.prowess $gte 6` bearer filter, warrior-gated `stat-modifier` prowess +1 / body +1 (Aiglos dm-166 shape, no printed cap), and warrior-gated `stat-modifier` `untap-penalty` `op: "set"` `value: 0`.

- `on-event: attack-not-canceled` → `company-tap-characters` (Wild Fell Beast td-81) — the "Unless this attack is canceled, …" clause on a creature card. Canceling an attack returns straight to the enclosing phase without running `finalizeCombat` (`combat-cancel.ts`), so the `attack-not-canceled` scan in `combat-finalize.ts` *is* the not-canceled test. Alongside the existing `add-constraint creature-attack-boost` apply it now accepts the `company-tap-characters` triggered-action verb (shared with Heedless Revelry le-114's on-guard reveal): every still-**untapped** character in the defending company (`combat.companyId`) is tapped, honouring the verb's optional `filter` (context `{ target: { race, mind, name, skills, cardType } }`, no mind gate). Strikers who tapped to fight are already tapped and wounded ones are `Inverted`, so the sweep hits exactly the untapped survivors and the bystanders who never faced a strike.
