Paired on td-81 with the printed `combat-attacker-chooses-defenders` rule and `{s}{s}` keying (`regionTypes: ["shadow", "shadow"]` — the count-based `regionTypesMatch` requires two Shadow-lands in the path).
- `enqueue-site-wound-rolls` (Plague le-129) — the `on-event: end-of-turn` apply for a plague-style contagion carried by a permanent hazard **attached to a character**: it afflicts everybody standing at the bearer's site, not just the bearer. `fireEndOfTurnSiteWoundRolls` (`reducer-site.ts`, wired into both end-of-turn entry points beside `fireEndOfTurnCorruptionChecks`) walks the **active** player's characters, so it fires exactly at the end of the turn in which the bearer's controller is active — "the end of your opponent's turn" from the hazard player's seat. For every character standing at the same site as the bearer (matched by site *name*, the `hasCvCCAttackTargets` convention, so hero/minion versions of a location count as one place; **both** players' companies are scanned, and the bearer himself is included since he is at his own site) whose definition matches the apply's optional `filter` (bare definition fields — `race`, `cardType` — via `matchesDefinition`, **not** the `target.*` namespace), it enqueues one generic `dice-check` scoped to `Phase.EndOfTurn`: the character's **own controller** rolls 2d6, adds `modifier`, and on a total strictly greater than the character's *effective* body (`effectiveStats.body`, so Nothing to Eat or Drink-style body modifiers count) the existing `wound-or-eliminate` onPass verb (ba-54) wounds him — or eliminates him if he was already wounded. The rest of the card is precedent: the "at or moving to a non-Haven, non-Ruins & Lairs site" gate is a `play-target` `target: "character"` filter combining `target.race` `$ne`s with `company.siteType` `$ne`s (the destination-or-current site context the char-targeting hazard path already exposes, tw-94 precedent), and "Discard this card during the target's organization phase if he is at a Haven/Darkhaven" is the le-138 `on-event: organization-phase-start` self-discard `move` gated on `when: { "bearer.atHaven": true }`. "non-Wizard" excludes race `wizard` **and** `fallen-wizard` (the same five Istari — le-138 precedent); the Balrog is not excluded, the printed text names only Ringwraiths, Wizards and Elves.

- `target.isRevealedAvatar` in a **permanent-event** `play-target` character filter (While the Yellow Face Sleeps le-255) — the org-phase permanent-event generator (`legal-actions/organization-events.ts`) now exposes `target.isRevealedAvatar` on the character filter context, computed from `findPlayerAvatar` (the generally-controlled avatar). This is what distinguishes "playable on **your Ringwraith**" from "playable on a Ringwraith": a Ringwraith *follower* (`ringwraith-follower-slots`) is an avatar-race character controlled by the revealed avatar, so it never matches. The short-event play-option context already carried the same key (`buildPlayOptionContext`); this brings permanent events in line. le-255 pairs it with `play-condition` `requires: 'phase'` (`["organization"]`, so the card is not offered again in the site phase), `play-condition` `requires: 'site-type'` (`["haven"]` — a minion company's haven *is* a Darkhaven, since a minion location deck holds only minion sites), a `hand-size-modifier` +1 (reached through `collectCharacterEffects`, since a character-targeted resource permanent event lands in the bearer's `items`), an `on-event: bearer-company-moves` self-discard, and `duplication-limit` scope `player`. "Cannot be included in a Balrog's deck" needs no card data — le-255 is already listed in `BALROG_BANNED_CARD_IDS` (`deck-validation.ts`, rule 1.23).

- `play-condition` `player-state` on `player.avatar` as a named-avatar gate (Hoarmûrath Unleashed le-193) — the "Playable on \<named avatar\> (as your Ringwraith)" wording on a **combat** card. `player.avatar` (`buildPlayerStateContext`, `legal-actions/organization.ts`) is the name of the player's revealed avatar **in play** for every alignment, not just Fallen-wizards: `findFallenWizardAvatarName` resolves the in-play avatar first (`findPlayerAvatar`, which requires `controlledBy === 'general'`) and only falls back to the declared-but-unplayed identity for Fallen-wizards. So `{ "player.avatar": "Hoarmûrath the Ringwraith" }` holds exactly while that manifestation is the player's own Ringwraith, and fails for a different manifestation, for a rival Ringwraith avatar, and for a Hoarmûrath riding as another Ringwraith's **follower** (a follower is not controlled under general influence). This is the combat-window counterpart of the org-phase `play-target` name gate used by Akhôrahil Unleashed (le-162) (`target.name` + `target.isRevealedAvatar`): the from-hand cancel-attack loop in `cancelAttackActions` evaluates `play-condition` `player-state` but never `play-target`, so a "playable on X" clause that is really an identity prerequisite belongs in `play-condition`. Used by Hoarmûrath Unleashed (le-193): the gate plus a bare costless `cancel-attack` (no `when` — "an attack" covers hazard creatures, on-guard reveals and site automatic-attacks). No company binding is needed for "against any one of your companies": the cancel loop is already scoped to whichever company is defending and the player-state gate is company-independent, so Hoarmûrath cancels attacks on companies he is not travelling with.

- `ally-tap-extra-mh-phase` (Shadowfax tw-326) — the ally-carried sibling of `grant-extra-mh-phase`/the `extra-mh-phase` constraint above: an untapped **in-play ally** (attached to a character, not a company-bound card) offers a tap at the same end-of-M/H-phase decision point, gated on a company-composition `condition` rather than a destination requirement. `condition` (required) and `counts` (optional, named per-character filtered headcounts published as `count.<as>`) are evaluated with `buildCompanyCompositionContext` (`company-composition.ts`, exported for this reuse) — the same context `discard-self-when-company` builds (`company.characterCount` plus `count.<as>`). At the end of a company's M/H phase, `advanceAfterCompanyMH` (`mh-hazard-play.ts`) calls the new `findAllyTapExtraMHPhase`, which walks `company.characters` → `player.characters[id].allies` for an untapped ally carrying the effect whose condition holds; a match routes to the new `ally-tap-mh-offer` step instead of falling through to `finalizeCompanyMH`. `allyTapExtraMHOfferActions` (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) re-derives the same match and offers one `ally-tap-extra-mh-phase` action per qualifying ally plus `pass`; `handleAllyTapExtraMHOffer` taps the ally (`updateAttachment`) on accept and — unlike `grant-extra-mh-phase`, which flags the company and waits for its move to commit — switches the step directly to the shared `extra-mh-move-offer`, reusing its destination choice and `reveal-new-site` re-entry unchanged. Used by Shadowfax (tw-326): "If his company has only one character or one character and a Hobbit at the end of the movement/hazard phase, tap Shadowfax to allow his company to immediately move again; an additional site card may be played and an additional movement/hazard phase follows for that company." — `counts: [{ "as": "hobbit", "filter": { "character.race": "hobbit" } }]`, `condition: { "$or": [{ "company.characterCount": 1 }, { "$and": [{ "company.characterCount": 2 }, { "count.hobbit": 1 }] }] }`.

- `grant-extra-mh-phase` `movement: "under-deeps"` + `returnToHand` + `keyed-attacks-normal` (World Gnawed by the Nameless as-110) — the Under-deeps variant of the one-shot extra-M/H-phase short event. The play gate additionally requires the destination to carry the `under-deeps` keyword (`extraMHPhaseResourceActions`); resolution sets `extraMHPhasePending: 'under-deeps'`, keeps the card in its owner's hand instead of discarding it (`returnToHand` — "Return this card to your hand", so it can be replayed during the extra phase to chain Under-deeps moves), and installs a **turn-scoped `keyed-attacks-normal` active constraint** on the target company. `advanceAfterCompanyMH` enters the shared `extra-mh-move-offer` step with `extraMHMoveUnderDeeps` set on the M/H phase state, and the offer enumerates via `extraMHUnderDeepsDestinations` (mh-hazard-play.ts): `under-deeps`-keyword sites, Under-deeps-adjacent to the current site, still in the site deck, and **not attempted by this company this turn** — every Under-deeps declare-path records its destination in `underDeepsAttempts` on the phase state (mh-steps.ts), so a failed movement roll still counts as an attempt ("which it has not attempted to move to yet this turn"). The extra phase runs the normal Under-deeps declare-path/roll flow with no roll penalty (the phase-count penalty is Gangways-specific ba-60 text, not a general rule). The detainment clause ("All hazard creatures the company faces this turn keyed to Shadow-holds attack normally, not as detainment"): `isDetainmentAttack` gained `normalIfKeyedToSiteTypes` (from `companyKeyedAttacksNormalSiteTypes`, reducer-utils.ts) and `attackDeclaredSiteTypes` (the keying the attack was *actually* played by — declared site-type keying in `chain-reducer.ts` creature init, the site rule's own `keying.siteTypes` for `dynamic-auto-attack` played attacks in `reducer-site.ts`, union-of-`keyedTo` fallback for on-guard/Great Hunt); a match forces the attack normal, overriding `combat-detainment` and the §3.II alignment rules. Note the minion Under-deeps Shadow-holds (as-165/as-167) already print non-Nazgûl `attacks-not-detainment` site rules, so the clause's distinct bite is at dynamic played auto-attacks keyed to Shadow-holds (e.g. The Under-vaults as-168) and any Shadow-hold-keyed attack not covered by a site rule.
- `grant-extra-mh-phase` `movement: "under-deeps"`, bare (Into Dark Tunnels dm-145) — the hero-side sibling of as-110's Under-deeps variant, confirming `returnToHand`/`keyed-attacks-normal` are independent optional add-ons rather than part of the variant itself: `{ "type": "grant-extra-mh-phase", "movement": "under-deeps" }` alone reproduces "Playable at the end of the movement/hazard phase on a company that has moved to an Under-deeps site. That company may attempt to move to an additional site on the same turn." with no return-to-hand and no detainment override — the card simply discards after resolution like the base (non-Under-deeps) variant.
- `whip-discipline` (Where There's a Whip le-254) — an `on-event: self-enters-play` apply for a resource short event targeting a company's own bearer. Every OTHER character in the resolution-target's company that is currently **tapped**, has a printed mind greater than 0, and has a lower *effective* prowess than the bearer makes a body check with `modifier` added directly to the roll (CoE 3.I.1: "applies any modifications... to the roll"), compared against the character's effective body via the generic `dice-check` pending resolution (`comparison: 'gt'`, matching the authoritative direction used by combat body checks — CoE 3.I.1 "if the modified roll is higher than the entity's body, the entity fails" — and by `enqueue-body-check`'s `A Malady Without Healing` le-159 precedent: **generic PASS is the bad outcome**). Per CoE 3.I.3 a failing Orc/Troll is discarded instead of eliminated — approximated, like `force-check-all-company`'s Veils Flung Away (le-146) precedent, by comparing against the minimum of the character's printed `discardBodyCheck` array rather than an exact-value match; every other race is wounded (`set-character-status inverted`) instead of eliminated, which is moot in practice since only tapped (never-already-wounded) characters are ever checked. Implemented in `applyShortEventOnEntersPlay` (`reducer-events.ts`): company members excluded from the check (already untapped, no mind, or prowess not lower than the bearer's) are untapped immediately, since their outcome never depends on a roll; a checked member's `onFail` (safe) branch also untaps them — together these cover "each unwounded character in the company becomes untapped" without a separate aggregate sweep step. Each dice-check uses `continuation: { kind: 'dequeue-only' }` (no chain involvement — resource short events with a plain character `play-target` resolve immediately, per `handlePlayResourceShortEvent`'s default path). Used by le-254: `play-target` character filtered to an untapped Orc/Troll bearing an item named "Whip" (`target.itemNames $includes "Whip"`).
- Race-agnostic `creature-attack-boost` via inline `on-event: self-enters-play` → `add-constraint` (Wizard's Flame tw-361) — a plain resource short event ("all attacks against Wizard's company suffer -2 prowess for the rest of the turn") with no move/draw/combat shape resolves *inline* in `handlePlayResourceShortEvent`, not on the chain — so `attack-race-boost` (a chain-only primitive resolved by `applyAttackRaceBoost` in `chain-reducer.ts`) never fires for it. `creature-attack-boost` was already reachable from the generic company-targeting `add-constraint` `case` in `applyShortEventOnEntersPlay` (`reducer-events.ts`) for other kinds (`character-stat-modifier`, `hazard-limit-modifier`, `haven-return-option`, ...), whose surrounding logic already resolves the target company from the card's own `play-target`-chosen character (`action.targetCharacterId` → `findCharacterCompany`) — Wizard's Flame just needed the missing `case 'creature-attack-boost'` added, reading `onEvent.apply.prowess`/`strikes` (and `race` when present) into the constraint kind. Required one generalization to the constraint kind itself: `race` on `creature-attack-boost` (`types/pending.ts`) is now optional, and `collectCreatureAttackBoostEffects` (`effects/resolver.ts`) treats an absent `race` as "matches every attack" rather than filtering by one — so the same `resolveAttackProwess`/`resolveAttackStrikes` path `attack-race-boost` (Dwar of Waw tw-31) already used reaches hazard-creature attacks and site automatic-attacks alike regardless of creature race (CvCC combat is out of scope, matching every other "attack against a company" primitive in the engine). Card: "Spell. Wizard only. All attacks against Wizard's company suffer a -2 modification to prowess for the rest of the turn. Wizard makes a corruption check modified by -3." — `play-target` character `{ target.race: wizard }`, `on-event: self-enters-play` → `add-constraint` (`constraint: "creature-attack-boost"`, `scope: "turn"`, `prowess: -2`), and a second `on-event: self-enters-play` → `enqueue-corruption-check` (`modifier: -3`, targets `action.targetCharacterId` per the existing short-event convention).

- `strike-modifier` `dodge: true` + `requiredSkill` — the skill-gate `requiredSkill` field, previously honored only in default (prowess/body accumulator) mode, is now also enforced in dodge mode. `resolveStrikeActions` (`legal-actions/combat.ts`) skips a dodge-mode `play-strike-event` offer when the struck character lacks the required skill, and — matching the default-mode CoE 3.iv.5 "only one skill-requiring resource per strike" rule — skips it when a skill-requiring strike event has already been declared against the current strike (`currentStrike.requiredSkillEventPlayed`). `handlePlayStrikeEvent` (`combat-actions.ts`) now sets `requiredSkillEventPlayed` on the current strike assignment at declaration time for dodge mode too, not just default mode. No change to reroll mode, which gates on `filter` instead of `requiredSkill`. Used by Block (tw-199): "Warrior only. Warrior does not tap against one strike (unless he is wounded by the strike)" — `{ "type": "strike-modifier", "dodge": true, "requiredSkill": "warrior" }`, the Warrior-gated counterpart of the ungated Under His Blow (le-247) and Dodge (tw-209, which pairs `dodge` with `bodyPenalty` instead of `requiredSkill`).

- `strike-modifier` — a hand-played short event affecting the current strike during `resolve-strike`, in four modes (`docs/card-effects-dsl.md` §10): **cancel** (`cancel: true`, the strike is canceled outright with no roll, gated by an optional target `filter`, resolves immediately with no chain), **dodge** (`dodge: true`, no tap unless wounded, `bodyPenalty` on the resulting body check, resolved through the chain so the opponent may respond), **reroll** (`reroll: true`, two 2d6 rolls kept better, gated by an optional target `filter`), and **default** (`prowessBonus`/`bodyPenalty` accumulate on the strike assignment immediately, optionally gated by `requiredSkill` on the struck character enforcing CoE 3.iv.5 — only one skill-requiring resource per strike, tracked via `StrikeAssignment.requiredSkillEventPlayed`). Precedent: Dodge (tw-209, dodge + bodyPenalty -1, no skill gate), Under His Blow (le-247, dodge only, no body penalty), Bold Thrust (le-172, default mode, `requiredSkill: "warrior"`). `requiredSkill` previously gated only default mode (`legal-actions/combat.ts`'s dodge branch and `handlePlayStrikeEvent`'s dodge branch in `combat-actions.ts` skipped the skill check and never set `requiredSkillEventPlayed`); both were extended to run the same skill-presence and already-played checks as default mode, so a "Warrior only... does not tap" card is now representable as `dodge: true` + `requiredSkill: "warrior"` without a new effect shape. Used by Blow Turned (le-171): "Warrior only. Warrior does not tap against one strike. If wounded by the strike, his body check is modified by -1." — `strike-modifier` `dodge: true`, `bodyPenalty: -1`, `requiredSkill: "warrior"`.

- `strike-modifier` `cancel: true` — new mode added for Orc Stealth (le-217): "Orc scout only. Cancel one strike against an Orc scout." The struck character must match a race+skill combination, which the existing `requiredSkill` field can't express alone (it only checks skill), so this mode reuses reroll mode's `filter` mechanism instead — `{ "$and": [{ "target.race": "orc" }, { "target.skills": { "$includes": "scout" } }] }` — evaluated against the same `target.*` context (race/skills/name) already built for reroll's filter in `resolveStrikeActions` (`legal-actions/combat.ts`). Unlike reroll/dodge, cancel mode never rolls: the offered `play-strike-event` action carries `need: 0` and resolving it (`resolveChainStrikeModifier` in `combat-cancel.ts`) marks the current strike `resolved: true, result: 'canceled'` and calls `advanceStrikeOrFinalize` directly — the same outcome shape as the item-based `cancel-strike` (`handleCancelStrike`) and `flee-from-strike` (`handleFleeFromStrike`) handlers, and likewise resolved immediately with no chain (no opponent response window; `handlePlayStrikeEvent` in `combat-actions.ts` short-circuits to `resolveChainStrikeModifier` before the dodge/reroll/default branches). No `requiredSkillEventPlayed` accounting — CoE 3.iv.5's skill-requiring-resource limit is enforced only by the `requiredSkill` field (default/dodge modes), and outright strike cancellation is explicitly carved out by CoE 3.iv.5.1 ("A strike may be canceled up until the strike roll has been made, even if the attack could not be canceled").

- **Sage "artifact reforged" shape** — `storable-at` + `play-flag: bearer-cannot-untap-until-stored` on a sage-only permanent-event played at an untapped site where Information is playable. `play-target: site` (`playableResources $includes information`) + `play-target: character` (`target.skills $includes sage`, `target.status: untapped`) + `play-flag: untapped-site-required` + `play-flag: tap-site-on-play` + `play-flag: tap-character-on-play` gate and pay for the play (all pre-existing primitives, no new engine work); the card then attaches to the sage's `items` and installs a `bearer-cannot-untap` active constraint (`performUntap`, `reducer-untap.ts`) that holds until a `store-item` action (`legal-actions/organization-companies.ts` / `reducer-organization.ts`) moves it to the marshalling-point pile at a matching site (here `siteTypes: ["haven"]`), which also clears the constraint and runs the initial bearer's corruption check. A card printed with 0 marshalling points while borne and a nonzero value only once stored (the "stored item uses that value instead of the card's base MP" rule, §21 of the DSL doc) declares the printed value as `storable-at`'s `marshallingPoints` override rather than the card's own `marshallingPoints` field. "May not be transferred" needs no dedicated flag: the card's `cardType` is `hero-resource-event`, not `hero-resource-item`, so `handleTransferItem`'s `isItemCard` check (`reducer-organization.ts`) already refuses to move it between characters. Used by Dreams of Lore (tw-210): "Sage only during the site phase at an untapped site where Information is playable. Tap the sage and the site. Sage may not untap until Dreams of Lore is stored at a Haven [{H}] during his organization phase. May not be transferred." — the plain form of the shape, no extra ability. Reforging (tw-314) is the same shape plus an organization-phase "discard a stored Reforging to retrieve an item" ability — see the `fromStored` grant-action entry below.

- `grant-action` `fromStored: true` + `cost.tap: "sage-at-haven"` + `place-item-on-character` (Reforging tw-314) — a `grant-action` granted while its source card sits **stored** in the marshalling-point pile (`killPile`, a `storedAtSite` entry) rather than while attached to a bearer. A stored card has no bearer to anchor the ability, so it is scanned by a dedicated function, `storedCardGrantActions` (`legal-actions/organization.ts`), which walks each stored entry's definition effects directly instead of the attached item/ally/hazard scans — those scans (`extractGrantActions`) explicitly skip `fromStored` effects so the same effect is never double-offered while the card is still attached (before being stored) or double-counted. `cost.tap: "sage-at-haven"` enumerates every one of the acting player's own untapped sage characters currently at a Haven [{H}] as a candidate actor, independent of company (there is no bearer/company to inherit one from); the chosen sage's own company then supplies the recipient set for a `place-item-on-character` apply — reusing The Forge-master (wh-117)'s existing apply verb and its `filter`/`fetchFrom` fields unchanged, just sourced and recipient-scoped differently (one activation per (item, company-member) pair, `targetCardId`/`recipientCharacterId`). `cost.discard: "self"` is paid by a `killPile` fallback added to `applyDiscardSelf` (`cost-evaluator.ts`): when the source instance isn't found on the paying actor's `items`/`allies`/`hazards` (because it isn't attached to anyone), the cost payer checks the player's `killPile` for a `storedAtSite` entry matching the source id and removes it from there into the discard pile instead — a small, generic extension any future stored-card discard-self cost can reuse. Used by Reforging: "During your organization phase, you may tap a sage at a Haven [{H}] and discard a stored Reforging to retrieve any minor or major weapon, armor, or shield (even a hoard item) from your discard pile. The item must be placed under the control of a character in the sage's company." — the `filter` is `subtype $in [minor, major]` AND (`keywords $includes weapon` OR `armor` OR `shield`); a hoard item naturally still qualifies since the filter has no keyword exclusion for `hoard`, and the ability bypasses the normal site-based `item-play-site` gate entirely (there is no site check in this path), which is what makes a hoard item playable outside its normal hoard-site restriction.
- `mirror-automatic-attacks` add-constraint kind (Whole Villages Roused wh-31) — a hazard short-event played during M/H against a company moving to a hero Border-hold/Free-hold or a minion Shadow-hold/Dark-hold ("Playable on a hero Border-hold or Free-hold... playable on a minion Shadow-hold or Dark-hold"). Play-condition gate: `requires: "site-path"` with a `condition` matching the new `destinationSiteCardType` + `destinationSiteType` context keys (`checkSitePathCondition`, `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) — needed because the same `siteType` string occurs on both hero and minion printings (e.g. Raider-hold's hero and minion Border-holds, or Moria's hero and minion Shadow-holds), so `siteType` alone can't disambiguate; unlike the `company-site` play-condition's `destinationSite ?? currentSite` fallback, `site-path` never falls back to the current site, so a stationary company (no genuine arrival) correctly fails the gate. Resolution: `on-event: company-arrives-at-site` → `add-constraint` (`constraint: "mirror-automatic-attacks"`, `value: 2`, `scope: "turn"`); `constraint-kind.ts` resolves the destination site (same M/H-phase lookup `site-type-override` uses), finds the same-named opposite-alignment site definition in `state.cardPool` (every hero B/F-hold and minion S/D-hold has exactly one), and bakes in the detainment mode: hero-site mode stores `detainmentAgainstPlayer` set to the hero-aligned player's id (reusing the field *Long Grievous Siege* ba-40 introduced for `faction-siege` attacks — "detainment against hero companies"); minion-site mode stores the new `detainmentAgainstOvert: true` on {@link AutomaticAttack} ("detainment against overt companies" — detainment only when the defending company is racially overt per `isCovertCompany`, normal otherwise), consulted alongside `forceDetainment`/`detainmentAgainstPlayer` at every site auto-attack combat-initiation call in `reducer-site.ts`. Consumption: `manifestations.ts` `getActiveAutoAttacks` matches the constraint by `siteInstanceId` (not definition id, so only the targeted company's own copy of the site is affected — a turn-scoped, one-visit swap, unlike Vile Fumes' permanent `replace-automatic-attacks`) and returns the mirror site's `automaticAttacks`, each with `+value` prowess and the baked-in detainment field. The `company-site` play-condition also gained a `site.cardType` context key for the same disambiguation need, for future "at or moving to" style cards (Glance of Arien ba-19 precedent).

- New effect `tap-discard-in-play` + `cancel-attack` costless-skill/race-with-cost branch + `company-stat-modifier` player-wide target (Praise to Elbereth tw-305) — "For each of your characters in play that you choose to tap ... cancel one Nazgûl event or one Nazgûl attack against that character's company." Three independent pieces:
  1. **`cancel-attack`** gained a fall-through case in the from-hand emitter (`legal-actions/combat.ts` `cancelAttackActions`): when `cost` is present but neither `requiredSkill` nor `requiredRace` is set, `matchesRequirement` now returns `true` for every character in the defending company (previously it returned `false`, so this combination silently offered nothing — no shipped card had exercised it). The pre-existing "costless" fast path was tightened to also require `!cancelEffect.cost`, so a costed-but-ungated card no longer short-circuits into the free-cancel branch. tw-305: `cost: { "tap": "character" }`, `when: { "enemy.race": "ringwraith" }` — any character may tap to cancel a live Nazgûl attack.
  2. **`tap-discard-in-play`** (new top-level `CardEffect`, `filter: Condition`) — a repeatable "tap one of your own untapped characters, discard one untapped **opponent** in-play card matching `filter`" resource short-event ability, modeled on the `fetch-to-deck` loop-until-pass pattern rather than the chain: `handlePlayResourceShortEvent` (`reducer-events.ts`) now also collects any `tap-discard-in-play` effect into `interactiveEffects` (alongside `fetch-to-deck`), so the card moves to `cardsInPlay` and a `card-effect` pending effect is queued exactly like a fetch sub-flow. `pendingEffectLegalActions` (`legal-actions/index.ts`) dispatches to the new `tapDiscardInPlayLegalActions`, offering one `tap-discard-in-play` action per (own untapped character × opponent's untapped `cardsInPlay` card matching `filter`) pair plus `pass`; `applyTapDiscardInPlay` (`short-event-discard.ts`) resolves one pick — taps the character, moves the target straight to its owner's discard pile (`dropConstraintsSourcedBy` for any constraint it granted) — and leaves the pending effect queued so the same player can repeat; `pass` drains it via the existing generic `resolvePendingEffect` (discards the source card). Because every pick resolves synchronously inside this sub-flow with **no chain entry**, the opponent never gets a declare-priority window between a card being chosen as a pick's target and its discard — this is what realises "Nazgûl permanent-events that are targeted by Praise to Elbereth may not be tapped in response to its play" (the response window simply doesn't exist here), and the discard never triggers the target's own on-tap ability ("Nazgûl events discarded ... have no effect"). `heroResourceShortEventActions` (`legal-actions/long-event.ts`) gates the card's playability on there being either a live tap-discard target or an unconditional companion on-event effect (CoE 9.1), the `discardAllInPlay`-sweep precedent's shape. tw-305's targets are the Nine's `keywords: ["Nazgûl"]` permanent-event mode (River-horses tw-364 precedent for what "Nazgûl event" means in this data model).
  3. **`company-stat-modifier`** constraint kind gained a player-wide target: `collectCompanyStatModifierEffects` (`effects/resolver.ts`) now also matches `constraint.target.kind === 'player'` (via `findPlayerAndCompany`), alongside the existing company-target branch. Two independent apply sites read the new `target: "player"` value on the `add-constraint` apply (mirroring the existing `check-modifier`/`site-path-reduction` player-target precedent): `applyShortEventOnEntersPlay` (`reducer-events.ts`, the plain-resource-short-event inline-resolution path tw-305 actually uses) and `applyAddConstraintFromOnEvent`/`addDeclaredConstraint` (`chain-reducer.ts`, for a card whose on-event effects instead resolve at chain-resolution time, via the new `declaringPlayerTarget` option that bypasses the active-company lookup entirely). tw-305: `on-event: self-enters-play`, `when: { "inPlay": "Doors of Night" }`, `apply: { "type": "add-constraint", "constraint": "company-stat-modifier", "target": "player", "stat": "prowess", "value": 1, "scope": "turn" }` — "if Doors of Night is in play, characters gain +1 prowess until the end of the turn," scoped to every character the declaring player controls, not one company.

- `riddling-attempt` (Riddling Talk td-148) — new **two-stage** cancel-attack sibling to `flattery-cancel-attack`/`goodwill-cancel-attack`, where a successful roll only earns a follow-up guess rather than cancelling outright. Offering (`legal-actions/combat.ts`) mirrors `flattery-cancel-attack`: one `cancel-attack` action per company character, gated on the attacking creature's race matching a `thresholds` entry. Stage 1 (`chain-reducer.ts` enqueues a new `riddling-attempt` pending kind when the chain entry resolves un-negated; resolved by `applyRiddlingAttemptResolution`, `pending-reducers.ts`): roll 2d6 + `sageBonus` per Sage-skilled character in the maker's company (found via `findCharacterCompany`, not the whole `player.characters` set) + `hobbitBonus` per Hobbit-race character in that company; success (roll > threshold) enqueues a `riddling-guess` resolution instead of cancelling — failure resolves the chain entry normally. Stage 2 (`applyRiddlingGuessResolution`): the player names any card — one `riddling-guess` action per distinct name among `hazard-event`/`hazard-creature` definitions in `state.cardPool` (`riddlingGuessActions`, `legal-actions/pending.ts`), a blind guess since candidates aren't filtered by what's actually in the opponent's hand (that would leak information). On resolution the opponent's whole hand is revealed via `revealInstances` (`GameState.revealedInstances`); a name match cancels the attack (`resolveCancelAttackEntry`) and decreases `hazardLimitAtReveal` by `hazardLimitReduction` (M/H phase only, floored at 0), a mismatch leaves the attack in effect. Both stages route through the existing `PENDING_HANDLERS` registry (`pending-handlers.ts`), which force-checks every kind is paired at compile time.

- `followsAttackRaces` creature-keying restriction (Wolf-riders td-86) — a new `CreatureKeyRestriction` field for creatures whose *only* path into play is as a follow-up to a prior attack, rather than region/site keying. Matches when the target company has, during its **current M/H sub-phase**, already faced a creature-sourced hazard attack (played from hand — "not keyed to a site", i.e. not a site automatic-attack or on-guard reveal) by a creature of one of the listed races. Evaluated against `deriveFacedRaces(state, mhState.hazardsEncountered)` — moved from a chain-reducer.ts-local helper to a shared export in `reducer-utils.ts` so both the offering side (`findCreatureKeyingMatches`, `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) and the reducer-side validator (`checkCreatureKeying`, `mh-hazard-play.ts`) can consult it; `hazardsEncountered` only ever records creature-sourced attacks (`recordHazardEncountered`, `combat-finalize.ts`, gated on `combat.attackSource.type === 'creature'`), which is exactly "not keyed to a site" — no new state tracking was needed. The keying method recorded in `keyedBy.method` is `"follows-attack"`; `declaredKeyedBy.method === 'follows-attack'` falls through the existing `attackKeying`/`attackSiteKeyingTypes`/`attackKeyingRegionNames` ternaries in `chain-reducer.ts` to their empty-array branches, same as any other non-region/non-site-type method, so the resulting attack is correctly `attack.siteKeyed: false`. Used by Wolf-riders (td-86): "May be played following any Orc attack not keyed to a site" — `keyedTo: [{ "followsAttackRaces": ["orc"] }]`, no other keying entry (this creature has no ordinary region/site keying at all). Its second mode, "If played as a short-event, modify the prowess and strikes of a Wolf attack by +1" (CRF ruling: this paragraph's condition is independent of the first — playable any time there's a Wolf attack, not only as a follow-up), is a plain `modify-attack` (`fromHand: true`, `player: "attacker"`, `prowessModifier: 1`, `strikesModifier: 1`, `when: { "enemy.race": "wolf" }`) — no `creature-alt-event` wrapper needed since the card never sits in play as a permanent-event; `play-flag: playable-as-event` is retained for its declarative ½-creature deck-weight only.

- `keyedTo` `when` context field `hazardsEncountered` (Mûmak tw-66) — extends the `when`-context object built for creature-keying `when` gates (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts` `findCreatureKeyingMatches` and `mh-hazard-play.ts` `checkCreatureKeying`, previously just `inPlay` + `destinationSite.sitePath.*Count`) with `hazardsEncountered: mhState.hazardsEncountered` — the same per-company, current-M/H-sub-phase list of creature-sourced attack names that `followsAttackRaces` derives races from. This lets a **regionally-keyed** `keyedTo` entry additionally require a *specific named* creature to have already attacked the same company this turn, expressed with the existing generic `$includes` operator rather than a new dedicated field — `{ "regionNames": [...], "when": { "hazardsEncountered": { "$includes": "<name>" } } }` — because unlike `followsAttackRaces` (a standalone alternate keying method, OR'd with the card's other entries), this card's requirement is an AND: region match *and* companion-creature-faced, which only the `when` gate (evaluated before the entry's other fields) can express in one entry. Used by Mûmak (tw-66): "May also be played (on the same turn and on the same company as Corsairs of Umbar) keyed to Andrast, Anfalas, Belfalas, Lebennin; and at Ruins & Lairs [{R}] and Shadow-holds [{S}] in these regions." — `keyedTo: [{ "regionNames": ["Dagorlad", "Gorgoroth", "Horse Plains", "Ithilien", "Khand", "Nûrn", "Ûdun"] }, { "regionNames": ["Andrast", "Anfalas", "Belfalas", "Lebennin"], "when": { "hazardsEncountered": { "$includes": "Corsairs of Umbar" } } }]` (the "at sites/R&L/Shadow-holds in these regions" halves are the as-13/as-21/tw-90 precedent: region-name matching already covers every site in the region regardless of type, so no `siteTypes` restriction is added). "This card has no effect on a minion player" is the standard `play-restriction unplayable-when opponent.alignment: ringwraith` boilerplate (CRF 22 Card Effect Limitations list; td-89/tw-36/dm-47/dm-67/dm-72 precedent) — a hazard whose text says it has no effect on a minion player simply cannot be played against one at all, rather than being played-but-inert.
- `modify-attack` `fromHand: true` now correctly charges the company's hazard limit (CoE 8.12) for the **attacker's** own plain from-hand plays — a real gap found while certifying Wolf-riders (td-86): `modifyAttackActions` (`legal-actions/combat.ts`) and `handleModifyAttack`'s plain-hand-card branch (`combat-actions.ts`) previously had no hazard-limit gating or bookkeeping at all for this path (unlike the sibling `fromAltPermanentEvent` branch, which already charged one slot), even though Dragon's Desolation (tw-29) Mode A already documented `play-flag: no-hazard-limit` as meaningful for it. Fixed by mirroring the `fromAltPermanentEvent` shape: the legal-action offering now emits a non-viable `modify-attack` entry (reason `"Hazard limit reached"`) via `hazardLimitStatus` when the attacker's play would exceed the limit and the card lacks `no-hazard-limit`, and the handler increments `hazardsPlayedThisCompany` after removing the card from hand (both gated on `effect.player === 'attacker'` and `Phase.MovementHazard`, so a defender's from-hand `modify-attack` — a resource card reaction, not a hazard play — and site-phase on-guard reveals, which have no hazard-limit bookkeeping, are both unaffected). Verified against the existing fromHand-without-the-flag precedents (Black Vapour ba-14, FEAR! FIRE! FOES! as-29) and the flagged ones (Dragon's Desolation tw-29, Unabated in Malice ba-26) — all pre-existing card tests still pass unchanged.

- `burglary-attempt` (Burglary, td-103) — a site-phase alternative to facing a site's automatic-attacks, new territory (no prior "in lieu of combat" or "solo defender" precedent). New bespoke action pair `declare-burglary` (announce + tap character/site) and `burglary-attempt` (the roll), a new `burglary-attempt` pending-resolution kind registered in `PENDING_HANDLERS` (`pending-handlers.ts`), and two new `SitePhaseState` fields. Offering (`automaticAttacksActions`, `legal-actions/site.ts`): one `declare-burglary` action per untapped company character while `automaticAttacksResolved === 0` (no attack yet faced this slot) and no earlier burglary success/failure is in effect, gated on a hand card carrying the effect and the site having ≥1 active automatic-attack. Declaration (`handleSiteAutomaticAttacks`, `reducer-site.ts`): taps the character and the site (`siteNeverTaps` honored), discards the card, enqueues the `burglary-attempt` resolution — kept as its own pending kind (not the generic `dice-check`) so a future on-guard interaction (Half an Eye Open td-29: "-5" modifier when a burglary attempt is announced) has a natural hook. The roll (`burglaryAttemptRollActions`/`applyBurglaryAttemptResolution`) is 2d6 + `scoutBonus` (`Skill.Scout`) + `hobbitBonus` (`Race.Hobbit`) vs `threshold`, following the `riddling-attempt`/`flattery-attempt` bonus/need pattern. Success sets `autoAttacksSkipped: true` (the pre-existing Farmer Maggot as-48 "sequence abandoned" flag — `handleSiteAutomaticAttacks`'s `allAttacksDone` branch already treats it as "nothing left to face") and `burglaryItemUnlock: characterInstanceId`, which `playResourcesActions`/`handleSitePlayHeroResource` (`legal-actions/site.ts`/`reducer-site.ts`) consult to let that one already-tapped character receive one item normally playable at the site (one-shot, consumed on use) — the site is already tapped from the burglary attempt itself, so no double-tap accounting is needed. Failure sets `soloAutoAttackCharacterId: characterInstanceId`, threaded as `CombatState.soloDefenderInstanceId` into every automatic-attack combat built for the slot (`makeCombatState` calls in `handleSiteAutomaticAttacks`, including the race-duplicate/Incite-Defenders/No-Strangers-at-this-Time copies) and into the "each character faces one strike" pre-assignment. `assignStrikeActions` (`legal-actions/combat.ts`, both the `defender` and `attacker` assignment-phase branches) restricts the defending company to just that one character when `soloDefenderInstanceId` is set — no other company member, nor an ally hosted by one, can be assigned a strike; an ally hosted by the solo defender himself remains reachable via `findCompanyAllies` since it is "what he himself can provide" per CRF. On-guard creature combat is a separate code path from `handleSiteAutomaticAttacks` and is untouched, matching the CRF ruling that on-guard creatures are still faced by the whole company regardless of a burglary attempt's outcome. Both new `SitePhaseState` fields are explicitly reset to absent at every point a fresh per-company `SitePhaseState` is built.

- `transfer-item-free` `play-option` apply (Pledge of Conduct td-144) — a reactive corruption-check-window item transfer with no follow-up corruption check for the transfer itself, unlike the ordinary organization-phase `transfer-item` action (CoE 2.II.5, `handleTransferItem` in `reducer-organization.ts`). The `play-option` itself names no item or destination — `reactiveCorruptionCheckPlays` (`legal-actions/pending.ts`) special-cases `apply.type === 'transfer-item-free'`: instead of emitting one action per matching option (the `add-constraint` shape every other reactive corruption-check play uses), it enumerates one `play-short-event` action per (item borne by the checking character, other member of the same company) pair — mirroring `transferItemActions`' per-triple enumeration for the ordinary transfer — carrying the choice on two new `PlayShortEventAction` fields, `transferItemInstanceId`/`transferToCharacterId`. `handlePlayResourceShortEvent` (`reducer-events.ts`) dispatches the option inline (no chain, matching every other reactive corruption-check play): `removeAttachment`/`updateCharacter` move the item between the two characters' `items` arrays; no `enqueueCorruptionCheck` call follows. Works from every phase a corruption check can be pending in (M/H, organization, site) because all of them route `play-short-event` through the shared `dispatchShortEventByCardType` → `handlePlayResourceShortEvent` path, and `applyCorruptionCheckResolution` (`pending-reducers.ts`) already falls through (`return null`) for `play-short-event` so the per-phase reducer handles it while the pending resolution stays queued. Used by Pledge of Conduct (td-144): "Diplomat only. A character facing a corruption check in the diplomat's company may automatically transfer one item he bears to another character in his company. The item must be transferrable, and the new bearer must be able to bear it." — `play-target` character (`company.containsDiplomat`, the le-178 shape) + `play-option` (`when: pending.corruptionCheckTargetsMe`) → `transfer-item-free`. "Must be transferrable"/"new bearer must be able to bear it" are given the same weight as the ordinary `transfer-item` action already gives them (an `isItemCard` check; the engine has no per-item non-transferable flag or bearer-eligibility restriction yet, a pre-existing gap shared by both transfer paths, not specific to this card).
- `play-flag: untapped-site-required` / `tap-site-on-play` generalized to **short events** + `move` `revealToOpponent` (Far-sight tw-238) — the "sage taps himself and the site" shape (§218's `tap-site-on-play`/`tap-character-on-play` scaffold) previously only fired for permanent-event resolution (`resolvePermanentEvent`, `chain-reducer.ts`); Far-sight needed the identical site-tap cost on a **short** event whose only lasting effect is a one-shot deck fetch. Both play-flags now also gate/apply for short events played during the site phase: `untapped-site-required` is checked in `playResourceShortEventActions` (`legal-actions/organization.ts`, also reachable from the site phase per `legal-actions/site.ts`'s call with `currentPhase: 'site'`) against the active company's current site; the tap itself is applied by a new shared helper `applyTapSiteOnPlayFlag` (`reducer-utils.ts`, extracted from the inline block `resolvePermanentEvent` used) called from both `resolvePermanentEvent` and `handlePlayResourceShortEvent` (`reducer-events.ts`), right after the existing `play-target` character tap-cost application. Separately, the card-level `move` primitive gained a `revealToOpponent` field (previously only the internal `FetchToDeckEffect` pending-resolution shape carried it, for the hazard-only `fetch-agent-to-hand` dual-mode of Inner Cunning dm-68) — `moveToFetchToDeckPayload` (`reducer-move.ts`) now copies it onto the generated `FetchToDeckEffect`, so any card-level fetch-to-hand/deck `move` can declare "reveal to opponent" without a bespoke wrapper type. The sage's own tap cost, the interactive fetch-to-hand pick, and the "sage makes a corruption check" clause all reuse pre-existing primitives unchanged: `play-target` character (`sage` skill, `cost: { tap: "character" }`, the le-226 shape — untapped-only enforced by `eligiblePlayOptionTargets`'s `requiresUntapped`), `play-condition: site-has-resource` (`subtype: "information"`), and the Vilya (tw-358) `on-event: self-enters-play` → `enqueue-corruption-check` + interactive-fetch `postCorruptionCheck` deferral (`reducer-events.ts`) so the check fires after the pick (or pass) resolves, not up front. Used by Far-sight (tw-238): "Sage only. Playable during the site phase on an untapped sage at an untapped site where Information is playable. Tap the sage and the site. Search through your play deck and choose an item that you must reveal to your opponent. This item is placed in your hand and the play deck is reshuffled. The sage makes a corruption check." — `play-window` (`phase: site`) + `play-target` character (sage, tap cost) + `play-condition site-has-resource information` + `play-flag untapped-site-required` + `play-flag tap-site-on-play` + `move` (`select: target`, `from: [deck]`, `to: hand`, `shuffleAfter`, `revealToOpponent`, `filter: cardType hero-resource-item`) + `on-event self-enters-play → enqueue-corruption-check`.

- `play-condition site-path` on a **character-target** permanent event, and `on-event self-enters-play → set-character-status` for a permanent event attached to a character — two additions for "playable on <named character> while moving during his M/H phase if …" cards. (1) The character-target branch of `playPermanentEventActions` (`legal-actions/organization-events.ts`) now also honours a `play-condition` `requires: "site-path"` effect: for each candidate company, it requires that company to be the M/H phase's currently active *and moving* one (`state.phaseState.activeCompanyIndex` + `siteRevealed`, mirroring `buildPlayOptionContext`'s `companyMoving` derivation) and evaluates the condition against `regionTypeCounts(mhState.resolvedSitePath)` (the same `sitePath.*Count` context the hazard-side `checkSitePathCondition` uses) — previously this play-condition was only checked for company-target permanent events and hazard plays. A stationary or non-active company simply has no candidates (the condition context is only ever built for the genuinely-moving active company), so the gate degrades correctly outside M/H or before the company's path resolves. (2) `resolvePermanentEvent`'s `on-event: self-enters-play` handling (`chain-reducer.ts`) gained a `set-character-status` branch alongside the existing `heal-target-character`: with `target` absent/`"bearer"`/`"target-character"`, it sets the just-attached character's (`targetCharId`) status to `apply.status`, honouring an optional `when` evaluated against `{ target: { status } }` — lets a permanent event conditionally tap its own bearer on entry ("if untapped, tap him afterwards") without forcing an unconditional tap. Used by Herb-lore (dm-136): "Playable on Radagast while moving during his movement/hazard phase if there is at least one Wilderness [{w}] in his site path. If untapped, tap Radagast afterwards." — `play-target` character (`target.name: "Radagast"`) + `play-window` (`phase: "movement-hazard"`) + `play-condition site-path` (`sitePath.wildernessCount >= 1`) + `on-event self-enters-play → set-character-status` (`status: "tapped"`, `when: { "target.status": "untapped" }`). Its organization-phase ability, "Radagast can tap and discard this card to heal all characters in his company from wounded to untapped and to untap all tapped characters," needed no new primitive — a plain `grant-action` (`cost: { tap: "bearer", discard: "self" }`, `apply: set-character-status target: "company" status: "untapped"`, the ba-76 shape). Per the official ruling (Rules Digest 35, 1998), the company-wide untap catches Radagast himself too, even though he just tapped to pay the cost — the generic implementation (cost paid first, then the company-wide `apply` sets every member including the now-tapped bearer to untapped) reproduces this without any special-casing.
- `opponent-choose-tap-or-roll` (A Lie in Your Eyes as-23) — new hazard-short-event effect for "your opponent may either: tap X, tap Y, or let you roll" cards: a `tap-or-roll-choice` pending resolution (actor = the targeted character's controller) offers `choose-tap-or-roll` with `choice: "tap-character" | "tap-ally" | "roll"` (`tap-ally` cross-multiplied one action per **untapped** ally, omitted when none exist). Tapping resolves immediately (taps the target, marks the source chain entry resolved via the same `'target-character'` match `dice-check`'s `chain-entry` continuation already uses); "roll" enqueues a generic `dice-check` (roller = the card-player, `threshold` = the character's effective mind + the effect's `rollAddend`, `comparison: "gt"`, `onPass: { "type": "discard-character" }`) instead of rolling inline, reusing the P08 roll primitive rather than a bespoke one. New engine files/functions: `types/pending.ts` (`tap-or-roll-choice` kind), `types/actions-universal.ts` (`ChooseTapOrRollAction`), `chain-reducer.ts` (detection block mirroring `call-of-home-check`, does not mark the entry resolved), `legal-actions/pending.ts` (`tapOrRollChoiceActions`), `pending-reducers.ts` (`applyTapOrRollChoiceResolution`), registered in `pending-handlers.ts`. Two small supporting generalizations, both backward-compatible: the `dice-check` `discard-character` onPass/onFail branch verb (`applyDiceCheckBranch`) is now owner-agnostic (locates the target's actual controller instead of assuming the roller is the owner — matches the `eliminate-character`/`return-character-to-hand` branches), needed because the card-player rolls against the *opponent's* character; and the hazard character-targeting `play-target` filter context ("Character-targeting short events" block, `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) now also exposes `target.status`, so a hazard short-event can gate on "playable on an **untapped** character" the same way resource-side `play-target` already does. Used by A Lie in Your Eyes (as-23): "Playable on an untapped non-Ringwraith, non-Wizard character. Your opponent may either: tap the character, tap an ally the character controls, or choose for you to make a roll. If the result is greater than the character's mind plus 6, the character is discarded (along with all non-follower cards he controls)." — `play-target` character (`target.status: "untapped"`, non-wizard, non-ringwraith) + `opponent-choose-tap-or-roll` (`rollAddend: 6`).
- `protect-from-strike-assignment` — a short event played from hand during the defending player's `assign-strikes` sub-phase (`protectFromStrikeAssignmentActions`/`handleProtectFromStrikeAssignment`, `legal-actions/combat.ts`/`combat-actions.ts`): the chosen company member is added to `CombatState.protectedFromStrikeAssignment` and excluded from every strike-target list for the rest of the current attack. Eligibility is `requiredSkill` (a skill the *target* must have — Ruse le-225 mode B: "playable on a scout facing an attack") and/or a generic `filter` (`Condition`, evaluated per candidate against `{ target: {race, status, skills, name}, company: {hasShadowMagicUser} }` — the same shape organization-events.ts's play-target filter uses), combined by AND when both are present. An optional `corruptionCheck: { modifier, on: "shadow-magic-user" }` fires a corruption check as a side effect of playing the card, on the target's company's shadow-magic user (`companyShadowMagicUsers` in `reducer-utils.ts` — Ringwraith by race, or `skills` includes `"shadow-magic"` via `getEffectiveSkills`) rather than on the protected target: any qualifying Ringwraith exempts the whole company from the check (mirrors A Malady Without Healing le-159's caster rule in `reducer-events.ts`), otherwise the first non-Ringwraith user checks. The card discard now routes through `discardOrRecyclePlayedEvent` (previously a raw `discardPile` push — dead code for le-225, which isn't a magic card, but wrong for any magic-keyword card played this way) so Akhôrahil the Ringwraith's (le-51) magic-card recycling still applies. Used by Sojourn in Shadows (wh-49): "Playable before strikes are assigned on a character facing an attack in a shadow-magic using character's company. Target character cannot be assigned a strike from the attack. Unless he is a Ringwraith, the shadow-magic using character makes a corruption check modified by -4." — `filter: { "company.hasShadowMagicUser": true }` (no `requiredSkill`: any company member is a valid target, not just the shadow-magic user) + `corruptionCheck: { modifier: -4, on: "shadow-magic-user" }`.
- `protect-from-strike-assignment` `includeAllies`/`requireUntapped`/`tapTarget` (More Sense than You td-140) — three independent flags added for this card. `includeAllies` makes `protectFromStrikeAssignmentActions` also loop `findCompanyAllies` and offer each ally as a target (`requiredSkill`/`filter` are only evaluated against character candidates, not allies). `requireUntapped` skips any candidate — character or ally — whose `status` isn't `CardStatus.Untapped`. `tapTarget` makes `handleProtectFromStrikeAssignment` tap the resolved target as a side effect of playing the card: it resolves the target as a character (`defPlayer.characters[id]`) or falls back to `findAttachment(defPlayer, 'allies', id)`, then taps via `updateCharacter`/`updateAttachment` with a small generic `tap = (a) => ({ ...a, status: CardStatus.Tapped })` helper (mirrors the existing item-or-ally tap fallback at `handleDodgeStrike`). Fixed a latent deadlock this card exposed: protecting the sole character of a solo company (no allies) left both assignment phases with only `pass` as a legal action, and the pre-existing fizzle check in `handleCombatPass` (`reducer-combat.ts`) only covered "company has zero characters" or "zero strikes total" — not "company has characters but none are assignable." `handleCombatPass` now also re-evaluates the newly-exported `assignStrikeActions` as if it were the attacker's turn (the more permissive of the two phases, since it ignores tapped status) whenever `assignmentPhase` is `'defender'`/`'attacker'` and no strike has been assigned yet; if that yields only `pass`, the attack fizzles immediately (CoE-consistent with the card's French-print clarification: "if the company has only one character, the attack has no effect but is considered to have been faced"). Used by More Sense than You (td-140): "Playable before strikes are assigned on an untapped character or ally whose company is facing an attack. Tap target character or ally. He may not be assigned a strike from this attack." — `{ "type": "protect-from-strike-assignment", "includeAllies": true, "requireUntapped": true, "tapTarget": true }`.

- `skill-suppression` / `location-magic-restriction` — a pair of location-gated primitives for "at a site in <region>, or moving with <region> in his site path" clauses (distinct from `force-return-to-origin`/`tap-sites-in-play`'s **company**-scoped site-path context: these gate on a single **character**'s location, on demand, at any phase — not just at movement resolution). A character's location is resolved by the new `characterLocation` helper (`engine/reducer-utils.ts`): the current (arrived/stationary) site's containing region, via `siteRegionTypeOf` + the site definition's own `region` name, **plus** — only while that character's company is the *active mover* of an in-progress movement/hazard phase — `state.phaseState.resolvedSitePath`/`resolvedSitePathNames` (the engine does not persist a company's site path outside that window). `locationMatchesSpec(loc, { regionTypes?, regionNames? })` matches either fact against either field. Both effects carry `regionTypes?: RegionType[]`, `regionNames?: string[]`, and `noEffectOnMinion?: boolean` (the tw-36/dm-72 "no effect on a minion player" convention — checked against the *character's own* controller, not the carrying card's owner). `skill-suppression` (`skill: Skill`) is consulted inside `getEffectiveSkills` (`engine/effects/resolver.ts`) — filtering the final skill list — so every existing consumer (site `grant-action` costs like `sage-and-scout-in-company`/`sage-in-company`, `organization.ts`'s `sage-tap-ring-test`) respects it with no per-call-site change. `location-magic-restriction` (optional `keywords?: string[]`, default `["spell","sorcery","spirit-magic","shadow-magic","light-enchantment","ritual"]`) is enforced centrally by `applyLocationMagicRestriction` (new module `engine/location-magic-restriction.ts`), wired into `computeLegalActions` alongside `applyCardPlayProhibitions`: any *viable* evaluated action whose `cardInstanceId` resolves to a matching-keyword card, and which names an acting character via one of `characterId`/`scoutInstanceId`/`targetCharacterId`/`targetScoutInstanceId`, is turned into a `not-playable` entry when that character's location matches an active restriction — reaching `cancel-attack`/`cancel-influence` reactive spells and `play-target`-bound permanent-event rituals/light-enchantments alike, the same way `prohibit-card-play` reaches every play-from-hand window. `draw-modifier`'s resolver context (`mh-steps.ts`'s `transitionToDrawCards`) also gained a `player: { minion }` field (the moving company's own alignment) so a draw-modifier's `when` can use the same `player.minion` gate `force-return-to-origin`/`tap-sites-in-play` already exposed. Used by In the Heart of his Realm (dm-67): "Each company moving in a Dark-domain [{d}] draws one less card … (to no minimum). … any sage at a site in a Dark-domain [{d}] or Gorgoroth, or moving with a Dark-domain [{d}] or Gorgoroth in his site path, loses his sage skill. No character at a site in a Dark-domain [{d}] or Gorgoroth, or moving with a Dark-domain [{d}] or Gorgoroth in his site path, can use spells, light enchantments, or rituals. … This card has no effect on a minion player." — `draw-modifier` (`resource`, `-1`, `min: 0`, `appliesTo: "any-company"`, `when: { sitePath.darkCount >= 1, player.minion: false }`), `skill-suppression` (`"sage"`, `regionTypes: ["dark"]`, `regionNames: ["Gorgoroth"]`, `noEffectOnMinion`), `location-magic-restriction` (same location spec, `noEffectOnMinion`), plus the pre-existing `play-restriction unplayable-when opponent.alignment: "ringwraith"` and `on-event play-deck-exhausted` self-discard.
- `take-prisoner` (CoE 8.35/3.III) — marks a hazard permanent-event as a **hazard host**: played via `play-window { combat, resolve-strike }` + `play-target character` (filter on `attack.race`), a successful strike leaves the character unwounded but taken prisoner instead. `findTakePrisonerHazard`/`applyTakePrisoner`/`bindPrisoner` (`combat-hazard-play.ts`) draw the rescue site from `rescueSiteTypes` (an "or" list — multiple entries just widen the `.includes` gate, both at the `combatHazardPermanentPlays` playability check in `legal-actions/combat.ts` and at capture time) out of the hazard player's location deck, discard the prisoner's non-ring items (or, with `discardRings: true`, *all* items — an override of the CoE 3.III.3 default that lets a prisoner keep rings), revert followers to general influence, add a `character-is-prisoner` active constraint, and record a `HazardHost` in `state.hazardHosts`. Manual rescue (CoE 8.36) is generic site-phase machinery: `rescuablePrisonersAtSite`/`freePrisonersOfHost` (`reducer-utils.ts`/`reducer-site.ts`) offer `rescue-prisoner` once the company reaches the rescue site, sequence the host's fixed `rescueAttacks` (race/strikes/prowess) as a non-automatic combat, and on completion free the prisoner(s) and discard the (record-only) host card. An optional `untapBodyCheck: { modifier }` adds a periodic elimination risk: `enqueuePrisonerUntapBodyChecks`, called from every `enterUntapPhase` (`reducer-untap.ts`), scans the newly-active player's `character-is-prisoner` constraints and enqueues a generic `dice-check` (2d6 + `modifier` vs the prisoner's effective body, rolled by the host's owner per CoE 3.I.1, `onPass: eliminate-character`) for each prisoner whose host carries the field. The `eliminate-character` dice-check branch (`pending-reducers.ts`) now unconditionally calls the new `removePrisonerFromHost` (`reducer-utils.ts`) after eliminating — a no-op for a non-prisoner target, but for a prisoner it drops the stale `character-is-prisoner` constraint and the id from its host's prisoner list, discarding the (now-empty, record-only) host card so no instance is orphaned. The `autoRescue: { bodyCheckModifier, autoRescueThreshold }` field (dm-58) remains **data-only** — the "survive the body check, then roll to auto-escape" chain is not wired into the engine and is not covered by dm-58's test. Used by Flies and Spiders (dm-58): `rescueSiteTypes: ["ruins-and-lairs"]`, `rescueAttacks: [{ race: spider, strikes: 3, prowess: 9 }]`. Used by Spells of the Barrow-wights (dm-90): "Playable on a character facing an Undead strike. … taken prisoner at a Ruins & Lairs [{R}] or Shadow-hold [{S}]. Character must discard any rings along with his other items. At the start of each of his untap phases, make a body check for that character. Rescue-attack: Undead — 3 strikes with 8 prowess." — `rescueSiteTypes: ["ruins-and-lairs", "shadow-hold"]`, `rescueAttacks: [{ race: undead, strikes: 3, prowess: 8 }]`, `discardRings: true`, `untapBodyCheck: { modifier: 0 }`.
- `strike-shield` + `cancel-prisoner-taking` (Noble Hound dm-179) — `strike-shield` (`scope: "controlling-character"`, optional `alwaysCountsAsUntapped`) is enforced twice in `legal-actions/combat.ts`: the defender-phase ally offer OR-s `alwaysCountsAsUntapped` into the existing `alwaysUntapped` check so a tapped/wounded shield ally is still offered a strike, and `assignStrikeActions` withholds the controlling character from assignment while a `strike-shield` ally on their own company is still unassigned (checked for both the `defender` and `attacker` (automatic-attack) assignment sub-phases). `cancel-prisoner-taking` (`scope: "controlling-character"`) hooks the `take-prisoner`/Troll-purse prisoner outcome in `resolveStrikeCore` (`combat-strike.ts`): when a strike would take the struck character prisoner and `findCancelPrisonerTakingAlly` finds a qualifying ally among `charData.allies`, resolution **pauses** instead of calling `applyTakePrisoner`/`applyTakePrisonerAtSite` immediately — those draw a rescue site and add constraints that are not cleanly undoable — entering a new `cancel-prisoner-taking-choice` combat phase (`cancelPrisonerTakingOffer: { allyId }` on `CombatState`) rather than applying the effect provisionally. `cancelPrisonerTakingActions` (`legal-actions/combat.ts`) offers the defending player a `cancel-prisoner-taking` action (discard the ally) or `pass` (decline); the top-level reducer's phase-gated `pass` routing list (`reducer.ts`) had to add this phase alongside `trophy-offer` etc. so `pass` reaches the combat handler instead of falling through to M/H-phase handling. Accepting (`handleCancelPrisonerTaking`, `combat-actions.ts`) discards the ally via `removeAttachment(..., 'allies', ...)` and inverts the struck character (a plain wound — since the pause only ever fires for a non-detainment `'wounded'` result, `tapOnNonWounded`/`tapLowMindAfterStrike` are irrelevant: both are overwritten by/gated on the wound outcome, so the resumed path skips them entirely), then transitions directly to `body-check`/`bodyCheckTarget: "character"`, the same body check any other wounded character faces. Declining (`finalizeCombatFromCancelPrisonerTakingOffer`) recomputes `findTakePrisonerHazard`/`combat.trollPursePrisoner` (safe — nothing else can act while this phase is pending) and applies the prisoner outcome exactly as the unprotected path would. Used by Noble Hound (dm-179): "Playable at any tapped or untapped Border-hold. … Noble Hound must be assigned a strike before any strike can be assigned to its controlling character. If Noble Hound is tapped or wounded, treat it as though it were untapped for the purposes of assigning strikes. Discard Noble Hound to cancel any effect that would take its controlling character prisoner (does not protect other characters from being taken prisoner)." — `play-target site { siteType: "border-hold" }` (`requireTapped: false`), `strike-shield` (`alwaysCountsAsUntapped: true`), `cancel-prisoner-taking`.
- `hazard-limit-region-count` (Fair Sailing tw-232) — a new `add-constraint` kind for "the hazard limit for the target company decreases by one for every &lt;region type&gt; in its site path (to a minimum of two)" end-of-organization-phase cards. Unlike the existing `hazard-limit-modifier` (a flat delta baked in by `parseConstraintScope`/`applyShortEventOnEntersPlay` at play time), the reduction here scales with a region-type count that is only known once the target company's site path is actually resolved — which happens later, during that company's own movement/hazard phase, since starter-vs-region movement is chosen at `declare-path`, after the organization phase (where the card is played) has already ended. The card therefore installs a `scope: "turn"` constraint (`{ type: "hazard-limit-region-count", regionType, perCount, floor }`, target `company`) via the ordinary company-targeting `add-constraint` switch in `applyShortEventOnEntersPlay` (`reducer-events.ts`), reading `regionType`/`floor` (new `AddConstraintAction` fields) and reusing `value` for `perCount`. `snapshotHazardLimit` (`mh-steps.ts`) — which already threads a `pathNames` override past the stale `state.phaseState` at snapshot time (see as-18) — gained a parallel `pathRegionTypes` override (defaulting to `state.phaseState.resolvedSitePath`), and a second pass over `state.activeConstraints` counts occurrences of `regionType` in that path, applying `perCount * count` with the same floor semantics as `hazard-limit-environment` (a limit already at or below `floor` is left unchanged — the constraint only ever decreases, never raises to the floor). `applyOneConstraint` (`legal-actions/pending.ts`) passes the kind through untouched (`return base`), matching `hazard-limit-modifier`'s no-op — the constraint is consulted directly, not via broad legal-action filtering. Used by Fair Sailing (tw-232): "Playable at the end of the organization phase if target company plays a new site card. The hazard limit for the target company decreases by one for every Coastal Sea [{c}] in its site path (to a minimum of two). Cannot be duplicated on the same company." — `play-window` (`phase: "organization"`, `step: "end-of-org"`) + `play-target company` (`filter: { "company.moving": true }` — CoE 2.II.7's "declares movement by placing a site card" covers a company regardless of which movement type it later resolves via) + `on-event self-enters-play → add-constraint` (`constraint: "hazard-limit-region-count"`, `scope: "turn"`, `regionType: "coastal"`, `value: -1`, `floor: 2`) + `duplication-limit` (`scope: "company"`, `max: 1` — the `company`-scope dup check, previously only implemented for the character-scope end-of-org branch (Words of Menace and Deceit le-258 precedent), is now also checked in the end-of-org company-target branch of `playResourceShortEventActions`, `legal-actions/organization.ts`, against `state.activeConstraints` filtered by `sourceDefinitionId`/`target.companyId`). Fair Travels in Wilderness (tw-237) reuses this same primitive verbatim (`regionType: "wilderness"`) and is certified alongside it. The remaining "Fair Travels in Border-lands/Free-domains" siblings (tw-233/235) also reuse this primitive verbatim (just a different `regionType`) but are not yet certified; the "Fair Travels in Dark-domains/Shadow-lands" siblings (tw-234/236) use a different "at least one, doubled if Gates of Morning" shape and remain open.
- `hazard-limit-region-count` extended to hazard short events, live-during-M/H (Lost in Border-lands tw-51) — Fair Sailing's constraint kind (see above) previously worked only when installed by a *resource* short event before reveal, since `snapshotHazardLimit` folds it into `hazardLimitAtReveal` exactly once, at reveal time. A *hazard* short event with the same "hazard limit increases/decreases by N per <region type> in the site path" text is instead played **after** the target company's site path is already resolved (hazard cards are played during the target's own M/H phase, past `set-hazard-limit`), and hazard short events resolve through the chain's generic self-enters-play add-constraint path (`applyAddConstraintFromOnEvent`/`addDeclaredConstraint` in `chain-reducer.ts` → `buildConstraintKind` in `constraint-kind.ts`) rather than `applyShortEventOnEntersPlay` (`reducer-events.ts`, resource-only). `buildConstraintKind` gained `hazard-limit-modifier` and `hazard-limit-region-count` cases (previously only reachable from the resource-side switch) so a hazard short event can install either kind too. Consumption is the real fix: `effectiveHazardLimit`/`currentHazardLimit` (`hazard-limit.ts`) — the *live* limit check every hazard play/action gates on, as opposed to `snapshotHazardLimit`'s one-time bake — previously only walked `hazard-limit-modifier` constraints added after reveal; it now also walks post-reveal `hazard-limit-region-count` constraints, counting `regionType` occurrences against a new `resolvedSitePath` parameter (`currentHazardLimit` passes `mhState.resolvedSitePath`, always populated for the rest of the company's M/H phase once resolved) with the same `perCount * count`, floor-only-clamps-a-decrease semantics `snapshotHazardLimit` already used. The three additional `effectiveHazardLimit` call sites (`render-debug-panels.ts`, `render-player-names.ts`, the sim's `exposure.ts`) were updated to pass the site path through too, so displayed/AI-estimated limits stay accurate once such a constraint is live mid-phase. Used by Lost in Border-lands (tw-51/le-118): "Playable on a moving company. Its hazard limit increases by one for every Border-land [{b}] in its site path." — `play-condition` requires `site-path`, `{ destinationSiteType: { "$exists": true } }` (a stationary company never gets a `destinationSiteType`, per `checkSitePathCondition`'s docstring — this is the "playable on a *moving* company" gate, with no minimum-count clause unlike Lost at Sea tw-50) + `play-target company` + `on-event self-enters-play → add-constraint` (`constraint: "hazard-limit-region-count"`, `scope: "turn"`, `regionType: "border"`, `value: 1`, `floor: 0` — floor is inert for a positive `perCount`). The "Lost in Shadow-lands/the Wilderness" siblings (tw-54/55, le-120/121) reuse this primitive verbatim (just a different `regionType`) but are not yet certified.
- `hazard-limit-region-count` extended to hazard short events, live-during-M/H (Lost in Shadow-lands tw-54) — Fair Sailing's constraint kind (see above) previously worked only when installed by a *resource* short event before reveal, since `snapshotHazardLimit` folds it into `hazardLimitAtReveal` exactly once, at reveal time. A *hazard* short event with the same "hazard limit increases/decreases by N per &lt;region type&gt; in the site path" text is instead played **after** the target company's site path is already resolved (hazard cards are played during the target's own M/H phase, past `set-hazard-limit`), and hazard short events resolve through the chain's generic self-enters-play add-constraint path (`applyAddConstraintFromOnEvent`/`addDeclaredConstraint` in `chain-reducer.ts` → `buildConstraintKind` in `constraint-kind.ts`) rather than `applyShortEventOnEntersPlay` (`reducer-events.ts`, resource-only). `buildConstraintKind` gained `hazard-limit-modifier` and `hazard-limit-region-count` cases (previously only reachable from the resource-side switch) so a hazard short event can install either kind too. Consumption is the real fix: `effectiveHazardLimit`/`currentHazardLimit` (`hazard-limit.ts`) — the *live* limit check every hazard play/action gates on, as opposed to `snapshotHazardLimit`'s one-time bake — previously only walked `hazard-limit-modifier` constraints added after reveal; it now also walks post-reveal `hazard-limit-region-count` constraints, counting `regionType` occurrences against a new `resolvedSitePath` parameter (`currentHazardLimit` passes `mhState.resolvedSitePath`, always populated for the rest of the company's M/H phase once resolved) with the same `perCount * count`, floor-only-clamps-a-decrease semantics `snapshotHazardLimit` already used. The three additional `effectiveHazardLimit` call sites (`render-debug-panels.ts`, `render-player-names.ts`, the sim's `exposure.ts`) were updated to pass the site path through too, so displayed/AI-estimated limits stay accurate once such a constraint is live mid-phase. Used by Lost in Shadow-lands (tw-54/le-120): "Playable on a moving company. Its hazard limit increases by one for every Shadow-land [{s}] in its site path." — `play-condition` requires `site-path`, `{ destinationSiteType: { "$exists": true } }` (a stationary company never gets a `destinationSiteType`, per `checkSitePathCondition`'s docstring — this is the "playable on a *moving* company" gate, with no minimum-count clause unlike Lost at Sea tw-50) + `play-target company` + `on-event self-enters-play → add-constraint` (`constraint: "hazard-limit-region-count"`, `scope: "turn"`, `regionType: "shadow"`, `value: 1`, `floor: 0` — floor is inert for a positive `perCount`). The "Lost in Border-lands/the Wilderness" siblings (tw-51/55, le-118/121) reuse this primitive verbatim (just a different `regionType`) but are not yet certified.
- `character-tap-extra-mh-phase` (Carambor le-5) — the character-carried sibling of `ally-tap-extra-mh-phase` (Shadowfax tw-326): the bearer is a character in the company itself (not an attached ally), and there is no company-composition `condition` — any company containing an untapped bearer qualifies. At the end of a company's M/H phase, `advanceAfterCompanyMH` (`mh-hazard-play.ts`) calls the new `findCharacterTapExtraMHPhase`, which walks `company.characters` for an untapped one carrying the effect; a match routes to the new `character-tap-mh-offer` step instead of falling through to `finalizeCompanyMH`. `characterTapExtraMHOfferActions` (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) re-derives the same match and offers one `character-tap-extra-mh-phase` action per qualifying character plus `pass`; `handleCharacterTapExtraMHOffer` taps the character (`updateCharacter`) on accept and switches the step directly to the shared `extra-mh-move-offer`, threading the effect's optional `requiresDestinationSitePathIncludes` onto the new `MovementHazardPhaseState.extraMHMoveRequiresSitePathIncludes` field (cleared on either exit from that step, mirroring `extraMHMoveUnderDeeps`). `extraMHMoveDestinations` (shared by `grant-extra-mh-phase`/`ally-tap-extra-mh-phase`/this effect) gained an optional fourth parameter that filters candidates down to sites whose static `sitePath` includes at least one of the required region types. Used by Carambor (le-5): "May tap at the end of his company's movement/hazard phase to allow it to move to an additional site on the same turn. Another site card may be played and another movement/hazard phase immediately follows for his company. The new site path must contain at least one Wilderness [{w}]." — `requiresDestinationSitePathIncludes: ["wilderness"]`.

- `reveal-opponent-hand` grant-action apply + `discard-target-corruption-card` grant-action apply + `targets.scope: "own-hazard-corruption-cards"` + `player.inPlayNames` grant-action context field (Palantír of Amon Sûl tw-296) — three additions backing "look at your opponent's hand", "remove one corruption card from an Elf or a Wizard under your control", and gating a borrowed ability on a specific *other* named card being in play. `reveal-opponent-hand` is a type-only marker (`runGrantApply`, `grant-action-apply.ts`) that reveals the opponent's **whole** hand via `revealInstances` (cards stay in hand) — unlike `peek-opponent-hand` (le-203), no random subset and no discard cost. `own-hazard-corruption-cards` (`enumerateGrantActionTargets`, `legal-actions/organization.ts`) scans every one of the activating player's own characters (all companies) for attached `hazard-corruption` cards; uniquely among `targets` scopes, its `filter` matches the **bearer character's** definition rather than the candidate corruption card's own (corruption cards carry little else worth filtering on). `discard-target-corruption-card` (`runGrantApply`) discards the chosen candidate to its owner's discard pile via the standard `ownerOf` hazard-owner routing (`discard-target-character`'s hazard-handling precedent). `player.inPlayNames` (`buildGrantActionContext`) is the new `buildPlayerItemNamesInPlay(state, player.id)` (`recompute-derived.ts`) — the activating player's *own* in-play card names, **including character-borne items** (unlike the narrower, pre-existing `buildControllerInPlayNames`, which intentionally covers only bare `cardsInPlay` for its player-wide-effect-source use cases and so misses a Palantír borne as an item) and distinct from the pre-existing global `inPlay` (both players merged) — letting a `when` clause target one specific other card ("if either one is in play") regardless of whether that card sits in `cardsInPlay` or on a character. Card: "tap Palantír of Amon Sûl to look at your opponent's hand or tap it to use the abilities of either the Palantír of Annúminas or the Palantír of Elostirion if either one is in play. Bearer makes a corruption check." — modeled as three sibling `grant-action`s on Amon Sûl itself (not a lookup into the other cards' own `effects`): `amon-sul-peek-hand` (`reveal-opponent-hand` + `enqueue-corruption-check`), `amon-sul-use-annuminas` (gated on `player.inPlayNames $includes "Palantír of Annúminas"`, applying `enqueue-pending-fetch` with the new `sage-only` keyword filter — see below), `amon-sul-use-elostirion` (gated on `player.inPlayNames $includes "Palantír of Elostirion"` **and** `bearer.skills $includes sage`, applying `own-hazard-corruption-cards` + `discard-target-corruption-card`). The borrowed ability's own extra requirement ("if the bearer is a sage") evaluates against Amon Sûl's own bearer, per "with its bearer able to use a Palantír" already scoping the whole ability before the borrowing clause.
