New cross-card fetch-filter keyword `"sage-only"`, tagged on all 28 existing "Sage only"-text resource cards across TW/LE/TD/WH (`matchesDefinition` has no text-substring search, only field/keyword matching, so cards found this way need an explicit tag — same convention as the pre-existing `"environment"`/`"palantir"` tags), lets `enqueue-pending-fetch`'s `filter: { "keywords": { "$includes": "sage-only" } }` implement "search … for a 'sage only' card" for real, across the whole pool, not just already-certified cards. Palantír of Annúminas (tw-297) itself now carries the ability natively — the identical `grant-action` (tap self, `when: bearer.canUsePalantir`, same `enqueue-pending-fetch` apply) minus the Amon Sûl in-play gate, data only, no new engine work; the fetch has no sage gate on the bearer ("sage only" restricts the fetched card, not the user).

- `enqueue-pending-fetch` gained `unlockTappedSitePlay: boolean` (Dragon-lore td-108) — lets a fetch land its card in hand *and* immediately be playable at the site that gated the search, despite that site already being tapped (the ordinary item-play `siteIsTapped` gate otherwise blocks any item play once the site has been tapped this site phase). When set alongside `fetchTo: "hand"`, the flag rides the `enqueue-pending-fetch` apply into the `fetch-to-deck` pending effect (`grant-action-apply.ts`) and, once `handleFetchFromPile` (`reducer-utils.ts`) resolves the pick to hand, is recorded as `SitePhaseState.tappedSiteItemUnlock` (the fetched card's own instance ID) — the item-instance-keyed sibling of the pre-existing `burglaryItemUnlock` (Burglary td-103), which instead keys off a *character* instance. `playResourcesActions` (`legal-actions/site.ts`) treats `tappedSiteItemUnlock === cardInstanceId` as one more bypass of the "site is already tapped" item gate (alongside `itemAllowsTapped`/`burglaryUnlockActive`/etc.), scoped to that one fetched card only — every other hand item remains blocked at a tapped site. `attachItemAction`/the item-play reducer (`reducer-site.ts`) consumes (clears) the flag the moment that specific item instance is played, mirroring `burglaryItemUnlock`'s one-shot consumption; all three per-company-slot `SitePhaseState` resets (`select-company` entry, `finishDissolvedCompanySlot`, `advanceSiteToNextCompany`) clear it alongside `burglaryItemUnlock` so it never leaks across companies/slots. Used by Dragon-lore (td-108): "Playable on a sage during the site phase at an untapped site where Information is playable. Tap the sage and the site. During a site phase at a tapped Dragon's lair, tap bearer and discard Dragon-lore. Search your play deck and/or discard pile for any item playable at the Dragon's lair. This item may be immediately played with bearer's company." — `play-target` site (`playableResources $includes information`) + `play-target` character (untapped sage, cost tap character) + `play-flag`s `untapped-site-required`/`tap-site-on-play` + `grant-action` `dragon-lore-search-item` (`activeSitePhase: true`, `cost: { tap: "bearer", discard: "self" }`, `when: { "site.hasDragonAutoAttack": true, "site.isTapped": true }` — both fields already exposed by the existing grant-action `site` context, no new context work needed) whose `apply` is `enqueue-pending-fetch` (`fetchFrom: ["deck", "discard-pile"]`, `fetchTo: "hand"`, `playableAtBearerSite: true`, `unlockTappedSitePlay: true`, `filter: { cardType: "hero-resource-item" }`).

- `removal-protection` + `offer-corruption-removal-at-site` (Elf-song tw-223) — two new primitives for "while this long-event is in play" rules gated on a **dynamic**, location-based population (any character currently at a matching site, either player's), distinct from the existing `protect-from-removal` (23a, a turn-scoped constraint on one *selected* character). `removal-protection` (`siteTypes: SiteType[]`) is a continuous effect with no constraint to install or clear: `isSiteRemovalProtected` (new module `engine/removal-protection.ts`) resolves a candidate character's company/`currentSite` (`findPlayerAndCompany`, `reducer-utils.ts`) and its effective site type (`getEffectiveSiteType`, `effective.ts`), then scans both players' `cardsInPlay` for a matching clause — live, at every removal attempt, so a character gains/loses the protection exactly as it enters/leaves a matching site. Folded into `isCharacterRemovalProtected` alongside the pre-existing Tookish Blood constraint check, so both sources reach the same two central helpers (`returnCharacterToHand`/`discardCharacter`, `pending-reducers.ts`) that already back every removal path — dice-check returns, CoE 3.47 influence-overflow returns/discards, body-check discards — with no per-path wiring. `offer-corruption-removal-at-site` (`siteTypes: SiteType[]`, under `on-event: self-enters-play`) offers every character — either player's — at a matching site and bearing ≥1 corruption card (`cardType: "hazard-corruption"` or the `"corruption"` keyword) a one-time "may remove one corruption card" choice: one new `remove-corruption-offer` pending-resolution kind per eligible character (registered in `PENDING_HANDLERS`; legal actions in `legal-actions/pending.ts` `removeCorruptionOfferActions`, apply in `pending-reducers.ts` `applyRemoveCorruptionOfferResolution`, mirroring `transfer-returned-item`'s always-offered-decline shape), removal routing the chosen corruption card to its own owner's discard pile exactly like the pre-existing `discard-target-corruption-card` grant-action apply. Dispatched from a new shared helper, `applyOfferCorruptionRemovalOnResolve` (`chain-reducer.ts`), called from **both** `resolveLongEvent` (Elf-song's own resolution path — long-event resource cards resolve via a separate, simpler function that does not run the generic self-enters-play loop) and the generic `resolvePermanentEvent` self-enters-play loop, so any future permanent-event carrying the same clause is covered too. Used by Elf-song (tw-223): "When Elf-song comes into play, each character at a Haven [{H}] may immediately remove one corruption card. While Elf-song is in play, no character at a Haven may be discarded or returned to its owner's hand for any reason." Because the central removal helpers back every removal path, the protection also reproduces the CRF-22 ruling "Elf-song will effectively stop influence attempts against characters" (the CoE 3.47 return-to-hand path) with no extra wiring, and "when Elf-song saves a character from being discarded it also saves that character's items" falls out for free since a blocked removal is a full no-op — the character's items are never touched.

- `SitePhaseState.allyOrFactionPlayedAtSite` + item-branch `active-company` play-condition + `no-transfer`/`no-store` play-flags (Ent-draughts tw-227) — three additions for "playable at X in addition to an ally or faction successfully played at X this turn" bonus-item cards, and for items whose text overrides the default transfer/storage rules. `allyOrFactionPlayedAtSite` (`types/state-phases.ts`, alongside the `uniqueHeroFactionPlayedAtFreeHold`/`prisonersRescuedAtDolGuldurThisSitePhase` precedents) is set on the active company's `SitePhaseState` whenever an ally attaches in `handleSitePlayHeroResource` (`isAlly` branch) or a faction's influence attempt succeeds in the influence-attempt chain resolution (`reducer-site.ts`) — both already resolve against the active company's current site, so no extra site lookup is needed. The `active-company` `play-condition` (previously checked only for permanent events and factions in `legal-actions/site.ts`) is now also evaluated for **items**: a new check ahead of the per-character bearer loop reads `findPlayConditionEffect(itemDef, 'active-company')` and builds the context via `buildActiveCompanyContext`, which gained an optional fourth `extra` parameter (`legal-actions/organization.ts`) merging caller-supplied fields into `company` — here, `{ allyOrFactionPlayedAtSite: siteState.allyOrFactionPlayedAtSite ?? false }`, a `SitePhaseState` flag the shared builder has no access to on its own. Because the qualifying ally/faction play normally taps the site itself, the item also needs `item-play-site`'s existing `allowTapped: true` (Blasting Fire wh-51 precedent) to remain playable "in addition to" it. `no-transfer`/`no-store` are two new closed `PlayFlag` values (`types/effects.ts`): `no-transfer` is checked in `transferItemActions` (`legal-actions/organization-companies.ts`) via `hasPlayFlag`, skipping the item entirely, with a matching reducer-level backstop in `handleTransferItem` (`reducer-organization.ts`); `no-store` is checked in `storeItemActions` (same file) by overriding the computed `isStorable` back to `false` when the flag is present — the item-flag-driven counterpart to The One Ring's hardcoded `the-one-ring` keyword exception — with a matching backstop in `handleStoreItem`. Used by Ent-draughts (tw-227): "Only playable at Wellinghall in addition to an ally or faction that has been successfully played at Wellinghall this turn. +1 to prowess. This item may not be stolen, transferred, or stored. Cannot be duplicated on a given character." — `item-play-site` (`sites: ["Wellinghall"]`, `allowTapped: true`) + `play-condition` (`requires: "active-company"`, `condition: { "company.allyOrFactionPlayedAtSite": true }`) + `play-flag`s `no-transfer`/`no-store` + `duplication-limit` (`scope: "character"`, `max: 1`); the +1 prowess is the plain structural `prowessModifier` field (no competing `stat-modifier` effect on the card, so `recompute-derived.ts`'s legacy-field fallback applies it). "May not be stolen" has no engine counterpart to gate — no mechanic in the codebase moves an item between players without the owner's consent — so it is satisfied by the absence of any such mechanism, the same way a card's "cannot be played by X" clause needs no code when X cannot otherwise happen.

- `sacrifice-of-form` (Sacrifice of Form tw-321) — a from-hand Wizard-only combat permanent-event playable after strikes are assigned against a company containing the player's Wizard avatar, before any strike of that attack resolves (`combat.phase` is `choose-strike-order`, or `resolve-strike` with every `strikeAssignment` still unresolved), never against a `company-attack`, and never a second time for the same Wizard (`sacrificeOfFormActions`, `legal-actions/combat.ts`, checks no in-play card already carries `sacrificeOfFormCharacterInstanceId` for that Wizard's instance). `handleSacrificeOfForm` (`combat-actions.ts`) moves the card into the controller's `cardsInPlay` (stamped `sacrificeOfFormCharacterInstanceId`, not yet `attachedTo`), sets `CombatState.forcedStrikeDefeat` with `forcedDefeatBodyCheckModifier` raised by `+3` — reusing Liquid Fire (wh-52)'s mechanism so every remaining strike of the attack auto-defeats with a `+3` creature-body-check bonus — and records `pendingSacrificeOfForm` (host + Wizard instance IDs). The Wizard is **not** discarded immediately (his `CharacterInPlay` data must stay available for any other strikes of the same attack still to resolve, per the CRF ruling that he "faces any effects of a failed strike that was assigned to him", e.g. Dragon's Blood); instead a new module, `engine/sacrifice-of-form.ts`, hooks two sweeps into `postReduce` (`reducer.ts`) via the same prev/next `combat` diff `enqueuePostAttackPlayOffers` uses to detect "an attack just ended" regardless of teardown path (strikes resolved, trophy taken/declined, tidings queue exhausted, …): `sweepSacrificeOfForm` discards the Wizard (allies discarded/returned-to-hand via the existing `partitionLeavingAllies`, attached hazards returned to their own owners, followers dispersed to general influence via `freeOrDiscardFollowers` — not discarded, per CRF) once the attack concludes, places his items "off to the side" with the host via `placeCardSetAside` (MEAS §1, `set-aside.ts`) with `keepOnHostRemoval: true` (items stay in play even if the host is later discarded), and sets `PlayerState.wizardSacrificed` (once, never cleared — CoE 2.II.2.1.1 bars revealing a *different* avatar for the rest of the game, not just until the same one replays, unlike the analogous but temporary `ringwraithReturnedToHand`). `sweepSacrificeOfFormReturn` watches for the same Wizard's instance ID newly appearing in `characters` while some in-play host still names it with no `attachedTo`; it reattaches the host and moves every item from its `setAside` list back onto the Wizard's `items[]`. Because `collectCharacterEffects` (`effects/resolver.ts`) has no generic pathway that turns a plain `attachedTo`-attached permanent-event's own `effects` into bearer stat modifiers (unlike `attachedToItem`, which does), the host's `+1 prowess/body/direct-influence` `stat-modifier` effects are synthesised at reattach time into `until-cleared` `character-stat-modifier` active constraints on him — the same delivery mechanism opposed-roll outcomes and Vilya-style bonuses already use. "You may not play a different Wizard" is a new pair of `CHARACTER_PLAY_RULES` gates (`rules/definitions/character-play.ts`) reading `PlayerState.wizardSacrificed`, mirroring the existing Ringwraith-return-to-hand gates; "your opponent may not play the Wizard you sacrificed" is covered by the same `wizardSacrificed` field read on the opponent side, though ordinary ownership invariants (a player can never play a card from another player's discard pile) already make that clause unreachable in practice — the gate exists for symmetry with the ruling text. Used by Sacrifice of Form (tw-321): "Spell. Wizard only. All of the strikes from one attack against your Wizard's company fail; +3 to any body checks made to determine if the attack is defeated. Discard the Wizard … Place any items he controls under this card and keep these off to the side … If the Wizard is put back into play, return his items to him and place Sacrifice of Form with him. Wizard receives +1 to his prowess, body, and direct influence. Cannot be duplicated on a given Wizard. Cannot be used in company vs. company combat. After Sacrifice of Form is played, you may not play a different Wizard and your opponent may not play the Wizard you sacrificed. This card is played after strikes are assigned."

- `tap-at-site` + `reattach-to-item` + `activeWhileAttachedToItem` + grant-action `self`/`site.keywords` context (Map to Mithril td-133) — a "tap this card once bearer is ever at a named site (permanently); once tapped and at a race-flavor site, move it onto a chosen item mid-game and only then grant a stat bonus" shape. `tap-at-site` (`siteNames: string[]`) is a level-triggered passive check on a resource permanent-event sitting as its bearer's own `items` entry: the new `sweepTapAtSiteItems` `postReduce` sweep (`reducer-utils.ts`, wired into the sweep chain in `reducer.ts`) re-runs after every action and taps a matching untapped item whenever its bearer's company's current site name is in `siteNames` — fires on arrival, on staying put, and even if the company was already there when the card was played, unlike an arrival-edge trigger (`company-arrives-at-site`, which only fires on genuine M/H movement and only for bare `cardsInPlay`/short-events, not character-borne items). Paired with the pre-existing `play-flag: "no-auto-untap"`, extended in this certification to also apply to items borne by a character (`reducer-untap.ts`'s per-item untap loop now checks `hasPlayFlag(itemDef, 'no-auto-untap')` exactly as the top-level `cardsInPlay` loop already did) so the tap is permanent. The later re-parenting ability is a `grant-action` gated on two new `buildGrantActionContext` (`legal-actions/organization.ts`) fields: `self.status` (the tapped/untapped/inverted status of the specific card instance carrying the grant-action — an item/hazard/ally, or the bearer's own card — resolved from `sourceInstanceId` against `char.items`/`char.hazards`/`char.allies`/`char.instanceId`, for abilities gated on their own source card's status rather than the bearer's) and `site.keywords` (the current site's DSL `keywords` array, added alongside the existing `type`/`region`/`isTapped`/etc. site fields, letting a grant-action gate on a race-flavor site tag rather than only the formal `siteType` — backs the new `dwarf-hold` keyword, tagged onto Blue Mountain Dwarf-hold tw-377 and Iron Hill Dwarf-hold tw-403). The `targets.scope: "company-items"` enumeration (pre-existing, Gandalf's `test-gold-ring`) supplies the chosen item's instance id; the new `reattach-to-item` `TriggeredAction` verb (`runGrantApply`, `grant-action-apply.ts`) re-parents the source card from the bearer's `items` onto that item's `attachedToItem` binding — the same shape Barrow-blade (dm-119) gets at play time, applied here mid-game — with no card instance lost (it simply changes zones, keeping its current tapped status). The new `activeWhileAttachedToItem` `stat-modifier` flag mirrors `activeWhileStored`'s dormant-until-state-reached shape: `collectCharacterEffects`'s own-items loop (`effects/resolver.ts`) skips a flagged modifier so it never leaks onto the bearer while the card merely sits as his own item; the pre-existing item-attached-events scan (unconditional, Barrow-blade's path) picks it up once the card is genuinely bound via `attachedToItem`. Used by Map to Mithril (td-133): "Playable on a Dwarf during the site phase at a site at which Information is playable. Tap the Dwarf and site. Tap Map to Mithril if bearer is ever at Moria; this card never untaps. If Map to Mithril is at a Dwarf-hold and it is tapped, the bearer may tap himself and place this card with a non-unique weapon in his company. This gives the weapon a +3 prowess bonus."

- `targets.scope: "company-characters"` (+ `enqueue-corruption-check target: "target-character"`) — a grant-action `targets` scope enumerating the bearer's own company (including the bearer), skipping already-untapped members, optionally narrowed by `filter` against each candidate's card definition (`enumerateGrantActionTargets`, `legal-actions/organization.ts`; wired into the item-borne grant-action loop's generic per-candidate fallback alongside the existing character-definition loop's fallback). Companion change: `enqueue-corruption-check` gained `target: "target-character"` (`grant-action-apply.ts`), reading the activation's chosen `targetCardId` instead of the activating (bearer) character — so a grant-action can force a *different* company member's corruption check than the one who paid the tap cost. Used by The Arkenstone (tw-341): "may be tapped to untap a Dwarf character in the same company, but target Dwarf must make a corruption check modified by -2" — `targets: { scope: "company-characters", filter: { race: "dwarf" } }` + `apply: sequence[set-character-status target-character untapped, enqueue-corruption-check target-character modifier -2]`. The card's unconditional "+3 direct influence against Dwarves and Dwarf factions" reuses the Thráin II (td-94) two-entry `stat-modifier` shape (`reason: influence-check` / `target.race: dwarf` and `reason: faction-influence-check` / `faction.race: dwarf`), and its bearer-conditional "5 MP / 4 CP if held by a Dwarf" reuses the Durin's Axe (tw-212) `mp-modifier` + `stat-modifier corruption-points` `when: { bearer.race: dwarf }` shape — no new support needed for either. New field **`excludeBearer`** (Waybread td-165) drops the bearer from the enumerated `"company-characters"` candidates, for abilities that untap the bearer directly (`target: "bearer"`) and separately let the player choose one *other* company-mate to untap too. Used by Waybread: "Discard to untap bearer or bearer and one other character in his company" is three sibling `grant-action`s all costed `{ discard: "self" }` — `untap-bearer` (`when: { bearer.status: "tapped" }`, Cram td-105 precedent) for the bearer-only mode, and `untap-bearer-and-company-character` (`targets: { scope: "company-characters", excludeBearer: true }`, `apply: sequence[set-character-status bearer untapped, set-character-status target-character untapped]`) for the two-character mode. Because all three modes (plus the shared Cram `extra-region-movement` clause for "additional region card") share the one-shot `discard: "self"` cost against the same card instance, activating any one of them removes the card and naturally forecloses the others — no `oncePerTurn` lock or same-action-name discrimination needed, unlike the *repeatable* "A or B" pattern (Keys to the White Towers wh-89).

- `ResolverContext.bearer.naturalSkills` (Magic Ring of Courage tw-271) — a new field alongside the effective-stats context's `bearer.skills`, for cards that both `grant-skill` a skill and condition a `stat-modifier` bonus on the bearer *already* having it. `bearer.skills` in that context is the merged set (printed + every item grant, including the modifier's own card's `grant-skill` — see the Mechanical Bow wh-53 precedent that motivated the merge), so a self-referential "if already a warrior" check against `bearer.skills` would trivially always pass once the granting card is in play. `buildEffectiveStatsContext` (`recompute-derived.ts`) now also sets `naturalSkills: charDef.skills` (printed skills only, untouched by any grant) alongside the merged `skills` on the same `charInfo` object, mirroring the existing `requiresNaturalSkill` convention on `item-slot-modifier` (Swordmaster tw-498) but as a general condition-context field usable by any `stat-modifier` `when`. Used by Magic Ring of Courage (tw-271): "Gives the bearer warrior skill. If the bearer is already a warrior, he gets +2 to prowess." — `grant-skill` (`warrior`) + `stat-modifier` (`prowess`, `+2`, `when: { "bearer.naturalSkills": { "$includes": "warrior" } }`) + `duplication-limit` (`scope: character`, `max: 1`). Also fixed a missing `magic-ring` keyword on tw-271 (it only carried `ring`, so the gold-ring test's `ring-play-offer` path — which matches a hand card's `keywords` against the rolled `RingCategory` — could never have offered it; Magic Ring of Stealth tw-274 already carried both keywords).

- `cancel-chain-entry` gained an optional `threshold` (Wrath of the West le-151) — gates the existing Ire-of-the-East-style `select: "target"` cancel behind a 2d6 roll instead of applying it unconditionally. `resolveEntry` (`chain-reducer.ts`) checks the resolving entry's own card for a `select: "target"` `cancel-chain-entry` apply carrying a numeric `threshold`; when present it rolls 2d6 (honouring `cheatRollTotal`), emits a `dice-roll` {@link GameEffect}, and only calls the existing `resolveEnvironmentCancel` negation when the total is at or above `threshold` — a failed roll leaves the target chain entry untouched and this card still discards normally. (Required hoisting the function-local `resolveEffects` accumulator earlier in `resolveEntry` so the roll effect can be collected before the block that previously declared it.) Used by Wrath of the West (le-151): "Playable on a minion resource short-event declared earlier in the same chain of effects. Make a roll—if the result is greater than 6, the event is canceled and discarded" — `filter: { target.cardType: "minion-resource-event", target.eventType: "short" }` (no `declaredBy.alignment` restriction, unlike wh-24) + `threshold: 7`, no `removeFromGame`.

- `play-target` character filter context field `company.destinationSiteRegionType` (Secret Entrance tw-324) — the {@link RegionType} of the region containing the candidate's company's **declared destination site**, resolved via the existing `siteRegionTypeOf` (`reducer-utils.ts`) off `charCompany.destinationSite`'s definition; `null` when no destination is declared. Populated in `buildPlayOptionContext` (`legal-actions/organization.ts`), so it is available during the **organization** phase as soon as `plan-movement` sets a destination — unlike `destinationRegionTypes` (the M/H-only array of *traversed* regions), it needs no resolved travel path. Reuses the shipped `no-creatures-keyed-to-site` constraint kind (Crack in the Wall le-177) with no `unlessSiteRegionType` gate: "no hazard creatures keyed to the site may be played on the company" drops only site-keyed hazard-creature plays against the protected company, leaving region-keyed plays of the same creature legal. Used by Secret Entrance (tw-324): "Sage only. Playable only at the end of the organization phase; may not be played on a company moving to a site in a Dark-domain [{d}]. Tap a sage in the company, and no hazard creatures keyed to the site may be played on the company." — `play-window` organization/`end-of-org`, `play-target` character (`filter: { "$and": [ { "target.skills": { "$includes": "sage" } }, { "target.status": "untapped" }, { "company.destinationSiteRegionType": { "$ne": "dark" } } ] }`, `cost: { tap: "character" }`), `on-event self-enters-play` → `add-constraint no-creatures-keyed-to-site` (`scope: turn`, target `target-company`). (Corrects a prior data error: the card previously used the broader `no-creature-hazards-on-company` — Stealth's "no creature hazards" — which over-blocks region-keyed creature plays the printed text does not restrict; The Way is Shut's dm-98 test updated to match.)

- `combat-strike-effect` (Thief tw-102, Pick-pocket tw-79) — self-bound creature version of the agent-attack `strikeEffect: "discard-item"` precedent: a successful strike does not wound the defending character; instead an item must be discarded (defender's choice). Field: `strikeEffect`, either `"discard-item"` (Thief) or `"discard-item-character"` (Pick-pocket). `initiateCreatureCombat` (`chain-reducer.ts`) reads the creature's own `combat-strike-effect` entry and threads it onto `CombatState.strikeEffect`, reusing the generic `strikeEffect` resolution path in `combat-strike.ts` shared with the agent-attack modifier (Taladhan dm-25) and `tap-agent-at-site` (An Article Missing dm-43): a wounding strike is rewritten to `'success'` (no body check) and combat opens the `discard-item-from-company` phase. The item pool built there is the only place the two values diverge: `"discard-item"` collects every item held anywhere in the whole defending company, matching "an item held by the defending company"; `"discard-item-character"` collects only `strike.characterId`'s own items (`companyCharIds = [strike.characterId]` instead of `company.characters`), matching "an item the defending character bears". Everything downstream — the `discard-item-from-company` legal actions (`discardItemFromCompanyActions`) and the `handleDiscardItemFromCompany` reducer (removes the chosen item via `removeAttachment`, scans all of the defender's characters regardless of how the pool was scoped) — is unchanged and scope-agnostic; only the pool passed in as `discardItemOptions` differs. Detainment attacks never trigger either variant (inherited from the shared path). Pick-pocket (tw-79/tw-80) additionally carries the plain `combat-attacker-chooses-defenders` rule ("Attacker chooses defending characters"). Used by Thief (tw-102): "One strike. For each successful strike, an item held by the defending company must be discarded (defender's choice); the defending character is not harmed." Used by Pick-pocket (tw-79): "Men. One strike. Attacker chooses defending characters. For each successful strike, an item the defending character bears must be discarded (defender's choice); he is not harmed."
- Pure reuse, second printing of a certified card (Barrow-wight tw-14) — a distinct pool entry (different card number, identical text/stats) for a non-unique creature already certified under another ID (Barrow-wight tw-015). No engine change: the sole rule, "each character wounded by Barrow-wight makes a corruption check modified by -2," is the shipped `on-event: character-wounded-by-self` → `force-check` corruption (`modifier: -2`) `target: "wounded-character"` primitive (le-353 Barrow-downs precedent). Own card test (`tw-14.test.ts`) mirrors `tw-015.test.ts` with a locally-declared `CardDefinitionId` constant rather than reusing `BARROW_WIGHT` (which is bound to tw-015).
- `FactionCardBase.noOpponentInfluence` — a structural (non-effect) faction-data field, sibling to the existing `requiredInfluencerName`, that suppresses **every** engine route by which an opponent can target the card with a re-influence attempt once it is in play, not just the primary one. Three legal-action generators enumerate an opponent's in-play factions as influence targets, and all three now skip a faction with this field set, before computing influencers/candidates: the CoE rule 8.3 site-phase re-influence loop (`opponentInfluenceActions`, `legal-actions/site.ts` — no `opponent-influence-attempt`, plain or identical-card-reveal, is offered); the rule-10.14 agent-tap-influence faction branch (`agentInfluenceActions`, `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`); and Twisted Tales (dm-96)'s bespoke `agent-tap-faction-influence` target enumeration (also `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`). Used by Army of the Dead (tw-193): "Unique. Playable at Vale of Erech. May only be played by Aragorn II on the same turn that he plays Paths of the Dead. May not be influenced by an opponent." — combines `requiredInfluencerName: "Aragorn II"` (only Aragorn II may bring it into play), a `play-condition` `requires: 'active-company'` on `company.specialMovement: "paths-of-the-dead"` (only playable the turn Paths of the Dead tw-302 grants that special movement), and the new `noOpponentInfluence: true` (protects it from being influenced away afterward, through any of the three routes).
- `fw-kill-mp-full` carried by a bare `cardsInPlay` permanent-event, not just a character (A Merrier World wh-59) — `playerHasKillMpExemption` (`reducer-utils.ts`), previously scanning only `player.characters` (Alatar wh-1, a character card), now additionally scans `player.cardsInPlay` for the same effect, so a stage resource-event sitting bare in play (never attached to a bearer) grants the MEWH §4 kill-MP exemption just as well as a character carrying it. No new effect type or apply logic — same exemption, one more place it can be sourced from. Used by A Merrier World (wh-59): "Playable if you have more than 7 stage points. Hazards your companies defeat (for which you otherwise get 1 MP) are each worth full kill marshalling points. Cannot be duplicated by a given player." — `stage-points` (`value: 2`), `play-condition` `player-state` (`player.stagePoints $gt 7`, the shipped Gatherer of Loyalties wh-70 / A Strident Spawn wh-61 primitive), `fw-kill-mp-full`, `duplication-limit` `scope: player` `max: 1`.
- `check-modifier` `onSuccess: "draw-card"` (Lordly Presence tw-267) — the success-side mirror of The Dark Power's `onFailure: "shuffle-faction-into-deck"`. The `add-constraint check-modifier` apply may carry `onSuccess: "draw-card"` (threaded through `chain-reducer.ts` onto `constraint.kind.onSuccess`, `types/effects.ts` / `types/pending.ts`); when the consuming faction-influence roll **succeeds**, `resolveInfluenceAttemptRoll` (`reducer-site.ts`) draws one card for the influencer's controller via `drawCardsExhausting` (the same exhaust-and-reshuffle helper `draw-cards` uses) before the `successful-influence-attempt` triggers fire. Failure is unchanged (faction discarded as usual). The play-target uses a skill filter (the Muster shape — `target.skills $includes diplomat`), not `target.isInfluencing`, since the card names the skill rather than pinning to the live attempt by omission. Used by Lordly Presence (tw-267): "Diplomat only. +5 to an influence check against a faction. If the influence check is successful, draw a card." — `play-target` character (`target.skills $includes "diplomat"`) + `play-option` add-constraint check-modifier influence +5, scope `until-cleared`, `onSuccess: "draw-card"`.
- `set-company-special-movement` gained a third mode, `"belegaer"` (Belegaer td-100), alongside `'gwaihir'`/`'paths-of-the-dead'` — but unlike those two, it does *not* reuse their "no path traversed" handling, since the card explicitly states a fictional site path. `play-target` character filter context field `company.siteRegion` (`buildPlayOptionContext`, `legal-actions/organization.ts`, alongside the existing `company.siteName`/`company.siteType`) exposes the origin site's region name so the card's own filter can gate on it via `$in` against a fixed coastal-region list. `organization-companies.ts` `planMovementActions` hardcodes the same region list (`BELEGAER_REGIONS`) to filter plan-movement destinations once `specialMovement === 'belegaer'` — any site in the region list is offered regardless of region adjacency (the Gwaihir/Paths-of-the-Dead precedent for hardcoding special-movement destination logic per card). `mh-steps.ts`'s `declare-path` handler special-cases `'belegaer'` to set `resolvedSitePath` to three `RegionType.Coastal` entries instead of leaving it empty, so hazard-creature keying and other region-type consumers see the crossing as three coastal-sea regions. `snapshotHazardLimit` (`mh-steps.ts`) applies a flat `-2` floored at `2` for `'belegaer'`, applied after every other modifier (same position as the Going Ever Under Dark ba-37 movement-restriction reduction). Used by Belegaer (td-100): "Playable during organization phase on a company moving without region cards. Company may move from a site of origin in one of the following regions to a new site in one of the following regions: [13 coastal regions]. The site path is [{c} {c} {c}] and the hazard limit is decreased by two to a minimum of two." — `play-window` organization, `play-target` character (`filter: { "company.siteRegion": { "$in": [...] } }`), `on-event self-enters-play` → `set-company-special-movement` `specialMovement: "belegaer"` `target: "target-company"`.
- **Dual-`play-target` end-of-org short-events** (Anduin River tw-191 and the "mountain-crossing" family Ash Mountains tw-194, Misty Mountains tw-284, Mountains of Shadow tw-287, White Mountains tw-359) — a card may now carry **two** `play-target` effects for mutually-exclusive end-of-org modes on the same company, generalizing the single-`play-target` assumption `getPlayTargetEffect`/`endOfOrgEligibility` previously baked in. `getPlayTargetEffects` (plural, `legal-actions/organization.ts`) returns every `play-target` on the card; the end-of-org branch of `playResourceShortEventActions` tries each independently (via `endOfOrgEligibility`'s new explicit `playTarget` parameter) and unions the resulting actions, so both modes are offered whenever their own eligibility holds, sharing one `play-window`/company-scoped `duplication-limit`. Mode selection at resolution time is read straight off the played action's shape — a tapped-character mode carries `targetScoutInstanceId`, a no-cost mode carries only `targetCompanyId` — so `reducer-events.ts`'s `add-constraint` switch gates each new constraint kind on the presence/absence of `targetScoutInstanceId` rather than needing a `when` clause (mirrors the existing `extra-mh-phase` "destination not final at play time" deferred-check pattern, td-135). New cost `tap: "skilled-character-in-company"` + `skill` (`ActionCost`, `types/effects.ts`) generalizes `sage-in-company` to an arbitrary skill for a `play-target: "company"` cost: taps an untapped character in the target company bearing the named skill (printed or item-granted), one legal action per candidate. New `play-target` company-filter context field `company.skills` — the union of every skill (printed or item-granted) carried by any character in the company, via `getEffectiveSkills` (`legal-actions/organization.ts`) — backs "on a company containing a ranger" (`{ "company.skills": { "$includes": "ranger" } }`) without a `company.moving` gate (unlike Fair Sailing tw-232): the shortcut mode must be playable *before* a destination is declared so it can widen which sites `planMovementActions` subsequently offers for that company (plan-movement never re-runs once a destination is set). Two new constraint kinds: **`region-adjacency-shortcut`** (`pairs: [string,string][]`, scope `turn`, target company) — "tap the ranger to move as if the following pairs of regions were adjacent" — consumed by `withExtraRegionAdjacency` (new export, `movement-map.ts`: overlays extra bidirectional region edges onto a `MovementMap` and recomputes all-pairs distance without mutating the shared card-pool-derived map, cheap since the region graph is ~52 nodes), consulted at both `planMovementActions` (`organization-companies.ts`) and the M/H declare-path region-path search (`findRegionPaths` call in `movement-hazard.ts`) — the same dual-consult shape `evilHourRegionBonus` (ba-48) established, but widening *adjacency* rather than *distance*; a no-op for a company that ends up using starter/Under-deeps movement instead ("if the company uses region cards for its site path"), since only the region-movement path search ever asks for it. **`hazard-limit-region-name-match`** (`regionNames: string[]`, `value`, `floor`, scope `turn`, target company) — "if the site moved to is in one of the regions listed above, the hazard limit is reduced by two (to a minimum of 2)" — read directly from `snapshotHazardLimit` (`mh-steps.ts`), matched against the company's **destination site's static `region` field** (not the resolved region-*type* path `hazard-limit-region-count` reads), so it reads correctly regardless of which movement type the company ends up using. Used by Anduin River (tw-191): mode A taps a ranger to install `region-adjacency-shortcut` with the five listed region pairs (Rohan/Dagorlad, Anórien/Dagorlad, Anórien/Ithilien, Lebennin/Ithilien, Lebennin/Harondor); mode B (no tap) installs `hazard-limit-region-name-match` with the same six region names, `value: -2, floor: 2`; both share `duplication-limit` scope `company`.
- `cancel-attack` gained `forceSiteAutoAttacksNormalReface` (All the Bells Ringing as-44), for cancelling a **CvCC** attack (paired with `requiresCvCC: true`) and forcing the attacking company back through the site's automatic-attacks — this time as normal, non-detainment attacks — before it may declare the CvCC attack again. `applyEffect`'s `cancel-attack` branch (`apply-dispatcher.ts`) captures `attackingPlayerId`/`attackSource` off the combat before `resolveCancelAttackEntry` clears it, then calls the new `triggerBellsRingingReface` (`combat-cancel.ts`): if the attacking company's site has no automatic-attacks, `opponentInteractionThisTurn` is reset to `null` immediately (so `declare-company-attack` accepts a new declaration); otherwise it builds a repeated-attack combat via `buildSiteRepeatedAttackCombat` (relocated from `reducer-site.ts` to the new leaf module `site-repeated-attack.ts` — shared by the Troll-purse dm-95 / rescue-attack re-face callers and this one, so `combat-cancel.ts` can build it without an import cycle through `chain-reducer.ts`) with a new **`forceNormalOverride`** opt that unconditionally zeroes `detainment` (bypassing `combat-detainment` site effects, forced-detainment site rules, and the §3.II alignment-based computation alike), and enters a new `SiteStep` `'bells-ringing-attacks'` (mirrors `'troll-purse-attacks'`, sequenced by the new `handleSiteBellsRingingAttacks` in `reducer-site.ts`) that, once every automatic-attack has been re-faced, returns to `'declare-company-attack'` with the interaction marker cleared instead of to `'play-resources'`. The card's `when` also gained a new `whenContext` field, `attack.minionCompany` (`legal-actions/combat.ts`), the Ringwraith-alignment counterpart of the existing `attack.heroCompany` — true only for a CvCC combat whose attacking player is a Ringwraith. Used by All the Bells Ringing (as-44): "Playable during opponent's site phase before strikes are assigned on a hero company at a Free-hold [{F}] or Border-hold [{B}] if a minion company attacks. The attack is canceled and the minion company must face all automatic-attacks of the site—which attack normally, not as detainment. Afterwards, the minion company may attack the hero company again." — `cancel-attack` (`requiresCvCC: true`, `forceSiteAutoAttacksNormalReface: true`, `when: { $and: [{ "attack.minionCompany": true }, { "site.type": { $in: ["free-hold", "border-hold"] } }] }`).
- `company-combat-boost` `itemFilter` (per-item stacking) + `stat: "creature-body"` + `character-stat-modifier` `max` (Biter and Beater! as-46) — three additions to the combat-only short-event boost. **`itemFilter`** (optional, `{ item: { name, keywords, cardType, subtype } }`, same shape/matcher as `in-play-item-modifier`'s `itemFilter`) switches the boost from "once per matching character" to "once per matching borne item" — a bearer of two qualifying items receives the modifier twice (`filter`/`companyFilter` are ignored when `itemFilter` is set). The offering (`companyCombatBoostActions`, `legal-actions/combat.ts`) treats the card as eligible when any company member bears a matching item; the reducer (`handlePlayResourceShortEvent`) loops each matching item on each company member, adding one `character-stat-modifier` (or `character-creature-body-modifier`, see below) constraint per item. **`stat: "creature-body"`** installs a new `character-creature-body-modifier` active constraint (`types/pending.ts`) instead of `character-stat-modifier` — the short-event counterpart of an item's `enemy-modifier` (stat body, op subtract), reducing the *attacking creature's* body-check target for strikes the named character faces, without requiring the reduction to live on a borne item. Consumed in `combat-actions.ts` `handleBodyCheckRoll`'s `bodyCheckTarget === 'creature'` branch, alongside `resolveEnemyBody`'s item-sourced reduction (`Math.max(0, body - value)`); only matters for attacks against a body-checkable creature (`combat.creatureBody !== null`) — a no-op otherwise, matching the printed rarity of "defeatable" creatures. **`character-stat-modifier`'s new optional `max` field** (`types/pending.ts`, threaded into the synthesized `stat-modifier` effect in `effects/resolver.ts`) caps the running stat total immediately after the constraint's own bonus is added, mirroring a JSON `stat-modifier`'s `max` — needed because the card's French reference text clarifies "the maximum values indicated by the weapons still apply": the extra +2 must not push a named weapon's prowess contribution past that weapon's own printed ceiling. For the prowess boost, the reducer resolves each matching item's *currently active* max by re-collecting that one item's own `stat-modifier(prowess)` effects through the shared resolver (`collectCharacterEffects` + `buildBearerContext`, same combat context — `reason: "combat"`, `enemy.race`) and picking the override entry over the base entry (mirroring `resolveStatModifiers`'s own base/override selection), so a race-conditional higher cap (Glamdring's max 9 vs Orcs) is honored when applicable. `when` also gained an `enemy.overt` context field (`legal-actions/combat.ts` + `reducer-events.ts`, both mirroring each other): for a CvCC attack only, resolved from the attacking company via `isCovertCompany`, `true`/`false`; absent for a creature/automatic-attack. Used by Biter and Beater! (as-46): "Playable on a company facing an Orc attack or in combat with an overt company. Also playable during opponent's site phase. Every Sword of Gondolin, Orcrist, and Glamdring in target company give an additional +2 prowess bonus and lower the body of strikes their bearers face by 1." — two `company-combat-boost` effects sharing `itemFilter: { "item.name": { "$in": ["Sword of Gondolin", "Orcrist", "Glamdring"] } }` and `when: { "$or": [{ "enemy.race": "orc" }, { "enemy.overt": true }] }`: `stat: "prowess", value: 2` and `stat: "creature-body", value: 1`. "Also playable during opponent's site phase" needed no engine change — `company-combat-boost` is offered whenever `state.combat` exists and `playerId === combat.defendingPlayerId`, independent of whose site-phase turn is active (combat pre-empts phase-based legal actions).
- `play-target` `itemFilter` + a standalone-event route to the `can-use-palantir` constraint (Use Palantír tw-355) — until now `can-use-palantir` was only ever placed by a card's *own* grant-action (Palantír of Elostirion le-332), whose `source` is implicitly the granting item's own instance. Use Palantír is a bare resource short-event that grants the ability to a sage from *outside* any item, and must still scope it to exactly one Palantír when the sage bears several ("enable him to use **one** Palantír he bears"). New `itemFilter` field on `PlayTargetEffect` (`types/effects.ts`): restricts a `target: "character"` play-target to a bearer of at least one item matching the condition (evaluated per-item via `matchesDefinition`, the shared `itemsMatchingFilter` helper in `reducer-utils.ts`) and designates which item the card resolves against. The legal-action emitters (`long-event.ts`'s `longEventActions`, and `organization.ts`'s `playResourceShortEventActions` — since CoE 2.1.1 makes the card legal in any phase) cross each eligible sage with every matching item and emit one `play-short-event` per pair, carrying the pick as the new `targetItemInstanceId` field on `PlayShortEventAction`. The reducer's generic `on-event: self-enters-play` → `add-constraint` dispatch (`reducer-events.ts`) gained a `can-use-palantir` case that reads `targetScoutInstanceId`/`targetItemInstanceId` and sources the constraint from the **chosen item's** instance rather than the event card's own — so `buildGrantActionContext`'s pre-existing `constraint.source === sourceInstanceId` match (unmodified) scopes the ability to that one Palantír, identical to le-332's own semantics. Also added `itemKeywords` to `buildPlayOptionContext`'s `target` context (`legal-actions/organization.ts`) — short-event play-target filters previously lacked this field even though the DSL doc already documented it as part of the shared filter-context shape (only the permanent-event path, `organization-events.ts`, had it), so Use Palantír's `filter: { "target.itemKeywords": { "$includes": "palantir" } }` also closes that gap for any future short event. Card: "Sage only. Tap sage to enable him to use one Palantír he bears for the rest of the turn." — `play-target` character (`filter`: sage skill + itemKeywords includes palantir, `itemFilter`: item keywords includes palantir, `cost: { tap: "character" }`) + `on-event self-enters-play` → `add-constraint can-use-palantir` (`scope: turn`).
- Pure reuse, plain `combat-one-strike-per-character` (Silent Watcher tw-88) — a Pûkel-creature keyed to Shadow-hold/Dark-hold ({S}{D}) whose sole rule, "Each character in the company faces one strike," is the base (no `onlyWounded`/`excludeAvatars`) form of the shipped primitive, identical in shape to Watcher in the Water (tw-110) and Horse-lords (le-78). No engine change. `keyedTo` cross-checked token-by-token against `attributes.playable` "{S}{D}" — two site-type entries, no region tokens. Own card test (`tw-88.test.ts`) mirrors `tw-110.test.ts`'s combat assertions (1/2/3-character company strikesTotal) with site-type keying instead of region/named-site keying.
- `targets.scope: "company-hazard-corruption-cards"` (Athelas tw-195) — the company-scoped counterpart of the pre-existing `own-hazard-corruption-cards` scope: `enumerateGrantActionTargets` (`legal-actions/organization.ts`) restricts the `hazard-corruption` scan to characters in the *bearer's own company* rather than every character the player controls, backing "remove a corruption card from a character in his company" (as opposed to Palantír of Amon Sûl tw-296's "under your control" reach). Pairs with the existing `discard-target-corruption-card` apply unchanged. Athelas's other clause reuses `heal-company-character` (the Foul-smelling Paste le-310 / Healing Herbs tw-255 precedent) with `status: "tapped"` instead of `"untapped"` on its `set-character-status` apply — "change from wounded to well, character remains tapped" is simply the existing heal primitive targeting the Inverted-status company member but leaving it Tapped rather than fully untapping it — and gates the ability with `when: { "bearer.race": "dunadan" }` (a plain, pre-existing grant-action `when` context field) so only a Dúnadan bearer may use it; the corruption-removal clause is gated `when: { "bearer.name": "Aragorn II" }` on the same item. Card: "A Dúnadan can tap and use this item to heal a character in his company (change from wounded to well, character remains tapped). Aragorn II can also tap and use this item to remove a corruption card from a character in his company. Discard after use." — two `grant-action`s sharing `cost: { tap: "bearer", discard: "self" }`.
- `play-target: "nazgul-permanent-event"` + `resolveAttackBody` `op: "set"` on a bodyless attack (Helms of Iron dm-64) — two new primitives. First, a `play-target` kind for a **hazard** permanent event playable only while the hazard player controls a Nazgûl permanent-event of their own (`isNazgulPermanentEvent`, `reducer-utils.ts`): `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts` emits one `play-hazard` action per matching instance in the hazard player's own `cardsInPlay`, carrying the choice on the new `PlayHazardAction.targetNazgulInstanceId` / `ChainEntryPayload.targetNazgulInstanceId`; with zero candidates no action is emitted, which alone implements "Playable only if …" with no separate `play-condition`. `resolvePermanentEvent`'s `self-enters-play` move loop (`chain-reducer.ts`) threads that id into `MoveContext.targetCardId`, so a plain `{ "select": "target", "from": "in-play", "to": "discard" }` move discards exactly the chosen card (its `toOwner` default already resolves to the located card's own owner, not the declaring player). Second, `resolveAttackBody` (`engine/effects/resolver.ts`) previously returned a `null` (bodyless) base attack body unchanged, since additive/multiplicative `all-attacks` modifiers have nothing to add to; it now special-cases `op: "set"` modifiers, collecting and applying only those when the base is `null` (additive/multiply modifiers are still skipped there) and excluding them from the normal path once a body exists (so they can't clobber a printed body — the card's separate `+1` handles that case). Used by Helms of Iron (dm-64): "Playable only if you have a Nazgûl permanent-event in play. Discard the Nazgûl when this card is brought into play. All Orc, Troll, and Man attacks with body have their body modified by +1; and all Orc, Troll, and Man attacks with no body have 4 body." — `play-target: "nazgul-permanent-event"`, `on-event self-enters-play` → `move` (`select: target`, `in-play` → `discard`), plus the two `stat-modifier` `stat: "body"` `target: "all-attacks"` entries (`value: 1` and `op: "set", value: 4`) gated on `{ "enemy.race": { "$in": ["orc", "troll", "man"] } }`.
- Self stat-modifier gated on `attack.keying` (Pirates le-88) — extends the existing Orc-lieutenant-style "creature's own untargeted `stat-modifier` effects apply to its own attack" mechanism (`resolveAttackProwess`, `resolver.ts`) so the `when` condition can also see **which region type this specific play was keyed to**, not just company/defender state. `CreatureSelfContext` gained an optional `attackKeying` field (array of {@link RegionType}); `initiateCreatureCombat` (`chain-reducer.ts`) now computes `declaredKeyedBy`/`attackKeying` (the hazard player's declared `keyedBy` match, or the union of the card's `keyedTo` region types when undeclared — the same value later stored on `CombatState.attackKeying` for `cancel-attack`/`cancel-strike` conditions) *before* resolving prowess, and threads it into `creatureSelf`. `buildAttackContext` folds a non-empty `attackKeying` into `attack.keying`, mirroring the field name already exposed to `cancel-attack`/`cancel-strike`/`modify-attack` `when` clauses, so a card can gate its own prowess the same way another card gates canceling it. Used by Pirates (le-88): "Men. Three strikes. If any strike of Pirates wounds a character, the company must immediately discard one item (of defender's choice). Pirates receives +2 prowess when keyed to Coastal Seas [{c}]." — `keyedTo` one entry (`regionTypes: [wilderness, coastal-sea]`, `siteTypes: [ruins-and-lairs]`, cross-checked against `{w}{c}{R}`), `on-event: character-wounded-by-self` → `force-discard-one-company-item` (Brigands le-64 precedent), and `stat-modifier` prowess +2 `when: { "attack.keying": { "$includes": "coastal-sea" } }` (no `target`, so it self-applies per the Orc-lieutenant precedent).
- Company-targeting `play-option` + `random-discard-hand` (Drowning Seas tw-30) — `play-option` was previously wired only for character-targeting and untargeted short-event modes; this extends it to `play-target: "company"`. In the company branch of `playHazardsActions` (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`), a card carrying `play-option` effects is offered as one `play-hazard` action per option (carrying `optionId`) instead of the single-action path, each option's `when` evaluated against the existing company filter context plus `inPlay` (so an alternative gated on a permanent-event like Doors of Night is only offered while it's actually in play); with no matching option the play is non-viable. `chain-reducer.ts`'s new `applyCompanyPlayOption` dispatches the chosen option's `apply` on un-negated resolution — gated on the card's own `play-target` being `"company"` so it never collides with the existing `optionId && !targetCharacterId` untargeted-mode branch (every hazard short-event's payload carries `targetCompanyId` regardless of its actual target kind, so that alone can't discriminate). Two apply kinds resolve against the chain's active M/H company: `company-return-to-origin` (reused as a `play-option` apply, not just a top-level card effect — same CoE 2.IV.4 mechanism as `applyCompanyReturnToOrigin`/Fell Turtle tw-34's `attack-strike-successful` verb) and `force-discard-one-company-item` (reused outside combat — previously only fired from `character-wounded-by-self`, e.g. Brigands le-64/tw-17; here it enqueues the same `discard-one-company-item` pending resolution directly, with no `characterId` narrowing, so the choice spans every item in the company, actored by the company's controller). New primitive **`random-discard-hand`** (`{ count }`): the target company's controller discards `count` cards drawn at random from hand (capped at hand size) — a seeded `shuffle` (same pattern as `reveal-hand-cards-per-character`/Crebain tw-25) picks the discarded slice, and `state.rng` advances. `sequence` composes `force-discard-one-company-item` + `random-discard-hand` for the card's non-alternative mode. Used by Drowning Seas (tw-30): "Environment. Playable on a company that moved this turn to a site with a Coastal Sea [{c}] in its site path. Target company loses one item of its choice and its player must randomly discard two cards from his hand. Alternatively, if Doors of Night is in play, target company must immediately return to its site of origin." — `play-target` company, `play-condition` site-path (`sitePath.coastalCount >= 1`, the Lost at Sea tw-50 shape), `play-option "item-loss-and-discard"` (no `when`) → `sequence` [`force-discard-one-company-item`, `random-discard-hand` count 2], `play-option "return-to-origin"` (`when: { inPlay: "Doors of Night" }`) → `company-return-to-origin`.
- `company.moving` on a character-targeting short event's `play-target` filter + arrival-override co-offering (Gloom tw-41) — generalizes New Moon's (tw-68) "Alternatively" shape (§56c) from its `tap-character` main mode to an ordinary `play-target: "character"` main mode. The short-event character-targeting branch (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`, "Character-targeting short events") now exposes `company.moving` (`!!targetCompany.destinationSite`) in its per-character filter context, alongside `target.*`, so a card can gate "playable only on a company that is moving this turn" per candidate character rather than only via a whole-company `play-target: "company"` filter. The same branch also offers the arrival-override modes' single untargeted action (identical eligibility check to the `tap-character` branch) whenever the card carries `on-event company-arrives-at-site` effects, so a `play-target: "character"` card can carry Doors-of-Night alternative modes too. `applyShortEventArrivalTrigger`'s mutual-exclusion guard (`chain-reducer.ts`) now checks for `tap-character` **or** a `play-target` `target: "character"` effect, so a chosen `targetCharacterId` suppresses the arrival-override modes under either shape. The main mode's `-1 prowess` needs no new machinery — it is the existing `on-event self-enters-play` → `character-stat-modifier` synthesis (Glance of Arien ba-19 precedent), which already resolves its target off `action.targetCharacterId` regardless of how that id was offered. Used by Gloom (tw-41): "Environment. Playable only on a company that is moving this turn. One character (attacker's choice) in that company suffers -1 to his prowess until the end of the turn. Alternatively, if Doors of Night is in play, treat one Border-land [{b}] as a Wilderness [{w}] or one Border-hold [{B}] as a Ruins & Lairs [{R}] until the end of the turn. Cannot be duplicated." — `play-target` character (`filter: { "company.moving": true }`), `on-event self-enters-play` → `character-stat-modifier` (prowess -1, scope turn), two `on-event company-arrives-at-site` modes (`region-type-override` border→wilderness, `site-type-override` border-hold→ruins-and-lairs, both gated `environment.doorsOfNightInPlay`), `duplication-limit` scope turn.
- `skip-untap-and-heal` `ActiveConstraint` kind + `untap.resourcePlayerUntapped` grant-action context field (Morgul-knife tw-64) — a corruption card removed "instead of untapping or healing" rather than by tapping the bearer (contrast Icy Touch td-33's tap-and-roll, organization-phase variant). The `grant-action` (`action`, any name — deliberately *not* `remove-self-on-roll`, since rule 7.3's automatic no-tap -3 variant only applies to cards that "state a character must tap to make a roll to remove it") declares `cost: {}`, `anyPhase: true`, and `when: { "phase": "untap", "bearer.atHaven": true, "untap.resourcePlayerUntapped": false }`; `buildGrantActionContext` (`legal-actions/organization.ts`) now exposes `untap.resourcePlayerUntapped` (only populated while `phase === "untap"`, read off `UntapPhaseState.untapped`) so the ability's window closes once the bulk `untap` action for the phase has already run — the printed "instead of" has no meaning after the sweep already happened. The `apply` is a `sequence` of a `roll-then-apply` (discarding the card on success) *followed by* an unconditional `add-constraint skip-untap-and-heal` (`scope: "turn"`, `target: "bearer"`) — that ordering matters: `add-constraint` before the roll would have its just-added constraint immediately swept by the roll's own self-discard move (`dropConstraintsSourcedBy`, `reducer-move.ts`, strips every constraint sourced by a card the instant it leaves play), so the constraint must be added *after* the roll settles whether the card leaves play or not. `performUntap` (`reducer-untap.ts`) collects `skip-untap-and-heal`-constrained character ids into a new `skipUntapAndHealIds` set, checked before `prisonerIds`/`cannotUntapIds` in the per-character sweep — unlike `bearer-cannot-untap` (which still lets a wounded character heal at a haven), this blocks **both** the tapped→untapped and inverted→tapped transitions for the rest of that one untap-phase sweep. `applyOneConstraint` (`legal-actions/pending.ts`) treats the new kind as a pass-through (no legal-action filtering — it's consumed directly by `performUntap`). Also fixed `rollThresholdFor` (`granted-action-emit.ts`) to unwrap one level of `sequence` when locating a nested `roll-then-apply`'s threshold, so a gated roll like this one still reports its threshold on the emitted `ActivateGrantedAction` (previously only a bare top-level `roll-then-apply` was recognized). Used by Morgul-knife (tw-64) and The Pale Sword (tw-97), each with its own threshold: "... If at a Haven/Darkhaven [{H}] during his untap phase, a character with this card may attempt to remove it instead of untapping or healing. Make a roll: if this result is greater than 4 [tw-64] / 5 [tw-97], discard this card." Morgul-knife's combat clause reuses Icy Touch's `attachCorruptionOnWound` verbatim (`modify-attack`, `fromHand: true`, `player: "attacker"`, `prowessModifier: 2`, `when: { "enemy.race": "ringwraith" }`) plus `duplication-limit` scope `"attack"` (the Adûnaphel Unleashed le-161 precedent) and `stat-modifier corruption-points +4`; the printed "one character of *attacker's choice*" (vs. Icy Touch's "*next* character") is judged equivalent to the existing "first eligible wounded character" simplification in every realistic case, since Ringwraith characters carry no `strikes` stat and a CvCC attack against a company wounds at most one defender. The Pale Sword (tw-97, unique) reuses the same `attachCorruptionOnWound` shape at `prowessModifier: 1` and `stat-modifier corruption-points +6`, but adds a second from-hand mode ahead of it in the `effects` array for "if played on a company facing an attack from the Witch-king of Angmar, his prowess is increased by +1 plus the number of Nazgûl permanent-events in play" — see §10e-quinquies `prowessModifierExpr` and `countNazgulPermanentEventsInPlay` (`engine/reducer-utils.ts`) in `docs/card-effects-dsl.md`. Being `unique: true`, its two "cannot be duplicated" clauses are **not** modeled as an in-play `duplication-limit` (the tw-93 precedent: deck-level uniqueness already makes an in-play copy limit unreachable).
- `set-company-special-movement` gained a fourth mode, `"eagle-mounts"` (Eagle-mounts tw-220), alongside `'gwaihir'`/`'paths-of-the-dead'`/`'belegaer'` — printed near-identically to Gwaihir (tw-251) but with a genuinely different exclusion rule: Gwaihir excludes destinations by **region** type (Shadow-land [{s}]/Dark-domain [{d}], its printed lowercase symbols), while Eagle-mounts excludes by **site** type (Shadow-hold [{S}]/Dark-hold [{D}], uppercase symbols) — e.g. Moria is a Shadow-hold sitting in a wilderness region, reachable via `'gwaihir'` but not `'eagle-mounts'`. `organization-companies.ts` `planMovementActions` gained a sibling `'eagle-mounts'` branch to the existing `'gwaihir'` one, filtering candidates on `siteDef.siteType !== 'shadow-hold' && siteDef.siteType !== 'dark-hold'` instead of the destination region's type; both branches keep the shared MEAS §6(b) Under-deeps exclusion (a company at an Under-deeps site cannot use either special movement, and neither can ever target an Under-deeps site). `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`'s special-movement declare-path check and `mh-steps.ts`'s "no path traversed" fallback (empty `resolvedSitePath`, so only site-keyed hazard creatures match — "opponent may only play hazard creatures that are keyed to the company's new site") already generalized to any unlisted `specialMovement` value, so both needed only the new mode added to their explicit-list checks (`movement-hazard.ts`) or no change at all (`mh-steps.ts`). Used by Eagle-mounts (tw-220): "Playable only at the end of the organization phase on a company with a diplomat that begins the turn at Eagles' Eyrie. Company may move to any site that is not a Shadow-hold [{S}], Dark-hold [{D}], or Under-deeps. Opponent may only play hazard creatures that are keyed to the company's new site." — `play-window` organization/`end-of-org`, `play-target` character (`filter: { "$and": [{ "target.skills": { "$includes": "diplomat" } }, { "company.siteName": "Eagles' Eyrie" }] }`), `on-event self-enters-play` → `set-company-special-movement` `specialMovement: "eagle-mounts"` `target: "target-company"`.
- `add-constraint` `constraint: "hazard-limit-multiplier"` (Lost in Dark-domains tw-52) — a hazard-side (not resource-side) short event's turn-scoped, company-targeted multiplier over the *live* hazard limit, distinct from every other `hazard-limit-*` kind (which are additive deltas). `effectiveHazardLimit` (`hazard-limit.ts`) applies it as a final `limit *= value` pass, after summing `hazardLimitAtReveal` and every additive `hazard-limit-modifier`, so "doubled" always doubles whatever the company's limit already is rather than a fixed base. Built by `buildConstraintKind` (`constraint-kind.ts`, a new `case`) and exercised through the **hazard**-short-event self-enters-play path (`applyShortEventSelfEntersPlayConstraints`, `chain-reducer.ts` — the "Lost in Free-domains" precedent), not the resource-event `applyShortEventOnEntersPlay` switch (`reducer-events.ts`) the other `hazard-limit-*` kinds use. That path previously ignored `onEvent.when` entirely for any apply other than `character-stat-modifier`; it now evaluates `when` against `{ inPlay, sitePath }` (the latter only in the M/H phase, via `regionTypeCounts(resolvedSitePath)`) before falling through to the generic `applyAddConstraintFromOnEvent`, letting any hazard short-event gate its add-constraint apply on site-path composition, not just on cards in play. Card text separates a **base legality gate** ("Playable on a company that is moving this turn") from a **conditional effect** ("if the company has a Dark-domain … the hazard limit is doubled") — unlike Lost in Free-domains-style siblings where the region requirement *is* the legality gate. `checkSitePathCondition` (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) gained a `moving` context field (`targetCompany?.destinationSite != null`) for exactly this shape: `destinationSiteType` cannot serve as a "moving" proxy since it falls back to the *current* site's type for a stationary company (rule 2.IV — every company's M/H phase runs all 8 steps regardless of movement), so `{ "moving": true }` is the correct `play-condition requires: "site-path"` gate for "playable on any moving company, with a separate path-composition trigger for the effect." Used by Lost in Dark-domains (tw-52): `play-condition` (`condition: { "moving": true }`) + `play-target: "company"` + `on-event self-enters-play` (`when: { "sitePath.darkCount": { "$gte": 1 } }`) → `add-constraint hazard-limit-multiplier value: 2 scope: turn`.
- No new engine support (Lost in the Wilderness tw-55) — both halves of the card compose primitives already in this catalog, so it is certified without an engine change. The hazard-limit half reuses verbatim the `hazard-limit-region-count` constraint Lost in Border-lands (tw-51) introduced (see above), installed through the chain's generic self-enters-play `add-constraint` path (`buildConstraintKind`, `constraint-kind.ts`) and read *live* by `effectiveHazardLimit`/`currentHazardLimit` (`hazard-limit.ts`) against the company's already-resolved `resolvedSitePath`, with only the `regionType` differing (`"wilderness"`). The "playable on a moving company" half reuses the `moving` context field Lost in Dark-domains (tw-52) added to `checkSitePathCondition` (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`, `targetCompany?.destinationSite != null`) — note that `destinationSiteType`, the field tw-51 gates on, cannot serve as a "moving" proxy, since it is backfilled from the company's *current* site even when it is stationary (rule 2.IV — every company's M/H phase runs all its steps regardless of movement) and so cannot distinguish moving from non-moving on its own. Card: "Playable on a moving company. Its hazard limit increases by one for every Wilderness [{w}] in its site path." — `play-condition` (`requires: "site-path"`, `condition: { "moving": true }`) + `play-target: "company"` + `on-event self-enters-play` (no `when`) → `add-constraint hazard-limit-region-count` `regionType: "wilderness"` `value: 1` `floor: 0` `scope: turn` `target: "target-company"`.
- New `cvcc-capture-in-lieu-of-body-check` + `eliminate-captured-character` (No Better Use ba-41, docs §80) — an activated ability offered alongside `body-check-roll` whenever a CvCC character body check is pending (`captureInLieuOfBodyCheckActions`, `legal-actions/combat.ts`; `handleCaptureInLieuOfBodyCheck`, `combat-actions.ts`): tap the bearer to place the opposing character "off to the side" with the card instead of rolling, marking the strike `result: 'captured'` (the `take-prisoner` disposition, so wound triggers don't fire). The capture (`engine/no-better-use.ts::captureCharacterInLieuOfBodyCheck`) reuses Press-gang's (ba-22) off-to-the-side shape verbatim — discard every item/ally/hazard, revert followers to general influence, mark with a distinct `character-captured-by-bearer` constraint kind (treated identically to `character-pressed` for scoring/no-untap in `recompute-derived.ts`/`reducer-untap.ts`/`influence-overflow.ts`/`reducer-free-council.ts`) carrying `bearerCharacterId`/`bearerOwnerId`/`bearerLastKnownSite` (`types/pending.ts`). One-time-use is a persistent (`scope: 'until-cleared'`) `granted-action-used` lock, distinct from the existing turn-scoped `oncePerTurn` use of that same constraint kind. Release is a new `postReduce` sweep, `sweepNoBetterUseCaptures` (alongside `sweepSetAside`/`sweepPressGang`): each pass checks the capture's recorded bearer — untapped and un-wounded refreshes the site snapshot; wounded discards the host and releases; not found at all (any removal path, covered uniformly with no per-seam interception, unlike Press-gang's five discard-only seams) releases using the last snapshot — forming a fresh one-character company at that site via a new `releaseCapturedCharacterToNewCompany` helper (`Company.siteCardOwned: false`, no planned movement). The card's own `grant-action` ("during the site phase at Shelob's Lair, tap and discard this card to eliminate opponent's character — kill MP to you") is `anyPhase: true` + `when: { "phase": "site", "site.name": "Shelob's Lair" }` (the documented single-phase-gating pattern) + `cost: { "tap": "bearer", "discard": "self" }`, resolved by the new `eliminate-captured-character` apply (`grant-action-apply.ts`) — looks up the held character via its `character-captured-by-bearer` constraint, relocates the bare card to the activating player's kill pile, removes the constraint. Added `name` (the current site's printed name) to `buildGrantActionContext`'s `siteCtx` (`legal-actions/organization.ts`) — the first grant-action `when` to gate on a specific site by name.
- `modify-attack` `firstExcessStrikePenalty` (Pierced by Many Wounds dm-79) — overrides the prowess cost of a defending character's *first* excess strike this attack, instead of a flat -1 per excess strike. The excess-strike penalty formula was duplicated in two places (`combat-strike.ts`'s actual strike resolution, both the creature-vs-character and CvCC paths, and `legal-actions/combat.ts`'s `resolve-strike` "need" preview); both were consolidated onto one shared helper, `excessStrikePenalty(combat, excessStrikes)` (`reducer-utils.ts`, importable by both without creating a module cycle), which returns `excessStrikes` unchanged when `CombatState.firstExcessStrikePenalty` is unset, and `firstExcessStrikePenalty + (excessStrikes - 1)` when it is — so only the character's first excess strike is affected; a second, third, etc. excess strike on the same character still costs -1 each on top of it. The from-hand `modify-attack` `when` context (`buildPlayedModifyAttackContext`) gained `attack.strikesTotal` so a card can gate "more strikes than defending characters" as `{ "attack.strikesTotal": { "$gt": "defender.companySize" } }` (the existing `$gt` context-path-operand support, Whip le-348 precedent). Card: "Playable on an attack with more strikes than defending characters before strikes are assigned; does not count against the hazard limit. The first excess strike assigned to each character gives a -4 modification to his prowess instead of -1. Cannot be duplicated on a given attack." — `play-flag: no-hazard-limit` + `modify-attack` (`fromHand`, `player: "attacker"`, `firstExcessStrikePenalty: 4`, `when: { "attack.strikesTotal": { "$gt": "defender.companySize" } }`) + `duplication-limit` scope `attack`.

- `faction-influence-untethered` grant-action apply + `targets.scope: "own-hand-factions"` (Roäc the Raven tw-320) — an ally's own activated ability to declare and immediately resolve an influence attempt against a faction card in hand, entirely independent of the bearer's company's current site: no site-match against the faction's `playableAt`, no untapped-site requirement, and — win or lose — the site is never touched (contrast the ordinary faction-play path in `legal-actions/site.ts`, which gates on both). New `targets.scope` value `"own-hand-factions"` enumerates the activating player's own `hero-resource-faction`/`minion-resource-faction` hand cards (optionally narrowed by `filter`); unlike every other `targets.scope`, this scope is read directly in the **ally** grant-action scanner (`legal-actions/organization.ts`'s "Scan allies attached to this character" loop, which previously pushed a single untargeted activation per ally-borne effect) rather than through the shared `enumerateGrantActionTargets` (which only sees `{companies, characters}`, not `hand`) — gated additionally on the bearer's company being the *active* site-phase company (`state.phaseState.activeCompanyIndex`), matching "during the site phase … his company." The new `apply.type: "faction-influence-untethered"` (`grant-action-apply.ts`) runs synchronously (no chain/on-guard window — CoE 2.V.6 only opens on-guard for a resource "that would tap the site if successfully played", and this one never does): computes a modifier from the source ally's own printed `directInfluence` (0 if unprinted) plus player-wide `check-modifier` constraints and the game-wide influence check-modifier (mirroring the **ally** branch of `resolveInfluenceAttemptRoll` — Radagast's Black Bird wh-114 — but omitting every *site*-tied modifier, since the card text detaches the check from any real site), rolls 2d6, and moves the faction straight from hand into `cardsInPlay` untapped (honoring `playedAfterFactionMpPin`, Await the Onset wh-96) on success or to the discard pile on failure — no site is ever touched either way, so `resourcePlayed`/`minorItemAvailable` stay unaffected. The ally itself is already tapped-and-discarded by the shared `cost: { tap: "self", discard: "self" }` payment (`cost-evaluator.ts`) *before* this apply runs, so unlike the Black Bird "stays in company, just tapped" precedent, Roäc leaves play as the activation cost regardless of whether the roll succeeds. Card: "Unique. Playable at any site in Northern Rhovanion. During the site phase you can tap and discard Roäc the Raven to attempt to bring any faction into play — treat this influence check as if it was made by a diplomat at any site where the faction could be played. Using Roäc the Raven to make an influence attempt does not tap a site, and may be done if his company is at a tapped site." — `playableAt: [{ "region": "Northern Rhovanion" }]` (the Noble Steed wh-33 region-`playableAt` precedent) + one `grant-action` (`activeSitePhase: true`, `cost: { tap: "self", discard: "self" }`, `targets: { scope: "own-hand-factions" }`, `apply: { type: "faction-influence-untethered" }`).
- `attached-faction-mp-bonus` (Tribute Garnered as-104) — a new primitive for a resource permanent event bound to a single faction *instance* (`play-target: "faction"`, no filter — reuses the existing own-in-play-factions scoping and `CardInPlay.attachedTo` binding Long Grievous Siege ba-40 established) that grants that one target faction's owner a flat MP bonus in a declared `category`, independent of the target's own printed `marshallingCategory`. Distinct from `faction-mp-bonus` (as-45's race-diversity-gated bonus over a whole class of factions): this one is anchored to a single attached instance, not a race. Collected in a dedicated pass in `recompute-derived.ts` (after the `faction-mp-bonus` pass) over the controller's own `cardsInPlay` entries carrying `attachedTo`, adding `value` to `mp[category]` while the target instance is still present in the same `cardsInPlay` list (the pre-existing `discardOrphanedFactionAttachedEvents` sweep guarantees the carrier is discarded, not orphaned, once the target faction leaves play). Used by Tribute Garnered (as-104): "Playable on a faction in play. That faction gives an additional miscellaneous marshalling point. Cannot be duplicated on a given faction. Discard when any play deck is exhausted." — `play-target` faction (no filter) + `attached-faction-mp-bonus` (`value: 1, category: "misc"`) + `duplication-limit` scope `faction` + `on-event play-deck-exhausted` self-discard `move` (the Safe from the Shadow as-54 shape).
- `check-modifier` `check: "riddling"` reaching the `riddling-attempt` pending resolution, plus a new `pending.riddlingAttemptTargetsMe` reactive-play gate (Wit td-168) — previously `"riddling"` was a recognized `CheckKind` only at the generic `resolveCheckModifier` utility level; no `riddling-attempt` roll (Riddling Talk td-148) actually consulted `state.activeConstraints`. `riddlingAttemptRollActions` (`legal-actions/pending.ts`) and `applyRiddlingAttemptResolution` (`pending-reducers.ts`) now both sum one-shot `check-modifier` constraints with `check: "riddling"` targeting the attempting character into the roll's bonus/need, and the reducer consumes them after the roll — the exact `corruption`-check precedent (`totalModifier`/consumption loop), just re-keyed. `buildPlayOptionContext` (`legal-actions/organization.ts`) gained `pending.riddlingAttemptTargetsMe`, mirroring `pending.corruptionCheckTargetsMe`: true only while a `riddling-attempt` resolution is awaiting its roll for that exact character. The reactive-play scan itself needed **no new code** — `riddlingAttemptRollActions` now also calls the existing `reactiveCorruptionCheckPlays` (`legal-actions/pending.ts`), which is check-kind-agnostic (it just matches `play-target`/`play-option` against the resolving character and lets each option's own `when` decide relevance); `applyRiddlingAttemptResolution` grew the matching `if (action.type === 'play-short-event') return null` carve-out so the reducer falls through to the ordinary short-event handler, the same pattern `applyCorruptionCheckResolution` already used. Because the reactive window is keyed off "a `riddling-attempt` resolution is queued", not off any particular phase, a card using this shape is automatically playable in *any* phase a future riddling-roll mechanic might fire in (including an opponent's site phase) with no phase-specific carve-out. Used by Wit (td-168): "Modify one riddling roll by +3. If applicable, this card may also be played during your opponent's site phase if a riddling roll is called for." — `play-target: "character"` (no filter) + `play-option` (`when: { "pending.riddlingAttemptTargetsMe": true }`) → `add-constraint check-modifier check:"riddling" value:3 scope:"until-cleared"` (the Halfling Strength tw-253 shape, re-keyed).
- `stat-modifier` `target: "attacker-chooses-defenders-attacks"` (More Alert than Most dm-150) — a passive strike reduction that only makes sense once an attack's *final* `attackerChoosesDefenders` flag (printed creature/site rule OR'd with any global grant, `resolveAttackerChoosesDefenders`) is known, which happens at combat creation — after `resolveAttackStrikes` has already produced the base strikes total from the ordinary `all-attacks`/`all-automatic-attacks` collection. Reusing `all-attacks` for this would double-count unrelated all-attacks strikes modifiers (e.g. The Moon Is Dead's +1 strike to Undead attacks), since this needs to run as a second, later pass over the already-resolved total.
