New exported resolver function `resolveAttackerChosenStrikeReduction(state, strikesTotal, attackerChoosesDefenders, inPlayNames)` (`effects/resolver.ts`) collects only this dedicated target scope and folds it via the existing `resolveStatModifiers`; it is a no-op when `attackerChoosesDefenders` is false. `makeCombatState` (`reducer-utils.ts`) is the single choke point where both `strikesTotal` and `attackerChoosesDefenders` are simultaneously final for every combat-creation call site (25 of them across the engine), so it now takes `state` as its first argument and applies this reduction there rather than at each call site individually — every caller was updated to pass its local `GameState` variable. The `on-event: attack-defeated` discard scan (`combat-finalize.ts`) no longer requires `combat.creatureRace` to run (More Alert than Most's discard condition is race-agnostic) and now also exposes `attack.attackerChoosesDefenders` (`combat.attackerChoosesDefenders ?? false`) in its context, alongside the pre-existing `enemy.race`/`attack.isolated`/`attack.isAutomaticAttack`. Used by More Alert than Most (dm-150): "The number of strikes of any attack that chooses defending characters is reduced by one (by 2 if Gates of Morning is in play) to a minimum of one. Discard when such an attack is defeated. Cannot be duplicated." — two `stat-modifier` `stat: "strikes"` `target: "attacker-chooses-defenders-attacks"` effects (`value: -1, min: 1` unconditional, and a second `value: -1, min: 1` gated `when: { "inPlay": "Gates of Morning" }`, each floored independently so the pair nets -1 or -2 to a floor of 1 regardless of order), `on-event: attack-defeated` → `move` self→discard gated `when: { "attack.attackerChoosesDefenders": true }`, plus `duplication-limit` scope game.
- Combat-reactive `short` hazard-events carrying `on-event self-enters-play-combat` → `add-constraint constraint: "company-stat-modifier"` (Words of Power and Terror tw-115) — until now `self-enters-play-combat` (the Dragon's Curse td-16 window: `play-window` combat/`resolve-strike` + `play-condition requires: "combat-creature-race"`) only supported `modify-current-strike-prowess` and only accepted `eventType: "permanent"` cards, which stay attached to the targeted character. `isCombatCompanyStatModifierShortEvent` (`legal-actions/combat.ts`) and its mirror in `handleCombatPlayHazard` (`combat-hazard-play.ts`) recognize a `short` event whose `self-enters-play-combat` apply is `add-constraint`/`company-stat-modifier`: `combatHazardPermanentPlays`'s `eventType !== 'permanent'` gate now admits these too, and the reducer branches on `isCombatShortCompanyModifier` to discard the card immediately (to the hazard player's discard pile) instead of attaching it, then adds a turn-scoped `company-stat-modifier` constraint (`stat`/`value` off the apply, `parseConstraintScope`) targeting `combat.companyId` — the defending company as a whole, not just the struck character — via the existing generic `addConstraint`/`collectCompanyStatModifierEffects` machinery (Orc-draughts precedent), so every character in the company gets the modifier for the rest of the turn regardless of which strike is currently resolving. Because the card discards rather than staying in `cardsInPlay`/hazards, its `duplication-limit scope: "company"` cannot be checked by scanning attachments (the existing `character`-scope check's approach) — `combatHazardPermanentPlays` instead counts active `company-stat-modifier` constraints on the target company sourced from a same-named definition (`constraintsOnCompany`, `pending.ts`), which alone matters since the constraint's own `scope: "turn"` sweep already bounds its lifetime to the card's own "until the end of the turn." Used by Words of Power and Terror (tw-115): "Modify the prowesses of all characters in a company attacked by a Nazgûl by -1 until the end of the turn. Cannot be duplicated on a given company." — `play-window` combat/`resolve-strike`, `play-condition combat-creature-race race: "ringwraith"`, `play-target: "character"` (any defender facing the Nazgûl attack, to resolve the company), `on-event self-enters-play-combat` → `add-constraint company-stat-modifier stat: "prowess" value: -1 scope: "turn"`, `duplication-limit scope: "company" max: 1`.

- `reveal-deck-choose-set-aside` + `play-condition` `active-player-deck-size` (Great Secrets Buried There dm-63) — a dual-mode permanent event (hazard mode against the opponent's deck, plus `playable-as-resource` self-mode with roles reversed) that reveals the top N cards of the deck owner's play deck and, if any match `itemFilter`, forces a choice of one to place off to the side under the host, out of play and scoring no marshalling points. `active-player-deck-size` (`reducer-utils.ts`) gates the hazard mode on the *active* player's (the moving company owner's, i.e. the hazard target's) deck size — the "opponent" from the hazard-playing card-player's perspective; `card-player-deck-size` (dm-70) is the mirror-image gate for the declaring player's own deck. On play, `reveal-deck-choose-set-aside` reveals the top `count` cards (`revealInstances`) and, if at least one matches `itemFilter`, enqueues a `great-secrets-choose-item` pending resolution actored by the deck owner (not the card player) listing `eligibleInstanceIds`; `choose-set-aside-item` (the deck owner's only legal action while it's pending) moves the chosen instance out of the deck and into the host's `setAside` list (`placeCardSetAside`, `noMp: true`) via `set-aside.ts`, then shuffles the remaining revealed cards back into the deck. With zero eligible cards, everything is simply shuffled back and nothing is set aside — no pending resolution at all. The set-aside item is later playable "as though in hand" at an Under-deeps site (`legal-actions/site.ts`'s `playResourcesActions` hand-card loop, generalized by dm-116 into the site-type-driven `item-cache-play-source` primitive) regardless of which player controls the host, provided the reader is the item's *owner*; a follow-up fix scoped this Under-deeps replay strictly to items whose `setAsideHost` names a Great-Secrets-shaped host (not any `setAside`-capable card, e.g. an Armory dm-116 cache), since only Great Secrets Buried There grants the "as though in hand" replay text. Card: "Playable if opponent has at least ten cards in his play deck. Opponent reveals the top ten cards of his play deck to himself. If one is available, opponent must choose a non-special, non-hoard item from the revealed cards to place off to the side under this card (item does not give marshalling points and is considered out of play). If none are available, opponent must show you the cards he revealed to himself. Opponent shuffles all remaining revealed cards into his play deck. Opponent may play this item as though it were in his hand at any Under-deeps site where it could be normally playable. Alternatively, you may play this card as a resource on yourself if you have at least ten cards in your play deck. In this case, you and your opponent reverse roles." — `play-condition` `active-player-deck-size` (`minDeckSize: 10`), `play-flag: "playable-as-resource"`, `reveal-deck-choose-set-aside` (`count: 10`, `itemFilter`: hero/minion resource item, non-special/non-hoard subtype).

- `modify-attack` `scope: "current-strike"` gains a `passive: true` mode, plus a new `on-event: "bearer-strike-defeated"` self-discard trigger (Morgul-blade le-205) — the item-borne single-strike modifier (§ Arrows Shorn of Ebony td-99 / Shield of Iron-bound Ash tw-327 above) was always an *activated* ability, gated behind a `cost` (`tap`/`discard`) and an explicit `tap-item-for-strike` action. `passive: true` (mutually exclusive with `cost`/`fromHand`) instead applies automatically, with no action and no consumption, to every strike whose target is the item's own bearer: `passiveModifyAttackProwessBonus` (`combat-strike.ts`) sums matching items' `prowessModifier` and folds it directly into the defender's own effective prowess for the strike — in `resolveStrikeCore`'s `prowess` (creature/agent strikes) and `resolveStrikeCvCC`'s `defProwess` (CvCC), the same field (`StrikeAssignment.strikeProwessBonus`'s destination) the activated path already writes to, so "the attack receives -1 prowess" and "the defender gets +1 prowess against this strike" are one code path either way. `bodyModifier` is *not* read in passive mode — a passive body reduction against the attacker instead composes with the pre-existing `body-check-modifier` `scope: "bearer-combat"` `when: { "bodyCheck.fromFailedStrike": true }` (Flame of Udûn ba-58 precedent), which already covers both the creature-body-check and CvCC `attacker-character`-body-check cases passive `modify-attack` does not reach. The new `on-event: "bearer-strike-defeated"` fires per-strike (not at combat finalization, unlike `bearer-wounded`) immediately after a strike against the item's bearer resolves as a genuine parry (captured as `strikeFailedAgainstDefender`/`defResult === 'success'` *before* any discard-item/absorb-wound/take-prisoner override that substitutes a different outcome for a wound) — `dischargeBearerStrikeDefeatedItems` (`combat-strike.ts`) scans the struck character's items for a self-discard `move` apply on this event and discards matches into the owner's discard pile, so a later strike within the *same* attack no longer benefits from an already-discarded item. Card: "Playable on your Ringwraith or a Ringwraith follower. Each strike against the Ringwraith receives -1 body and -1 prowess. Discard Morgul-blade after a strike against the Ringwraith fails. Cannot be duplicated on a given Ringwraith. Cannot be included in a Balrog's deck." — "a Ringwraith follower" is itself always a Ringwraith-race avatar (CoE 2.II.2.1.R4), so a single `play-target` `filter: { "target.race": "ringwraith" }` covers both halves of "playable on"; "cannot be duplicated on a given Ringwraith" is `{ "$not": { "target.itemNames": { "$includes": "Morgul-blade" } } }` on the same filter (the existing `target.itemNames` play-target context, no new field needed).

- `swap-new-site` (Winds of Wrath td-82) — a hazard short-event substitutes a site card from the **hazard player's own** location deck for a moving company's already-declared `destinationSite`, overriding CoE 2.II.7's normal rule that a company's new site always comes from its own owner's deck. `requiresDestinationSitePathIncludes` (array of {@link RegionType}) gates both the company's current destination and the replacement on their static `sitePath`. Legal-action enumeration is inlined in the hazard short-event branch of `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts` (guarded by the effect's presence): one `play-hazard` action per matching site left in the hazard player's own `siteDeck`, each carrying `PlayHazardAction.replacementSiteInstanceId`. `handleSwapNewSite` (`mh-hazard-play.ts`) returns the original destination untapped to its own owner's location deck (mirrors `clearPlannedMovement` — never entered, only declared), installs the replacement as the company's new untapped `destinationSite`, and refreshes the M/H phase state's cached `destinationSiteName`/`destinationSiteType` (already resolved earlier in the phase) to describe it; `resolvedSitePath` is untouched (a movement-path record, not a site-identity one). New `player-state` context field **`player.sameLocationDeckTypeAsOpponent`** (`buildPlayerStateContext`, `legal-actions/organization.ts`) — `true` when `isMinionOrBalrog` agrees for the player and their opponent (CoE 1.4.W1/R1: two Wizards or two Ringwraiths always match). The `player-state` play-condition, previously read only by resource short-events, is now also wired into the M/H phase's play-hazards step for hazard short-events via `playerStateGateMet` (imported from `organization.ts` into `movement-hazard.ts`) — a no-op for any existing card lacking the play-condition. Card: "Playable if Doors of Night is in play and opponent is using the same type of location deck (minion/hero) as yourself. Replace the new site card of a moving company with a Coastal Sea [{c}] in its site path with a card from your location deck that has a Coastal Sea [{c}] in its site path." — `play-condition` (`requires: "card-in-play"`, `cardName: "Doors of Night"`) + `play-condition` (`requires: "player-state"`, `condition: { "player.sameLocationDeckTypeAsOpponent": true }`) + `swap-new-site` `requiresDestinationSitePathIncludes: ["coastal"]`.

- `play-condition` `requires: "region-movement"` + `roll-then-swap-new-site` (Chance of Being Lost dm-49) — a roll-gated variant of `swap-new-site` above: the replacement only happens if a 2d6 roll, modified by -2 per ranger-skilled character in the moving company, exceeds a threshold, and the eligible replacement pool is gated by **region adjacency** (same region as the destination, or a region adjacent to it — `regionAndAdjacentRegions`/`regionAdjacentSwapEligibleSites`, `reducer-utils.ts`) rather than a static site-path region-type match. `region-movement` (`checkRegionMovement`, `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) is a bare `movementType === 'region'` gate — like `region-through-or-leave` (Cruel Caradhras td-9) but with no named-region restriction. Because the roll's outcome (and thus whether any swap happens at all) isn't known until after the card is played, this doesn't fit `swap-new-site`'s "offer one action per candidate site up front" shape; instead `handleRollThenSwapNewSite` (`mh-hazard-play.ts`) discards the card immediately (like `swap-new-site`, and consistent with every hazard short-event that bypasses the chain — Pilfer Anything Unwatched as-33) and enqueues a generic `dice-check` pending resolution (`modifiers: [{ kind: "constant", value: rangerCount * rangerModifier }]`, `comparison: "gt"`, `continuation: { kind: "dequeue-only" }`) whose `onPass` is a new `TriggeredAction` verb, `offer-swap-new-site`. That branch (`applyDiceCheckBranch`, `pending-reducers.ts`) computes the eligible replacement sites for the moving company (`ctx.targetCompanyId`) and, only if the list is non-empty, enqueues a second pending-resolution kind, `swap-new-site-choice` (`companyId`), so the hazard player picks one (`swapNewSiteChoiceActions` / `applySwapNewSiteChoiceResolution`, mirroring the two-stage roll-then-choice shape of `riddling-attempt` → `riddling-guess`). A failed roll, or a passed roll with zero eligible sites, ends the effect with no further consequence — the "must replace" clause simply has nothing to bite on. `applySwapNewSiteChoiceResolution` performs the same site-identity mechanics as `handleSwapNewSite` (original destination returns untapped to its own owner's deck, replacement installed as the company's new untapped `destinationSite`, cached `destinationSiteName`/`destinationSiteType` refreshed) but from within a pending-resolution apply rather than the initial play-hazard handler. Card: "Playable on a moving company using region movement if opponent is using the same type of location deck (minion/hero) as yourself. Make a roll modified by -2 for each ranger in the company. If the result is greater than 6, you must replace company's new site card with a different site from your location deck that is located in the same region or an adjacent region as the company's new site." — `play-condition` (`requires: "region-movement"`) + `play-condition` (`requires: "player-state"`, `condition: { "player.sameLocationDeckTypeAsOpponent": true }`) + `roll-then-swap-new-site` (`threshold: 6, rangerModifier: -2`).
- `acts-as-site` + `declare-virtual-site-movement` (Wondrous Maps td-171) — a permanent-event resource card that, once played, functions as its bearer company's *site*: "Playable at the end of the organization phase on a company using region cards with the last one being a Shadow-land [{s}]. … This card is used as a site card, Ruins & Lairs [{R}] (automatic-attack: Orcs—4 strikes at 7 prowess, items: (minor, major), cards opponent draws: 3, you draw: 1). The company may only leave the site using region movement. Discard Wondrous Maps when the company moves to a new site." `acts-as-site` (`types/effects.ts`) declares the printed site-box data (siteType, automatic attacks, playable resources, resource/hazard draws) plus movement-legality requirements (`requiredMovementType`, `requiredLastRegionType`) and ongoing site rules (`leaveRequiresRegionMovement`, `discardOnLeaveSite`) directly on the card's own `effects` array. At card-pool load time (`data/index.ts`, `synthesizeActsAsSiteDefinition`) every such card gets a companion `SiteCard`-shaped definition synthesized into `state.cardPool` under id `` `${cardId}-site` `` (carrying the same effect object in its own `effects`, so either side of a move can read the requirements without stripping the id suffix); its `region`/`nearestHaven` are deliberately empty so `buildMovementMap` never indexes it into the shared region graph — unreachable as a generic destination, reachable only via this card's own apply. `declare-virtual-site-movement` (a `TriggeredAction`, resolved in `chain-reducer.ts`'s `resolvePermanentEvent` self-enters-play loop) sets the target company's `destinationSite` to the resolving card's own instance — sharing one instance ID between the card's `cardsInPlay` entry (definitionId: the resource-event card) and the company's `destinationSite`/`currentSite` (definitionId: the synthesized site companion); no site-deck card is drawn. `resolveInstanceId` (`types/state.ts`) now checks company sites *before* `cardsInPlay` so both structural readings of the shared instance resolve correctly — a no-op reordering for every other card, since no other card has one instance ID live in two zones simultaneously. The declared path's legality is validated in `handleRevealNewSite` (`mh-steps.ts`): when either endpoint carries `acts-as-site`, the movement type must equal `requiredMovementType` (destination side) / must be `region` when `leaveRequiresRegionMovement` (origin side), and the destination's resolved path must end in `requiredLastRegionType`, or the `declare-path` action is rejected (CoE 2.IV.i.2 — mirrors Master of Esgaroth td-135's deferred site-type check). Because the synthesized companion's `region` is empty, `revealNewSiteActions` (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) gained a parallel region-movement branch for either endpoint being `acts-as-site`: the origin side substitutes the company's `virtualSiteRegionName`; the destination side enumerates every region of the required type reachable within range (no single fixed destination region exists). `planMovementActions` (`legal-actions/organization-companies.ts`) needs the same origin substitution to offer any onward destination at all from a virtual site — it patches a local copy of the movement map's `siteRegion` before calling `getReachableSites`, and filters results to `movementType === 'region'` for `leaveRequiresRegionMovement`. New `Company.virtualSiteRegionName` (`types/state-cards.ts`) records which named region the company is actually "in" while at a virtual site (the last entry of the resolved path), captured on arrival in `endCompanyMH`'s (`mh-hazard-play.ts`) Step 8; the same function intercepts Step 8's site-of-origin teardown so a departing `acts-as-site` origin with `discardOnLeaveSite` never touches the site deck/site-discard-pile — instead its own `cardsInPlay` instance is discarded. Every other site-derived behavior (automatic attacks, item playability, resource/hazard draws, hazard-limit computation) needs no special-casing: all of it already resolves site data via `defById(state, company.currentSite.definitionId)` / `isSiteCard(...)`, which treats the synthesized companion exactly like a printed site once it is a real `cardPool` entry. The `target: "company"` `play-target` filter context for permanent events (`organization-events.ts`) gained a `target.moving` field (`company.destinationSite !== null || !!company.specialMovement`) for "on a company [not already moving]", mirroring the short-event `endOfOrgEligibility`'s existing `company.moving`.

- `ahunt-attack` `detainmentAgainstMinion: true` + `creature-alt-event` `returnToHandOption: true` (Spider of the Môrlat dm-110) — two small additions for a dual creature/permanent-event whose ahunt-attack sub-effect needs its own detainment rule and whose permanent-event mode's only "tap" ability is a plain return to hand, not a short-event conversion. Previously `buildAhuntCombat` (`mh-steps.ts`) computed `isDetainmentAttack` from race/alignment alone, with no way for one `ahunt-attack` entry on a card to declare "(detainment against minion companies)" independently of the card's own direct creature-attack mode (which is not detainment) — a card-level `combat-detainment` effect would have wrongly leaked onto that other mode. `detainmentAgainstMinion: true` on the `AhuntAttackEffect` short-circuits `buildAhuntCombat`'s detainment computation to `isMinionOrBalrog(defendingPlayer) && !playerConvertsDetainmentToNormal(...)`, scoped to that one ahunt-attack alone. Separately, `creature-alt-event`'s existing `persistent: true` (§56d) means "no tap-to-short-event conversion — stays in play until its own rules discard it," which doesn't fit a card whose only in-play action is "you can return this to your hand, counting one against the hazard limit." `returnToHandOption: true` (combinable with `persistent`) adds a third parallel action alongside `tap-alt-permanent-event`/`attack-alt-permanent-event`: a new `return-alt-permanent-event` action (`returnAltPermanentEventActions`, `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`; `handleReturnAltPermanentEvent`, `mh-hazard-play.ts`) offered during the opponent's M/H phase for any untapped matching permanent-event, charging the hazard limit exactly like `tap-alt-permanent-event` but moving the card to the hazard player's **hand** instead of discarding it, and resolving with no chain entry (there is no on-tap short-event to run — the return is the entire ability). Card: "May be played as a hazard creature (with two strikes) or as a permanent-event. As a creature, she may be played at Dol Guldur and The Sulfur-deeps. If Doors of Night is in play, she may also be keyed to Southern Mirkwood, Heart of Mirkwood, or Woodland Realm; or at any adjacent site of The Sulfur-deeps. If played as a permanent-event, all Spider attacks receive +1 strike. Additionally, any company moving in Southern Mirkwood, Heart of Mirkwood, Woodland Realm, Dagorlad, or Brown Lands faces a Spider attack of 2 strikes with 10 prowess (detainment against minion companies). You can return Spider of the Môrlat as a permanent-event to your hand—which counts as one against the hazard limit." — keying uses dm-107's `siteNames`/`adjacentToSiteNames` fields (base cost `siteNames: ["Dol Guldur", "The Sulfur-deeps"]`, plus two Doors-of-Night-gated alternates for the named regions and for any site adjacent to The Sulfur-deeps); the "+1 strike to all Spider attacks" is an ordinary `stat-modifier` `target: "all-attacks"` `when: { "enemy.race": "spider" }` that — being sourced from the same card sitting in `cardsInPlay` — self-applies to the card's own `ahunt-attack` (printed 2 strikes → 3 while she is in play), with no extra gating needed since the ahunt-attack can only ever fire while the card is actually in `cardsInPlay` (i.e. in permanent-event mode).

- `return-chain-entry-card-to-hand` + `add-constraint` `constraint: "nazgul-boost-pending"` (Morgul-horse tw-63) — a two-mode hazard short-event, both modes reusing existing primitives. Mode 1: a new `on-event: self-enters-play` apply, `return-chain-entry-card-to-hand`, played as a **chain response** while a Nazgûl permanent-event's tap-to-short-event conversion (`creature-alt-event` §56c, `handleTapAltPermanentEvent`) sits unresolved on the chain — its card already moved to its owner's discard pile at declaration, per the existing tap-conversion handler. `select: 'target'` + a generic `filter` (evaluated against `{ target: { cardType, eventType, name, keywords } }`, extended here with `keywords` for the first time) name which pending entry qualifies; `playReturnChainEntryCardActions` (`legal-actions/chain.ts`, mirroring `playSkillCancelChainActions`'s shape) only offers entries the *same* player declared (`entry.declaredBy === playerId`) whose card is still actually sitting in that player's discard pile — excluding a live creature attack or a permanent-event still entering play, neither of which has been discarded yet. Declared via the ordinary `play-short-event` action (`targetInstanceId` = the pending entry's card instance), routed through the existing `handlePlayShortEvent` (`reducer-events.ts`) exactly like an environment-cancel play. Resolution differs from `cancel-chain-entry`, though: `resolveEntry` (`chain-reducer.ts`) checks whether the resolving entry's card carries a `return-chain-entry-card-to-hand` apply and, if so, calls the new `resolveReturnChainEntryCardToHand` instead of `resolveEnvironmentCancel` — it moves the card from discard back to hand **without negating the targeted entry**, so the Nazgûl's own on-tap ability (e.g. Akhôrahil's -1 body) still resolves normally; only the card's own final resting place changes. Mode 2 is the alternate-keying grant already built for Fell Beast (tw-33, `nazgul-boost-pending` `keyingRegionTypes`) — zero new code, just the same standalone (`state.chain === null`) `on-event: self-enters-play` → `add-constraint` shape with `keyingRegionTypes: ["shadow"]` and no `keyingSiteTypes`/`strikesModifier`/`prowessModifier`/`grantAttackerChoosesDefenders` (the printed text grants only alternate region keying, no combat boost); the "must be played as the first declared action or else this card returns to hand" clause is the exact same `nazgul-boost-pending` unconsumed-at-company-M/H-phase-end handling Fell Beast already relies on (`finalizeCompanyMH`, `mh-hazard-play.ts`), confirmed by an identically-worded CRF ruling for both cards. Card: "This card allows you to place a tapped Nazgûl permanent-event back into your hand instead of discarding it. Alternatively, allows a Nazgûl to be played keyed to a Shadow-land [{s}]." CRF: "To bring a Nazgûl permanent-event back into your hand, Morgul-horse must be declared after tapping the Nazgûl is declared and before it resolves."
- `modify-current-strike-prowess` on a **short** hazard-event (not just permanent-events like Dragon's Curse td-16) + `force-attacker-kill-on-resolution` (Fury of the Iron Crown tw-492) — a combat-reactive short event that boosts one strike's prowess *and* forces the attacking creature out of play regardless of the strike's real outcome. `isCombatReactiveShortEvent` (`reducer-utils.ts`) replaces the old card-specific `isCombat(CompanyStatModifier)ShortEvent` checks duplicated in `combat-hazard-play.ts`/`legal-actions/combat.ts`: any `short` hazard-event whose `self-enters-play-combat` on-event declares `add-constraint`/`company-stat-modifier` (tw-115), `modify-current-strike-prowess`, or `force-attacker-kill-on-resolution` now resolves-and-discards instead of attaching. `handleCombatPlayHazard` threads a `workingCombat` draft across a card's on-event effects (a card may declare more than one) instead of overwriting `state.combat` per-effect. The new `force-attacker-kill-on-resolution` apply (`excludeRace?`, `offerCardName?`) is recorded on a new `CombatState.forcedCreatureKillOnResolution` field and consumed in `combat-finalize.ts`'s `finalizeCombat`: a new disposal branch (checked before the detainment-§3.II.3 downgrade and the normal `allDefeated` kill-pile branch) routes the attacking creature into the defender's kill pile whenever `combat.creatureRace !== excludeRace`, independent of the true `allDefeated` value — which is left untouched everywhere else so on-event effects keyed to genuine defeat status (`attack-not-defeated`, etc.) still evaluate off the real strike outcome. The MELE §8.37 trophy-offer gate was widened to `(allDefeated || forcedKillApplied)` since the card's own text explicitly permits the defender to take the creature as a trophy instead of scoring it. If the forced kill fires and `offerCardName` names a card the defender then holds in hand, `finalizeCombat` enqueues a new `named-card-play-offer` pending-resolution kind (`types/pending.ts`; legal actions `namedCardPlayOfferActions` and apply `applyNamedCardPlayOfferResolution` in `legal-actions/pending.ts`/`pending-reducers.ts`, wired through `pending-handlers.ts`) — a `play-named-card-offer` action attaches the named card from hand onto any character in the given company (mirroring `ring-play-offer`'s item-attach shape), or the actor passes. Also fixed a real gap in `isCardNameInPlayOrCharacters` (`reducer-utils.ts`, used by `card-not-in-play`/`card-in-play` play-conditions): it previously only scanned characters-by-name and `cardsInPlay`, missing named cards attached as a character's `items` (e.g. a hero-resource-item like The Iron Crown) — now mirrors `countCopiesInPlay`'s item scan. Card: "Unique. May not be played if The Iron Crown is in play. The prowess of one strike of an attack by an Orc, Troll, Man, or Nazgûl creature is increased by +4. After the attack is resolved, if the creature is not a Nazgûl: the creature is removed from play (defender receives the marshalling points); and, in addition, if the defender has The Iron Crown in his hand, he may immediately play it with a character in the defending company." — `play-window` (combat/resolve-strike) + `play-condition` (`requires: "card-not-in-play"`, `cardName: "The Iron Crown"`, now checked in `combatHazardPermanentPlays`) + `play-target` (`filter: { "attack.race": { "$in": ["orc","troll","man","ringwraith"] } }`, per the DSL-over-magic-keyword policy rather than extending the single-race `combat-creature-race` condition) + two `self-enters-play-combat` on-events (`modify-current-strike-prowess value: 4`, `force-attacker-kill-on-resolution excludeRace: "ringwraith" offerCardName: "The Iron Crown"`).

- `replace-item-on-play` + `modify-attack` `grantsDefenderFreeStrikeAssignment` + `corruption-check-boost` (Phial of Galadriel dm-176) — three primitives for a unique item that upgrades another item already in play. **(1) `replace-item-on-play`** (`itemName`, `tapCompanionNamed`) — the bearer-eligibility gate itself ("bearer of Star-glass") is just the existing item-play bearer `play-target` `filter` reading a new `target.itemNames` context field (`legal-actions/site.ts`, via the shared `defNamesOf` helper — the same aggregation `company.itemNames`/The Precious tw-98 already used one layer up); this effect only adds the companion-tap/removal mechanics. The item-play loop (`legal-actions/site.ts`) additionally requires an untapped, differently-instanced companion named `tapCompanionNamed` in the same company, resolving its instance id into a new `PlayHeroResourceAction.companionCharacterId` field. `handleSitePlayHeroResource` (`reducer-site.ts`, just before its final return) taps that companion and splices `itemName` out of the bearer's `items` into `PlayerState.outOfPlayPile` flagged `removedFromGame: true` — the CoE "remove from the game" zone (distinct from a discard pile), matching the card's non-English print ("retirez ... du jeu"/"elimina del juego") despite the English "remove from play" wording. The ordinary tap-on-play flow (bearer + site, since the card carries no `no-tap-on-play`) is untouched. **(2)** `modify-attack` gained `grantsDefenderFreeStrikeAssignment: true` (whole-attack item scope): activating sets `CombatState.defenderFreeStrikeAssignment` — the flag `assignStrikeActions` (`legal-actions/combat.ts`) already honored for the passive `free-strike-assignment` environment (Cloudless Day td-104) to drop its untapped-only strike-assignment gate — and, mirroring the existing `removeAttackerChoosesDefenders` ally path, clears `CombatState.attackerChoosesDefenders`/hands assignment back to the defender if the attack already carried that rule. The item `modify-attack` `when` context (`modifyAttackWhenContext`, `legal-actions/combat.ts`) gained `attack.siteKeyingTypes` alongside the pre-existing `attack.keying`/`attack.siteKeyed` — `cancel-attack`/`cancel-strike` already exposed this field via `buildAttackKeyingCtx`; it was missing here until a card needed to gate on specific site-keying types (Dark-hold/Shadow-hold) rather than the boolean "keyed to some site". **(3) `corruption-check-boost`** (`cost`, `value`) — a tap-item bonus to a corruption check made by its own bearer, riding the existing `support-corruption-check` action (`SupportCorruptionCheckAction` gained `supportingItemInstanceId`, mutually exclusive with `supportingCharacterId`) and the same one-shot `check-modifier` constraint every corruption-check modifier already produces — unlike the CoE 7.1.1 character-support sibling it rides alongside, it is not gated by the pending check's `allowSupport` flag and is legal on the bearer's own check. Wired into both windows a corruption check can be pending: the mid-game unified pending-resolution window (`corruptionCheckEntryActions`/`pending-reducers.ts`) and the Free Council support window (`supportActions`/`legal-actions/free-council.ts`, `handleSupportCorruptionCheck`/`reducer-free-council.ts`). Used by Phial of Galadriel (dm-176): "Playable on a non-Wizard, non-Dwarf bearer of Star-glass at a Haven [{H}] in the same company as an untapped Galadriel. Tap Galadriel, replace Star-glass with Phial of Galadriel, and remove Star-glass from play. Tap Phial to cancel any Undead attack against the bearer's company. Tap Phial to modify the prowess of any hazard creature attack against the bearer's company keyed to a Dark-domain [{d}], Shadow-land [{s}], Dark-hold [{D}], or Shadow-hold [{S}] by -2 — you choose targets of such an attack's strikes (regardless of tapped status, wounded status, and the normal abilities of the attack). Tap Phial to give +2 to any corruption check by its bearer. Cannot be transferred." — `cancel-attack` (`cost: {tap:self}`, `when: enemy.race undead`) reuses the Star-glass tw-330 shape but keeps the item's own status as the gate (`cost: {tap:"self"}`, not `{tap:"bearer"}`), and `play-flag: no-transfer` is the pre-existing Ent-draughts tw-227 primitive.

## Partially implemented

- `mp-modifier` — works for elimination pile with numeric values only; expression strings are ignored (`packages/shared/src/engine/recompute-derived.ts`)
- `on-event` — infrastructure exists but `matchesTrigger()` is stubbed to always return false; no triggers fire (`packages/shared/src/engine/chain-reducer.ts`). Exception: `self-enters-play` with `discard-cards-in-play` is implemented directly in the reducer play handlers for permanent events (`packages/shared/src/engine/reducer.ts`). The environment-cancel pattern is expressed generically as `on-event self-enters-play` → `move` `select: filter-all`, `from: in-play`, `to: discard` with a definition `filter`; `resolvePermanentEvent` (`chain-reducer.ts`) runs it on resolution for both resource permanent events (Gates of Morning tw-243) and hazard permanent events (Doors of Night tw-28 / le-110). Because there is a single `hazard-event` cardType but two resource-event cardTypes, a hazard environment that "discards all resource environment cards" must filter `cardType: { $in: ["hero-resource-event", "minion-resource-event"] }` + `keywords $includes environment` to also catch minion resource environments (Skies of Fire le-228, which "acts as Gates of Morning"); the mirror resource card filters `cardType: "hazard-event"` + environment. "Cannot be duplicated" is `duplication-limit` scope `game` max 1, enforced by card **name** (`countCopiesInPlay`) so the tw-28 and le-110 printings share one in-play copy. Used by Doors of Night (le-110), certified alongside a corrected tw-28 filter.
- Combat-rule effects — `combat-attacker-chooses-defenders`, `combat-multi-attack`, and `combat-cancel-attack-by-tap` are implemented. Site auto-attack `combatRules` strings `cannot-be-canceled` (sets `uncancelable`), `wound-eliminates` (immediate elimination on wound, no body check), and `weapons-ineffective` (sets `CombatState.weaponsIneffective` — "weapons do not modify prowess against these strikes", e.g. Trap/Lava Flows/Rock Fall) are implemented (`reducer-site.ts`, `reducer-combat.ts`). The item `modify-attack` `when` context (`legal-actions/combat.ts`) exposes `attack.weaponsIneffective` so a whole-attack `modify-attack` can target either an Orc/Troll attack (`enemy.race`) or such a weapons-ineffective attack. Used by Dwarven Light-stone (dm-168): `item-play-site` (any Under-deeps site) + `under-deeps-roll-modifier` (value 2) + `modify-attack` (`cost: {tap: self}`, `prowessModifier: -2`, `when: $or [enemy.race in orc/orcs/troll/trolls, attack.weaponsIneffective]`). The same `modify-attack` effect may combine `prowessModifier` with `strikesModifier` in one entry (both already-independent resolver fields — `combat-actions.ts` applies each and clamps the strike floor to 1) rather than needing two effects. Used by Noldo-lantern (dm-175), the dm-168 sibling: identical `item-play-site` + `under-deeps-roll-modifier`, plus `modify-attack` (`cost: {tap: self}`, `prowessModifier: -2`, `strikesModifier: -1`, `when: enemy.race $in [undead, ringwraith, orc, troll]`) — "-2 prowess and one less strike (to a minimum of one) to any Undead, Nazgûl, Orc, or Troll attack".
- `combat-one-strike-per-character` `onlyWounded` + `combat-body-check-modifier` + `combat-tap-to-cancel-strike` — a hazard creature that strikes each **wounded** company character, boosts every resulting body check, and lets untapped characters tap to protect a wounded ally. `combat-one-strike-per-character` gains `onlyWounded: true`: `strikesTotal = wounded (inverted) characters`, one strike pre-assigned to each wounded character (unwounded characters never struck); with no wounded characters the creature is discarded without combat (no trivial "all strikes defeated" kill MP). `combat-body-check-modifier` (`value`) is threaded into `CombatState.bodyCheckModifier` at initiation and added to every character body-check roll in `handleBodyCheckRoll` (on top of the already-wounded +1). `combat-tap-to-cancel-strike` opens a `cancel-by-tap` sub-phase (`CombatState.cancelStrikeAgainstWounded`, `cancelByTapRemaining` = untapped-character count): each untapped company character may tap to remove one pre-assigned strike, choosing the wounded target via the `cancel-by-tap` action's `strikeCharacterId` (`cancelByTapActions` in `legal-actions/combat.ts`, `handleCancelByTap` in `combat-cancel.ts`); canceling all strikes ends combat and discards the creature. When no cancel window applies (no untapped defender / no such ability) the creature opens directly at `choose-strike-order`/`resolve-strike` like a site "each character faces one strike" attack (`eachCharacterFacesOneStrike` gates the whole-attack cancel window). Implemented in `chain-reducer.ts` `initiateCreatureCombat`. Used by Carrion Feeders (ba-11): "Each wounded character faces one strike. All body checks resulting from successful strikes are modified by an additional +1. Each untapped character in the company may tap to cancel a strike against a wounded character."
- `flee-from-strike` + `skip-next-untap` constraint — a from-hand combat **permanent-event** the defending player plays during the `resolve-strike` sub-phase to make a named character flee a strike he would likely lose. `fleeFromStrikeActions` (`legal-actions/combat.ts`, wired into the resolve-strike window) offers the `flee-from-strike` action when the current strike is assigned to a **character** (not ally) whose name equals `effect.characterName`, the strike's prowess (agent rolled total when known, else `combat.strikeProwess`) is **strictly greater** than that character's `effectiveStats.prowess`, and no copy of the card is already in play (`countCopiesInPlay` by name — "Cannot be duplicated" via a `duplication-limit` scope `game`). `handleFleeFromStrike` (`combat-actions.ts`, registered in `COMBAT_HANDLERS`) cancels the current strike (result `'canceled'`, advances via `nextStrikePhase`/`finalizeCombat`), taps the named character if untapped, moves the card from hand into the controller's `cardsInPlay` (`attachedTo` the character), and installs a one-shot `skip-next-untap` active constraint (`scope: until-cleared`, new kind in `types/pending.ts`, pass-through in `legal-actions/pending.ts`). `performUntap` (`reducer-untap.ts`) collects `skip-next-untap` constraints, holds the character tapped for that one untap (reusing the `cannotUntapIds` path), then after the untap sweep removes the constraint and discards the in-play card to its owner's discard pile. Used by Fled into Darkness (ba-18): "Playable before the strike sequence on The Balrog facing a strike with a prowess higher than his. The strike is canceled and The Balrog taps, if untapped. The next time The Balrog would otherwise untap, make him tapped instead and discard this card. Cannot be duplicated." — `flee-from-strike` `{ characterName: "The Balrog" }` + `duplication-limit` scope `game` max 1.
- `roll-untap-site` + `skip-next-untap-on-play` — a pair of on-play effects on a resource **permanent-event** played on a character at a site (`play-target character` sage-skill+untapped, `play-target site` siteType Border-hold/Free-hold, `play-flag tapped-site-only`, `play-flag tap-character-on-play`). During the site phase this rides the shared `legal-actions/site.ts` char+site play-target path; since the card's text (unlike site-tapping siblings such as Rescue Prisoners tw-315 or Andúril tw-192) declares no "during the site phase" restriction, rule 2.1.1 keeps it playable in any other phase too, evaluated by a dedicated `roll-untap-site`-gated branch in `legal-actions/organization-events.ts`'s `playPermanentEventActions` (mirroring The White Tree tw-348 / Return of the King tw-316). On play (`resolvePermanentEvent`, `chain-reducer.ts`), **`roll-untap-site`** enqueues a generic `dice-check`: the card player rolls 2d6 + the target character's effective mind (`effectiveStats.mind` ?? printed mind; a Wizard's printed mind is `null` → 0) + `wizardBonus` when the character's race is `wizard`; if the modified total is strictly greater than `threshold`, the **new `untap-site` onPass verb** (a branch in `applyDiceCheckBranch`, `pending-reducers.ts`, locating the character's company and untapping its `currentSite`) untaps the site. The roll surfaces as its own explicit `resolve-dice-check` action. **`skip-next-untap-on-play`** installs a one-shot `skip-next-untap` active constraint on the target character (the ba-18 constraint kind); the character was tapped by the play cost, so at his next untap `performUntap` (`reducer-untap.ts`) holds him tapped once and discards the source card. The `performUntap` skip discard sweep was generalized to find the source card whether it lives in `cardsInPlay` (ba-18) or attached to a character's `items` (a resource permanent-event, dm-130). Used by Fireworks (dm-130): "Ritual. Playable on an untapped sage at a tapped Border-hold [{B}] or Free-hold [{F}]. Tap sage. Make a roll and add the mind of the sage (+10 if a Wizard) — if the result is greater than 12, the site untaps. The next time the sage would otherwise become untapped make him tapped instead and discard this card." — `roll-untap-site` `{ threshold: 12, wizardBonus: 10 }` + `skip-next-untap-on-play` + the shared char+site play-target/flag scaffold (keyword `ritual`).
- `untap-mind-roll` — a game-wide untap-phase restriction carried by a hazard **long-event** in either player's `cardsInPlay`. In `performUntap` (`reducer-untap.ts`), `findUntapMindRollEffect` scans both players' `cardsInPlay`; when found and not suppressed by `noEffectOnMinion` (see below), every tapped character of the untapping (active) player is checked by `isExemptFromUntapMindRoll`: a Wizard (`race === "wizard"`) or a character whose company's current site resolves (`getEffectiveSiteType`) to one of `exemptSiteTypes` untaps normally; every other tapped character stays tapped and a generic `dice-check` is enqueued instead (`modifiers: [{ kind: "constant", value: effectiveMind }]`, `threshold`, `comparison: "gt"`, `onPass: { type: "set-character-status", status: "untapped" }`, scoped `{ kind: "phase", phase: Untap }`). No `onFail` branch — rolling has no downside, so the printed "may instead make a roll" is modeled as an always-enqueued roll rather than an interactive decline; the pending resolution outranks further untap-phase actions (including a phase advance to Organization when the hazard player already passed) until resolved. `noEffectOnMinion: true` skips the whole restriction when the untapping player's alignment is `ringwraith` (the `ahunt-attack`/`faction-influence-restriction` "minion player" precedent — Balrog is not included). Used by Worn and Famished (td-89): "Each non-Wizard character that is not in a Haven [{H}], Free-hold [{F}], or Border-hold [{B}] does not untap normally during his untap phase. Character's player may instead make a roll adding his mind. If the result is greater than 12, he untaps. This card has no effect on a minion player. Cannot be duplicated." — `untap-mind-roll` `{ threshold: 12, exemptSiteTypes: ["haven","free-hold","border-hold"], noEffectOnMinion: true }`, plus the pre-existing `play-restriction unplayable-when opponent.alignment: "ringwraith"` and `duplication-limit` scope `game` max 1 (tw-36/dm-72 "no effect on a minion player" precedent of also blocking play against a Ringwraith opponent outright).
- `cancel-attack` — in-play item sources support cost `{ tap: "self" }` (item taps only; bearer status irrelevant), `{ tap: "bearer" }`, and `{ tap: "self-and-bearer" }` (`legal-actions/combat.ts`, `reducer-combat.ts`). The `when` context exposes `attack.heroCompany` (true only for CvCC where the attacker is a hero-side company) so items can refuse to cancel hero-company combat. Used by Helm of Fear (as-126). The `whenContext` also exposes `attack.siteKeyingTypes` (the site types a creature is keyed to, e.g. `ruins-and-lairs`), `site.type` (the defending company's current site type), and `defender.covert`. A dual-alignment faction may carry `handModeRequiresCovert: true` — the dual-faction cancel with two sources emitted in `cancelAttackActions` and applied by `handleCancelAttackByInPlayFaction`/`handleCancelAttack` (`combat-cancel.ts`): the controlled faction in `cardsInPlay` (paid with the effect's own in-play cost, no covert/alignment gate) and the card in hand played as a minion resource (covert company + Ringwraith player, routes through the chain, always spent by discarding from hand). The in-play cost is either `cost: { discard: "self" }` (discard the faction, Wild Hounds wh-40) or `cost: { tap: "self" }` (tap the faction in place, leaving it in play — Beasts of the Wood wh-38, which requires the faction untapped). A faction `playableAt` entry's `when` clause now also sees `site.regionType` (folded from the region card via `siteMatchesEntry`), and the faction-influence resolver context exposes `controller.wizard` (the player's Wizard avatar name) for "Standard Modifications: if <Wizard> is your Wizard (+N)". The cancel `when` context also exposes `attack.keyingRegionNames` (the specific region names a creature is keyed to *by name*, populated in `chain-reducer.ts`, threaded through `combat.attackKeyingRegionNames`). Used by Wild Hounds (wh-40): playable at a Ruins & Lairs in a Wilderness (tapped or untapped), +3 if Radagast is your Wizard, discard/covert-resource cancel of an automatic-attack at a R&L or an attack keyed to Wilderness/R&L. Also by Beasts of the Wood (wh-38): a non-unique Animal faction playable via a `playableAt` `{ any: true, when }` entry at any non-Haven/non-Dark-hold site in Woodland Realm/Western Mirkwood/Heart of Mirkwood/Southern Mirkwood/Fangorn/Cardolan, +3 if Radagast, tap in-play (or covert-hand) cancel of an attack keyed by name to one of those six regions.
- `cancel-attack` `when` context gains `bearer.skills` for in-play **item** sources (Magic Ring of Nature tw-273) — the item loop in `resolveCancelAttackActions` (`legal-actions/combat.ts`) now merges the bearing character's *printed* skills (`charDef.skills`, not `getEffectiveSkills`) into `bearer.skills` before evaluating the effect's `when`, mirroring the existing `cancel-strike` convention so a ring's own `grant-skill` never satisfies its own "if the bearer is already a &lt;skill&gt;" gate. Used by Magic Ring of Nature (tw-273): `grant-skill` (`"ranger"`) + `cancel-attack` (`cost: { tap: "bearer" }`, `when: { "bearer.skills": { "$includes": "ranger" } }`) + `duplication-limit` (scope `character`, max 1) — "Gives the bearer ranger skill. If the bearer is already a ranger, he may tap to cancel an attack against his company."
- `modify-attack` `setStrikesTo` + `cancel-attack` `alsoCancelLaterAttack` (dual-mode Balrog combat short-event) — a from-hand `modify-attack` may **set** the attack's strike count to an exact value via `setStrikesTo: N` (never increasing the count, clamped to ≥1) in place of the delta `strikesModifier` ("the attack is reduced to one strike"). A `cancel-attack` carrying `alsoCancelLaterAttack: true` grants, when the cancel resolves on the chain (`apply-dispatcher.ts`), a turn-scoped `free-attack-cancel` constraint on the defending player; `cancelAttackActions` then offers a costless `cancel-attack` (`mode: "free-later-cancel"`) during any *later* combat this turn whose defending company contains The Balrog (`restrictToBalrogCompany`), consumed by `handleCancelAttack` (`combat-cancel.ts`) with no card and no chain. Both the from-hand `modify-attack` and `cancel-attack` `when` contexts (`legal-actions/combat.ts`) now expose `defender.companyContainsBalrog` (the defending company holds The Balrog avatar) and `defender.inPlay` (an attachment-aware in-play-names list for the defending player — `inPlayNamesForPlayerDeep` in `reducer-utils.ts`, catching a permanent event on a character such as Great Shadow ba-62). Used by Darkness Wielded (ba-55): "Playable on an attack against The Balrog's company if Great Shadow is in play … The attack receives -2 prowess, -1 body, and is reduced to one strike. Alternatively, cancel this attack and a latter attack of your choice against his company this turn." — a `modify-attack` (fromHand defender, `-2`/`-1`/`setStrikesTo: 1`) + a costless `cancel-attack` (`alsoCancelLaterAttack`), both gated on `defender.companyContainsBalrog` + `defender.inPlay $includes "Great Shadow"`, plus the shared Balrog sideboard-relocation `move` (ba-78 primitive). "Balrog specific" is a deck-construction keyword only.
- `item-play-site` — supports `sites`/`filter` restriction plus `allowTapped` and `doesNotTapSite` (leaves the site untapped on play; `reducer-site.ts`). Used by Helm of Fear (as-126), playable at a tapped/untapped Barad-dûr without tapping it. Aiglos (dm-166) combines the whole existing weapon toolkit on one hero item with no new machinery: `filter` matching under-deeps sites of *either* hold type via `$in` on `site.siteType`; warrior-gated prowess with a base/`overrides` pair (`+2`, `+5 when inPlay: Doors of Night`, both `max: 11`); a warrior-gated `body +1 max 10` `stat-modifier` (the structural `prowessModifier`/`bodyModifier` fields stay as printed-attribute metadata — `recompute-derived.ts` suppresses them once the item declares any `stat-modifier`); a warrior-gated `enemy-modifier` body `subtract: 2` ("-2 to target's body", firing on both creature body checks and the CvCC defender path like Ancient Black Axe); and an item-borne `direct-influence +3` gated on warrior AND (`influence-check`/`target.race: elf` OR `faction-influence-check`/`faction.race: elf`) — the first *item*-sourced conditional DI, collected by the same `collectCharacterEffects` paths as Firiel's/Glorfindel's character-intrinsic versions. `sites` (name match) and `filter` (site-card condition) may be supplied **together** — the engine ORs them (`matchesSiteList || matchesFilter` in both `legal-actions/site.ts` and `reducer-utils.ts::isCardPlayableAtSiteDef`), so a card whose restriction is "at <named site> or <site type>" uses both. Used by Gold Ring that Sauron Fancies (le-312): `sites: ["Bag End"]` + `filter` (`site.siteType: ruins-and-lairs` AND `site.playableResources $includes gold-ring`) — playable at Bag End or any Ruins & Lairs where gold rings are playable.
- `ring-test-table` / `ring-test-search` (gold-ring test, Rules 9.21–9.22) — a minion/hero gold-ring item declares `ring-test-table` (rows `{ category, min, max }` over the 2d6 roll total; `category` ∈ `lesser-ring`/`magic-ring`/`dwarven-ring`/`spirit-ring`/`the-one-ring`) and, optionally, `ring-test-search` (`category`) to let the player pull an eligible replacement ring from play deck / discard pile as well as hand. The forced test is enqueued (e.g. via `on-event untap-phase-end → force-check corruption`, or the Darkhaven auto-test); resolving it discards the gold ring, rolls 2d6 with the usual item/FW modifiers, computes eligible categories via `eligibleRingCategories` (`legal-actions/pending.ts`), and enqueues a `ring-play-offer` pending resolution carrying `eligibleCategories` and (only when a `ring-test-search` covers an eligible category) `searchCategories`. `ringPlayOfferActions` offers `play-ring-after-test` for each special ring (subtype `special`, keyword `ring`) whose category keyword is eligible — from hand always, and from deck/discard only under `searchCategories`; `applyRingPlayOfferResolution` attaches the chosen ring to the former gold-ring bearer (or `pass`). Duplication-limit `scope: "character"` is honored. Used by Gleaming Gold Ring (le-311), The Least of Gold Rings (le-315), Gold Ring that Sauron Fancies (le-312, no `ring-test-search` — The One Ring 11+, Dwarven 8+, Magic 1–5, Lesser any), Precious Gold Ring (tw-306, The One Ring 10+, Dwarven 8+, Magic 1–5, Lesser any), and Fair Gold Ring (tw-231, The One Ring 11+, Dwarven 9+, Magic 1–6, Lesser any — data only, no new engine work; its 7–8 gap falls between the Magic and Dwarven bands, so only a Lesser Ring is offered there). The hero route into the same resolution is the Wizard tap-test — see the `enqueue-gold-ring-test` entry below. Tests drive that route through the shared `testGoldRingViaWizard` / `ringPlayOffer` / `offeredRingInstanceIds` helpers (`tests/test-helpers-dispatch.ts`).
- `stat-modifier` prowess `when: { "combat.strikeMode": "tap" }` — a prowess modifier that applies only when the character **taps to face a strike**. `computeCombatProwess` (`recompute-derived.ts`) now takes the strike-resolution `strikeMode` (`'tap'`/`'untap'`/`'dodge'`/`'reroll'`) and exposes it as `combat.strikeMode`; the creature-facing path (`combat-strike.ts` `resolveStrikeCore`) passes the actual mode, and the tap/untap need shown by `legal-actions/combat.ts` is computed per-mode. Because `combat.strikeMode` is absent outside strike resolution, the modifier never leaks into effective-stats. Used by Stabbing Tongue of Fire (ba-81): a unique Balrog-specific "special" item — `item-play-site` (`allowTapped: true`, `filter` = `under-deeps` keyword AND `siteType != haven`, i.e. any non-Darkhaven Under-deeps site) + `play-target` character `{ target.name: "The Balrog" }` (may only be borne by The Balrog) + `stat-modifier` prowess +1 `when combat.strikeMode: tap` + two `body-check-modifier` `scope: "bearer-combat"` effects reused verbatim from Flame of Udûn (ba-58): "+1 to body checks from failed strikes against The Balrog" (`when bodyCheck.fromFailedStrike`) and "+1 to the defending character's body check" when The Balrog attacks successfully in CvCC (`when bodyCheck.target: character` + `combat.isCvCC`).
- `play-target` cost `{ check: "corruption", modifier: N, failureMode? }` on a character-targeting hazard short-event — when the chain entry resolves the engine enqueues a corruption check (modifier `N`) on the targeted character (generic path in `chain-reducer.ts`, e.g. Dragon-sickness td-18). The optional `failureMode` refines the failed-check consequence in `applyCorruptionCheckResolution` (`pending-reducers.ts`): `"discard-ring-only"` discards only the bearer's Ring (The Ring's Betrayal); `"discard-instead-of-eliminate"` downgrades any would-be **elimination** outcome to a plain `discard` (character + non-follower possessions to the discard pile, followers freed to general influence). The character-target filter context exposes `target.itemKeywords`/`target.itemSubtypes` (aggregated over borne items) so the play-target can gate on what the character bears. Used by The Roving Eye (le-135): playable on a non-Wizard, non-Ringwraith character bearing a Palantír / greater item / non-gold ring; forces a corruption check at -2; would-be elimination becomes a discard.
  A companion `company.itemNames` field (The Precious tw-98) is now exposed on this same M/H character-targeting filter context (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`, aggregated once per company via `defNamesOf` over every member's items) so a card can gate on "in the same company as `<named item>`" without being the bearer — combined with `{ "$not": { "target.possessions": { "$includes": "..." } } }` to exclude the bearer himself. A new cost field `alsoDiscardItemName` (`types/effects.ts` `ActionCost`) names an item to discard on a **failed** check *in addition to* the target's own fate, even though the item sits on a different company member than the one making the check: `chain-reducer.ts` resolves the name to a concrete instance within the target's company at chain-resolution time (`findCharacterCompany` + a scan of each member's items) and carries it as `alsoDiscardItemId` on the pending `corruption-check` (`types/pending.ts`, `pending.ts` `enqueueCorruptionCheck`). On failure, `applyCorruptionCheckResolution` (`pending-reducers.ts`) pulls that item off its *real* bearer (not the checking character) before the normal discard/eliminate branch runs, then folds its instance into the same `failedPossessions` list used for the character's own items — reusing the existing discard-pile routing (hazard vs. non-hazard ownership split) rather than a bespoke move. Used by The Precious (tw-98): "A character in the same company (hazard player's choice) as The One Ring (not the bearer himself) must make a corruption check modified by -2. If he fails, discard The One Ring along with the target character." Not wired into the Press-gang-capture branch (an unrelated Balrog mechanic that redirects the character's own discard before this code runs) — an acceptable gap since the two mechanics have no realistic overlap.
- `auto-attacks-normal` + `site.siteType` in the attack-stat context — a global (in-play long hazard-event) rule making every automatic-attack at a site whose effective `SiteType` is in `siteTypes` resolve as a **normal** (non-detainment) attack, whichever company defends. Computed by `siteTypeForcesAutoAttacksNormal` (`reducer-utils.ts`, scanning both players' `cardsInPlay`) and OR-ed with the per-player `playerConvertsDetainmentToNormal` into a single `forcesNormalAttacks` flag threaded into `isDetainmentAttack`'s `defenderForcesNormalAttacks` (and guarding the site-forced-detainment OR) at every site auto-attack call site in `reducer-site.ts` (initiate, Moon-Is-Dead / Incite-Defenders duplicates, `buildSiteRepeatedAttackCombat`, dynamic played-auto-attack). The strike-doubling reuses the `site.siteType` gate already exposed on `resolveAttackStrikes` for Awaken Minions (tw-10): `reducer-site.ts` threads the defending company's `effectiveSiteType` into every site automatic-attack `resolveAttackStrikes` call, so a `stat-modifier` with `target: "all-automatic-attacks"` can gate on it (`when: { "site.siteType": { "$in": [...] } }`). Used by Awaken Defenders (le-103): "strikes for each automatic-attack at a Free-hold or Border-hold doubled" (`stat-modifier` strikes `op: multiply` value 2, `when site.siteType $in [free-hold, border-hold]`) + "each detainment automatic-attack at a Free-hold or Border-hold becomes normal" (`auto-attacks-normal siteTypes [free-hold, border-hold]`) + `duplication-limit` scope game. The `site.siteType` gate + `op: multiply` generalise the whole "Awaken" family (Awaken Denizens le-104/tw-9 at Ruins & Lairs, Awaken Minions tw-10 at Shadow/Dark-holds).
- `play-target` target `"stored-item"` + `displace-stored-item` + `mp-in-pile` — a hazard permanent-event played on one of the opponent's **stored items** (an item in the opponent's marshalling-point pile / `killPile`). The legal-action generator (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) emits one `play-hazard` per opponent stored item during the M/H play-hazards step, carrying `targetStoredItemInstanceId` (threaded through the `permanent-event` chain payload). On resolution `resolveDisplaceStoredItem` (`chain-reducer.ts`) returns the stored item from whichever pile holds it to that owner's hand (discarding any attached cards — `returnItemTo: "owner-hand"`) and places the resolving card into that same owner's marshalling-point pile (`selfTo: "owner-mp-pile"`); no instance is lost. The `mp-in-pile` effect (`{ category, value }`) then scores a flat value for whichever player's `killPile` holds the card, in the killPile loop of `recompute-derived.ts`. Used by Neither so Ancient Nor so Potent (dm-73): "Playable on a stored item. Return item to opponent's hand (discarding all attached cards). Place this card in opponent's marshalling point pile. It gives 2 item marshalling points."
- `enqueue-gold-ring-test` (`on-event: self-enters-play` apply, Rule 6.2) — a resource short-event that runs the full active gold-ring test on a chosen ring. The card carries `play-window` (`phase: organization`) + `play-target` `character` filtered on `{ target.skills: { $includes: sage } }` (no tap cost) + `on-event self-enters-play → enqueue-gold-ring-test` (optional `rollModifier`, default 0). The legal-action emitter (`playResourceShortEventActions`, `legal-actions/organization.ts`) reuses the same `crossesGoldRing` machinery as `enqueue-ring-play-offer`: it crosses each eligible sage with the gold rings borne by characters in that sage's company and emits one `play-short-event` per distinct ring via `targetGoldRingInstanceId` — enforcing "test a gold ring in a sage's company". On resolution the reducer (`applyShortEventOnEntersPlay`, `reducer-events.ts`) locates the ring's bearer and enqueues the shared `gold-ring-test` pending resolution (2d6 + `rollModifier` + item/FW modifiers → the ring's own `ring-test-table` → discard ring → `ring-play-offer`). Unlike `enqueue-ring-play-offer` (Secrets of Their Forging le-226, which skips the roll and offers every table category), this apply performs the actual dice roll. Used by Test of Fire (le-239, `rollModifier: 0`), its hero counterpart Test of Form (tw-338, `rollModifier: 0` — data only, no new engine work; the same emitter/reducer path serves hero resources unchanged), and Test of Lore (tw-340, `rollModifier: -1` — "subtract one from the result of the roll"; data only, the existing `rollModifier` parameter already folds into the pending resolution's total ahead of the ring's `ring-test-table` lookup). Tests roll the queued resolution through the shared `rollGoldRingTest` helper (`tests/test-helpers-dispatch.ts`), the non-Wizard sibling of `testGoldRingViaWizard`.
  The same apply is also accepted as a **`grant-action` apply** (`grant-action-apply.ts`), where the tested ring is the action's `targetCardId`: this is the Rule 9.21 Wizard tap-test. Gandalf (tw-156) and Gandalf FW (wh-4) pair `cost: { tap: "self" }` + `targets: { scope: "company-items", filter: { subtype: "gold-ring" } }` with `apply: { type: "enqueue-gold-ring-test" }`, so activating taps the Wizard and enqueues the shared `gold-ring-test` resolution instead of rolling inline. Enqueuing is what makes the tested ring's own `ring-test-table` (and the MEWH §10 Fallen-wizard −1 on a hero gold ring) apply on the hero path — an inline `roll-check` cannot consult the ring's table.
- `cancel-strike` (self-tap item/ally) + `attack` keying `when` context — a `cancel-strike` effect with `cost: { tap: "self" }` and no `target` (or `target: "self"`) declared on an item or ally attached to a character taps to cancel the current strike against its bearer. Offered by `selfCancelStrikeActions` (`legal-actions/combat.ts`) during `resolve-strike`; the item/ally must be untapped; `handleCancelStrike` (`combat-actions.ts`) taps it and marks the strike `'canceled'`, advancing via `nextStrikePhase`/`finalizeCombat`. (The `target: "other-in-company"` variant — Fatty Bolger — and cost `{ check: "corruption" }` — The One Ring — are also implemented.) The self-tap `when` context now exposes the same attack-keying fields as `cancel-attack` conditions, via the shared `buildAttackKeyingCtx` helper: `attack.source` (attack-source discriminator), `attack.keying` (region types the creature is keyed to), `attack.siteKeyingTypes` (site types), and `attack.keyingRegionNames` (region names) — populated only for creature hazards, so automatic attacks (all keying fields empty) never satisfy a keying-gated cancel. Used by Shadow-cloak (le-344): "Tap Shadow-cloak to cancel one hazard creature strike against bearer keyed by type to a Shadow-land [{s}], Shadow-hold [{S}], Dark-domain [{d}], or Dark-hold [{D}]. Cannot be duplicated on a given character." — `cancel-strike` (`cost: {tap: self}`, `when: $or [attack.keying shadow, attack.keying dark, attack.siteKeyingTypes shadow-hold, attack.siteKeyingTypes dark-hold]`) + `duplication-limit` scope `character` max 1 (item character-scope dup-limit already enforced in `legal-actions/site.ts`). Elven Cloak (tw-225) reuses the identical shape for a single region type: "Tap Elven Cloak to cancel one hazard creature strike against bearer keyed by type to one or more Wilderness [{w}]. Cannot be duplicated on a given character." — `cancel-strike` (`cost: {tap: self}`, `when: {attack.keying: wilderness}`) + `duplication-limit` scope `character` max 1.
- `move` `alsoDiscardCompanyFollowers` (Ringwraith *mode* card `organization-phase-start` self-discard) — an optional boolean on the self-discard `move` apply of a company-bound permanent-event. When the `reducer-untap.ts` Untap→Organization sweep discards the mode card (its `when` gates on `company.atHaven`), it additionally calls `purgeCompanyFollowers` (`reducer-utils.ts`) on the bound company: every follower character (`controlledBy !== 'general'`) is discarded together with its own attached allies/items (→ owner) and hazards (→ opponent), while the non-follower avatar and **its** allies are left untouched (the text targets Ringwraith followers only — contrast Fell Rider's on-play `purgeCompanyAlliesAndFollowers`, which also discards the avatar's allies). The Ringwraith-mode machinery is otherwise shared: `ringwraith-mode` establishes the bound mode (read by `resolveCompanyRingwraithMode`, surfaced as `bearer.ringwraithMode`), `ringwraithHasModeCard` lifts the Darkhaven-only movement gate, `play-target` company (`target.siteType: haven`) restricts play to a Darkhaven, and `duplication-limit` scope `company` enforces "cannot be duplicated on a given company". Used by Black Rider (le-170): "Playable at a Darkhaven on your Ringwraith's own company. The company may move to a non-Darkhaven site. Discard this card and any other Ringwraith followers in the company during any of your following organization phases the company is at a Darkhaven. Cannot be duplicated on a given company." — no stat change and no on-play purge (unlike Fell Rider le-183); followers ride out and are discarded only when the company returns to a Darkhaven. Heralded Lord (le-190) reuses the identical mode/movement/self-discard shape but adds a company-wide stat change and carries no duplication-limit (the printed text has no "cannot be duplicated" clause): "Playable on your Ringwraith's company at a Darkhaven during the organization phase. -2 prowess, +3 direct influence to entire company. His own company may move to a non-Darkhaven site. Discard this card and any other Ringwraith followers in the company during any of your following organization phases the company is at a Darkhaven." — the "to entire company" stat swing is two `company-modifier` effects (`prowess -2`, `direct-influence 3`), the existing `collectCompanyPermanentEventEffects` synthesis path (see the `company-modifier` entry above) already used by Fellowship (tw-240), applied here to a company-bound *mode* card rather than a character-bound one.

- `site-rule: no-storage` + `site-rule: hazard-site-type-override` (a minion Haven that bars storage and "counts as a Ruins & Lairs for playing/interpreting hazards") — two site-rules for a dangerous Darkhaven. **(1) `no-storage`** — "Resources may never be stored at this site." `storeItemActions` (`legal-actions/organization-companies.ts`, the sole generator feeding both the org-phase store offers and any `allow-store-eot` end-of-turn window) skips a company whose current site carries the rule, and `handleStoreItem` (`reducer-organization.ts`) rejects a store attempt with an error as a backstop. **(2) `hazard-site-type-override`** (`siteType`, optional `sitePath`) — overrides only the *creature-keying pass*: `applyHazardSiteTypeOverride` (`mh-steps.ts`, called at the top of `enterSetHazardLimitAndAutoAdvance`, the common funnel for both the non-moving `pass` and every declare-path movement branch) replaces `mhState.destinationSiteType` with `siteType` and, when declared, `mhState.resolvedSitePath` with `sitePath` for the effective site (destination if moving, else current). A Haven normally blocks hazards emergently (its `haven` type and empty path match no keying via `findCreatureKeyingMatches`), so the override re-exposes a company here to hazards keyed to the override site-type / region-path; every other purpose (movement, healing/untap, draws, storage, the printed `siteType`) is untouched. The `deny-character` site-rule (le-359 Carn Dûm precedent) also supports an **empty `filter` `{}`** (matches every character via `matchesDefinition`), so "no character may be brought into play here (unless home site)" is `deny-character` `filter {}` + `exceptHomesite`. Used by Geann a-Lisch (le-374): minion Haven in Old Pûkel-land — `deny-character` (`filter {}`, `exceptHomesite`) + `no-storage` + `hazard-site-type-override` (`siteType: ruins-and-lairs`, `sitePath: {s}{w}{w}{w}{w}` — the path from Carn Dûm).
- `malady-without-healing` + `enqueue-body-check` + kill-MP-for-hero (standalone corruption→body check chain on an any-player target) — a minion short-event `self-enters-play` apply (`malady-without-healing`, `reducer-events.ts`) that targets a character (possibly the opponent's) and enqueues a corruption check (modifier -1, rolled by the target's controller) carrying `awardKillMpTo` (the caster) and an `enqueue-body-check` `onSuccess`. When the target survives the corruption check, the success branch of `applyCorruptionCheckResolution` (`pending-reducers.ts`) enqueues a standalone body check as a generic `dice-check` (`onPass: eliminate-character` with `awardKillMpTo`; threshold `body - tapMod`, comparison `gt`, where `tapMod = +1` if the target is tapped/wounded — CoE 3.I.2.1). A **hero-character** eliminated by either the corruption check (eliminate branch) or the body check (`eliminate-character` dice-check branch) credits the caster the hero's marshalling points via `player.bonusKillMarshallingPoints`, folded into `mp.kill` by `recompute-derived.ts`. Separately, unless the caster's co-located shadow-magic user (Ringwraith, or a `shadow-magic`-skilled character) is a Ringwraith, that user makes a corruption check at -5. `play-target` gains `targetScope: "any-player"` (candidates from both players) and `requiresControlledShadowMagicUserAtSite` (candidate must be co-located with a controlled shadow-magic user), enumerated by `eligibleMaladyTargets` (`legal-actions/organization.ts`); the `corruption-check` pending kind and the `eliminate-character` triggered action gain an optional `awardKillMpTo`. Used by A Malady Without Healing (le-159): site-phase, on a non-Ringwraith/non-Wizard character at the same site as a controlled shadow-magic user; -1 corruption check then +1-if-tapped body check; a hero eliminated by these credits the caster his kill MP; the shadow-magic user makes a -5 corruption check unless he is a Ringwraith; may target an opponent's character.
- `mount-slain` + race-aware `after-attack` play window + target-less after-attack resolution (Mount Slain as-50) — extends the combat `after-attack` resource play window (`engine/post-attack-play.ts`) with a new `attack.ringwraithStrikeFailed` `when` context field: true when at least one strike in the just-ended combat was delivered by a ringwraith-race attacker and did not wound (`StrikeAssignment.result` `'success'`/`'survived'`/`'tie'` — CoE 3.iv.7), checking `combat.creatureRace` for a creature-sourced attack or the specific attacking character's race for a CvCC strike (`hasFailedRingwraithStrike`). Separately, a card with an `after-attack` window and **no** `play-target: character` effect is now offered/resolved without a chosen bearer (`afterAttackCharacterPlayTarget` guards the bearer-eligibility checks in `matchingHandCards`/`postAttackPlayOfferActions`/`applyPostAttackPlayOfferResolution`, all in `post-attack-play.ts`/`legal-actions/pending.ts`/`pending-reducers.ts`); the `mount-slain` `on-event: self-enters-play` apply (`resolveMountSlain`, `chain-reducer.ts`) discards the card itself immediately (it is placed bare via `in-play-general` then moved right back out — it never lingers in play) and, if the opponent has a revealed Ringwraith avatar (`findPlayerAvatar`, race `ringwraith`), enqueues a standalone body check on it (`onPass eliminate-character`, `onFail discard-character`); no avatar in play → fizzle. Used by Mount Slain (as-50): "Playable during any player's turn if a strike against one of your companies from a Ringwraith attack or Nazgûl creature fails. If still in active play following its body check, discard the Ringwraith." — no CRF ruling exists for "the Ringwraith"; read (consistent with "your Ringwraith" elsewhere in the AS/LE/WH pool) as the opponent's own avatar, not necessarily the specific striker.
- `enemy-modifier` body `when: { "combat.strikeMode": "tap" }` — the `enemy-modifier` body reduction (`resolveEnemyBody`, `effects/resolver.ts`, used unconditionally by Ancient Black Axe as-122 / Khamûl le-55) can now be gated on the bearer **tapping to face the strike**. `StrikeAssignment.strikeMode` (`'tap'`/`'untap'`/`'dodge'`/`'reroll'`) is recorded in `resolveStrikeCore` (`combat-strike.ts`) when a strike resolves; `handleBodyCheckRoll` (`combat-actions.ts`, creature body-check path) threads that mode into `resolveEnemyBody`, which now exposes it as `combat.strikeMode` in the combat context (`buildCombatContext` gained an optional `strikeMode` param). So the reduction fires only for the body check that follows a strike the bearer **taps** to face; in stay-untapped mode the `when` fails and the creature's body is unreduced. Omitting the mode (e.g. the CvCC attacker path at `combat-actions.ts:765`) leaves `combat.strikeMode` absent, so as-122's unconditional modifier is unaffected while a tap-gated one never matches. Used by Mechanical Bow (wh-53): a non-unique major weapon — `stat-modifier` body -1 (unconditional, surfaced in `effectiveStats.body`, mirrors Smart and Secret le-229's +1 body), warrior-gated `stat-modifier` prowess +2 `max: 8`, and warrior-gated `enemy-modifier` body -1 gated `$and [bearer.skills $includes warrior, combat.strikeMode: tap]` = "-1 to the body of any strike its bearer faces if he taps to face the strike".
- `move` fetch-to-deck (from sideboard **or** discard) **OR** `roll-then-apply` roll-to-untap-your-tapped-Ringwraith (dual-mode short event) — a resource short event offering two mutually-exclusive modes, discriminated by the presence of a `targetCharacterId` on the `play-short-event` action. **Mode 1 (fetch, no target):** a `move` (`select: target`, `from: [sideboard, discard]`, `to: deck`, `shuffleAfter`, `count: 1`) filtered by card `name` (`$in`). Because the card is not all-fetch, this rides the generic inline play path (`handlePlayResourceShortEvent`, `reducer-events.ts`): the event enters `cardsInPlay` and opens the shared `fetch-to-deck` sub-flow (`fetchFromPileLegalActions` + `handleFetchFromPile` reshuffle the play deck after the pick, then discard the event). **Mode 2 (roll-to-untap, `targetCharacterId`):** a `play-target` (character, filter `target.isRevealedAvatar` + `target.status: "tapped"`) plus an `on-event: self-enters-play` `roll-then-apply` (`threshold`, `onSuccess: set-character-status untapped target-character`). The emitter (`playResourceShortEventActions`, `legal-actions/organization.ts`) detects the `play-target` + `roll-then-apply`-on-enter shape and emits **both** modes: a no-target fetch action when the sideboard/discard holds a filter match, plus one `targetCharacterId` action per eligible tapped revealed avatar (`eligiblePlayOptionTargets`). The reducer's dedicated `resolveShortEventRollUntap` helper (`reducer-events.ts`) rolls 2d6 (honouring `cheatRollTotal`), emits a `diceRollEffect`, applies the `onSuccess`/`onFailure` `set-character-status` branch to the target on threshold, then discards the event. Used by The Ring Leaves Its Mark (le-223): fetch a Black Rider / Fell Rider / Heralded Lord (le-170/le-183/le-190) into the play deck, or roll >6 (threshold 7) to untap your tapped Ringwraith; Balrog-deck-banned via `BALROG_BANNED_CARD_IDS`.

- `play-flag: no-tap-on-play` + `play-flag: influences-factions` + `return-to-hand` effect + wizard-specific ally control (a Radagast-specific ally with several new primitives). **(1) Wizard-specific ally control** — a `<wizard>-specific` ally (via the existing `radagast-specific` keyword, `wizardSpecificName`) may only be controlled by the matching Fallen-wizard avatar; the ally-play emitter (`legal-actions/site.ts`) filters the controlling-character candidates to that avatar. **(2) `play-flag: no-tap-on-play`** — playing the ally taps neither the controlling character nor the site; the emitter also lets a **tapped** controller play it (it never taps, "need not tap himself"), and `handlePlayHeroResource` (`reducer-site.ts`) leaves both character and site untapped and does not consume the site's resource slot / opening-minor-item bonus.
