New: `ringPlayOfferActions` (`legal-actions/pending.ts`) now also enforces **"Unique."** on that route (`isUniqueRingAlreadyInPlay`, via `countCopiesInPlay`), so a unique ring cannot be tested into play while a same-named card is already in play — the item-play path's uniqueness rule, applied to the ring-test path. `stat-modifier` `target: "company"` reaches the bearer too ("every character in the bearer's company"): tw-347's holder carries 6 printed + 1 = 7 corruption points.
- `allow-character-play` as a **starting-company** enabler + drafted Stage resources put into play (Bad Company wh-63) — the draft half of the Orc/Troll permission, previously only enforced on the legal-action side. **(1)** `applyDraftResults` (`init.ts`) now honours CoE 1.9.F4 literally: a drafted Stage resource that is neither a recruitment vehicle (placed with a character) nor site-targeting (Hidden Haven wh-75) and carries no `starting-company-placement` effect (Open to the Summons wh-46, Orders from Lugbúrz as-94, the Balrog ba-60/ba-70 pair — those wait in hand for the item-draft placement step) **enters play** with the starting company instead of going to hand, so its permission and stage points are live from turn 1. The placement test is the new shared `hasStartingCompanyPlacementEffect` (`reducer-utils.ts`), which `hasStartingCompanyPlacementInDeck` now reuses. **(2)** `handleCharacterDraft` (`reducer-setup.ts`) enforces two draft gates the reducer previously left to the legal-action layer alone: the `not-starting-character` play-flag ("You cannot start with a character that says he cannot be in the starting company" — Wûluag as-6, Fram Framson td-91) and rule 1.43 / CoE 1.9.F2 (a Fallen-wizard needs a drafted Stage resource whose `allow-character-play` filter matches the Orc/Troll candidate). **(3)** A player-scoped `duplication-limit` now also bites at draft time via `stageResourceDuplicationLimitReached` (`reducer-utils.ts`), used by both the reducer and the new `stage-resource-not-duplicated` rule in `STAGE_RESOURCE_DRAFT_RULES` (`rules/definitions/character-draft.ts`, context key `ctx.duplicationLimitReached`) — every drafted Stage resource lands in that player's play area, so "Cannot be duplicated by a given player" must hold before the pick, not just when playing a second copy from hand.

- `site-entry-roll-attack` + `site-entry-attack` (dice-check verb) + `site-entry-attack` site sub-step — Doubled Vigilance (dm-51): a hazard **permanent-event** attached to a site (`play-target target: "site"`) that gates *entering* the bound site behind a roll. `advanceThroughSiteEntryGates` (`reducer-site.ts`) runs at both points where a company would be handed on to the site's attack sequence — the `enter-or-skip` → `enter-site` transition and the close of `reveal-on-guard-attacks` — finding the first host bound to the company's current site (`attachedToSite`, either player's `cardsInPlay`) not yet in `SitePhaseState.siteEntryGatesFaced`. It parks the step at the new `site-entry-attack` sub-step, records where the company was heading in `siteEntryReturnStep` (both fields cleared on `select-company` / company hand-off, so each host fires once per **company** site phase), and enqueues a generic `dice-check` owned by the company's controller: 2d6 with the company's effective size (CoE 3.24 half-character rule, `companyEffectiveSize`) as a negative `constant` modifier, against `threshold`/`comparison`. Its `onFail` is the new `site-entry-attack` triggered-action verb (`applyDiceCheckBranch` → exported `buildSiteEntryAttackCombat`), which initiates a combat with `attackSource` `{ type: "site-entry-attack", eventInstanceId }` — **not** an automatic-attack, so it carries no site keying (the §3.II site-type detainment branch cannot fire for it) and no auto-attack modifiers apply; detainment still derives from the attacking race, the defender's alignment/covert status and the site's `combat-detainment` rules. Because the step is parked, the attack resolves ahead of every automatic-attack; passing then opens any further unfired gate or continues to `siteEntryReturnStep`. Two supporting generalizations: (a) a `play-target` `target: "site"` filter is now matched against `sitePlayTargetContext` (`recompute-derived.ts`) — the site definition's fields at the top level **plus** `environment.doorsOfNightInPlay` — so "…or on X if Doors of Night is in play" is a plain DSL filter (existing `siteType` filters are unaffected); (b) the `reveal-on-guard-attacks` window (`legal-actions/site.ts`) admits an event carrying this effect (CoE 2.V.i.1: adding an attack counts as affecting the site's automatic-attacks), gated on that same site filter, and `handleRevealOnGuardAttacks` now stamps `attachedToSite` on a revealed site-targeting permanent event so it behaves exactly like a hand-played copy. "Discard when the site card is discarded or returned to its location deck" needs no effect — the existing `discardOrphanedSiteAttachedEvents` sweep covers it.

- `fw-mp-none` + `draw-cards` as a grant-action apply + `can-use-palantir` constraint (Palantír of Elostirion le-332) — three primitives for the minion Elostirion. **(1)** `fw-mp-none` is a field-less marker meaning "this card gives no marshalling points to a Fallen-wizard regardless of other cards in play". `deniesFallenWizardMp` (`recompute-derived.ts`) is consulted at the top of the borne-item scoring loop and inside `addItemMP`, i.e. **before** the Await-the-Onset pin, `noncharacter-mp-override`, the MEWH §4 clamp and its `fw-mp-full (cards: items)` exemption (Saruman wh-9), and the global `in-play-item-modifier` MP delta — so no card in play can restore the points; non-Fallen-wizard players score the card normally. Also added to the Palantír of Orthanc pair (tw-300 / le-334), which print the same clause. **(2)** `draw-cards` (already a resource short-event effect, Dark Tryst as-80) is now also a `TriggeredAction` verb: a `grant-action` `apply` draws `count` cards from the activating player's play deck, stopping at deck exhaustion. Used in a `sequence` with `enqueue-corruption-check` for "tap Palantír of Elostirion to draw a card. Bearer then makes a corruption check." **(3)** `can-use-palantir` is a new `ActiveConstraint` kind added by a `{ tap: "bearer" }` grant-action (`when bearer.skills $includes sage`, apply `add-constraint` scope `turn`, target the new `bearer` selector → `{ kind: 'character', characterId }`). `buildGrantActionContext` now takes the **source card instance** whose ability is being gated and reports `bearer.canUsePalantir` true for a matching constraint only when `constraint.source` equals that card — encoding "the bearer is able to use **this** Palantír this turn if he taps", so focusing one Palantír never enables another the same character bears. The card's remaining clauses reuse shipped primitives: `item-play-site` sites `["The White Towers"]` (minion copy le-412), `on-event: bearer-company-moves` → self-discard `move` ("Discard if the bearer moves"), and a `hand-size-modifier` **on the item** gated on `bearer.skills` (resolveHandSize already exposes `bearer` and collects item effects).

- `opposed-roll` + `play-condition` `requires: "phase"` + `character-stat-modifier` `requiresSourceBorne` (No More Nonsense le-210) — three primitives for a card that resolves a **contest between two of the controller's own characters**. **(1) `opposed-roll`** — a card effect declaring `opponent: "chosen-company-member"`, `addStat` (`prowess`/`body`/`mind`), `comparison` (`gt` default / `gte`), and ordered `onWin`/`onLose` outcome lists. The play-target is the *challenger*; the *opponent* is a second character chosen at play time, so the organization-phase permanent-event emitter (`legal-actions/organization-events.ts`) crosses each eligible target with every **other** character in its company and stamps the pick into a new `opposedCharacterId` field on `play-permanent-event` (threaded through the chain payload). A target with no company mate offers no play. `chain-reducer.ts` enqueues an `opposed-roll` {@link PendingResolution} when the card enters play; the controller dispatches one `opposed-roll` action for the challenger (its total is stored on the requeued resolution) and one for the opponent, and the second roll adds each side's `addStat`, compares, and applies the branch (`pending-reducers.ts`, registered in `pending-handlers.ts`). Rolling one at a time — rather than two RNG draws inside one reducer step — keeps each roll a distinct game event and lets tests pin them separately via `cheatRollTotal`. A roller who has left play forfeits: the contest is dropped with no outcome. Outcomes are a small closed union, each naming its roller with `on: "challenger" | "opponent"`: `discard-attached` (`filter?`) reuses the `move` primitive's `hazards-on-target` locator so each removed hazard goes to its **owner's** discard (same routing as The Sun Unveiled as-56), and `stat-modifier` (`stat`, `value`) installs an `until-cleared` `character-stat-modifier` constraint. **(2) `play-condition` `requires: "phase"`** with a `phases` list — a permanent resource-event is otherwise offered in the organization phase, the M/H phase (rule 2.1.1) and the site phase alike; this gates it to the phases the text names. Checked in `playPermanentEventActions` and in the site-phase permanent-event branch of `legal-actions/site.ts`. That branch's character `play-target` filter context also gained `target.keywords`, which it was missing relative to the organization-phase emitter. **(3) `character-stat-modifier` `requiresSourceBorne`** — a constraint-kind flag making the modifier apply only while the constraint's `source` instance is still attached to the constrained character (`effects/resolver.ts`). It is what lets a permanent event whose value was fixed by a roll (le-210's leader ±2 direct influence) hold an `until-cleared` constraint without outliving its own card.

- `charactersInPlayAnywhere` context + manifestation "would leave play" via `discard-self-when` + covert/alignment fields on the hazard-limit context (Gandalf the White Rider as-11) — the Maia "hunter manifestation" of a Wizard, the as-13 dual creature/permanent-event shape extended in three places. **(1)** `charactersInPlayNames(state)` (`manifestations.ts`, now also backing `isNamedCharacterInPlay`) is exposed by `buildPlayerStateContext` as `charactersInPlayAnywhere` — the names of every **character** either player has in play. `inPlay`/`inPlayAnywhere` cover `cardsInPlay` only and never see characters, so this is what a "if &lt;person&gt; comes into play" rule must read; matched by name, so every printing counts (Gandalf is hero Wizard tw-156 and Fallen-wizard wh-4). as-11's `discard-self-when` `{ "charactersInPlayAnywhere": "Gandalf" }` is then swept by the existing `sweepDiscardSelfWhen` postReduce pass (wh-106 machinery). **(2)** `blockingManifestationForCharacterPlay` (`manifestations.ts`) gains the second direction of g.man.1's "unless the current manifestation would leave play … when the new manifestation is played" clause: besides the ba-3/tw-12 case (the incoming character discards the sister via `on-event self-enters-play`), an in-play sister that carries a `discard-self-when` whose condition holds against a **hypothetical** context — `charactersInPlayAnywhere` plus the character being played — stops blocking. So `manifestId: "tw-156"` still bars playing as-11 in either mode while Gandalf is in play (as-13 precedent), while Gandalf himself stays playable and the White Rider self-discards on his arrival. A condition over any other context path simply fails to match, so the sister keeps blocking (conservative default). **(3)** `buildCompanyHazardContext` (`mh-steps.ts`) gains `company.alignment` and `company.covert` (`isCovertCompany`), so `hazard-limit-environment` can say "against all overt minion companies": `value: 1, appliesTo: "all"` with `when: { "company.alignment": "ringwraith", "company.covert": false }`. Everything else is shipped data: `play-flag playable-as-event`, `creature-alt-event` permanent-event `persistent: true`, `combat-detainment` hero/covert-FW, `stat-modifier` prowess -3 vs hero (as-8 shape), `on-event play-deck-exhausted` self-discard. **Keying fix:** the canonical `playable` string is `{f}{F}`, so the base entry is `regionTypes: ["free"]` **plus** `siteTypes: ["free-hold"]` (the `{F}` arm had been dropped); the six named regions are a second entry, whose "or at sites in these regions" half is the region name appearing in the destination's site path (as-21 precedent). Siblings still open on the same shape: Alatar the Hunter as-7, Pallando the Soul-keeper as-17, Radagast the Tamer as-18 — all three carry the same truncated `{f}` base keying.

- faction-aware context for `check-modifier` `target: "all-in-play"` + the opponent-influence arm (Lord of the Carrock as-14) — the second AS "hunter manifestation" (as-13 shape: region-name `keyedTo`, `play-flag: playable-as-event`, persistent `creature-alt-event` permanent-event, hero/covert-FW `combat-detainment`, `on-event: play-deck-exhausted` self-discard, `manifestId: "tw-126"` for Beorn). Its passive is a game-wide `check-modifier` influence `-2` that must be **race-gated**, which needed two fixes to the `all-in-play` scope shipped with Times Are Evil (td-76). **(1)** All three faction-influence seams — the influence-attempt display (`legal-actions/site.ts`), the paused faction-influence-roll display (`legal-actions/pending.ts`), and the roll resolver (`reducer-site.ts` `resolveInfluenceAttemptRoll`) — previously passed `collectGlobalCheckModifier` a bare `{ reason: 'faction-influence-check' }`, so a game-wide modifier could not see *which* faction was being influenced; each now passes the full faction context (`faction.name/race/playableAt/playableRegions` + `influenceTarget`), matching the context the per-character and `player-in-play` (ba-38) collectors already get. **(2)** `handleOpponentInfluenceAttempt` (`reducer-site.ts`) now folds `collectGlobalCheckModifier(state, 'influence', oppInfluenceCtx)` into `boostModifier`, so an `all-in-play` modifier also reaches influence attempts against an opponent's card — the glossary defines an influence attempt as "trying to influence a faction **or** an opponent's card", so as-14's "all influence attempts against Man factions" covers both playing a Man faction and influencing an opponent's Man faction away (td-76's unconditional -3 now reaches that path too). The card's `when` is an `$or` over `{ reason: faction-influence-check, faction.race: man }` and `{ reason: opponent-influence-check, target.kind: faction, target.race: man }`.

- Bearer-conditional `stat-modifier` bands + `checkConditionalEffects` + deck-exhaust sweep of character attachments (Power Relinquished to Artifice wh-28) — a character-targeting hazard permanent-event that debuffs prowess **and** direct influence on a sliding scale. The card data itself is pure reuse of the Inner Rot (wh-23) shape: `play-target` character filtered `target.race $in [wizard, fallen-wizard, ringwraith]` (the Ringwraith avatar is an ordinary member of its company, so the existing target loop in `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts` already offers it), `duplication-limit` scope character, `on-event: play-deck-exhausted` → self-discard, and one `stat-modifier` per printed tier — the five Fallen-wizard bands expressed as half-open `bearer.stagePoints` ranges (`<=5` / `5<x<=10` / `10<x<=15` / `15<x<=20` / `>20`) so exactly one matches ("use the first modifier that applies"), each also gated on `bearer.name $in ["Alatar", "Radagast"]` or its `$not` form to implement "For Alatar and Radagast, reduce the modifier to 0 for prowess and double it for direct influence". Three engine generalizations were needed. **(1) `bearer.stagePoints` in the combat-prowess context.** `computeCombatProwess` (`recompute-derived.ts`) re-resolves prowess from the *printed* value rather than adjusting `effectiveStats`, so a stage-point-tiered prowess penalty vanished the moment the bearer faced a strike; the owning player's running total is now exposed there exactly as in the effective-stats context. **(2) `checkConditionalEffects`** (`engine/effects/resolver.ts`, backed by the new `conditionPaths` walker in `effects/condition-matcher.ts`) — a `direct-influence` modifier borne by a character was being counted **twice** in every influence check: once inside `effectiveStats.directInfluence`, and again by the per-check folds that add target-specific bonuses on top (`availableDI` in `legal-actions/organization.ts`, faction influence in `legal-actions/site.ts` / `reducer-site.ts` / `legal-actions/pending.ts`, opponent influence in `reducer-site.ts`). Those folds now keep only the modifiers whose `when` reads the check context (`reason`, `target.*`, `faction.*` — e.g. Glorfindel II tw-161, The Arkenstone le-418, Trifling Ring le-346, Whip le-348); anything ungated (Narsil tw-289) or gated on the bearer alone (wh-28, Dragon-helm dm-167) is already inside the effective stat and is dropped. Only the `collectCharacterEffects` portion is filtered — ally / player-scoped / faction-card influence effects are never in effective DI and pass through. **(3) deck exhaustion reaches character attachments.** `completeDeckExhaust` (`reducer-utils.ts`) only swept `cardsInPlay`, so a "Discard when any play deck is exhausted" permanent-event played *on a character* was immortal; `discardAttachedOnDeckExhaust` now sweeps every character's `hazards` and `items`, routing an attached hazard to the **opposing** player's discard pile and an attached item to the holder's, matching the `move` zone conventions in `reducer-move.ts`.

- `fw-site-alignment-restriction` (Heart Grown Cold wh-21) — locks which **alignment of a site card** a Fallen-wizard may use for a location. His location deck may hold both the hero and minion card for the same place (CoE 1.28) and they play very differently (hero Lórien tw-408 is a Haven, minion Lórien as-155 a plain Free-hold). Fields: `require` (`"minion"`/`"hero"` — the *opposite* version is barred), `siteTypes` (printed types of the barred card), optional `when` matched **per restricted Fallen-wizard** against `{ player: { alignment, stagePoints } }`, so one card escalates with stage points. `fwSiteVersionForbidden` (`reducer-utils.ts`) scans both players' `cardsInPlay`; only `hero-site`/`minion-site` cards can be barred — a `fallen-wizard-site` (any Wizardhaven) is both hero and minion (MEWH §10) and never locks. Consumed at movement declaration (`organization-companies.ts` `planMovementActions`): the barred card is dropped while candidates are built, *before* it claims the location's name → instance slot, so the other version becomes the destination used and a location with no surviving version is simply unreachable; the same check drops a sibling company's in-play site (rule 2.II.7.2). Agent movement is untouched (CoE 4.F1 already pins Fallen-wizard agents to hero site cards).

- `discard-substitute` (Leaf Brooch dm-171) — a **replacement effect** on an item: when another card in the bearer's own company must be discarded by a hazard or resource effect, its owner may discard this card instead and keep the doomed one. Fields: `scope: "company"` and an optional `filter` matched with `matchesDefinition` against the doomed card's definition (dm-171 uses `{ "subtype": { "$ne": "special" } }`). All plumbing lives in `engine/discard-substitute.ts`: every forced-discard site calls `enqueueDiscardSubstituteOffer` **instead of** moving the cards, which returns either a state carrying a `discard-substitute-offer` pending resolution (a covering substitute is in the company) or `null` ("no substitute — discard them yourself"). The resolution owns the card movement either way, so no call site carries its own replacement branch. The owner answers with `use-discard-substitute`: with an `itemInstanceId` to name one doomed card to save (the substitute is discarded in its place) or with the field omitted to decline (every remaining doomed card is discarded); the resolution re-queues while another substitute *and* another doomed card remain, so two copies save two cards from one requirement. `discardSubstituteOfferActions` (`legal-actions/pending.ts`) + `applyDiscardSubstituteOfferResolution` (`pending-reducers.ts`). Wired into the three effect-driven discard paths: `discard-one-company-item` (Brigands tw-17/le-64, Pirates le-88, and Scourge of Fire ba-75 where the *opponent* picks the item), the `move { from: 'items-on-wounded' }` wound strip in `combat-finalize.ts` (Trolls tw-016/tw-103/tw-112), and the Rule 9.21 gold-ring test in `applyGoldRingTestResolution` — there the offer is queued *behind* the `ring-play-offer` and the ring stays borne meanwhile, matching CRF 22's ruling that the gold ring's bearer, not the brooch's, receives the special ring (a ring stored at a Darkhaven sits in the MP pile, not a company, so it cannot be saved). A requirement phrased as "discard one item of the **defender's** choice" (An Article Missing dm-43's combat `discard-item-from-company` sub-phase) needs no replacement machinery: the substitute is itself an item in the company, so naming it already fulfils it. Leaf Brooch's other rule, "Only playable at Lórien", is the shipped `item-play-site` `sites` form

- `company-size-unlimited` + `company-influence-exempt` + `company-character-play-exempt` + `discard-self-when-company` (An Unexpected Party dm-114) — the company-composition family, for a resource permanent-event bound to a company (`play-target` `target: "company"`, `CardInPlay.companyId`), all living in `engine/company-composition.ts`. Three of them match a card-authored `filter` against a shared **per-character context** (`character.name` / `.race` / `.mind` / `.unique` / `.isAvatar` / `.keywords`; `mind` is the *effective* mind and is **absent** for avatars, so `{ "$gt": n }` never matches one). **(1) `company-size-unlimited`** lifts the CoE 2.II.3.1 seven-character cap on the bound company — read in `organization-companies.ts` by `moveToCompanyActions` and `mergeCompaniesActions`; on a merge only the *target* company's marker counts, since the target is the company that survives (contrast `extra-leader-slot` ba-70, which exempts one Leader from the same headcount). **(2) `company-influence-exempt`** makes matching characters in the company cost no influence: `recompute-derived.ts` stops subtracting their mind from the general-influence pool, and `organization-characters.ts` offers them under general influence at cost 0 at a site where such a company stands (the direct-influence branch is deliberately untouched). **(3) `company-character-play-exempt`** lets matching characters join the bound company regardless of the one-character-per-turn limit *and* without consuming that turn's slot (gate in `organization-characters.ts`, bookkeeping in `reducer-organization.ts` `handlePlayCharacter`) — CRF 22: "one non-Dwarf character and any number of Dwarves all in the same organization phase". **(4) `discard-self-when-company`** is the company-scoped sibling of `discard-self-when`: a `postReduce` sweep (`sweepDiscardSelfWhenCompany`) discards the card the moment a condition over the bound company's roster holds. Its optional `counts: [{ as, filter }]` declares named filtered headcounts published to the condition as `count.<name>`, alongside `company.characterCount` / `.atHaven` / `.siteType` — that is what keeps multi-clause wordings like "more than one non-Wizard character with a mind greater than 5 or more than two non-Dwarf characters or no Dwarf with a mind greater than 5" in card data instead of engine branches. dm-114 also establishes that `play-condition` `requires: "phase"` with `phases: ["organization"]` is what confines a company-bound *resource* permanent-event to the organization phase (the site-phase path in `legal-actions/site.ts` honours the same gate).

- `force-discard-target-item` + `characterId`/`itemFilter` narrowing on the `discard-one-company-item` pending kind (Indûr Dawndeath tw-46) — the fifth Nazgûl dual creature/permanent-event (tw-2/tw-47/tw-83/tw-107 shape: `{d}{D}` base keying + a `regionNames` alt entry for Harondor/Imlad Morgul/Gorgoroth/Khand, `keywords: ["Nazgûl"]`, `play-flag: playable-as-event`, `creature-alt-event` mode permanent-event, tap during the opponent's M/H via `tap-alt-permanent-event` counting one against the hazard limit, plus the dm-36 "any Nazgûl permanent-event" `permanent-event-auto-attack` — Nazgûl 1×15/10, `discardAfterUse`; no dm-35 entry, that site names only Khamûl/Adûnaphel). Its on-tap short-event carries the new **`force-discard-target-item`** effect for "makes any wounded character discard an item of his choice (but not a ring)" — a *split* decision: the card-player names the victim, the victim's own controller names the item. Both halves are DSL conditions, not keywords: **`targetFilter`** is matched against the shared play-option context (`buildPlayOptionContext`, so it reads state — `{ "target.status": "inverted" }` **is** "wounded"), and **`itemFilter`** against each candidate item's definition (`{ "$not": { "keywords": { "$includes": "ring" } } }` — every ring in the pool carries the `ring` keyword, gold-ring and tested-`special` alike). `tapAltPermanentEventActions` (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) emits one tap action per **opponent** character (CoE 2.1.2) that matches `targetFilter` *and* bears an `itemFilter`-eligible item, and a single non-viable action when none does, so the tap is never a play without effect (CoE 9.6). On resolution `applyForceDiscardTargetItem` (`chain-reducer.ts`) enqueues the existing **`discard-one-company-item`** pending resolution for the victim's controller, which gained two optional narrowing fields — `characterId` (only that character's items are offered; absent = whole company, Brigands tw-17) and `itemFilter` (re-checked both in `discardOneCompanyItemActions` and in `applyDiscardOneCompanyItemResolution`, so a forged `discard-item-from-company` naming a ring or another character's item is rejected). Reusing that resolution also inherits the Leaf Brooch (dm-171) `discard-substitute` interposition for free. Siblings still open on the same Nazgûl shape: Akhôrahil tw-4 (body -1), Dwar of Waw tw-31 (+1 prowess to Wolf/Spider/Animal attacks), Hoarmûrath of Dír tw-44 (+1 strike).

- `site-type-remap` + the `next-long-event-phase` constraint scope (Witch-king of Angmar tw-113) — the sixth Nazgûl dual creature/permanent-event, and the first **class-wide site retype**. The card was re-modeled from a bare `hazard-event` into the tw-2/tw-46/tw-47/tw-83/tw-107 shape: `hazard-creature` 1×17/12, kill MP 6, race ringwraith, `{d}{D}` base keying plus a `regionNames` alt entry (Angmar, Gundabad, Gorgoroth, Imlad Morgul), `play-flag: playable-as-event` for the ½-creature deck weight, `creature-alt-event` mode permanent-event, and the shipped `permanent-event-auto-attack` at dm-33/dm-40/dm-36 (Nazgûl 1×17/12, `discardAfterUse`). Its on-tap conversion is the new **`site-type-remap`** effect (`from`, `to`, optional `duration`): `applySiteTypeRemap` (`chain-reducer.ts`) installs one `site.type` `override` `attribute-modifier` filtered by the sites' **printed** type — the third filter shape understood by `siteConstraintFilterMatches` (`engine/effective.ts`), `{ "site.printedType": … }`, alongside `site.definitionId` (as-88) and `site.name` (wh-27). Bound to no definition, it retypes every matching site at once and needs neither an active company nor a destination; the printed type is now threaded to the filter at all four override readers (`getEffectiveSiteType`, `checkCreatureKeying`, `findCreatureKeyingMatches`, `reducer-untap.ts`). The MEAS §6(d) Under-deeps short-circuit still wins, so an Under-deeps Shadow-hold keeps its printed type. **`duration: "long-event"`** maps to the new `next-long-event-phase` {@link ConstraintScope} (declarer + creation turn), swept by the new `long-event-phase-end` {@link ScopeBoundary} raised in `handleLongEvent`'s pass branch at the same moment [2.III.3] discards the hazard player's long-event *cards* — one full turn cycle, since the declarer's own next turn has him as the resource player. This is what "becomes a long-event … when resolved, the long-event effect will remain and this card is discarded" needs (CRF 22: "he is discarded when the effect resolves just like other Nazgûl. The long-event effect will remain until the appropriate time"): the card leaves play immediately, so only the constraint carries the duration. Rule 5.24 (Sideboarding with a Nazgûl) was gated on `cardType === 'hazard-event'` and would have stopped recognising the Nine once tw-113 became a creature; it now routes through `isNazgulPermanentEvent` (`reducer-utils.ts`), which accepts a Nazgûl-keyworded `hazard-event` **or** any card carrying `creature-alt-event` mode `permanent-event` (a creature can only be in `cardsInPlay` by having been played in that mode), so all nine Ringwraiths qualify. The repo's `text` field for tw-113 was also wrong (it described a corruption-check short-event) and is corrected from the authoritative card DB.

- `modify-attack` `fromAltPermanentEvent` (Hoarmûrath of Dír tw-44) — the sixth Nazgûl dual creature/permanent-event (tw-2/tw-47/tw-83/tw-107/tw-113 shape: `{d}{D}` base keying + a `regionNames` alt entry for Dagorlad/Ithilien/Gorgoroth/Khand, `play-flag: playable-as-event`, `creature-alt-event` mode `permanent-event`, plus the dm-36 "any Nazgûl permanent-event" `permanent-event-auto-attack` — Nazgûl 1×15/9, `discardAfterUse`; no dm-35 entry, that site names only Khamûl/Adûnaphel). Its on-tap short-event "gives +1 strike to any one attack", which needs an attack to name — so the conversion is **not** the play-hazards-step `tap-alt-permanent-event`. Instead `ModifyAttackEffect` gains a third source flag, **`fromAltPermanentEvent`**, next to `fromHand`: `altPermanentEventModifyAttackActions` (`legal-actions/combat.ts`) offers the tap in the same pre-assignment combat window as a from-hand `modify-attack`, gated on `Phase.MovementHazard` (the printed timing) and on the defending company's hazard limit, and `handleModifyAttack` (`combat-actions.ts`) grows a third arm of its no-`characterInstanceId` branch — same strike/prowess/body math as a hand card, but the source leaves `cardsInPlay` for the discard pile and charges one `hazardsPlayedThisCompany` slot (CoE 8.12 / the printed "tapping counts against the hazard limit"). Cards carrying the flag are skipped by both `tapAltPermanentEventActions` and `handleTapAltPermanentEvent`, so the two conversion paths can never double-fire. The from-hand `when` context builder was extracted as `buildPlayedModifyAttackContext` and is shared by both played sources.

- `attack-race-boost` + multi-race / player-targeted `creature-attack-boost` (Dwar of Waw tw-31) — the seventh Nazgûl dual creature/permanent-event (tw-2/tw-44/tw-46/tw-47/tw-83/tw-107/tw-113 shape: `{d}{D}` base keying + a `regionNames` alt entry for Harondor/Imlad Morgul/Gorgoroth/Ithilien, `keywords: ["Nazgûl"]` so rule 5.24 sideboarding sees it, `play-flag: playable-as-event`, `creature-alt-event` mode `permanent-event`, tap during the opponent's M/H via `tap-alt-permanent-event` counting one against the hazard limit, plus the dm-36 "any Nazgûl permanent-event" `permanent-event-auto-attack` — Nazgûl 1×15/10, `discardAfterUse`; no dm-35 entry, that site names only Khamûl/Adûnaphel). Its on-tap short-event "gives +1 prowess to all Wolf, Spider, and Animal attacks until the end of the turn" — untargeted and standing, so unlike tw-44 it needs no live attack and stays on the play-hazards-step tap path. The new **`attack-race-boost`** effect (`races`, `prowess`, `strikes`) resolves through `applyAttackRaceBoost` (`chain-reducer.ts`) into a **turn-scoped `creature-attack-boost` constraint** — Chill Douser's (dm-106) kind, generalised twice rather than duplicated: its `race` field now accepts `Race | readonly Race[]`, and the constraint may target a **player** as well as a company. The target is the opponent of the declaring player (the side facing hazards this turn), and a player target reaches every company they control, which is what "all X attacks" means. `collectCreatureAttackBoostEffects` (`effects/resolver.ts`) resolves both target shapes and matches the race list, so the boost lands on hazard-creature attacks and site automatic-attacks alike (printed plurals normalise via `normalizeCreatureRace`, so a site's "Wolves" attack is boosted). Siblings still open on the same Nazgûl shape: Akhôrahil tw-4 (body -1).

- `event-maintenance` (renamed from `hazard-maintenance`) + `prohibit-card-play` `filter` (Balance Between Powers dm-118) — two generalisations of shipped primitives, both driven by the environment lock/upkeep card. **(1) `event-maintenance`** is the old le-142 `hazard-maintenance` upkeep, widened past its hazard-only framing: `trigger` gains `"controller-organization-phase-start"` (fired from `advanceToOrganization`, `reducer-untap.ts`, for the active player's own `cardsInPlay`) beside `"opponent-long-event-end"`, and an optional `counterChain: { challengeCount, counterCount }` turns the upkeep into a bidding war. The exchange runs as alternating `event-maintenance` pending resolutions (`engine/event-maintenance.ts`): `upkeep` (controller, 1 card — `discard-self` or pay) → `challenge` (opponent, `challengeCount` — `decline` or pay to kill the card) → `counter` (controller, `counterCount` — `decline` or pay to save it) → `challenge` again, until a side declines or cannot pay. Multi-card stages are paid one `pay-event-maintenance` action at a time (`remainingToPay` counts down; the opt-out is offered only while `remainingToPay === stageCount`), and a stage is enqueued only when its actor can afford to finish it — an unaffordable challenge leaves the card in play, an unaffordable counter discards it. The old `hazard-event-maintenance` pending kind and `pay-hazard-event-maintenance` action are renamed to match (the sim's `ACTION_TYPES` string was renamed in place, preserving its index). **(2) `prohibit-card-play`** gains an optional definition `filter` beside `cardNames`, for class-wide locks ("No environment cards can be played"), and its enforcement moves out of the hazard legal-action layer into `applyCardPlayProhibitions` (`engine/card-play-prohibition.ts`), called from `computeLegalActions` — so the lock now covers every phase menu plus the chain and combat response windows, turning any viable `play-short-event`/`play-long-event`/`play-permanent-event`/`play-hazard` for a locked card into one `not-playable` entry. The one-time "discards every copy already in play" sweep stays tied to `cardNames`, so a `filter` lock is purely forward-looking. Third rule is pure reuse of the `on-event: play-deck-exhausted` self-discard.

- `target-character-stat-modifier` + character body checks read `effectiveStats.body` (Akhôrahil tw-4) — the eighth and last Nazgûl dual creature/permanent-event (tw-2/tw-31/tw-44/tw-46/tw-47/tw-83/tw-107/tw-113 shape: `{d}{D}` base keying + a `regionNames` alt entry for Harondor/Horse Plains/Gorgoroth/Khand, `keywords: ["Nazgûl"]` so rule 5.24 sideboarding sees it, `play-flag: playable-as-event`, `creature-alt-event` mode `permanent-event`, tap during the opponent's M/H via `tap-alt-permanent-event` counting one against the hazard limit, plus the dm-36 "any Nazgûl permanent-event" `permanent-event-auto-attack` — Nazgûl 1×16/9, `discardAfterUse`; no dm-35 entry, that site names only Khamûl/Adûnaphel). Its on-tap short-event "modifies any one character's body by -1 for the rest of this turn" — targeted like tw-46's, but at a *character* rather than an item, so it stays on the play-hazards-step tap path. The new **`target-character-stat-modifier`** effect (`stat`, `value`, optional `targetFilter`) resolves through `applyTargetCharacterStatModifier` (`chain-reducer.ts`) into a **turn-scoped `character-stat-modifier` constraint** bound to the named instance — Vilya's / Glance of Arien's (ba-19) kind, reused unchanged. `tapAltPermanentEventActions` (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) emits one tap action per **opponent** character (CoE 2.1.2) passing `targetFilter`, and a single non-viable action when none does. It deliberately does *not* ride ba-19's `on-event: self-enters-play → add-constraint` shape: that fires on permanent-event **entry**, whereas this card must grant nothing until tapped — so the effect resolves on the short-event chain path alone. Making the -1 bite also required fixing a live gap: `handleBodyCheckRoll` (`combat-actions.ts`) read a character's **printed** body, so every character body modifier (this card, ba-19, Vilya, item `bodyModifier`s) was inert in combat; it now reads `charData.effectiveStats.body`, which `recomputeDerived` refreshes after every action. Allies bear no items and carry no `effectiveStats`, so they keep their printed/override body.

- `enqueue-gold-ring-test` `rollCount` + `choose-gold-ring-test-roll` (Wizard's Test tw-365) — the multi-roll gold-ring test: "make two rolls and choose one result to use for the test". The `enqueue-gold-ring-test` apply (and the `grant-action` variant) gains an optional `rollCount` (default 1) threaded into the `gold-ring-test` pending kind alongside a new `rolledTotals` accumulator. `goldRingTestActions` (`legal-actions/pending.ts`) emits one `gold-ring-test-roll` per outstanding roll (labelled "roll N of M") and, once every roll is in, one **`choose-gold-ring-test-roll`** per *distinct* total, each explaining the ring categories that total opens; the engine never picks, because a ring's `ring-test-table` can map low totals to Magic Rings and high totals to Dwarven Rings. `applyGoldRingTestResolution` (`pending-reducers.ts`) was split: the roll half computes the modified total (company `gold-ring-test` check-modifiers now swept with the tested ring **excluded** from its bearer's items rather than by pre-discarding it, so the roll can precede the discard) and either records it via the new `replaceResolutionKind` (`engine/pending.ts`) — an **in-place** requeue, so a check the same card enqueued behind the test cannot jump the queue between rolls — or hands off to the extracted `finalizeGoldRingTest`, which discards the ring, reads the table at the chosen total, and enqueues `ring-play-offer` (+ any `discard-substitute` offer) exactly as the single-roll path always did. Also: the filter-only branch of the (target × gold ring) emitter in `legal-actions/organization.ts` now carries the play-target on the action as `targetCharacterId`, which is what lets tw-365 pair the test with a second `self-enters-play` apply — `enqueue-corruption-check` `modifier: -1` on the Wizard ("Wizard makes a corruption check modified by -1"), queued behind the test so it comes due only once the test has resolved. Card data: `keywords: ["spell"]` (so Wizard's Staff td-170's "+2 to any corruption check required by a spell" sees it via `source.keywords`), `play-window` organization, `play-target` character `{ target.race: wizard }` with no tap cost.

- `company-cannot-move` now strips an already-declared destination (Hiding tw-256) — the hero counterpart of Hide in Dark Places (le-192) needed no new effect type, only a correction to the constraint's install path. le-192 may only be played on a non-moving company, so its `company-cannot-move` install never had to undo anything; tw-256 ("Scout's company may not move to another site this turn") carries no such precondition and end-of-org cards are played *alongside* the rest of the organization phase, so its target may already have declared a move. The `add-constraint` dispatch in `reducer-events.ts` therefore calls `clearPlannedMovement` before installing the kind — dropping `destinationSite` / `movementPath` and returning the site card to the location deck — mirroring what Siege tw-87's `lock-company-movement` dice-check verb already did on a failed movement roll. `clearPlannedMovement` moved from `reducer-organization.ts` to `reducer-utils.ts` (re-imported by `reducer-organization.ts` and `pending-reducers.ts`) so the event reducer can reach it without a module cycle. The rest of tw-256 is pure reuse of the Stealth/le-192 shape: `play-window` organization/`end-of-org`, `play-target` character filtered on `target.skills $includes scout` with `requiredSkill: "scout"` (no tap cost — the card does not tap the scout) and two `on-event: self-enters-play` → `add-constraint` effects targeting `scout-company`, the second being `no-creature-hazards-on-company` for "Cancels all hazard creature attacks against the scout's company this turn".

- Persistent `creature-alt-event` permanent-event + `in-play-item-modifier` on a hazard creature (Saruman the Wise as-19) — no new engine support was needed: the Maia hunter-manifestation shape certified with Gandalf the White Rider (as-11) composed with Rumor of the One's (le-224) ring modifier. Creature mode is 3 strikes 13/9 with `combat-detainment` gated on hero / covert-FW defenders and `stat-modifier` prowess `-3` `when { defender.alignment: hero }` (as-11 shape). Keying follows the canonical `playable` `{f}{F}`: a base entry of `regionTypes: ["free"]` **plus** `siteTypes: ["free-hold"]` (the two symbols are alternatives, not an AND — the legal-action generator's per-`keyedTo`-entry arms each contribute independently), and a second entry `regionNames: ["Gap of Isen", "Rohan", "Enedhwaith", "Old Pûkel-land"]` whose "or at sites in these regions" half is the destination site's own region appearing in `resolvedSitePathNames` (as-13/as-21 precedent). Permanent-event mode is `creature-alt-event` `persistent: true` (no tap-to-short-event conversion) whose passive — "all ring items give one additional corruption point" — is `in-play-item-modifier` with `itemFilter { item.keywords $includes ring }` and `corruptionPoints: 1` and **no** `marshallingPoints` (le-224 gives both; as-19 gives corruption only). `collectInPlayItemModifiers` (`recompute-derived.ts`) scans every card in either player's `cardsInPlay` regardless of card type, so a hazard creature sitting there as a permanent-event feeds it unchanged, and the modifier reaches both players' rings. "Manifestation of Saruman" is `manifestId: "tw-181"` plus `discard-self-when { charactersInPlayAnywhere: "Saruman" }` — the name match covers the Fallen-wizard printing wh-9 too, and the `blockingManifestationForCharacterPlay` "would leave play" clause (as-11) keeps the Wizard avatar revealable at Isengard/Rivendell while the Wise is in play. Closed out with `play-flag: playable-as-event` (½-creature deck weight) and `on-event: play-deck-exhausted` self-discard. Siblings still open on the same shape with the same truncated `{f}` base keying: Alatar the Hunter as-7, Pallando the Soul-keeper as-17, Radagast the Tamer as-18.
- `grant-creature-keying` `source: "faced-this-turn"` — lets a **hazard creature** (rather than an in-play environment) carry a keying grant that becomes active against a company only once that company has faced it this turn. `inPlayGrantsCreatureKeying` was split into `collectCreatureKeyingGrants` + `grantsCreatureKeying` (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`): the collector still scans both players' `cardsInPlay` for the default `source: "in-play"` grants, and additionally walks `state.cardPool` for definitions whose **name** appears in the company's `MovementHazardPhaseState.hazardsEncountered` (rule 8.03 — an attack counts as faced even when canceled) and which carry a `source: "faced-this-turn"` grant. The pool lookup is needed because the granting creature is long gone from play by the time the grant is used (discarded, or in the defender's kill pile). Matching, `requiresNonCoastalKeying`, and the `keyedBy: { method: "keying-bypass" }` hand-off to the reducer are unchanged, so the new source composes with the existing site-type / keyword / region-type branches. Used by Dwarven Travelers (as-9): "Maia hazard creatures may be keyed to Border-holds [{B}] or Ruins & Lairs [{R}] against any company that has faced Dwarven Travelers this turn." — `grant-creature-keying` (`source: "faced-this-turn"`, `creatureFilter { race: "maia" }`, `siteFilter.siteTypes [border-hold, ruins-and-lairs]`), alongside the standard AS `combat-detainment` "hero and covert companies" clause and a `{w}{b}{R}{B}` `keyedTo` entry.

- `un-eliminate-creature` + instance-targeted untargeted `play-option` modes (Returned Beyond All Hope as-35) — the first card whose *every* mode acts on one card instance the player declares at play time, and the first "un-elimination". `play-option` gains two generic fields: **`candidates`** (`own-discard` | `own-in-play` | `eliminated`) names the pool an untargeted option's declared instance is drawn from, and **`eventMode`** (`short-event` | `permanent-event`) names the event mode the option is played as when it differs from the card's printed `eventType`. The hazard short-event emitter (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`, `untargetedOptionCandidates`) enumerates the pool, filters it by the apply's own `filter` (always excluding the source card, which already sits in its own discard pile), and emits one `play-hazard` per (mode x candidate) carrying `optionId` + the new `optionTargetInstanceId` action/chain-payload field; an empty mode is emitted non-viable, and a card with no non-empty mode is simply unplayable. This branch runs only for a short event carrying no `play-target`. Modes 1 and 2 apply a plain `move` (`discard`/`in-play` -> `hand`), dispatched in `chain-reducer.ts` by feeding `optionTargetInstanceId` to `applyMove` as `ctx.targetCardId` — "to your hand" is read as the playing player's own cards, hence the `own-*` pools. Mode 3 sets `eventMode: "permanent-event"`, so `handlePlayHazardCard` (`mh-hazard-play.ts`) routes the short-event card down the permanent-event chain path (the card rides the chain instead of being pre-discarded) and `resolveEntry` hands it to the new **`un-eliminate-creature`** apply (`threshold`, `filter`, `selfTo: "opponent-mp-pile"`) instead of `resolvePermanentEvent` — so, like dm-73's `displace-stored-item`, it never enters `cardsInPlay`. `resolveUnEliminateCreature` rolls 2d6 (honouring `cheatRollTotal`, emitting the dice effect through `ResolveResult.effects`): on a total >= `threshold` the declared creature leaves whichever terminal pile held it for its **owner's** discard pile (owner from the instance-id prefix, falling back to the opponent of the pile holder) and the card is placed into the opponent's marshalling-point pile, where its `mp-in-pile` (2 kill MP) scores; on failure — or when the declared creature is gone or no longer matches `filter` — the card goes to its own player's discard pile. Either way it lands in exactly one pile. "Eliminated" spans **both** terminal piles of both players: `outOfPlayPile` and `killPile`, the latter per the CRF 22 ruling that the card "may target creatures still in play as trophies"; and because the creature leaves those piles, `isManifestationDefeated` stops reporting its chain as defeated — the other CRF 22 ruling, that the card "«un-eliminates» a hazard creature, allowing any manifestation of that character to be played".
- `play-option` `candidates: "opponent-in-play"` (Many Sorrows Befall td-46) — a third pool for the as-35 instance-targeted untargeted `play-option` shape, alongside `own-discard`/`own-in-play`/`eliminated`: the **opponent's** `cardsInPlay` rather than the playing (hazard) player's own. `untargetedOptionCandidates` (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) takes an extra `opponentPlayer` argument (the M/H hazard-play loop's already-resolved `resourcePlayer`) and, for this pool, filters `opponentPlayer.cardsInPlay` instead of `player.cardsInPlay`; `applyMove`'s `from: "in-play"` lookup is unaffected (it already scans both players' `cardsInPlay` by instance id regardless of owner) and the default `toOwner: "source-owner"` sends the discarded card to its actual owner — the resource player — with no `toOwner` override needed. Used by Many Sorrows Befall (td-46): "Forces the discard of one resource long-event" — `candidates: "opponent-in-play"`, apply `move` (`select: target`, `from: in-play`, `to: discard`, `filter: { cardType: { $in: ["hero-resource-event", "minion-resource-event"] }, eventType: "long" }`), no cost, playable during the normal M/H hazard-play step (counts against the hazard limit as usual — no `no-hazard-limit` play-flag). The card's alternative mode — "cancel one resource short-event declared earlier in the same chain of effects" — reuses the Ire of the East (wh-24) `cancel-chain-entry` `select: "target"` + generic `filter` chain-response shape verbatim, minus wh-24's `declaredBy.alignment` restriction (any resource short-event, either alignment) and its `removeFromGame`/`no-hazard-limit` riders (neither is in this card's text): `filter: { "target.cardType": { "$in": [...] }, "target.eventType": "short" }`.

- `hazard-limit-environment` `company.regionNames` gate (Radagast the Tamer as-18) — the fourth AS Maia "hunter manifestation" (as-7/as-11/as-17 shape: `{f}{F}` base keying — `regionTypes: ["free"]` **plus** `siteTypes: ["free-hold"]`, the two symbols being alternatives — `play-flag: playable-as-event`, persistent `creature-alt-event` permanent-event, `combat-detainment` hero/covert-FW, `stat-modifier` prowess -3 vs hero, `discard-self-when` `charactersInPlayAnywhere`, `on-event: play-deck-exhausted` self-discard, `manifestId` tw-178). Two things are new. **(1)** `buildCompanyHazardContext` (`mh-steps.ts`) gains **`company.regionNames`** — the region names of the company's declared movement path — so a `hazard-limit-environment` can name the regions it covers: "all companies moving in Southern Mirkwood, Western Mirkwood, Woodland Realm, and/or Heart of Mirkwood have their hazard limit increased by one" is `value: 1` (default `appliesTo: "moving"`, which supplies the "moving" half on its own) with an `$or` of `{ "company.regionNames": { "$includes": <region> } }` clauses. The names are threaded into `snapshotHazardLimit(state, company, pathNames)` from the caller's `MovementHazardPhaseState`, because the snapshot runs before the freshly-resolved M/H state reaches `state.phaseState`; the parameter defaults to the current phase state's `resolvedSitePathNames`. **(2)** The alt-keying entry pairs `regionNames` (the four Mirkwood regions) with `siteNames: ["Rhosgobel"]` in one entry — fields inside a `keyedTo` entry are alternatives, so "or Rhosgobel" reaches every printing of the site by name, including the Fallen-wizard Wizardhaven wh-57, which no site-type arm of the card can key to. Note this card's text has **no** "or at sites in these regions" clause (unlike as-7/as-11/as-13), so the region names stand on the movement path alone.

- Pure composition, no new engine work (Knights of the Prince as-12) — "Detainment against hero companies" (`combat-detainment` `defender.alignment: hero` — as-13/as-14/as-16 shape, which also fires for Fallen-wizard defenders per rule 2.IV.vii.F1's `detainmentAlignmentLabel` mapping regardless of covert status) plus "unless the attack is canceled, all untapped characters in defending company are tapped following this attack", the exact `on-event: attack-not-canceled` → `company-tap-characters` clause certified two days prior on Wild Fell Beast (td-81) — unlike td-81 this card carries no `combat-attacker-chooses-defenders`, so the defender assigns its own strikes normally. Keying is a single `regionNames` entry (Anfalas/Belfalas/Lamedon/Lebennin/Anórien) whose "or at sites in these regions" half is the as-21 destination-site-name precedent.
- `on-event: attack-strike-successful` + `company-return-to-origin` apply verb (Fell Turtle tw-34) — the first creature whose *own successful strike* (rather than a short-event or an in-play environment) forces the defending company back to its site of origin. New `on-event` event name `attack-strike-successful`, checked in `finalizeCombat` (`combat-finalize.ts`) right after the `attack-not-defeated` block: gated on `struckCharIds.length > 0` (the existing wounded-or-eliminated set already computed for wound-triggered passives, detainment strikes excluded) and `state.phaseState.phase === Phase.MovementHazard`. Its one supported apply, the new **`company-return-to-origin`** `TriggeredAction` verb (type-only marker, no extra fields), mirrors `applyCompanyReturnToOrigin`'s short-event mechanism inline: sets `MovementHazardPhaseState.returnedToOrigin = true` (skipped if already set, or if the defending company has no `destinationSite`) and adds a `site-phase-do-nothing` constraint (`scope: company-site-phase`, sourced from the attacking creature instance) so `endCompanyMH` leaves the company at its origin and blocks its next site phase (CoE rule 2.IV.4). Card is a plain `{c}` Animal keying (1 strike, 15 prowess, no detainment) — "If any strike is successful, the defending company must return to its site of origin (defending characters are wounded normally)" needed no other effect since the normal wound/body-check path is untouched.
- `alsoCancelLaterAttack` generalized to non-Balrog grants + `tap-on-strike-assignment` constraint + `company-site-phase-do-nothing` `unless`/`escape` + `discard: "named-card"` cost (Fifteen Birds in Five Firtrees dm-129) — four pieces, all extending shipped `cancel-attack`/constraint machinery rather than adding new effect types. **(1)** The `free-attack-cancel` constraint kind gains optional `restrictToCompanyId` and `requireNonUniqueCreature` alongside the existing (now-optional-in-practice, still required-boolean) `restrictToBalrogCompany`; three sibling flags on `cancel-attack` — `alsoCancelLaterAttackRestrictToBalrogCompany` (ba-55, preserving its exact prior hardcoded behavior explicitly), `alsoCancelLaterAttackSameCompanyOnly` (captures `combat.companyId` at grant time), `alsoCancelLaterAttackRequireNonUnique` — tell `apply-dispatcher.ts`'s `cancel-attack` branch which restrictions to install. `cancelAttackActions` (`legal-actions/combat.ts`) checks all three when offering `mode: "free-later-cancel"`; `enemy.unique` is now hoisted above the `whenContext` closure so both the `when`-context builder and the free-cancel loop share one computation. **(2)** New constraint kind **`tap-on-strike-assignment`** (turn-scoped, company-targeted, no extra fields) — installed by the same `cancel-attack` branch when the effect carries `installsTapOnStrikeAssignment: true`. Purely reducer-side: `applyTapOnStrikeAssignment` (`reducer-combat.ts`) runs from both "new" strike-assignment branches of `handleAssignStrike` (normal single-defender and force-single-target multi-attack; excess-strike and CvCC assignments are untouched) and taps the assigned character in place when the combat is hazard-creature-sourced and the character was untapped — `applyOneConstraint`'s switch passes the kind straight through (`return base`), since it's consumed directly by the reducer, not the legal-action layer. **(3)** `CompanySitePhaseDoNothingEffect` gains optional **`unless`** (a `Condition`, evaluated via `buildTargetCompanyConditionContext` exactly like `company-return-to-origin`'s — which now also exposes **`company.containsWizard`**, true if any company character has `race === "wizard"`) and optional **`escape`** (a `GrantedActionConstraintPayload`). `applyCompanySitePhaseDoNothing` (`chain-reducer.ts`) skips installing the constraint entirely when `unless` matches, and when `escape` is present installs a second `granted-action` constraint alongside `site-phase-do-nothing` — same source card, same scope/target — so `remove-constraint` (`select: "constraint-source"`) clears both together, mirroring River's `on-event`-triggered pair but installed directly on the short event's own resolution instead. **(4)** New `ActionCost.discard` variant **`"named-card"`** (+ `discardCardName`) discards a hand card by definition name with **no character actor** — `applyCost` (`cost-evaluator.ts`) finds and discards it directly; because `canPayCost` only gates on tap status, both granted-action legal-action paths (`granted-action-constraints.ts`'s window-specific emitter *and* `legal-actions/pending.ts`'s generic constraint pass-through — a card's escape is reachable through either depending on when it's queried) independently pre-check hand presence before offering the action. Card: "Playable on a moving company facing a non-unique hazard creature if Gates of Morning is in play. All attacks of the creature are cancelled and all attacks of the next non-unique hazard creature the company faces this turn are also canceled. An untapped character in the company must tap to face any strike from a subsequent hazard creature attack for the rest of the turn. The company can do nothing during its site phase unless it contains a Wizard or you discard Eagle-mounts from your hand. Cannot be duplicated on a given turn." — `cancel-attack` (gated `attack.source $in [creature, on-guard-creature, played-auto-attack]` + `enemy.unique !== true` + `defender.inPlay $includes "Gates of Morning"`) carrying all three `alsoCancelLaterAttack*` flags plus `installsTapOnStrikeAssignment`; `company-site-phase-do-nothing` with `unless: { company.containsWizard: true }` and the Eagle-mounts `escape`; `duplication-limit` scope `turn`.
- `play-condition requires: card-in-play` on **hero-resource long-events** (Fog tw-241) — no new effect type, one more play path honouring an existing gate. `longEventActions` (`legal-actions/long-event.ts`) previously checked only `duplication-limit` (scope game) and `play-condition player-state` before offering `play-long-event`; it now also checks `findPlayConditionEffect(def, 'card-in-play')` via the same `isCardNameInPlayForPlayer` helper the resource short-event and permanent-event paths already use (own play area, attachment-aware), emitting `not-playable` naming the missing card when unmet. This closes the last resource-event shape (long-event) that didn't yet honour the gate — hazard long-events (Snowstorm tw-91, Morgul Night tw-62), resource short-events, and resource permanent-events all already did. Fog reuses the Morgul Night `region-type-remap` shape (`free→border`, `border→wilderness`, `shadow→wilderness`, `dark→shadow`, **no `when` gate** — Gates of Morning is a play condition only, so the remap survives Gates of Morning leaving play) plus `duplication-limit` scope game. Card: "Environment. Playable only if Gates of Morning is in play. Treat all Free-domains [{f}] as Border-lands [{b}] and all Border-lands [{b}] as Wildernesses [{w}] and all Shadow-lands [{s}] as Wildernesses [{w}] and all Dark-domains [{d}] as Shadow-lands [{s}]. Cannot be duplicated."
- Pure composition, no new engine work (Daelomin tw-26) — second user of the `siteNames`/`regionNames` `keyedTo` shape introduced by Smaug (tw-90): a `siteNames: ["Dancing Spire"]` base entry (always allowed) plus a `regionNames` alt entry (Grey Mountain Narrows/Iron Hills/Northern Rhovanion/Withered Heath) gated `when: { inPlay: "Doors of Night" }`, whose "or at sites in these regions" half is covered by the same destination-site-region-name matching tw-90 relies on. Plus the plain printed `combat-attacker-chooses-defenders` (Dragon, 3 strikes at 13/8). No new engine code — confirms the tw-90 `siteNames` primitive generalizes cleanly to a second named-lair Dragon.
- New `grant-action` `add-constraint` kind `defeat-attack-strikes` (Liquid Fire wh-52) — the same `item-play-site`/`activeSitePhase` shape as its siblings Blasting Fire (wh-51) and Vile Fumes (wh-54), but where those either skip the site's automatic-attacks outright (`skip-automatic-attacks`) or replace them (`transform-site`), Liquid Fire lets the attack proceed and still lets the defending company attempt to kill the creature — just at worse odds. A single-use `company-site-phase` constraint consumed by the site auto-attack initiation (`reducer-site.ts`, alongside the existing `auto-attack-boost` lookup): if the attack's creature race is in `excludeRaces` (Dragon/Ringwraith/Balrog for wh-52) the constraint is left untouched for a later qualifying attack at the same visit; otherwise it's consumed and threaded onto the combat as `forcedStrikeDefeat: true` + `forcedDefeatBodyCheckModifier` (new `CombatState` fields). `combat-strike.ts`'s `resolveStrikeCore` short-circuits the roll outcome to `'success'` (creature body check if the creature has body) whenever `forcedStrikeDefeat` is set, and `handleBodyCheckRoll` (`combat-actions.ts`) folds `forcedDefeatBodyCheckModifier` into the creature-body-check `effectiveRoll` alongside the wounded-agent bonus and `bearerCombatBodyCheckModifier`. `defeat-attack-strikes` is a no-op in `applyOneConstraint`'s exhaustive switch (`legal-actions/pending.ts`) — like `auto-attack-boost`, it's consumed directly by the reducer, not by legal-action filtering. Card: "Discard to cause all strikes from all attacks of a non-Dragon, non-Nazgûl, non-Balrog creature keyed to a site to fail (resulting body checks for the creature are modified by -2)."
- New `on-event` apply `enqueue-reveal-hazards-choice` + `reveal-hazards-choice` pending resolution + `only-revealed-hazards-on-company` constraint (Here Is a Snake! dm-137) — the first resource event that hands the *opponent* an open-ended "reveal any number of cards from your own hand" choice with a printed alternative. The `enqueue-reveal-hazards-choice` apply (`reducer-events.ts` `applyShortEventOnEntersPlay`) resolves the played card's `targetCompanyId` and enqueues a `reveal-hazards-choice` resolution for the opponent, `company-mh-subphase`-scoped. `revealHazardsChoiceActions` (`legal-actions/pending.ts`) offers one repeatable `reveal-hazard-for-snake` per not-yet-revealed hand hazard-creature/hazard-event (each reveal recorded live in `revealedInstances` via the shared `revealInstances` helper and appended to the resolution's accumulator — the `arrange-deck-top` accumulate shape, but unbounded, so there is no target count and `pass` finalizes explicitly), plus — only while nothing has been revealed yet — one `tap-reveal-agent-for-snake` per eligible face-down untapped agent, reusing `revealAgentActions`' home-site candidate generation (now exported from `movement-hazard.ts`) filtered to `CardStatus.Untapped`. `applyRevealHazardsChoiceResolution` (`pending-reducers.ts`) resolves `tap-reveal-agent-for-snake` by delegating to the existing `handleRevealAgent` (rule 9.04 placement + movement-history/uniqueness checks) and then tapping the surviving agent — CoE rule 4.3 covers a face-down agent tapped "to initiate a hazard effect" — and dequeues with **no** constraint (the printed alternative escapes the restriction entirely); `pass` installs the new `only-revealed-hazards-on-company` constraint (`company-mh-phase` scope) with the accumulated instance IDs as its allow-list, even when empty. `applyOnlyRevealedHazardsOnCompany` (`legal-actions/pending.ts`, wired into `applyOneConstraint`'s exhaustive switch) drops `play-hazard` actions against the protected company (creature or event) not in the allow-list; an empty allow-list therefore blocks every hazard play against the company for the rest of its M/H phase. "including on-guard cards" is filtered separately, directly inside `onGuardWindowActions` (same file): while ANY pending resolution is queued for the actor, `computeLegalActions` (`legal-actions/index.ts`) short-circuits straight to `resolutionLegalActions` and never reaches the generic `applyConstraints` post-filter (which only wraps the phase-switch branch) — so `reveal-on-guard` actions have to be filtered at the source instead. `onGuardWindowActions` now looks up an `only-revealed-hazards-on-company` constraint targeting the on-guard window's company and skips any not-yet-revealed on-guard card whose instance isn't in its allow-list. Card: "Playable on a company during its movement/hazard phase after cards have been drawn. Opponent may reveal to you any number of hazards from his hand. He may only play hazards he revealed to you (including on-guard cards) for the remainder of target company's movement/hazard phase. Alternatively, a face-down agent is tapped and revealed." — `play-window` movement-hazard/`play-hazards`, `play-target` company, `on-event: self-enters-play` → `enqueue-reveal-hazards-choice`. This is also the first `play-target: company` **hero-resource-event outside the organization phase's end-of-org window** — `heroResourceShortEventActions` (`legal-actions/long-event.ts`, the generic scanner covering M/H, site, long-event, end-of-turn, and combat) previously had no `company` branch in its target dispatch (only the org-phase `playResourceShortEventActions`' end-of-org path did), so it fell through to the untargeted `emitPlay(undefined)` and never set `targetCompanyId`. Added a `playTarget.target === 'company'` branch there, gated by the new `resolveActiveCompanyForTarget` (only resolves for `playerId === state.activePlayer`, reading `activeCompanyIndex` off `state.phaseState` for `'movement-hazard'`/`'site'` — null, hence not-playable, everywhere else), reusing the org-phase filter shape (`company.atHaven` / `company.moving`) so both paths agree on the same DSL context.
- New self-enters-play apply `discard-bearer-corruption` + `company.siteCharacterNames` play-target context field (Three Golden Hairs td-157) — a site-phase hero-resource permanent-event played onto a character. The `on-event: self-enters-play` apply **`discard-bearer-corruption`** (type-only marker) is dispatched from the existing `resolvePermanentEvent` apply loop (`chain-reducer.ts`) using the chain entry's `targetCharacterId`; the new `discardBearerCorruptionCards` helper scans only that bearer's `hazards` for a corruption card — `cardType: "hazard-corruption"` or a `hazard-event` carrying the `"corruption"` keyword, the same CoE-7.2.1 test `movement-hazard.ts`/`organization.ts` already use, since every printed "Corruption." hazard in the data files today is the latter — and routes each match to its owner's discard pile (owner from the instance-id prefix) — scoped to one character, unlike the board-wide `discard-named-in-play`. Separately, the site-phase `play-target: "character"` filter context (`playResourcesActions`, `legal-actions/site.ts`) gained **`company.siteCharacterNames`**: the names of every character in one of the player's companies currently at the same site as the candidate's company (across companies, including its own), letting a plain `$includes` filter express "at the same site as <Named Character>" without a new play-condition keyword — the site-phase sibling of `same-site-has-character-race` (organization-events.ts), which is race-scoped and org-phase-only. Card: "Unique. Playable at any site on a non-Wizard, non-Hobbit diplomat character (other than Galadriel) at the same site as Galadriel. All corruption cards on the bearer are discarded when this card comes into play. +2 to all corruption checks by bearer." — `play-target` character filter (`target.race $ne wizard` + `target.race $ne hobbit` + `target.skills $includes diplomat` + `target.name $ne Galadriel` + `company.siteCharacterNames $includes Galadriel`), `on-event self-enters-play` → `discard-bearer-corruption`, and a bearer-scoped `check-modifier corruption +2`.
- New `keyedTo` field `movingBetweenSiteNames` + keying method `moving-between-sites` (The Great Goblin tw-95) — site-to-site movement keying: the entry lists site names, and matches when the moving company's origin (current) site name and destination site name are both in the list and differ, so one entry covers both directions ("moving from Rivendell to Lórien or from Lórien to Rivendell"). A non-moving company never matches (its destination name equals its origin name). Evaluated in `findCreatureKeyingMatches` (offer side, origin from the target company's `currentSite`) and `checkCreatureKeying` (validation side, origin from the active company's `currentSite`); `keyedBy.method` records `"moving-between-sites"` with the route as value. The card's other clauses are shipped precedent: base `{D}` → `siteTypes: ["dark-hold"]`, and "keyed to High Pass and at sites in High Pass" is a single `regionNames: ["High Pass"]` entry whose sites-in-region half is the destination site's own region appearing in `resolvedSitePathNames` (as-13/as-21/tw-90 precedent). No `effects[]` — 1 strike 12/7 Orc handled structurally.

- Pure composition, no new engine work (Hobgoblins td-30) — plain Orc creature, 2 strikes/prowess 10/no body, keyed `{w}{w}` via the shared `regionTypes: ["wilderness", "wilderness"]` two-wilderness keying shape (ba-10 precedent). No target-company restriction, no additional effects. Confirms the region-type keying primitive generalizes to a bare, restriction-free creature.
- `grant-creature-keying` `siteFilter.excludeSiteTypes` (denylist) + new `companyFilter` field (The Nazgûl are Abroad tw-96) — two extensions to the in-play creature-keying grant. **(1)** `excludeSiteTypes` is the inverse of `siteTypes`: the site-type branch matches any effective site type *not* in the list (still ANDed with `siteKeywords` when present), for "any site except …" grants where a positive site-type list would be unwieldy. **(2)** `companyFilter` (a `Condition`) additionally gates the grant on the *target company being attacked*, evaluated via `buildTargetCompanyConditionContext` (`reducer-utils.ts`) — which now also collects **`company.itemNames`** and **`company.itemKeywords`** (every item borne by any character in the company, and their combined `keywords`) alongside its existing `alignment`/`homeSites`/`characterNames`/etc. fields. `grantsCreatureKeying` (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) takes a new `owner: PlayerState` parameter (the defending company's controller, passed as `resourcePlayer` from all three call sites) to build this context and pass `defenderAlignmentLabel(owner.alignment)` as the `company.alignment` value. Both checks run **after** the site/region branch matches, so a `companyFilter` miss falls through to the next grant rather than aborting the whole scan. Used by The Nazgûl are Abroad (tw-96): two `grant-creature-keying` effects for `creatureFilter: { keywords: { $includes: "Nazgûl" } }` — "may attack a hero company containing the bearer of The One Ring at any site that is not a Free-hold or Haven" is `siteFilter.excludeSiteTypes: [free-hold, haven]` + `companyFilter: { company.alignment: hero, company.itemKeywords: { $includes: "the-one-ring" } }`; "may attack any hero company possessing any Ring in a Shadow-land or Shadow-hold" is `siteFilter: { siteTypes: [shadow-hold], regionTypes: [shadow] }` + `companyFilter: { company.alignment: hero, company.itemKeywords: { $includes: "ring" } }`. The card's third clause — "If Doors of Night is in play, at the end of each turn, each player may return one Nazgûl permanent-event from his discard pile to his hand" — needed no new engine work: it's the exact `on-event: end-of-turn` / `actor: "both"` / `when: { inPlay: "Doors of Night" }` shape (`fireEndOfTurnFetchEffects`, `reducer-site.ts`) already shipped for From the Pits of Angband (td-27) and reused by Master of the House (as-16), with a `move` `filter: { keywords: { $includes: "Nazgûl" } }` (the same filter already used by Praise to Elbereth tw-305's `tap-discard-in-play`). Plus a standard `duplication-limit` scope `game` max 1 for "Cannot be duplicated."

- New `keyedTo` field `adjacentToSiteNames` + keying method `adjacent-to-site-name` (Durin's Bane dm-107) — the named-site sibling of `adjacentToSiteKeywords`: matches when the destination site is adjacent (under-deeps sense, `resolveAdjacency`/`isUnderDeepsAdjacent`) to any site printing one of the listed *names* (resolved across every same-named printing — hero/minion/balrog — in `state.cardPool`), rather than to a keyword-tagged site. Evaluated in `findCreatureKeyingMatches` (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) and `checkCreatureKeying` (`mh-hazard-play.ts`), mirroring the existing `adjacentToSiteKeywords` blocks in both files line-for-line except for the name-vs-keyword site lookup. Card: "May be played at The Under-gates and at all of its adjacent sites. If Doors of Night is in play, Durin's Bane may be played at any Under-deeps site." — three `keyedTo` entries: the base `siteNames: ["The Under-gates"]`, a new `adjacentToSiteNames: ["The Under-gates"]` for the "and all of its adjacent sites" clause, and the Doors-of-Night alternate `siteKeywords: ["under-deeps"]` gated `when: { inPlay: "Doors of Night" }` (the dm-109 shape). Also confirms `combat-body-check-modifier` (previously only shipped bundled with Carrion Feeders' `combat-one-strike-per-character`/`combat-tap-to-cancel-strike` trio) works standalone on a plain 2-strike creature: "all body checks resulting from a successful strike are modified by +1" is `{ "type": "combat-body-check-modifier", "value": 1 }` alone, no companion combat-rule effects needed. The card's deck/play restrictions ("Cannot be included in a Balrog's deck and cannot be played if your opponent is a Balrog player") are the plain, already-shipped `deck-restriction` `excluded-from-deck` (rule 1.23, checked by `validateDeck`/`deck-validation.ts`) + `play-restriction` `unplayable-when` (checked by `applyDeclaredPlayRestrictions`, `legal-actions/index.ts`) pair — both fully implemented; a blocked `unplayable-when` play is rewritten into a generic `{ type: 'not-playable', cardInstanceId }` action (`notPlayable()`, `action-builders.ts`), not a non-viable `play-hazard`, so tests must key off the card's instance id rather than filtering by `action.type === 'play-hazard'`.
- New `keyedTo` `when`-context field `destinationSite.region` (Huorn tw-45) — lets a `siteTypes` entry be scoped to sites located in specific named regions, distinct from a plain `regionNames` entry (which matches the company's *movement path* — `resolvedSitePathNames` — and only exists for a **moving** company, CoE rule 2.IV.vii.2 bullet 1). A `siteTypes` entry works whether the target company is moving or stationary (bullet 2: "the company's new site, i.e. current site if not moving"), so it is the only way to key a stationary company at a qualifying site inside those regions. Added alongside the existing `destinationSite.sitePath.*Count` context (Rain-drake td-57): `findCreatureKeyingMatches` (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) and `checkCreatureKeying` (`mh-hazard-play.ts`) both now resolve the destination site's printed `region` field and expose it as `destinationSite.region` alongside `sitePath`. Card: "May also be played at Drúadan Forest, Old Forest, and Wellinghall. May also be played keyed to Heart of Mirkwood, Southern Mirkwood, Western Mirkwood, and Woodland Realm; and may also be played at Ruins & Lairs [{R}] and Shadow-holds [{S}] in these regions." — four `keyedTo` entries: base `regionTypes: ["wilderness"]`, `siteNames: ["Drúadan Forest", "Old Forest", "Wellinghall"]`, `regionNames` for the four Mirkwood regions (covers the plain region-keying half, moving companies only), and `siteTypes: ["ruins-and-lairs", "shadow-hold"]` gated `when: { "destinationSite.region": { "$in": [<the same four regions>] } }` for the region-scoped site-type half (reaches a stationary company too, but only at a qualifying site type, unlike the unrestricted "sites in these regions" shape used by as-13/as-21/tw-90/tw-95/tw-46, where a bare `regionNames` entry is sufficient because those cards impose no site-type restriction).

- `multi-faction-check` + `dice-check` kind field `alwaysFailRolls` (News of Doom le-127) — a new hazard short-event effect for cards that sweep **every faction in play for both players** rather than one `play-target: "faction"`-chosen faction (Muster Disperses le-126/tw-67). Carries no `play-target`, so it is offered as a single untargeted `play-hazard` action via the existing no-play-target fallback (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`). On chain resolution (`chain-reducer.ts`, alongside the Muster Disperses block) it scans both players' `cardsInPlay` for `isFactionCard` entries and enqueues one `dice-check` {@link PendingResolution} per faction found — `roller`/`actor` = that faction's own owner, modifier = that owner's unused GI, `onFail` a `move` discarding the faction to its own owner's pile (`toOwner: "source-owner"`, which resolves from the located `in-play` source card, not the declaring player) — sharing `continuation: { kind: "chain-entry", match: "source", drainSameSource: true }` so the chain entry stays unresolved until every faction's roll is in (the `force-check-all-company` "all company members" pattern generalized across both players' zones instead of one company). The `threshold`/`comparison` fields compare the modified total as usual; the new `alwaysFailRolls: number[]` field on the `dice-check` kind (`types/pending.ts`, consulted in `applyDiceCheckResolution`, `pending-reducers.ts`) additionally fails the check whenever the **raw, pre-modifier** 2d6 roll is in the list, regardless of the modified total — needed because "Discard any faction if its result is 2 or 3, **or** if its result plus GI is less than 10" reads the unmodified roll for the first clause. Used by News of Doom (le-127): `{ "type": "multi-faction-check", "threshold": 10, "comparison": "gte", "alwaysFailRolls": [2, 3] }`. The card's "Remove News of Doom from the game" is the standard `play-flag: "remove-from-game"`, but performed **inline** inside the `multi-faction-check` block before it pauses with `needsInput: true` — the generic remove-from-game block (`chain-reducer.ts`, positioned after the resource-event `new-hand` shuffle so Favor of the Valar tw-239's spent card is already in the discard pile when it runs) never reaches a card whose *other* effect paused resolution earlier in the same pass, since a drained multi-resolution entry is later marked resolved via `resolveChainEntryAndContinue` rather than a fresh call into `resolveEntry`.
- New `play-condition` `requires: "card-count-exceeds"` + `bareCardGrantActions` generalized to bearer-less resource-events + bearer-less `enqueue-pending-fetch` (Earth-eater wh-67) — the Delver's Harvest (wh-65) sibling: same site-phase-only `play-condition requires: "active-company"` gate on `site.name: "Deep Mines"`, plus a new count comparison for "you have more Delver's Harvest cards in play than you have Earth-eater cards". `card-count-exceeds` carries `cardName`/`comparedToCardName`; checked in `legal-actions/site.ts`'s site-phase permanent-event loop via two `countPlayerHeldCopies` calls (own count must exceed, not merely meet, the compared count — ties are not "more"). For the tap ability ("Tap Earth-eater to take a … item … to your hand"), the previously faction-only `inPlayFactionGrantActions` (`legal-actions/organization.ts`) was renamed **`bareCardGrantActions`** and its eligibility broadened from `isFactionCard` to `isFactionCard || isResourceEventCard` — filtered additionally on `cip.companyId === undefined` so it never doubles up with the company-bound sibling `inPlayCompanyTapGrantActions` (site.ts) for cards like Pass the Doors of Dol Guldur (dm-154) that carry a `play-target: "company"` binding. Its non-targeted branch now also accepts `apply.type === "enqueue-pending-fetch"` (previously `add-constraint` only). `handleInPlayCardGrantAction` (`grant-action-apply.ts`) gained a matching `enqueue-pending-fetch` branch for bearer-less sources — mirrors the bearer-borne version in `runGrantApply` (Strider ba-1) minus `playableAtBearerSite` (no bearer character to derive a site from).
