Card: `grant-action` `cost: { tap: "self" }`, `apply: { type: "enqueue-pending-fetch", fetchFrom: ["discard-pile", "sideboard"], fetchTo: "hand", filter: { unique: false, subtype: "major", keywords $includes one of weapon/armor/shield/helmet } }` — the same non-unique-major-equipment filter shape as The Forge-master (wh-117), reused verbatim.
- `creature-alt-event` fields `when` + `attacksAsCreature`, and a new `attack-alt-permanent-event` action (Shelob tw-86) — two extensions to the dual creature/permanent-event primitive (§56a/§56c in the DSL doc). `when` (a `Condition`) gates the permanent-event mode's *offering* on `{ inPlay: buildInPlayNames(state) }` — "may be played as a permanent-event **if Doors of Night is in play**" — checked in the `creature-alt-event` block of `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts` alongside the existing `targetCompany`/`requiresMovingCompany` checks; the creature mode's own `keyedTo` (a plain `regionNames: ["Imlad Morgul", "Gorgoroth"]` entry) carries no such gate. `attacksAsCreature: true` marks the in-play permanent-event as convertible into a **full creature attack** (using its own printed stats plus active global effects) rather than a §56c short-event: a new `attack-alt-permanent-event` action (`attackFromAltPermanentEventActions` legal-actions, `handleAttackFromAltPermanentEvent` reducer in `mh-hazard-play.ts`) charges one hazard-limit slot and pushes a `{ type: "creature" }` chain entry sourced from the card **without removing it from `cardsInPlay` first** — unlike every other creature/event play. This is required because Shelob's own passive `stat-modifier` (+1 prowess/+1 strikes, `target: "all-attacks"`, `when: enemy.race $in [spider, animal]`) is itself sourced from this same in-play card; pulling it out before the chain resolves into combat would hide the modifier from `resolveAttackProwess`/`resolveAttackStrikes` when computing her own attack, breaking "she may opt to attack … and receive these bonuses". `initiateCreatureCombat` (`chain-reducer.ts`) now checks whether the creature is already present in the hazard player's `cardsInPlay` before appending it, so the attack-from-permanent-event path doesn't duplicate the entry. `finalizeCombat`'s existing creature disposal (discard, or the defender's kill pile if defeated) removes the card once the attack resolves ("discard when Shelob attacks"); "or if Doors of Night is not in play" is the plain `discard-self-when` primitive (§62), `condition: { "$not": { "inPlayAnywhere": "Doors of Night" } }` (The Will of Sauron tw-100 precedent) — no new support needed there. `tap-alt-permanent-event` is excluded for an `attacksAsCreature` card, mirroring how a `modify-attack fromAltPermanentEvent` card is excluded.
- `buildTargetCompanyConditionContext` gains **`company.hasWoundedCharacter`** + top-level **`inPlay`** (Morgul-rats td-49) — two additions to the shared "target company" condition context (`reducer-utils.ts`), consumed by the `play-condition requires: "target-company"` gate (le-78 precedent). `hasWoundedCharacter` is `true` when any character in the company is `CardStatus.Inverted`, letting a creature gate on "a character in target company is wounded" without a new keyword. The context also now exposes `inPlay` (via `buildInPlayNames`) alongside `company.*`, so a single condition can combine a company predicate with a named in-play prerequisite in one `$or` — no separate context lookup needed. Card: "Animals. 15 strikes. Only playable at a Shadow-hold [{S}] or at a Dark-hold [{D}], and only if a character in target company is wounded or Doors of Night is in play." — `keyedTo: [{ "siteTypes": ["shadow-hold", "dark-hold"] }]` (no `attributes.playable` token on record for this card; the site-type cost is text-only) plus one `play-condition requires: "target-company"` with `condition: { "$or": [{ "company.hasWoundedCharacter": true }, { "inPlay": "Doors of Night" }] }`. No `effects[]` beyond the play-condition — plain Animal, no combat-rule additions.
- `company-combat-boost` `when` + `costDiscard` (Alert the Folk td-97) — two additions to the combat-only short-event boost (§ `company-combat-boost`). `when` gates *which attack* the card may be played against, evaluated in both the offering (`companyCombatBoostActions`, `legal-actions/combat.ts`) and the reducer (`reducer-events.ts` `handlePlayResourceShortEvent`) against `{ enemy: { race, name } }` — `race` from `combat.creatureRace`, `name` resolved via `attackSourceCreatureInstanceId` + `resolveDef`/`defById` (empty string when the attack has no individual creature card, e.g. a bare automatic-attack). `costDiscard` (`{ source: "hand", filter, minCount, maxCount }`) replaces the fixed `value` with one computed at play time: candidate hand cards (excluding the played card itself) are matched against `filter` — extended with `faction.playableRegions` (`buildFactionPlayableRegions`) for faction candidates, same context shape as the Firiel dm-10 influence precedent — and one `play-short-event` action is offered per combination of `minCount`..`maxCount` matches, carrying the chosen instances as the new `PlayShortEventAction.costDiscardInstanceIds` field. On resolution the reducer re-validates the chosen cards against the count bounds and `filter`, moves them to the controller's discard pile, and sums their printed `marshallingPoints` as the boost `value`. Used by Alert the Folk (td-97): "Playable on a company facing a Dragon or Drake attack (not Eärcaraxë). Discard from your hand any one or two factions playable at sites in Northern Rhovanion, Iron Hills, Woodland Realm, or Anduin Vales. All characters facing the attack gain a bonus to their prowess equal to the total marshalling point values … of the factions discarded." — `when: { "$and": [{ "enemy.race": { "$in": ["dragon", "drake"] } }, { "enemy.name": { "$ne": "Eärcaraxë" } }] }`, `costDiscard: { source: "hand", filter: { "$and": [{ "marshallingCategory": "faction" }, { "$or": [four region "faction.playableRegions" $includes checks] }] }, minCount: 1, maxCount: 2 }`.
- New faction-card self-effect `block-influence-boost` (Angmarim as-58) — a faction's own printed "(Muster has no effect on this attempt)" restriction, distinct from the region-scoped `faction-influence-restriction` environment (Mordor in Arms dm-72), which lives on an *other* in-play hazard and is gated by the site's region. `block-influence-boost` instead sits directly in the faction card's own `effects[]` and applies unconditionally to every attempt to influence it, regardless of site/region. Both the influence-attempt legal-action generator (`legal-actions/site.ts`) and the roll resolver (`resolveInfluenceAttemptRoll`, `reducer-site.ts`) now fold `factionDef.effects`/`def.effects` entries of this type into the same `blockedBoostCardNames`/`blockedBoosts` set already populated by `collectFactionInfluenceRestriction`, so a one-shot `check-modifier` constraint from a named card (Muster tw-288's `add-constraint check-modifier` shape) is consumed but contributes zero — the existing "suppressed, still consumed" log/behavior is reused verbatim, just fed from a second source. Card: "Unique. Manifestation of minion Angmarim. Playable at Carn Dûm if the influence check is greater than 11 (Muster has no effect on this attempt). Standard Modifications: Wizards (-5), Men (+1)." — `playableAt: [{ site: "Carn Dûm" }]`, `influenceNumber: 12` (`>11` ⇒ `total >= 12`), two `check-modifier` `bearer.race` entries (wizard -5, man +1, the td-138/tw-* Standard Modifications shape), plus `{ "type": "block-influence-boost", "blockCards": ["Muster"] }`.
- `modify-attack` `scope: "current-strike"` gains a `cost: { discard: "self" }` variant, a per-strike `bodyModifier`, and `cascadeDefeatOnSuccess` (Arrows Shorn of Ebony td-99) — three extensions to the item-borne single-strike modifier (previously tap-only, prowess-only; Shield of Iron-bound Ash tw-327). `tapItemForStrikeActions`/`handleTapItemForStrike` (`legal-actions/combat.ts`/`combat-actions.ts`, both still dispatched via the `tap-item-for-strike` action for either cost variant) now also accept `cost: { discard: "self" }`, which has no tapped-status requirement and removes the item to its owner's discard pile instead of tapping it. `bodyModifier` here writes a new per-strike `StrikeAssignment.strikeCreatureBodyModifier` (read in `handleBodyCheckRoll`'s creature branch, `combat-actions.ts`) rather than the whole-attack scope's persistent `CombatState.creatureBody` change — it affects only the one creature body check this strike triggers, not any other strike of the same attack. `cascadeDefeatOnSuccess: true` writes `StrikeAssignment.cascadesOnDefeat`; once that strike's fate is finally known as defeated (`result` ends `'success'` — immediately in `resolveStrikeCore` when the creature has no body, or in `handleBodyCheckRoll` once that strike's own body check confirms the creature died) every other still-unresolved strike of the same attack is auto-defeated by setting `CombatState.forcedStrikeDefeat` **mid-combat** (previously only ever set once, at combat initiation, by Liquid Fire wh-52's `defeat-attack-strikes` constraint). The `when` context gained `attack.siteKeyed`/`attack.source` parity with the whole-attack scope via a new shared `modifyAttackWhenContext` helper. Card: "Hoard item. Warrior only: discard Arrows Shorn of Ebony to modify a strike from a hazard creature attack not keyed to a site by -1 prowess, -2 body. If this strike is defeated, all other subsequent failed strikes from this attack are automatically defeated." — `modify-attack` `scope: current-strike`, `cost: { discard: "self" }`, `prowessModifier: 1` (a +1 defender bonus is the single-strike-comparison equivalent of -1 creature prowess, same convention as tw-327), `bodyModifier: -2`, `cascadeDefeatOnSuccess: true`, `when: bearer.skills $includes warrior AND attack.source: creature AND attack.siteKeyed: false` (the td-102 "not keyed to a site" shape).

- New `named-creature-hunt` effect + `hunt-attack` `AttackSource` + `CombatState.spellsIneffective` (The Hunt dm-143) — a one-shot, player-driven sibling of `reveal-and-attack`/The Great Hunt (wh-91, §35a): instead of revealing several creatures from the top of a chosen pile, the controller names one *already-known* hazard-creature instance to force an immediate solo attack. "Revealed to you ... and discarded" is modeled by `GameState.handRevealedInstances` (the engine's public-identity ledger for cards sitting in an otherwise-private pile): `findHuntCandidates` (`engine/hunt.ts`) scans the opponent's play deck **and** discard pile for hazard-creature instances recorded there — "unless eliminated or prevented from being in play" falls out for free, since a creature that left both piles is simply not offered. Flow: `handlePlayResourceShortEvent` (`reducer-events.ts`) discards the event card and enqueues a `hunt-target-choice` pending resolution; `huntTargetChoiceActions` (`legal-actions/pending.ts`) recomputes candidates live, offering one `choose-hunt-target` per candidate or a mandatory `pass` when none exist (fizzle — no tap, no attack); `applyHuntTargetChoiceResolution` (`pending-reducers.ts`) re-validates the choice and calls `buildHuntCombat`, which reshuffles the opponent's play deck first if the creature was found there ("reshuffling his play deck if it was searched" — a discard-pile find needs none) and then builds a `hunt-attack` combat referencing the creature in place (never moved, like `great-hunt-attack` — `attackSourceCreatureInstanceId` doesn't recognize the new source type, so `finalizeCombat` neither discards nor awards it). "As though he were a one-character company" reuses the existing `CombatState.soloDefenderInstanceId` primitive (Burglary td-103 precedent) to restrict strike assignment to the bearer alone. "Cannot use or benefit from spells against the attack" is the new `CombatState.spellsIneffective` flag, enforced centrally (unlike the per-card-`when` `weaponsIneffective` precedent) at its two actual points of leverage: `cancelAttackActions` (`legal-actions/combat.ts`) filters out any `cancel-attack` action sourced from a `spell`-keyword card (Vanishment tw-356, Wizard's River-horses tw-364) once the normal per-source loops finish; `collectCreatureAttackBoostEffects` (`effects/resolver.ts`) takes a new `CreatureAttackBoostContext.suppressSpellSources` flag and skips `creature-attack-boost` constraints sourced from a `spell`-keyword card (Wizard's Flame tw-361's prowess reduction). "If untapped, tap [the bearer] afterwards" is `tapHuntBearerAfterwards` (`engine/hunt.ts`), called from both `combat-finalize.ts` and `combat-cancel.ts` for a `hunt-attack` source so the tap applies whichever way the forced attack concludes. Card: "Playable on Alatar during the organization phase. Name a specific hazard creature card your opponent revealed to you through a mechanism of the game and discarded. Unless eliminated or prevented from being in play, your opponent then finds this particular card (reshuffling his play deck if it was searched). This creature immediately attacks Alatar as though he were a one-character company. Alatar cannot use or benefit from spells against the attack. If untapped, tap Alatar afterwards." — `play-window` organization, `play-target` character filter `target.name: "Alatar"` (no cost — the generic filter-only-target path already offers `targetCharacterId`), `named-creature-hunt`.

- Item-cache primitives `item-cache-hand-store` / `item-cache-alt-storage` / `item-cache-play-source` / `item-cache-count-bonus` (Armory dm-116) — a permanent event that caches a player's own minor items off to the side (`engine/set-aside.ts`, MEAS §1), as an alternative to both hand and the marshalling-point pile. `item-cache-hand-store` (`subtypes`) adds a new organization-phase-only action (`store-item-in-cache`, `itemCacheHandStoreActions` in `legal-actions/organization.ts`, `handleStoreItemInCache` in `reducer-organization.ts`) moving a hand item straight into the host's `setAside` (`placeCardSetAside(..., noMp: true)`), no bearer/site/corruption-check. `item-cache-alt-storage` (`siteTypes`, `subtypes`) extends the existing `store-item` action/reducer (`organization-companies.ts` `storeItemActions`, `reducer-organization.ts` `handleStoreItem`) with an optional `cacheHostInstanceId` field offered alongside the normal kill-pile destination wherever the item is already storable at a matching site type; the corruption check and `bearer-cannot-untap` cleanup are unchanged, only the destination differs. `item-cache-play-source` (`siteTypes`) generalizes the Great Secrets Buried There (dm-63) "set-aside item playable as though in hand" shape (`legal-actions/site.ts`, `playResourcesActions`'s hand-card loop) from a hardcoded Under-deeps keyword check to a declared site-type list matched against the *effective* site type (`getEffectiveSiteType`); the pre-existing `fromSetAside` reducer path (`reducer-site.ts` `handleSitePlayHeroResource`, `removeItemFromSetAside`) needed zero changes since it was already host-agnostic. `item-cache-count-bonus` (`count`, `mp`) scores a flat MP bonus in `recompute-derived.ts` once a host's own `setAside.length` reaches the threshold — the same shape as `leader-control`'s `groupBonus`, applied to a card count. All four effects key off the *querying player's own* `cardsInPlay`/`hand`, so "only you and your companies can use" this kind of cache needs no separate effect — it falls out of the existing per-player scoping.

- `modify-attack` `attachCorruptionOnWound` + `CombatState.pendingCorruptionAttach` (Icy Touch td-33) — a from-hand `modify-attack` (attacker-played) that additionally attaches *itself*, as a corruption card, to whichever character the attack ends up wounding — the first genuine "the target isn't known until after combat" corruption card, unlike every prior corruption card (always played onto a specific character up front, e.g. Dragon's Curse td-16, Pale Dream-maker dm-78). The card is still discarded immediately when played, exactly like any other from-hand `modify-attack` (`handleModifyAttack`, `combat-actions.ts`); the only addition is `CombatState.pendingCorruptionAttach` (`{ sourceCardInstanceId, sourceCardDefinitionId, ownerPlayerIndex }`), marking the just-discarded instance eligible for reattachment. At combat finalization (`finalizeCombat`, `combat-finalize.ts`) the engine reuses the `woundedCharIds` list already computed for `woundedByRaceThisTurn` (strike-array order; empty under detainment), scans it for the first character who is still in play and has not already had a corruption card played on him this turn (checked against `MovementHazardPhaseState.corruptionCardsPlayedPerChar`, Movement/Hazard phase only — site-phase automatic attacks have no such bookkeeping and treat every wounded character as eligible), splices the card out of the owner's discard pile onto the matched character's `hazards`, and updates `corruptionCardsPlayedPerChar`. No eligible wound → the card simply stays discarded, so "discard if not played with a character" needs no separate code path. The 2 corruption points are an ordinary `stat-modifier` on the card, picked up automatically once it sits in `char.hazards` (same mechanism as every other corruption card). Card: "Playable on a company facing an Undead attack. The prowess of the attack is modified by +1. Corruption. The next character wounded by the attack (on whom a corruption card has not already been played this turn) receives 2 corruption points (place this card with the character). Discard Icy Touch if it is not played with a character. During his organization phase, a character with this card may tap and attempt to remove it. Make a roll: if this result is greater than a 6, discard this card." — `modify-attack` (`fromHand: true`, `player: "attacker"`, `prowessModifier: 1`, `attachCorruptionOnWound: true`, `when: { "enemy.race": "undead" }`), `stat-modifier corruption-points +2`, `grant-action remove-self-on-roll` (`cost: { tap: "bearer" }`, threshold 7). Note: "the *next* character wounded" is read as strike-array order (the same simplification `woundedCharIds` itself already makes), not strict dice-roll chronology — a multi-strike attack whose defender deliberately reorders resolution via `choose-strike-order` is not tracked precisely.

- `play-target` `target: 'agent'` + `duplication-limit` `scope: 'agent'` + per-agent `extra-agent-actions` scoping (Never Seen Him dm-74) — a hazard permanent-event played on one of the hazard player's own agents, generalizing the attachment mechanism Inner Cunning (dm-68) introduced for `agent-reveal-site-override`: `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts` offers one `play-hazard` action per agent (any status, face-up or face-down — unlike dm-68's face-down-only gate), carrying `targetAgentId`; on resolution the card lands in the hazard player's `cardsInPlay` bound via the existing `CardInPlay.attachedToAgentId` (no new chain-reducer code needed, since that binding was already generic). `duplication-limit` `scope: 'agent'` counts existing copies by name across `cardsInPlay` entries sharing the target agent's `attachedToAgentId`. The card's own bonus (`extra-agent-actions`) previously only supported two shapes — untargeted-global (Great Need or Purpose dm-62, summed across *all* agents of *both* players) and agent-self `whileRevealed` (My Precious dm-29) — neither of which can express "this specific *other* agent gets the bonus". `countExtraAgentActions` (`mh-agents.ts`) gained an optional `agentId` parameter: passed, it scopes both the self-`whileRevealed` sum and a new `cardsInPlay` filter (only untargeted entries plus ones whose `attachedToAgentId` matches this agent) to that one agent; omitted, it preserves the old cross-agent-summed behavior for legacy callers. `reducer-untap.ts` (previously its own hand-rolled untargeted-only sum) now calls `countExtraAgentActions(state, agent.id)` per agent when setting `remainingActions`, so a self/attached bonus now actually grants a *second* action (previously such a bonus only marked the single available action as hazard-limit-free without increasing the action count — a latent gap in dm-29's original certification, fixed here since dm-74 needs the same per-agent total to be correct). The five call sites in `mh-agents.ts` that gate an action's "is this the free one" check (`chargeAgentActionTail`, `handleAgentMove`, `handleAgentMoveBack`, both `handleAgentReturnHome` branches) and `agentTurnActions` (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) now all pass the acting agent's own id. `discardOrphanedAgentAttachedEvents` (`reducer-utils.ts`) — previously unconditional "discard when the bound agent leaves face-down-agent status (revealed or gone)" — now only discards-on-reveal for a card additionally carrying `agent-reveal-site-override` (dm-68's own marker); a card without it (dm-74) stays attached through reveal and only discards once its bound agent leaves play entirely. Cannot be played against a minion/Balrog opponent (`isMinionOrBalrog`, the established agent-card convention, not a generic `play-restriction`).

- `ItemInPlay.playedAtSiteDefId` stamped on the **direct** `play-target: character` attach path (Vein of Arda dm-162) — the `select-card-bearer` shape (Rescue Prisoners tw-315, The Windlord Found Me dm-164) already stamped this field so a `duplication-limit` `scope: "site"` checks where the card was *played*, not the bearer's live location; the Dreams-of-Lore-shape direct attach (`resolvePermanentEvent` → `applyMove` `in-play-on-character`, no `trigger-attack-on-play`/pending resolution involved) did not, because no prior card combined that shape with a site-scoped duplication limit. `applyMoveToInPlay`'s `in-play-on-character` destination (`reducer-move.ts`) now resolves the bearer's company via `findCharacterCompany` and stamps `playedAtSiteDefId` from its `currentSite` on every attach through this path, matching the `select-card-bearer` precedent (`countPermanentEventCopiesAtSite`, `reducer-utils.ts`, unchanged — it already read the field generically). Used by Vein of Arda (dm-162): "Sage or Dwarf only during the site phase at any Under-deeps site. Tap the sage or Dwarf. Tap the site if it is not already tapped. Sage or Dwarf may not untap until Vein of Arda is stored at a Haven [{H}]. Cannot be duplicated at a given site." — `play-target` site (`keywords $includes under-deeps`, the ba-77 shape) + `play-target` character (`$or` `target.skills $includes sage` / `target.race: dwarf`, `target.status: untapped`) + `play-flag tap-site-on-play` (no `untapped-site-required` — the flag's existing already-tapped no-op guard in `applyTapSiteOnPlayFlag` already matches "tap the site if it is not already tapped") + `play-flag tap-character-on-play` + `storable-at` (Haven, 2 misc MP) + `play-flag bearer-cannot-untap-until-stored` (the Dreams of Lore tw-210 shape) + `duplication-limit scope: site`.

- `play-condition requires: card-in-play` on a **site-phase-only** resource permanent-event (Stone of Erech tw-334) — closes the last permanent-event shape that didn't yet honour the `card-in-play` gate. Oromë's Warders (wh-94) already covered the "any phase" permanent-event branch (`playPermanentEventActions`, `legal-actions/organization-events.ts`); a plain permanent-event with a bare site `play-target` and no other timing marker (no Stage `alignment`, no `roll-untap-site`/`company-mh-end-at-site`/`wizardhaven-conversion` trigger) is deliberately left unevaluated there and falls through to the site phase's own `play-resources` loop (`legal-actions/site.ts`), which had no `card-in-play` check at all. Added the identical `findPlayConditionEffects(eventDef, 'card-in-play')` / `isCardNameInPlayForPlayer` loop (own-play-area, attachment-aware) right after the existing `card-not-in-play` check in that loop — same helper, same semantics, no new effect type. Used by Stone of Erech (tw-334): "Unique. Playable at the Vale of Erech and if the Men of Lamedon are already in play. Discard if the Men of Lamedon leave play." — `play-condition card-in-play` "Men of Lamedon" + `play-target site` (`{ name: "Vale of Erech" }`) + `discard-on-card-leaves-play` (`filter { card.name: "Men of Lamedon" }`).

- `modify-attack` `grantAttackerChoosesDefenders` + `bodyCheckModifier`, and **multiple from-hand `modify-attack` effects on one card** (Adûnaphel Unleashed le-161) — a dual-mode CvCC-aware short event, the first card to declare two independent `modify-attack fromHand` effects. `modifyAttackActions` (`legal-actions/combat.ts`) and `handleModifyAttack` (`combat-actions.ts`) previously located a card's from-hand effect with a bare `.find(type === 'modify-attack' && fromHand)`, silently locking onto whichever entry came first regardless of `player`/`when` — a second differently-gated entry was simply unreachable. Both now `filter()` to all such effects and pick the first whose `player` matches the acting side **and** whose `when` (if any) matches, mirroring the established "modes tried in order" pattern (Choking Shadows); `handleModifyAttack` reuses the exact same context builder (`buildPlayedModifyAttackContext`, now exported) so the reducer always applies whichever effect the legal-action generator actually offered. Two new fields: `grantAttackerChoosesDefenders: true` sets `CombatState.attackerChoosesDefenders` and, when `assignmentPhase` is still `'defender'` (guaranteed, since no strikes are assigned yet), redirects it straight to `'attacker'` — the existing CvCC/creature assignment-phase machinery (`cvccAttackerAssignActions`, `handleCombatPass`'s CvCC branch) then runs unmodified from a cold start, exactly as it already does when a creature's own `combat-attacker-chooses-defenders` effect sets the flag at combat creation. `bodyCheckModifier: N` adds to `CombatState.bodyCheckModifier`, the pre-existing attack-wide body-check modifier (previously only ever set once, at creature-attack initiation, from a self-bound `combat-body-check-modifier` effect) — no new consumption code needed, `handleBodyCheckRoll` already folds it into every body check of the combat. `buildPlayedModifyAttackContext` also gained `defender.companySize`/`defender.characterNames` (any attack) and, for CvCC attacks only, `attacker.companySize`/`attacker.characterNames` (resolved from `attackSource.attackingCompanyId`) — letting a card gate on "the only character in her company" from either side without a hardcoded name/race keyword. Card: "Playable on any attack against Adûnaphel the Ringwraith (as your Ringwraith) if she is the only character in her company. The number of strikes of the attack is reduced to one and the attack's body is modified by -2. Alternatively, playable on any attack by a lone Adûnaphel the Ringwraith (as your Ringwraith). You choose defending characters. Any resulting body checks for defending characters are modified by +2. Cannot be duplicated on a given attack." — Mode A: `modify-attack` (`fromHand`, `player: "defender"`, `setStrikesTo: 1`, `bodyModifier: -2`, `when: defender.companySize == 1 AND defender.characterNames $includes "Adûnaphel the Ringwraith"`); Mode B: `modify-attack` (`fromHand`, `player: "attacker"`, `grantAttackerChoosesDefenders: true`, `bodyCheckModifier: 2`, `when: attacker.companySize == 1 AND attacker.characterNames $includes "Adûnaphel the Ringwraith"`); plus `duplication-limit scope: "attack"` (shared across both modes, keyed by card definition ID).

- `cancel-attack` `cancelsRemainingSiteAttacks` + `influenceAtSiteModifier` (Riven Gate as-98) — two new sibling fields for a from-hand `cancel-attack` card that cancels a site automatic-attack and leaves rest-of-turn site effects behind. `applyEffect`'s `cancel-attack` branch (`apply-dispatcher.ts`), after `resolveCancelAttackEntry`, sets `SitePhaseState.autoAttacksSkipped = true` when `cancelsRemainingSiteAttacks` is set and `state.phaseState.phase === Phase.Site` — the same "sequence abandoned" flag Farmer Maggot's site-swap and Burglary's success set (§9e `siteSwap`/`burglary-attempt`), so `handleSiteAutomaticAttacks` treats the rest of the company's automatic-attack sequence as done with no further combat: "All automatic-attacks at the site are canceled" (not just the one being faced). `influenceAtSiteModifier: N` adds a turn-scoped `influence-at-site-modifier` {@link ActiveConstraint} — the same constraint kind Blasting Fire (wh-51) installs via `add-constraint` — for the defending company's current site (resolved from `defendingCompanyId`/`defendingPlayerId` captured before the cancel clears `state.combat`), which `legal-actions/site.ts` already sums into every faction-influence attempt at that site regardless of which player is influencing. Used by Riven Gate (as-98): "Magic. Sorcery. Playable on a sorcery-using character when facing the automatic-attack at a Border-hold. All automatic-attacks at the site are canceled, and any influence attempt against a faction at the site this turn is modified by +2. Unless he is a Ringwraith, he makes a corruption check modified by -4." — `cancel-attack` (`requiredSkill: "sorcery"`, `cost: { check: "corruption", modifier: -4 }`, `costExemptRace: "ringwraith"` — the as-102 Tormented Earth shape verbatim, `cancelsRemainingSiteAttacks: true`, `influenceAtSiteModifier: 2`, `when: { attack.source: "automatic-attack", site.type: "border-hold" }`).

- `fireItemSelfPlayCorruptionCheck` — an item's own `on-event: self-enters-play` → `enqueue-corruption-check` effect, fired on its newly attached bearer the moment the item is played (`reducer-site.ts`'s `handleSitePlayHeroResource`, right alongside the existing `fireItemPlayCorruptionChecks` Greed-constraint firing for *other* characters at the site). Mirrors the short-event/permanent-event `self-enters-play` → `enqueue-corruption-check` handling already used by Vilya (tw-358) and siblings, extended to items so "bearer makes a corruption check when this item is played" needs no bespoke plumbing. Used by Wizard's Ring (tw-363), completed alongside its `no-transfer`/`no-store` `play-flag`s (the Ent-draughts tw-227 precedent, reused unchanged here) for "Cannot be stored, stolen, or transferred." "May not be stolen" again has no engine counterpart to gate, for the same reason tw-227 documents: no mechanic in this codebase moves an item between players without the owner's consent.

- `reveal-deck-choose-attacker` + `play-condition` `card-player-deck-size` (Long Dark Reach dm-70) — a hazard short-event that reveals the top of the **card-player's own** play deck (unlike `reveal-deck-choose-penalty` ba-16's opponent-deck reveal) and forces a named creature to attack the target company outside the normal hazard-limit/keying system entirely. `card-player-deck-size` is the "you" counterpart to `active-player-deck-size` (dm-63) — it gates on whichever player is actually declaring the play (`cardPlayerDeckSize`, `reducer-utils.ts`), needed because a hazard short-event's "you" is the *non*-active hazard player, not the moving company's owner; `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`'s play-condition dispatch was generalized to look up each `requires` kind independently (`findPlayConditionEffect`) rather than taking whichever `play-condition` effect came first, so a card can now carry both a `site-path` and a `card-player-deck-size` gate together. A revealed candidate is eligible when it is a hazard-creature whose race is one of `alwaysEligibleRaces` or any non-unique creature, AND its printed `keyedTo` offers a non-Coastal-Sea region — reusing `creatureHasNonCoastalRegionKeying` (newly exported from `movement-hazard.ts`, previously private to A Pack at the Door tw-497's identical eligibility check). With at least one eligible candidate the choice is mandatory (`reveal-deck-choose-attacker` pending resolution, no pass); with none, every revealed card is shuffled straight back to the top of the deck. On resolution (`engine/long-dark-reach.ts` `buildLongDarkReachCombat`) the chosen creature attacks the company normally (no solo-defender restriction), bypassing its own keying check for legality — whether it *would* have been legally keyed is still evaluated, via the newly-exported `creatureIsNormallyPlayableOnCompany` (wraps the same `findCreatureKeyingMatches` the ordinary M/H hazard-creature-play path uses), purely to apply `unplayableProwessPenalty`. The attack is spawned directly by chain resolution rather than through the hazard-limit-charging `play-hazard` action, so it never counts against the limit. The creature attacks "in place" — never extracted from the deck — exactly like `hunt-attack` (dm-143): a new `long-dark-reach-attack` `AttackSource` variant is excluded from `attackSourceCreatureInstanceId`'s generic disposal (default case), and `combat-finalize.ts` carries its own disposal block mirroring `hunt-attack`'s (kill pile on defeat, or the card-player's discard pile under detainment). The unused revealed cards are shuffled among themselves and returned to the top of the deck as part of building the combat; the chosen card is left resting directly beneath them. Card: "Playable on a moving company with at least one Wilderness [{w}] in its site path if you have at least 10 cards in your play deck. Reveal the top seven cards of your play deck. One revealed Nazgûl, Dragon, or a non-unique creature of your choice must immediately attack the company regardless of its playability requirements (not count against the hazard limit). The creature must be playable in a region besides Coastal Sea [{c}]. If the creature could not normally be played on the company, modify its prowess by -4. Shuffle all unused cards and return them to the top of your play deck." — `play-condition` `site-path` (`sitePath.wildernessCount >= 1`) + `play-condition` `card-player-deck-size` (`minDeckSize: 10`) + `reveal-deck-choose-attacker` (`count: 7`, `alwaysEligibleRaces: ["ringwraith", "dragon"]`, `requireNonCoastalKeying: true`, `unplayableProwessPenalty: -4`).

- `cancel-attack` `roll` from a **hand-played** short event (Trickery td-159) — previously `roll` (2d6-vs-threshold, optional `skillBonus`) only fired from the in-play-faction cancel path (`handleCancelAttackByInPlayFaction`, Going Ever Under Dark ba-37 / Crept Along Carefully ba-29). `applyEffect`'s `cancel-attack` branch (`apply-dispatcher.ts`) now checks `effect.roll` first: instead of calling `resolveCancelAttackEntry` immediately, it enqueues the same generic `dice-check` (roller = the defending player, `onPass: { type: "cancel-current-attack" }`, `continuation: { kind: "chain-entry", match: "source" }` so the chain entry marks resolved once the roll settles) used by the in-play path, sharing its skillBonus-counting logic. This lets a from-hand `cancel-attack` combine `requiredSkill`/`when` (the existing hand-play gating in `cancelAttackActions`, `legal-actions/combat.ts` — no cost, so any character with the skill in the company suffices, same as Many Turns and Doublings td-132) with a non-automatic roll-to-cancel. Used by Trickery (td-159): "Scout only against an Orc, Troll, Man, Elf, Slayer, Awakened Plant, or Giant attack against his company. Make a roll; if the result is greater than 5, the attack is canceled." — `requiredSkill: "scout"`, `when: { "enemy.race": { "$in": [...] } }`, `roll: { threshold: 5, comparison: "gt" }` (no `skillBonus`).
- `on-event: self-enters-play` → `set-character-status` `target: "company"` for a resource **short event** (Narya tw-290) — extends the existing single-character `set-character-status` self-enters-play handler (`applyShortEventOnEntersPlay`, `reducer-events.ts`) with a company-wide branch mirroring the grant-action `target: "company"` shape already used by Strangling Coils (ba-76)/Herb-lore (dm-136), but reached from the inline resource-short-event path instead of a grant-action activation. The target company is resolved from the played-on character (`action.targetCharacterId`, the card's own `play-target`), then every member is passed through the same tapped-only gate the single-character branch uses (`untapped` only applies to a currently-`Tapped` member; already-`Untapped` and `Inverted`/wounded members are left alone) — so "untap all unwounded characters in the company" needs no separate wounded-exclusion check, the tapped-gate already excludes both wounded and already-untapped members as a side effect. Card: "Playable on Gandalf. +4 prowess, +1 body, +2 direct influence for the rest of the turn. Immediately untap all unwounded characters in Gandalf's company. Gandalf makes a corruption check modified by -5." — `play-target` (character, `target.name: "Gandalf"`), three `character-stat-modifier` `add-constraint` effects (prowess/body/direct-influence, scope turn — the Vilya tw-358 shape), the new `set-character-status target: "company" status: "untapped"`, and `enqueue-corruption-check` `modifier: -5`.
- `strike-modifier` `cancel: true` — Halfling Stealth (tw-252): "Hobbit only. Cancel one strike against the Hobbit." No new engine work; reuses the Orc Stealth (le-217) cancel-mode `filter` mechanism with a single-clause filter (`{ "target.race": "hobbit" }`, no skill component), confirming the mode generalizes below le-217's `$and` race+skill shape.

- `CreatureKeyRestriction.when` gains `hazardsEncountered` context (Mûmak (Oliphant) tw-66) — exposes the target company's `MovementHazardPhaseState.hazardsEncountered` (the same already-faced-creature-name list backing `followsAttackRaces`/`deriveFacedRaces`) as a plain array in the `when` context built by both `findCreatureKeyingMatches` (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) and `checkCreatureKeying` (`mh-hazard-play.ts`), so a keyedTo entry can gate on a *named* companion creature having already attacked the same company this turn via the existing generic `$includes` operator — no new field or match method needed. Used for "may also be played (on the same turn and on the same company as X) keyed to …" clauses that name a specific companion card (as opposed to `followsAttackRaces`, which matches by race). Card: "Animals. Two strikes. May be played keyed to Dagorlad, Gorgoroth, Horse Plains, Ithilien, Khand, Nûrn, Ûdun; and may also be played at sites in these regions. May also be played (on the same turn and on the same company as Corsairs of Umbar) keyed to Andrast, Anfalas, Belfalas, Lebennin; and at Ruins & Lairs [{R}] and Shadow-holds [{S}] in these regions. This card has no effect on a minion player." — base entry `{ regionNames: [7 names], siteInRegionNames: [same 7 names] }` (the Smaug-cycle "sites in these regions" shape); alt entry `{ regionNames: [4 names], when: { "hazardsEncountered": { "$includes": "Corsairs of Umbar" } } }`; alt entry `{ siteTypes: ["ruins-and-lairs", "shadow-hold"], when: { "$and": [ { "destinationSite.region": { "$in": [4 names] } }, { "hazardsEncountered": { "$includes": "Corsairs of Umbar" } } ] } }` (the Huorn tw-45 region-scoped site-type shape, additionally gated on the companion attack); plus the pre-existing `play-restriction unplayable-when opponent.alignment $in [ringwraith, balrog]` for "no effect on a minion player".

- `CreatureKeyRestriction.when` gains `destinationSite.siteType` context, and `findCreatureKeyingMatches`'s `destSiteCard` (the offering-side `when`-context builder) gains the same by-name/alignment fallback `checkCreatureKeying` already had (Beorning Toll le-62) — the base `region`/`sitePath` fields on `destinationSite` only ever resolved from an **attached** `destinationSite` instance on the offering side, so a `when` clause referencing them silently saw `undefined` whenever a keying entry was evaluated before the company's destination instance existed (the same class of gap `checkCreatureKeying`'s own by-name fallback, added for Rain-drake td-57/Nameless Thing dm-109, already closed on the validating side — `findCreatureKeyingMatches` had drifted out of lockstep with it, only `siteInRegionNames`/`siteKeywords`/`adjacentToSite*` had their own local by-name resolution). Fixing `destSiteCard` itself (rather than adding a fourth local fallback) also makes `destinationSite.sitePath.*Count` conditions correctly see a by-name-resolved site on the offering side. Used to encode "and may also be played at non-Haven sites in these regions": a second `keyedTo` entry pairs `siteInRegionNames` (the Smaug-cycle "sites in these regions" shape) with `when: { "destinationSite.siteType": { "$ne": "haven" } }`, while the base `regionNames` entry (no `when`) stays unconditional — the printed "non-Haven" qualifier narrows only the site-arrival extension, not the ordinary path-based region keying (a company that merely travels through a listed region to a Haven elsewhere is still tolled). Card: "Men. Each character in the company faces one strike (detainment against covert and hero companies). May be played keyed to Anduin Vales, Wold & Foothills, High Pass, and Redhorn Gate; and may also be played at non-Haven sites in these regions. May not be played against a company containing a character with Beorn's House as a home site." — `combat-one-strike-per-character` + `combat-detainment` (hero, or covert fallen-wizard) + `play-condition target-company` (`$not company.homeSites: "Beorn's House"`), the same shape as its twin Horse-lords (le-78) (which never exercises the site-in-region path in its own test, so the gap went unnoticed there).

- `add-constraint` `constraint: "nazgul-boost-pending"` + a from-hand `modify-attack` sibling, and the paired permanent `nazgul-boost-used` marker (Fell Beast tw-33) — a hazard short-event with two independent, mutually-exclusive modes for boosting a Nazgûl (CRF: "Fell Beast can be played and resolved before any Nazgûl is played with it. A Nazgûl must be played as the first declared action in the chain of effects following the resolution of Fell Beast, or else this card is returned to its player's hand. This card can be played on an existing Nazgûl attack, but the extra playability this card provides would not apply."). Mode A (standalone, no target) is a plain `on-event self-enters-play` → `add-constraint` (scope `company-mh-phase`, target the company Fell Beast was played against — the existing generic `applyAddConstraintFromOnEvent`/`buildConstraintKind` path needed only a new constraint-kind case, no dispatcher changes) whose payload (`race`, `strikesModifier`, `prowessModifier`, `grantAttackerChoosesDefenders: true`, optional `keyingRegionTypes`/`keyingSiteTypes`) is consumed the moment a matching-race hazard-creature is actually played against that company: `mh-hazard-play.ts`'s creature-handling branch looks up the constraint, removes it, and folds `strikesBonus`/`prowessBonus`/`grantAttackerChoosesDefenders` into the `ChainEntryPayload` (`type: "creature"`, two new optional fields alongside the pre-existing `prowessBonus`), consumed in `chain-reducer.ts` exactly like Summons from Long Sleep's `prowessBonus` (added into `effectiveStrikes`/`effectiveProwess`) and OR'd into the `resolveAttackerChoosesDefenders` call that already handles a creature's own `combat-attacker-chooses-defenders` effect. The keying grant is a **synthetic extra `CreatureKeyRestriction` entry** appended to `def.keyedTo` at both consulting sites (`findCreatureKeyingMatches` in `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts` — the offering side — and `checkCreatureKeying` in `mh-hazard-play.ts` — the validating side, kept in lockstep as always) whenever an active `nazgul-boost-pending` constraint targets the company and matches the creature's race — letting a Nazgûl be keyed to a Shadow-land/Shadow-hold *in addition to* its own printed `keyedTo`, without touching the generic keying-match loop itself. "Cannot be duplicated on a given Nazgûl" is enforced by a second, **permanent** (`until-cleared`, never auto-cleared) constraint kind, `nazgul-boost-used` (target: the creature's owning player, payload: the boosted creature's definition id) — `hasNazgulBoostBeenUsed`/`markNazgulBoostUsed` (`engine/pending.ts`) gate both the keying-grant offer/validation and the Mode-A consumption, so a second Fell Beast can never re-boost the same uniquely-named Nazgûl even after it cycles back through the discard pile and is replayed later in the game. If the company's M/H phase ends with the Mode-A constraint still unconsumed (no matching creature was played), `finalizeCompanyMH` (`mh-hazard-play.ts`) — right before its existing `company-mh-end` `sweepExpired` call — walks any surviving `nazgul-boost-pending` constraints targeting that company and splices Fell Beast's own card instance out of its owner's discard pile back into hand (`returnCardFromDiscardToHand`), rather than letting the sweep silently drop it. Mode B (played on an attack already in progress) is a completely ordinary from-hand `modify-attack` (`player: "attacker"`, `strikesModifier: 1`, `prowessModifier: -2`, `grantAttackerChoosesDefenders: true`, `when: { "enemy.race": "ringwraith" }`, using the already-exposed `enemy.race`/`enemy.name` fields from `buildPlayedModifyAttackContext`), needing zero new engine code — except that the pre-existing "a short event whose only M/H-relevant effect is a from-hand `modify-attack` has no open M/H play, offer on-guard placement instead" suppression (`legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`) had to gain an exception: it now checks for a sibling `on-event self-enters-play` → `add-constraint` effect and skips the suppression when one is present, since Fell Beast is the first card to combine a genuine standalone open-play mode with a from-hand combat-modifier mode on the same card. `duplication-limit scope: "attack"` (the Adûnaphel Unleashed le-161 shape) covers "cannot stack two copies on the same live attack" for Mode B; the permanent per-creature marker above covers the cross-turn "on a given Nazgûl" reading Mode A actually needs.

- `stat-modifier` `target: "all-characters"` gated on `target.race` + `$not { target.skills $includes … }` (Night tw-69) — confirms the effective-stats `all-characters` context (`buildEffectiveStatsContext`, `recompute-derived.ts`) exposes `target.skills` (the character's *effective* skill set, via `charInfo`) alongside `target.race`, so a race-plus-skill-exclusion gate needs no new engine work, only the `$not` condition wrapper already used elsewhere (The Will of Sauron tw-100). Card: "Environment. The prowess of each non-ranger Dúnadan is modified by -1. Additionally, if Doors of Night is in play, the prowesses of all attacks are modified by +1 and the prowess of each Man and Dúnadan is modified by -1. Cannot be duplicated." — three `stat-modifier` effects: a base -1 `target: all-characters` gated `{ "$and": [{ "target.race": "dunadan" }, { "$not": { "target.skills": { "$includes": "ranger" } } }] }`; an unconditional (no race gate) +1 `target: all-attacks` gated `{ "inPlay": "Doors of Night" }` (the plain Clouds tw-22 shape, without the automatic-attack/agent exclusions since this card's "all attacks" is unrestricted); and a second -1 `target: all-characters` gated `{ "$and": [{ "target.race": { "$in": ["man", "dunadan"] } }, { "inPlay": "Doors of Night" }] }` — additive with the base clause (a non-ranger Dúnadan is -2 total with Doors of Night in play, a ranger Dúnadan only -1), plus `duplication-limit` scope game.

## Not implemented (type-only)

- `play-target: company` filter fields `company.atUnderDeepsSurfaceSite` / `company.siteUntapped` + `haven-return-option` `requiresMovedToKeyword` (Ancient Stair dm-115) — generalizes the Great-road (tw-249) end-of-turn site-replacement primitive to a non-Haven origin site, and adds a keyword gate on whether the return option is actually offered. `company.atUnderDeepsSurfaceSite` (`endOfOrgEligibility`'s company filter context, `legal-actions/organization.ts`) reuses `isUnderDeepsSurfaceSite` (`legal-actions/organization-companies.ts`, previously Tempest of Fire ba-77's exclusion check) to test whether the company's current site is the roll-0 surface entrance named in some Under-deeps site's `adjacentSites` map; `company.siteUntapped` reads `company.currentSite.status`. The `haven-return-option` constraint kind (`types/pending.ts`) gained an optional `requiresMovedToKeyword` field, populated from the new `AddConstraintAction.requiresMovedToKeyword` (`types/effects.ts`, the `requiresDestinationSiteType`/td-135 shape) when installing the constraint (`reducer-events.ts`). Unlike Great-road's unconditional offer, `havenReturnActions` (`legal-actions/end-of-turn.ts`) now checks this field against the company's **final** site definition's `keywords` before offering the `haven-return` action — the check runs at end-of-turn offer time (not at play time), since the company's destination for the turn is unknown when an end-of-org card is played. `handleHavenReturn`/`removeConstraint` (`reducer-end-of-turn.ts`) needed no change: its departure/origin site-card lifecycle logic (CoE 2.IV.viii) was already origin-type-agnostic, only branching on whether the *departure* site is a Haven, which was never true for dm-115's Under-deeps departure anyway. CRF ruling confirms the shape: "You can play Ancient Stair from Morannon, move to The Under-galleries, and zip back to Morannon at the end of the turn." Card: "Playable only at the end of the organization phase on a company that starts its turn at an untapped adjacent site of an Under-deeps site. Opponent may draw up to twice the normal number of cards for this company during the movement/hazard phase. If company moved to an Under-deeps site, at the end of the turn the company may replace its site card with the site card at which it began the turn." — `play-window` organization/`end-of-org`, `play-target` company (`filter: { "$and": [company.atUnderDeepsSurfaceSite, company.siteUntapped] }`), `on-event self-enters-play` → `add-constraint hazard-draw-multiplier` (`scope: company-mh-phase`, `value: 2`, the tw-249 shape verbatim) + `add-constraint haven-return-option` (`scope: turn`, `requiresMovedToKeyword: "under-deeps"`).

- `on-event: attack-not-canceled` → `reveal-hand-cards-per-character` (Crebain tw-25) — the reveal-side sibling of `company-tap-characters` in the same `attack-not-canceled` scan (`combat-finalize.ts`), fired unconditionally at combat finalization (cancellation short-circuits before `finalizeCombat` runs, so reaching it already is the "not canceled" test). Picks `min(defending company's post-attack character count, defender's hand size)` random cards from the defending player's hand (seeded `shuffle` off `state.rng`, the same slice-after-shuffle pattern `reveal-remove-from-discard` uses on a discard pile) and reveals their identity via `revealInstances` — the cards stay in hand, only their visibility changes (`GameState.handRevealedInstances`, not a move). Zero defending characters or an empty hand reveals nothing, no error. Paired with the existing `combat-one-strike-per-character` (Wandering Eldar le-97 / Watcher in the Water le-99 precedent: "Each character in the company faces one strike" needs no new engine work, just the effect on the card). Card: "Animals. Each character in the company faces one strike. After the attack, the defender must reveal one random card from his hand for each character in the defending company." `keyedTo` is the Barrow-wight (le-61/tw-15) combined-entry shape: one entry listing both `regionTypes` (border/wilderness/shadow/dark, one each) and `siteTypes` (ruins-and-lairs/shadow-hold/dark-hold) as OR'd alternatives, matching `{b}{w}{s}{d}{R}{S}{D}`.

- `force-agent-attack` + `discard-unrevealed-on-guard` (Ordered to Kill dm-152) — two new global, unconditional rules-changing effect types for an in-play permanent event. `force-agent-attack`: while any in-play copy exists (`agentAttackIsMandatory`, `reducer-utils.ts`, scans both players' `cardsInPlay`), `declareAgentAttackActions` (`legal-actions/site.ts`) omits the `pass` action whenever it offers a `declare-agent-attack` action for a *revealed* agent standing at the company's current site — the hazard player's normal option to skip an agent attack (CoE 2.V.iii) is removed, forcing the declaration. Face-down agents stay optional; `pass` remains available when only face-down options are offered. The engine still supports at most one declared agent attack per site visit (declare-agent-attack → resolve-attacks → play-resources does not loop back for a second agent) — a pre-existing architectural limitation this effect does not attempt to lift; it only removes the choice to skip the single opportunity already offered. `discard-unrevealed-on-guard`: while any in-play copy exists (`unrevealedOnGuardDiscarded`, `reducer-utils.ts`, same scan shape), `returnOnGuardCardsToHand` (`reducer-site.ts` site-phase cleanup) routes every company's leftover `onGuardCards` to the hazard player's discard pile instead of their hand — every card still in `onGuardCards` at cleanup is unrevealed by construction (a reveal always removes it from that list), so no extra filtering is needed. The card's remaining text reuses shipped primitives: `duplication-limit` (scope `game`, max 1) and `on-event: play-deck-exhausted` → `move` self to `discard`, matching the Prone to Violence (ba-42) precedent.
- `tap-sites-in-play`'s `sitePath` context now includes `coastalCount`/`freeCount`/`borderCount` alongside the existing `wildernessCount`/`shadowCount`/`darkCount` (`applyTapSitesInPlayOnResolve`, `chain-reducer.ts`, now built via the shared `regionTypeCounts` helper from `reducer-utils.ts` instead of a hand-rolled subset) — no behavior change for existing consumers (Foul Fumes tw-36, Long Winter le-117), just exposing the fields their `sitePath.*Count` conditions never needed. Used by Storms of Ossë (tw-92): "Environment. Playable if Doors of Night is in play. Each non-Haven/non-Darkhaven site in play with a Coastal Sea [{c}] in its site path is tapped. Cannot be duplicated." — `play-condition` (`requires: "card-in-play"`, `cardName: "Doors of Night"`, the Snowstorm tw-91 precedent: the *whole card* is gated, unlike tw-36/le-117 where only the site-tap clause carries a `requiresInPlay`), `tap-sites-in-play` (`condition: { site.type != "haven", sitePath.coastalCount >= 1 }`, no alignment gate — unlike tw-36, this card taps hero and minion sites alike), `duplication-limit` scope game.
- `restore-item` grant-action + `restored-item-stats` + `face-all-strikes-option` (Horn of Defiance td-183) — **fully implemented**, three new primitives. `restore-item` is an ordinary bearer-owned `grant-action` (`cost: { discard: "named-stored-card", discardCardName: "Reforging" }`, `apply: { type: "restore-item" }`) — the generalized `discard: "named-stored-card"` cost now pays through `applyCost` (`cost-evaluator.ts`) for any grant-action, not just the `fromStored` shape (Andúril tw-192): the chosen stored card's instance ID rides `activate-granted-action.targetCardId` into `CostContext.discardTargetId`. The `restore-item` apply (`runGrantApply`, `grant-action-apply.ts`) flags the source item's own `ItemInPlay.restored`, leaving it on its bearer (unlike Andúril's `place-source-with-item`, which relocates the source). `char.items` grant-action scanning (`legal-actions/organization.ts`) offers one activation per matching stored candidate in the bearer's kill pile, withdrawn once `item.restored` is set. `restored-item-stats` (`{ marshallingPoints, corruptionPoints }`) is read directly by `recompute-derived.ts`'s per-item MP/CP loops whenever `item.restored` is true, substituting for the printed values (other modifiers still layer on top). `face-all-strikes-option` offers an `assign-strike` action carrying `allStrikes: true` for the bearer exactly when `combat.strikeAssignments.length === 0` and `combat.strikesTotal > 1` (`assignStrikeActions`, `legal-actions/combat.ts`); `handleAssignStrike` (`reducer-combat.ts`) treats `action.allStrikes` like `combat.forceSingleTarget`, building one separate-sequence `StrikeAssignment` (`excessStrikes: 0`) per remaining strike instead of the "excess strikes" -1-prowess pool a repeat assignment would otherwise produce, and stamps `forceSingleTarget: true` on the resulting combat state. Card: "Unique. Hoard item. +2 direct influence. A stored Reforging may be placed with this item to 'restore' it. Once restored, Horn of Defiance gives 3 marshalling points and 2 corruption points. If its bearer is the first to face a strike, that character may choose to face all strikes of an attack. The character faces a separate strike sequence for each strike." — `item-play-site` (hoard filter) + `stat-modifier` direct-influence +2 + the three primitives above.

- `multi-strike-option` (Many Foes He Fought td-131) — **fully implemented**, new primitive; see `docs/card-effects-dsl.md` §80 for the full walkthrough. A hand-played short event, gated to the defender's pre-assignment window like `halve-strikes`/`protect-from-strike-assignment` (`multiStrikeOptionActions`, `legal-actions/combat.ts`; `enable-multi-strike-option` action; `handleEnableMultiStrikeOption`, `combat-actions.ts`), that stamps `combat.multiStrikeSkill` with the effect's `requiredSkill` for the rest of the attack. While set, `assignStrikeActions`'s defender branch also offers `assign-strike` with `extraSequence: true` for any company character already holding ≥1 strike this attack whose `skills` include that skill — repeatable, so the defender may take "any number." Unlike a plain repeat assignment (which merges into `StrikeAssignment.excessStrikes`, a pool counted toward `combat.strikesTotal`), `handleAssignStrike` gives `action.extraSequence` a dedicated branch that always appends a *new* entry (`excessStrikes: 0`, a genuinely separate strike sequence per CoE 3.i.5) with `strikeProwessBonus`/`strikeBodyPenalty` set to `-priorCount` (how many strikes that character already holds) — reusing the same per-entry modifier fields Risky Blow (tw-319) already established for prowess/body penalties, so no new resolution code was needed in `combat-strike.ts`/`combat-actions.ts`, only new callers of the existing fields. Card: "If defender chooses a warrior to be the target of a strike from an attack, that character may choose to face any number of the strikes from that attack. The character suffers a cumulative -1 prowess/-1 body for each additional strike faced. The character faces a separate strike sequence for each strike." — `{ "type": "multi-strike-option", "requiredSkill": "warrior" }`.
- `add-constraint check-modifier target:"player"` gains `autoPass`/`lasting`/`constraintWhen`, and `enqueue-corruption-check`'s `when` context gains `target.siteType` (Nenya tw-291) — **fully implemented**. Previously the `target: "player"` `check-modifier` branch (`applyShortEventOnEntersPlay`, `reducer-events.ts`, Terror Heralds Doom ba-78) only wired `check`/`value` onto the constraint kind; it now also forwards `autoPass`, `lasting`, and `constraintWhen` (→ `kind.when`) straight from the `AddConstraintAction` fields those already carry for the character/company-scoped shapes. Consumption is the new part: the corruption-check modifier collector (`corruptionCheckEntryActions`, `legal-actions/pending.ts`) and the corruption-check resolver (`applyCorruptionCheckResolution`, `pending-reducers.ts`) both gained a `target.kind === 'player' && target.playerId === <checking character's controller>` match alongside their existing `target: "character"` match, each re-checking `kind.when` against `{ target: { siteType } }` built from the checking character's current company site (`findCharacterCompany` + `defById`) — so a player-scoped constraint can gate on *where the checking character currently is*, not just who they are. With no `lasting` flag (the default), the constraint is consumed by the first check whose `when` matches, giving "any ONE corruption check by any of your characters, if `when` holds, auto-succeeds" with no per-character targeting needed at play time. `enqueue-corruption-check`'s own `when` (`applyShortEventOnEntersPlay`) gained the matching `target.siteType` field (alongside the pre-existing `target.race`) so two mutually-exclusive `on-event self-enters-play` entries can pick a different `modifier` depending on the *target character's* site. Card: "Playable on Galadriel. +2 prowess, +2 body, +2 direct influence for the rest of the turn. Galadriel makes a corruption check modified by -3, by -1 if in a Haven [{H}]. Any one corruption check made by a character not in a Shadow-hold [{S}] or Dark-hold [{D}] is automatically successful." — `play-target` (character, `target.name: "Galadriel"`), three `character-stat-modifier` `add-constraint` effects (the Vilya/Narya shape), two `enqueue-corruption-check` entries gated `when: { "target.siteType": "haven" }` (modifier -1) / `$not` (modifier -3), and one `add-constraint check-modifier target:"player" check:"corruption" value:0 autoPass:true scope:"until-cleared" constraintWhen:{ "$not": { "target.siteType": { "$in": ["shadow-hold","dark-hold"] } } }`.

### `agent-tap-multi-influence` + `agent-influence-boost` — multi-target agent influence grant, and a bankable boost (Good Sense Revolts dm-61)

Good Sense Revolts (dm-61) prints two mutually exclusive functions on one hazard short-event: "Playable on an untapped agent. Tap the agent who may then make an influence attempt against an ally, faction, or character. +4 to influence attempt. +8 if ally, faction, or character is playable at agent's home site. Alternatively, modify an influence attempt by an agent by +4. This card cannot serve both functions. Cannot be played if your opponent is a minion player."

`agent-tap-multi-influence` (mode A) is the multi-target counterpart of `agent-tap-faction-influence` (Twisted Tales dm-96, §71 above): it *grants* any of the hazard player's own untapped agents a rule-10.14 influence attempt against an opponent's ally, faction, or character — the same target kinds the native `agent-tap-influence` character ability covers (`targetKinds`) — without requiring the agent to carry that ability itself, and with no `agentFilter` restriction on dm-61. Instead of dm-96's auto-success tier, its own bonus is tiered on the standard rule-10.14 home-site condition: `attemptBonus` (+4) normally, `attemptBonusAtHomeSite` (+8) when the target shares (character/ally) or is playable at (faction) one of the agent's home sites.

| Field | Required | Description |
|-------|----------|-------------|
| `targetKinds` | yes | Which opponent target kinds the granted attempt may be made against: `character`, `ally`, `faction`. |
| `attemptBonus` | yes | Flat modifier added to the attacker's side of the influence attempt (+4). |
| `attemptBonusAtHomeSite` | no | Replaces `attemptBonus` when the target shares/is playable at one of the agent's home sites (+8). |

```json
{ "type": "agent-tap-multi-influence", "targetKinds": ["character", "ally", "faction"],
  "attemptBonus": 4, "attemptBonusAtHomeSite": 8 }
```

- **Legal actions** (short-event branch of `movementHazardActions`, `legal-actions/movement-hazard.ts`): for character/ally targets, one `play-hazard` action per (untapped agent, opponent character/ally in the *active company* at the agent's current site — reusing `companyTargetSiteName`, the same "agent must be at the company's site" rule the native `agent-tap-influence` ability's own emitter uses); for faction targets, one action per (untapped agent, opponent in-play faction whose `playableAt` names the agent's current site). Blocked when the opponent is a minion/Balrog player.
- **Reducer** (`handleAgentTapMultiInfluence`, `mh-agents.ts`, dispatched from `mh-hazard-play.ts`): taps **and reveals** the agent, discards the event, counts it against the hazard limit, and enqueues the standard `opponent-influence-defend` resolution. Duplicates the native ability's per-target-kind resolution (mind/value, rule-10.14 home-site zeroing, conditional DI) rather than sharing a helper — matching the precedent already set by `handleAgentTapFactionInfluence` duplicating the faction branch. The card's own tiered bonus rides as `boostModifier`, combined with anything folded in via `foldAgentInfluenceBoost` (below).

`agent-influence-boost` (mode B) banks a one-shot `check-modifier` {@link ActiveConstraint} (`check: "influence"`, `when: { reason: "opponent-influence-check" }`, `scope: { kind: "until-cleared" }`) on one of the hazard player's own agents (any tap status — this mode does not itself tap or reveal the target), the same constraint kind Mine or No One's (ba-68, §above) uses. Built directly via `addConstraint` rather than through the ordinary `add-constraint` play-option apply, because CoE 2.IV.vii.3 gives hazard-event `play-target: "character"` the *opponent's* characters by default — the hazard player's own agent sits outside the play-target/play-option chain's reach.

| Field | Required | Description |
|-------|----------|-------------|
| `attemptBonus` | yes | Modifier banked onto the target agent's next qualifying attempt (+4). |

```json
{ "type": "agent-influence-boost", "attemptBonus": 4 }
```

- **Legal actions**: one `play-hazard` action per the hazard player's own agent (any tap status). Blocked when the opponent is a minion/Balrog player (the same gate as mode A — both effects share one `isMinionOrBalrog` check since they are printed on the same card).
- **Reducer** (`handleAgentInfluenceBoost`, `mh-agents.ts`): discards the event, counts the hazard, and calls `addConstraint` with `target: { kind: "character", characterId: agentInstanceId }`. Does not touch the agent's tap/reveal state.
- **Consumption** (`foldAgentInfluenceBoost`, `mh-agents.ts`, mirroring the `check-modifier` fold in `handleOpponentInfluenceAttempt` at reducer-site.ts lines ~4243-4262): scans `state.activeConstraints` for a `check-modifier` targeting the acting agent's character instance whose `when` matches `{ reason: "opponent-influence-check" }`, sums matching `value`s, and returns the consumed ids for the caller to filter out of `activeConstraints`. Wired into both `handleAgentInfluenceAttempt` (the native `agent-tap-influence` ability path) and `handleAgentTapMultiInfluence` (dm-61's own mode A) — so a banked boost applies whether the agent's next attempt comes from its own printed ability or from a second copy of this same card. Not wired into `handleAgentTapFactionInfluence` (dm-96) — out of scope for dm-61's own certification; a future card needing that combination can extend the same helper.

Used by *Good Sense Revolts* (dm-61).

- `ahunt-attack` `underDeepsMove`, real "weapons do not modify prowess" enforcement, and `cancel-card-effects` extended past constraint-only effects (Earth-tremors dm-53) — **fully implemented**. `AhuntAttackEffect` gains an optional `underDeepsMove: boolean` (`types/effects.ts`); `collectMatchingAhuntAttacks` (`engine/mh-steps.ts`) matches it independently of `regionNames`/`regionTypes` (Under-deeps movement has no region path), gated on the company actually moving and its origin or destination carrying the `under-deeps` keyword via a new shared `companyMovesUnderDeeps` helper (`engine/reducer-utils.ts`) — also now used by rule 5.31's `findForcingEnvironment` in place of its old inline check (The Way is Shut dm-98). `buildAhuntCombat` threads the `cannot-be-canceled`/`weapons-ineffective` `combatRules` tokens onto the ahunt `CombatState` the same way site auto-attacks already do. Previously "weapons do not modify prowess against these strikes" only exposed `attack.weaponsIneffective` to one reactive item's own `modify-attack` `when` gate (Dwarven Light-stone dm-168) — it never actually suppressed the defender's own weapon bonus. `computeCombatProwess` (`engine/recompute-derived.ts`) now takes an optional `weaponsIneffective` parameter that drops every prowess `stat-modifier` sourced from a `weapon`-keyworded card before resolving, and `passiveModifyAttackProwessBonus` (`engine/combat-strike.ts`) likewise skips passive `modify-attack` bonuses from weapon items; both are threaded from `resolveStrikeCore` via `combat.weaponsIneffective`. Separately, `cancel-card-effects` previously only reached effects consumed through the `ActiveConstraint`-filtering switch (`applyOneConstraint`, `legal-actions/pending.ts`); a new shared `isCardNameEffectCanceled` helper (`engine/reducer-utils.ts`) — which `constraintSuppressedByCancelEffect` now calls for the constraint path — is also consulted directly at the two mechanisms outside that switch: `grant-extra-mh-phase`'s chain resolution (`chain-reducer.ts`, sets `Company.extraMHPhasePending`) and the `hazard-draw-multiplier` constraint (`mh-steps.ts` `transitionToDrawCards`, read directly rather than through `applyOneConstraint`). Card: "Any company moving to or from an Under-deeps site faces an attack (cannot be canceled): Rock Fall — 1 strike with 7 prowess against each character (weapons do not modify prowess against these strikes). In addition, cancels the effects of Into Dark Tunnels, Old Road, Great Road, and Bridge. Cannot be duplicated." — `ahunt-attack` (`underDeepsMove: true`, 1/7, `race: "special"` since Rock Fall has no printed creature race, `combatRules: ["cannot-be-canceled", "weapons-ineffective"]`), `cancel-card-effects` (`cardNames: ["Into Dark Tunnels", "Old Road", "Great-road", "Bridge"]` — matched against each source card's stored `CardDefinition.name`, hence the hyphenated "Great-road"), and `duplication-limit` scope game.

- `item.restored` resolver context field (Ringil td-184) — `collectCharacterEffects` (`effects/resolver.ts`) exposes `item: { restored: true }` in the resolver context only while collecting that specific item's own effects (never leaking onto the bearer or sibling items), letting a `stat-modifier`'s `when` pair a pre-restore and post-restore variant of the same bonus (`{ "item.restored": { "$ne": true } }` / `{ "item.restored": true }`). Ringil reuses Horn of Defiance's `restore-item` grant-action + `restored-item-stats` (4 MP / 3 CP) primitives above. Card: "Unique. Hoard item. Weapon. +1 body. Warrior only: +1 prowess (to a maximum of 8). A stored Reforging may be placed with this item to 'restore' it. Once restored, Ringil gives 4 marshalling points, 3 corruption points and +5 prowess (to a maximum of 11)." (`prowessModifier` corrected from an unconditional `1` to `0` — the bonus is Warrior-only and now lives entirely in `stat-modifier` effects; `weapon` keyword added, matching the printed "Weapon." text.)

- `GrantedActionConstraintPayload.targets` + `GrantActionTargets.scope: "company-wounded-characters"` (Warm Now Be Heart and Limb td-163) — a "each qualifying character in a company may voluntarily, repeatedly tap to act on a *shrinking* pool of company-mate targets" shape, for a **short event with no bearer** rather than an item/character-card ability. The constraint-based `granted-action` path (Great Ship tw-248's chain-cancel, River's cancel-constraint — previously untargeted, one activation per eligible actor with no `targetCardId`) gained an optional `targets` field mirroring the static `grant-action` effect's own `targets` (`types/effects.ts`'s `GrantedActionConstraintPayload`, and the matching `ActiveConstraint` `granted-action` kind in `types/pending.ts`), threaded through both `add-constraint` builders (`reducer-events.ts` for resource short-events, `constraint-kind.ts` for the hazard/chain path). `emitGrantedActionConstraintActions` (`legal-actions/granted-action-constraints.ts`) now calls the shared `enumerateGrantActionTargets` (exported from `legal-actions/organization.ts`) per eligible actor when `targets` is present, emitting one activation per (actor, candidate) pair instead of a single untargeted one — candidates are recomputed live off current state on every legal-action call, so a character healed by one sage's activation naturally disappears from a later sage's offered targets within the same organization phase, with no snapshot to go stale. The new `"company-wounded-characters"` scope (alongside the pre-existing `"company-characters"`, which admits any non-`Untapped` candidate — correct for an *untap* ability) restricts candidates to genuinely wounded (`Inverted`) company-mates, since a merely `Tapped` character is never a legal heal target; the acting sage can never appear among these candidates anyway, since paying a `tap` cost requires them to be `Untapped`, which is mutually exclusive with `Inverted`. `organizationActions` (`legal-actions/organization.ts`) now also calls `emitGrantedActionConstraintActions` once per company with `phaseKey: "organization"` — previously this emitter was wired only for the movement-hazard call sites (`chain.ts`, `movement-hazard.ts`), so no card had ever offered an organization-phase constraint-granted action before. `endOfOrgEligibility`'s company filter context gained `hasWoundedCharacter` (mirroring `buildTargetCompanyConditionContext`'s existing field of the same name), so a card's `play-target: company` filter can require a wounded company member to exist — CoE 9.1 bars playing a card that could not possibly have an effect. Used by Warm Now Be Heart and Limb (td-163): "Sage only. Ritual. Playable on a company during organization phase. Each sage in company that taps may heal one character (from wounded to tapped). Each sage so tapping makes a corruption check." — `play-window` organization/`end-of-org`, `play-target` company (`filter: { "$and": [ { "company.skills": { "$includes": "sage" } }, { "company.hasWoundedCharacter": true } ] }`), `on-event self-enters-play` → `add-constraint granted-action` (`scope: turn`, `grantedAction: { action: "heal-company-character", phase: "organization", cost: { tap: "character" }, when: { "actor.skills": { "$includes": "sage" } }, targets: { scope: "company-wounded-characters" }, apply: sequence[set-character-status target-character tapped, enqueue-corruption-check] }`). The corruption check apply deliberately omits `target: "target-character"` (unlike The Arkenstone's own use of `enqueue-corruption-check`) — "each sage **so tapping** makes a corruption check" means the tapping sage checks, not the healed target, so the default (actor-checks) behavior is exactly right.
- `enqueue-pending-fetch` gained `mustPlayOrDiscard: boolean` + new `play-or-discard-fetched-item` pending-resolution kind (Dwarven Ring of Bávor's Tribe tw-214) — the "search deck for an item playable at the bearer's site, then it must be played immediately or discarded" pattern shared by the Dwarven Ring cycle's major/greater-item search variants, distinct from `unlockTappedSitePlay` (td-108's "may be immediately played", optional, no blocking) and from the plain minor-item-to-hand variants (tw-218/219, as-123/124, which never force resolution). When set alongside `fetchTo: "hand"`, `handleFetchFromPile` (`reducer-utils.ts`) enqueues a `play-or-discard-fetched-item` {@link PendingResolution} (Shape A) instead of firing `postCorruptionCheck` immediately — the check (if any) is carried on the resolution's own `postCorruptionCheck` field and deferred until the choice resolves. While queued, `topResolutionFor` collapses the actor's legal actions to this resolution's own (Shape A semantics: every other action, including `pass`, is blocked) — computed by `playOrDiscardFetchedItemActions` (`legal-actions/pending.ts`), which calls the ordinary site-phase `playResourcesActions` (now exported from `legal-actions/site.ts`) in full and filters the result to the one fetched card instance, so every existing item-play precedent (recipient choice, site-tap gates, duplication limits, hoard/thorough-search bonuses) applies unchanged; a `discard-card` action for the same instance is always offered alongside, covering both a free decline and the case where no untapped character can carry the item. `applyPlayOrDiscardFetchedItemResolution` (`pending-reducers.ts`) resolves a matching `play-hero-resource` by delegating to `handleSitePlayHeroResource` (now exported from `reducer-site.ts`) unchanged, or a matching `discard-card` by moving the card from hand to the discard pile directly; either branch then dequeues the resolution and enqueues the deferred `postCorruptionCheck`, if any. If the search comes up empty (the actor `pass`es the `fetch-from-pile` step), nothing was fetched to hand, so the existing unconditional-pass codepath in `resolvePendingEffect` fires `postCorruptionCheck` immediately as before — no blocking resolution is created. Card: "Unique. Dwarven Ring. Playable only with a gold ring and after a test indicates a Dwarven Ring. Values in parentheses and brackets apply to a Dwarf bearer. Tap a Dwarf bearer to search your play deck for a greater item playable at the bearer's site. Play this item immediately or discard; reshuffle the play deck. Bearer makes a corruption check modified by +2." — `grant-action` `tw214-search-item` (`activeSitePhase: true` — items' grant-actions are organization-phase-only by default, same opt-in Dragon-lore td-108 uses; `cost: { tap: "bearer" }`, `when: { "bearer.race": "dwarf" }`) whose `apply` is `enqueue-pending-fetch` (`fetchFrom: ["deck"]`, `fetchTo: "hand"`, `fetchShuffle: true`, `playableAtBearerSite: true`, `mustPlayOrDiscard: true`, `postCorruptionCheck: true`, `postCorruptionCheckModifier: 2`, `filter: { cardType: "hero-resource-item", subtype: "greater" }`), plus the cycle-standard `stat-modifier`/`overrides` pair for prowess (2→4) and direct influence (2→7), `stat-modifier corruption-points +2` and `mp-modifier +2` (both `when` dwarf) matching the already-certified Durin's Tribe (tw-216) precedent.
- `company-combat-boost` `boostScope: "company"` (cost-bearing whole-company mode) — decouples the cost-bearing payer from the boost recipients. Previously, presence of `cost` on a `company-combat-boost` effect always meant "boost exactly the one chosen character who pays" (`requiredSkill`/`requiredRace` cost-bearing single-target mode, above). The new optional `boostScope` field (`"payer"` default, or `"company"`) is read only when `cost` is present: `companyCombatBoostActions` (`legal-actions/combat.ts`) still offers one `play-short-event` per company character satisfying `requiredSkill`/`requiredRace` that `canPayCost` accepts (unchanged — the payer selection logic doesn't care about recipients). `handlePlayResourceShortEvent` (`reducer-events.ts`) still charges `action.targetCharacterId` alone (or waives via `costExemptRace`), but with `boostScope: "company"` it does not stop there — it falls through into the same per-member loop the no-`cost` path uses (respecting `filter`/`companyFilter`/`itemFilter` if present), adding an attack-scoped `character-stat-modifier` constraint to every company member. Used by Kindling of the Spirit (tw-262): "Spell. Wizard only. +2 prowess against one attack for all characters in the same company as the Wizard. Wizard makes a corruption check modified by -2." — `requiredRace: "wizard"`, `boostScope: "company"`, `cost: { check: "corruption", modifier: -2 }`. Contrast with Wizard's Fire (tw-360), same shape minus `boostScope` (defaults to `"payer"`): only the Wizard is boosted.

### `war-forges-item-unlocked` site-flag + `fromSideboard` — tap-activated bonus item, sourced beyond hand (War-forges wh-83)

War-forges (wh-83) is a stage permanent-event bound to one of the player's
protected Wizardhavens (`play-target: "site"`, `filter: { effectiveSiteType:
"haven" }`, gated by `play-condition requires: "site-protected"` and a
`player.avatar: { "$ne": "Radagast" }` player-state check), like Guarded
Haven (wh-74) and Saruman's Machinery (wh-120). Unlike wh-120's automatic
`self-enters-play` unlock, War-forges' bonus is **activated**: `{ "type":
"grant-action", "action": "war-forges-unlock-item", "cost": { "tap": "self"
}, "apply": { "type": "add-constraint", "constraint":
